# Plan: Phase 8, workflows and cases

**Source:** `docs/PLAN.md` §2 (Workflows and Cases rows), §3 (`packages/workflow-engine`), §6 Phase 8; ADR 0002 (tiers and honesty), ADR 0005 (report honesty), ADR 0006 (signals). Four research notes were taken on 2026-10-27 for this plan and are committed with it:
- `docs/research/phase8-behaviour.md`: the public model of workflows (triggers, steps, versions, runs, about 45 actions) and of cases (templates, statuses, queues, assignment, SLA, comments, attachments, tags, held objects, search, export, redaction), with 37 workflow, 26 action and 33 case gap rows;
- `phase8-workflow-engine.md`: a durable engine on the existing stack (persisted state machine over Postgres, BullMQ as a wake-up only), triggers through the event outbox, waits, joins, timers, schedules, the expression and template threat model, engine licences, fairness and retention, with a benchmark on the pinned Postgres image;
- `phase8-actions-sandbox.md`: Evaluate Code in QuickJS, the SSRF-safe HTTPS request against the IANA registries, secrets, email, SMS and Slack, the authority a run acts with, effect keys, and GDPR Article 22 for automatic declines;
- `phase8-editor-cases.md`: the `@xyflow/react` editor and its accessibility, the definition format, case data model, queues and assignment, SLA business time, comments, attachments and scanning, export and print, and the reviewer's compare panel.

A fact-check pass re-read 110 load-bearing facts at their primary sources: 107 confirmed, 2 corrected in place (the run error types are examples, not a closed list; React Flow's assertive live region comes from its build, not its accessibility page), and 1 not re-measured (the engine benchmark, which means running a container). Each note ends with its table. Downloads made during research stay out of git.

**Complexity:** Very large. Three Workflows of at most ten agents each:
- **8A, the engine and its actions (ten agents):** contracts and plumbing for workflows, versions, runs, triggers, secrets and decisions; then, in parallel, the pure engine (definition checks, the interpreter, conditions, templates, schedules) and the Evaluate Code sandbox; then the API runtime, publishing, triggers, the object actions, and the egress actions (HTTPS, email, SMS, Slack, secrets), each after its Stage 2 dependency; then integration.
- **8B, cases (nine agents):** contracts and plumbing for cases, templates, queues, comments, attachments and SLA; then, in parallel, the pure case logic (status machine, assignment, business time, search) and the vision rendition endpoint; then the case API, the case files and exports, and the case side of workflows (case actions, the case trigger, the steps Phases 6 and 7 deferred that need cases); then integration.
- **8C, console and review (ten agents):** the workflow editor and runs console, and the case console with the reviewer's compare panel; then a review of the whole phase, a verifier and path-owned fixers.

**Why this split.** The engine is useful without cases (every action on inquiries, reports, lists, the graph and transactions), and cases are useful without workflows (queues, assignment, SLA, comments), but the case actions and the case trigger need both. 8A therefore builds the engine with an action registry that 8B extends, and 8B adds the case actions and the case trigger to it, as 7B indexed what 7A recorded. Each Workflow freezes its own contracts in its own Stage 1a. The console needs every API document and opens 8C, followed by the review of the whole phase.

**Stage 1b carries plumbing only**, as in Phases 6 and 7: migrations, repository and route stubs with frozen schemas, masking lists, shared hooks, queues and job stubs, configuration. Every algorithm stays with its owner behind a declared interface. Stage 2 starts as soon as the Stage 1a commits it needs land, beside Stage 1b, and each Stage 3 agent waits for its family's Stage 1b checkpoint and its named Stage 2 dependency (F07). Each checkpoint is committed with every suite green, so a pause resumes from the last one.

**Phase 7 dependency.** Phase 8 starts only once `docs/PROGRESS.md` records Phase 7 done. It builds on:
- the report engine (C69–C75) and its review rule (C84.3), which Run Report and the review recipe replace for installs that use workflows, and which now re-screens custom-list reports on `list.updated` (C127);
- the lists, their matching and `list-match.created` (C97–C99), the graph queries (C102) and its bulk actions (C103), and transactions (C104–C105), whose workflow steps Phase 7 deferred to Phase 8 (C110);
- devices (C91.2), which Trust Device marks (C127.9);
- the Phase 7 field-schema walker in `packages/core/src/transactions/` and the CSV writer in `packages/checks/src/csv/`, which case templates, case fields and case exports reuse (C130, C138);
- the event publisher's transactional `stage()` and the webhook outbox (C5, C14), the fenced claims of C74.1, the `SKIP LOCKED` sweeps and the repeatable-job registry (C54.1), the outbound client and its address guard (C53, C62), the Mailer and the SMS sender with their sandbox sink and limits (C55), the sealed-column helpers and `kek:rewrap`, the redaction services and the C16 retention pass.

Each Stage 1a re-verifies every fact under "Starting point" against the code it finds, and records drift as a "Workflow 8A (or 8B) Stage 1a amendment".

**Review of this plan.** Three adversarial reviews read the first draft:
- privacy, security and law (P01–P43);
- honesty and product coverage (H01–H35);
- feasibility and fit with the code (F01–F37).

Together they found 9 CRITICAL, 34 HIGH, 54 MEDIUM and 18 LOW: 115 in all. `docs/plans/phase-8-review-resolution.md` disposes of each; every one is accepted, and where a reviewer offered alternatives the one taken is named with its reason. Among the findings:
- **Forgery and leaks:** signing workflow requests with a webhook secret would have let any workflow author forge OpenVerify webhooks; run values masked only by marks that compute steps drop would have shown PII to viewers; a sandbox principal could replace production secret values; one QuickJS module served many tenants' evaluations.
- **Unlawful automation:** a static "human step" test would have recorded timeouts, CSV runs and bulk resolutions as human review, skipping the Art. 22 gate; biometric declines on `inquiry.failed` escaped the Art. 9 gate; a reopened inquiry could be declined again by the same automation; block-list adds and transaction declines had no record or gate.
- **False values:** Sync Graph answered `synced: true` on the false premise of synchronous indexing; a timed-out or pending report read `has-match: false`; the condition dialect turned unknown into false or else.
- **Feasibility:** an advisory lock inside `stage()` was not the last lock and could deadlock; messaging ran in a worker built without SMS, SMTP or the API's services; grants containing operator permissions could never be held by an API key; Stage 3 agents started before the Stage 2 logic they call; CI jobs are already within a minute of their timeouts.

**Verification round.** A fourth pass checked the revision. It confirmed 91 of the 115 dispositions, found 24 only partly resolved, and raised 28 new findings (11 HIGH, 13 MEDIUM, 4 LOW). All 52 are now resolved, and none is rejected. The main fixes: the wait race closed with commit-visibility snapshots and a lock only in engine transactions; an internal event cause (run, principal, test run, bulk) carried into `stage()` through a request-scoped context; list versions and list-caused report runs for the custom-list re-screen; a consent key for automated decisions; a list `purpose`, a decisions read route and contest routes for non-inquiry subjects; on-null for every condition position; a publisher-rights sweep; one messenger port for every message.

The main changes of the first round: workflow request signatures of their own; fail-closed run masking under a `workflow-runs:read` scope outside the viewer role; secrets bound to their environment and placements; a fresh module per evaluation and framed child I/O; `solely-automated` decided at run time and gates on every adverse step; biometric detection from triggers and statuses; `inquiry.reopened` with human review pending; outputs that carry status, coverage and completeness, with nullable paths that must declare `on-null`; PII taint through every compute step; waits matched after commit with no lock in domain transactions; the worker's own service container and messaging bindings; grants of API scopes plus a PII flag; `api-triggers` as 8A's tenth agent; named Stage 2 dependencies; CI split before Stage 2.

## Summary

OpenVerify decides inquiries through its decision engine and review rules today, and nothing else can act on an inquiry, a report, a list or a transaction without a person or an API client. It has no workflows, no cases, no queues and no secrets store. Phase 8 adds them, each at its tier (ADR 0002, extended to workflows by ADR 0007):
- **R** (real logic): the engine and every trigger and step type; every action on an OpenVerify object (inquiries, reports, lists, the graph, transactions, devices, tags, accounts, cases); Evaluate Code as an execution engine; the HTTPS request and its guard; cases with their templates, statuses, queues, assignment, SLA, comments, attachments, tags, held objects, search, export and print.
- **A** (an adapter with a deterministic sandbox provider): email, SMS and Slack messages, and the attachment scanner. Each sandbox provider records what it would have sent or found and is refused in production.
- **H** (heuristic): no new heuristic. Run Report and Get List Match pass H-tier results from earlier phases (fuzzy names, faces, backgrounds, approximate IP data) and are `beta`; their outputs carry each match's method, tier, stage and confidence, decision records keep them, and a production decline whose path reads one needs an explicit acknowledgement (C122.1, C128.3).

What lands:
- **Workflows** (C112–C121): environment-scoped workflows (`wfl_`) with a promote action; a JSON-graph definition validated by zod and a pure graph check; our own JSONLogic dialect for conditions (labelled ours; the public product publishes no format); value-level `{{ path }}` templates with per-sink encoding; a typed variable model; immutable versions (`wfv_`) with staged rollout (`wfd_`), restore and archive; eight trigger types, including the event trigger fed in the event's own transaction, scheduled runs (`srrun_`) and our recurring trigger; six step types (action, wait, parallel, conditional, loop, module) plus output nodes; runs (`wfr_`) with resume, rerun and cancel; a durable engine over Postgres with BullMQ as a wake-up, fenced claims, per-step effect keys, per-environment fairness and stated limits.
- **Authority** (C121): a `workflow-run` actor; a scope grant derived from each version's steps, bounded by its publisher and by whoever triggers a run; PII egress marked and audited.
- **Actions** (C122–C127): a versioned action registry with a snake_case key per action; Evaluate Code in QuickJS inside a child-process pool with no network, no host functions and no secrets; Make HTTPS Request on the existing outbound client with literal hosts, port and header policies and a request signature of its own that webhook verification refuses; workflow secrets (`wsec_`) sealed per environment and bound to hosts; email, SMS and Slack through adapters with recording sandbox providers; every object action Phase 6 and Phase 7 deferred.
- **Automated decisions** (C128): an `inquiry_decisions` record for every approve and decline; a declared basis and a contest route for a production decline that no person takes part in; biometric declines tied to recorded consent; a `declined → needs_review` reopen transition for human review (a C10 amendment).
- **Cases** (C129–C139): cases (`case_`) on versioned templates (`ctmpl_`) with statuses in the public categories (`initial`, `intermediate`, `end`), resolutions and the Phase 7 field-schema union; queues (`cqueue_`) and assignment (manual, pull, round robin, fewest open, case creator, inquiry creator) by assignment policies (`cap_`); SLA timers in business time on versioned calendars with `case.sla-expired` once per deadline; append-only comments (`cscm_`) with mentions; attachments in sealed storage with metadata-free renditions and a scanner port; tags; up to 1,000 held objects with their state as attached; search, bulk actions, CSV and JSON export, a print view, redaction and retention.
- **Case workflows** (C140): case actions and the case trigger with parameter forms, account actions with `POST /accounts/{id}/run-action`, the case steps, waits on case events, the graph explorer's "create case" bulk action, and the re-screen triggers Phases 6 and 7 deferred.
- **Console** (C141–C142): the workflow editor (`@xyflow/react`, an outline view that is the keyboard equivalent, `@dagrejs/dagre` layout on demand, our undo and diff), the runs view, versions and rollout, secrets, a sandbox test run; the case list, the case page with the default layout, the compare panel (`@panzoom/panzoom`), queues, templates, calendars and the print view.

ADR 0007, "Workflows: authority, egress, automated decisions and sandboxing", extends ADR 0002 to workflows:
- a workflow acts on a frozen grant no wider than its publisher's, and a run started by a principal acts with no more than that principal holds;
- nothing an operator writes (a condition, a template, code) ever becomes host JavaScript, reads another environment or reaches the network except through the HTTPS action and its guard;
- every side effect has an effect key, and the docs state each action's delivery guarantee (exactly once inside OpenVerify, at least once over HTTPS, at most once for messages);
- a workflow can decline a person only on a path the operator has declared and documented, and every automated decision is recorded as such;
- an execution failure is a step error, never a value: a report that errored is never "no match", a list that was not screened is never "not listed", a timed-out wait is reported as timed out.

## Starting point (interim, from the research notes; re-taken at the Phase 8 base)

The research notes read the code at `3b4ef08` and `3a3ef409` (Phase 6 in progress, Phase 7 not built); each "What exists today" table there carries its `path:line`. The full inventory at the Phase 8 base (Phase 7 done) is `contracts-8a`'s first deliverable, written to `docs/research/phase8-inventory.md`, and every claim below is re-checked there. In short:

| Area | Exists (at the research commits) | Does not exist |
|---|---|---|
| Identifiers | Lowercase-letter prefixes in two tables (`packages/contracts/src/ids.ts`, `packages/db/src/ids.ts`); `<prefix>_<ULID>` generated in the application with an 80-bit random tail; Phase 6 took `rep`, `rptp`, `rptpv`, `rpr`, `ract`, `rpm`; Phase 7 takes `dev`, `net`, `lst`, its list-item prefixes, `lmt`, `mprt`, `grphq`, `txn`, `txnf`, `txnl` and the `ovh_` token | `wfl`, `wfv`, `wfd`, `wfr`, `srrun`, `wsec`, `case`, `ctmpl`, `ctmplv`, `cqueue`, `cscm`, `cap`, `catt`, `cslap`, `ccal`, `cexp` are free |
| Events and outbox | `createEventPublisher().stage(tx, …)` validates the payload, inserts the sealed `events` row and one `webhook_deliveries` row per matching webhook in the caller's transaction; `dispatch()` after commit; a 30 s sweep re-enqueues overdue deliveries; only webhook simulation calls `createEvent` directly, with `simulated: true` | No in-process subscriber; no workflow trigger; no `caused_by_run_id` |
| Claims, sweeps, jobs | C74.1's fenced claim on `report_runs` (`claimed_by`, `claimed_until`, `attempt`, `not_before`, `enqueued_at`, an eleventh claim errors); sweeps take rows `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` in batches of 500; `JobQueue<Data>` producers with ids-only job data; repeatable jobs through `upsertJobScheduler`; `describeJobError` hands BullMQ codes, never messages; the left-over-id trap documented in `webhooks/queue.ts` | No workflow tables, queues or sweeps; `packages/workflow-engine` does not exist |
| Queue library | `bullmq` 6.3.11 pinned exactly; `cron-parser` 5.10.1 and `luxon` 3.7.2 in the lockfile through it; Valkey with `--appendonly yes` | No direct cron or date dependency |
| Inquiry actions | `performInquiryAction` / `applyInquiryAction` (approve, decline, mark for review, expire) under the row lock with events and audit; transitions `completed|failed → approved|declined|needs_review`, `needs_review → approved|declined`; `approved`, `declined`, `expired` terminal (`packages/core/src/inquiry-state-machine.ts`); a decline deletes the inquiry's face template and background rows (C42.2) | No decision record; no way back from `declined` |
| Principals and audit | 36 permissions in `packages/core/src/permissions.ts` (28 API scopes, Phase 6's report scopes included, and 8 operator permissions; Phase 7 adds its own, and its device routes use `inquiry-sessions:read`); viewers get every `:read` outside `NOT_FOR_VIEWERS` (`api-logs:read`, `audit-logs:read`, `sandbox:read`, and Phase 7's `graph:read`); `missingPermissions` stops a principal granting what it lacks; audit actor enum `user`, `api_key`, `system` (`packages/db/src/tables/enums.ts:107`) | No workflow or case scope; no `workflow-run` actor |
| Outbound HTTP | `createOutboundHttp()` over undici 7.30.1, one `Agent` per client; GET and POST; `https:` only; no redirects; 1 MiB and 10 s defaults; errors with a code and `retryable`, never the URL or body; `classifyAddress` in `apps/api/src/webhooks/ssrf.ts` against the IANA registries of 2026-10-13 (mapped, NAT64 well-known, 6to4, Teredo, metadata including `168.63.129.16`), with one install-wide `allowPrivateNetworks` boolean; `createVettedLookup` in `webhooks/http-sender.ts` resolves once and connects to the vetted address; SNI and the certificate stay on the URL's host | No PUT, PATCH, DELETE; no port policy; caller `Host` and hop-by-hop headers pass through; no NAT64 network-specific prefixes; no signing outside the webhook dispatcher |
| Webhook signatures | `OpenVerify-Signature: t=<unix>,v1=<hex HMAC-SHA-256 of "<t>.<raw body>">`, one `v1` per active secret (≤ 8), 300 s tolerance; `@openverify/node` verifies | — |
| Mail and SMS | One install-wide SMTP transport (`OPENVERIFY_SMTP_URL`, `OPENVERIFY_MAIL_FROM`); `SmsSender` (Twilio) and `SandboxSmsSender` per environment kind, the sandbox sink OTP-shaped and readable with `sandbox:read`; GCRA SMS limits that fail closed (per IP, inquiry, destination, prefix, country, environment) | No mail sandbox; no Slack; no message body in the SMS sink |
| Secrets at rest | `sealValue`/`sealJson` with row-bound AAD and a fresh DEK per value; webhook secrets shown once with the last four kept; pino redaction by key name | No workflow secret; no value-based scrubbing |
| Idempotency | C12 `Idempotency-Key` on server `POST`/`PATCH`, keyed by environment and principal, kept 24 h | No effect key that outlives a day |
| Files and storage | `FileStorage` over S3 that only sees ciphertext; `encryptBytes` with the file id as context; `files.verification_id` NOT NULL; magic-byte typing of JPEG, PNG, WebP and PDF; 10 MiB cap; the console proxies `/files/:id/content` with `inquiries:read` and `pii:view` and audits `file.viewed` | No attachment outside a verification; no rendition without metadata (originals keep their EXIF) |
| Dashboard | Next.js 16.4, React 19.3, Tailwind 4; `DataTable`, `Dialog`, `JsonEditor` (JSON Pointer errors), `NoteEditor`; headers only, no script CSP yet; the selfie card shows the ID front beside the centre pose with similarity, threshold and basis; Phase 7 adds Cytoscape with a table view and an injected-CSP test | No editor, zoom or Markdown library; no case pages |
| Phase 6 and 7 hand-offs | Phase 6 C88: the Run Report action and match conditions, cases holding reports; decision 28 and 48 `on-report-match`. Phase 7 C110: Get List Match, Add Item To List, Add Item To Face List, Sync Graph, Run Graph Query, Create Transaction, Update Transaction Status, Set Relation, Trust Device, re-screening custom-list reports on a list update, case modules, transaction children and their redaction cascade, re-screening on account field changes; C103's "create a case"; decision 18 (auto-decline on a signal only through a workflow) | — |

Records that Phase 8 corrects are listed in the behaviour note's "Corrections" and handled in C144. Carried-forward items in Phase 8's areas: the console's script CSP (left for a later phase since Phase 2; the editor and the compare panel are tested under an injected policy, C141.8), and the PGlite migration time under load (Risks).

## Patterns to mirror

| Category | Source | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| Registry with tiers | `packages/core/src/check-registry.ts`, `report-registry.ts`, Phase 7 `signal-registry.ts` | One entry per item with a version and a stage; constants as `{ value, basis, source }` |
| Resource contracts | `packages/contracts/src/accounts.ts`, `reports.ts`, `json-api.ts`, `list-filters.ts`, `fixtures/api/` | Zod attributes with `.meta({ pii: true })` and a `PII_<RESOURCE>_ATTRIBUTES` list; builders that validate output; golden fixtures; `pii-collisions.test.ts` |
| Versioned templates | `packages/db/src/tables/inquiry-templates.ts`, Phase 6 C71, Phase 7 C104 | Draft, published, superseded; one published; publish under the template lock; each object pins its version |
| State machines | `packages/core/src/{inquiry,report,report-match,verification}-state-machine.ts` | A pure transition table with an event per transition and a test per edge |
| Outbox and fenced claims | `apps/api/src/events/index.ts` (`stage`), `webhooks/dispatcher.ts`, Phase 6 C74.1 | Rows written in the caller's transaction; enqueue after commit; a sweep as the outbox; completion fenced by the claim token; a crash-loop cap |
| Repeatable jobs and queues | `apps/api/src/jobs/repeatable/index.ts`, `jobs/index.ts`, `webhooks/queue.ts` | A factory per job, gated registration, `unbuiltRepeatableJob` stubs; ids-only job data; jobs removed on completion and failure |
| Outbound HTTP | `apps/api/src/http/index.ts`, `webhooks/ssrf.ts`, `webhooks/http-sender.ts` | One client per caller with its own policy; vet every resolved address; errors carry codes only |
| A-tier adapters | `apps/api/src/providers/index.ts`, `docs/checks/sandbox-providers.md`, the SMS binding | A port, a real adapter, a deterministic sandbox refused in production, bound per environment kind |
| PII at rest | `packages/db/src/sealed.ts`, `encrypted-columns.ts`, `repositories/key-rotation.ts`, `repositories/redaction.ts` | Row-bound sealing; rewrap, redaction and coverage lists |
| Masking | `apps/api/src/auth/pii.ts`, `services/api-logs.ts` | By attribute name at every exit; a test per exit |
| Secrets | the webhook `secret` and `previous_secret` columns, `repositories/webhooks.ts` | Written once, never returned except to the executing code, last four kept, rotation with a previous value |
| Review rule | Phase 6 C84.3 (`on-report-match`) | Off by default; only `completed → needs_review`; a decided inquiry is untouched and the cause recorded |
| Console | `apps/dashboard/src/lib/api/page.tsx`, `components/data-table.tsx`, `components/json-editor.tsx`, `components/lifecycle/inquiry-view.tsx`, Phase 7's explorer | `renderPage`, server actions validated by contract schemas, `OptionalSection`s, masked values, a table or outline view beside every canvas |
| Tests | Vitest colocated; Testcontainers; Playwright | RED commit first; ≥ 80% per package; `docs/tdd/phase8-<agent>.tdd.md` |

## Locked contracts

C1–C111 stay locked except where a section below amends them. Stated amendments of earlier contracts:
- C5 (events): new subjects `workflow`, `workflow-version`, `workflow-run`, `case`, `case-comment`, `list`; the internal event cause (C121.3); an internal `caused_by_run_id` column on `events`, never in a payload (C118.8); `stage()` gains the trigger fan-out and the wait lookup (C118.1, C119.3);
- C10 (inquiry states): a `declined → needs_review` reopen transition for human review, users only, firing `inquiry.reopened` (C128.6); the decision record (C128.1);
- C12: the rate-limit policies of C143.3;
- C14 and C70.4: strict webhook filters for the workflow-run and case subjects (C143.4);
- C15 and C53.7: new PII attributes and request-body members on runs, steps, secrets, cases, comments and attachments; the collision test and the API-log sets extended to them (C143.5);
- C16: the retention settings of C120.8 and C139.3, and the settings of C143.7;
- C42.2: a reopened inquiry keeps no face template; the docs say a decline's biometric deletion is not undone by a reopen (C128.6);
- C53 (outbound HTTP): PUT, PATCH and DELETE, a port policy, a header policy, refusal of a non-identity `content-encoding`, NAT64 network-specific prefixes, all opt-in per client (C124.2);
- C54.1: repeatable jobs and queues (C143.1, C143.2);
- C55.10 and C55.11: workflow messages get their own sandbox lists with the kinds `workflow-sms`, `email` and `slack` (sealed bodies), beside the untouched `otp` list (C126.4);
- C3: the flow session answer gains `decision-contest-route` for declined inquiries (C128.7);
- C42.3: a fourth optional consent key, `consent.automated-decision` (C128.4); C13 and C104: account and transaction types gain an optional `contest-route` (C128.3);
- C69 and C72: report `origin` gains `workflow` (C127.2); C74: the report engine answers `list.updated` with `list_update` runs for monitored custom-list reports (C127.12);
- C63: the worker binds `sms`, `slack` and the mailer for workflow messages (C126.6, C143.8; decision 76);
- C5: events carry the internal `caused_by_principal` (C121.3); `inquiry.declined` carries `meta.decision` (C128.1); `dispatch()` schedules trigger deliveries and wait matches (C118.1, C119.3);
- C97–C99: lists gain a version and an optional `purpose` (`block`, `allow`, `watch`; default `block`, C122.1), and Phase 7's list write paths stage `list.updated` (C127.12); C74: `report_runs` gains `trigger_list_id` and `trigger_list_version` with a widened check and key (C127.12); C104: transaction types gain `adverse-statuses` (C122.1); C105: a transaction's `reviewer` and `related-objects` are filled (C127.7);
- C97–C105: workflow-run creators on list items, transactions and relations; the transaction redaction cascade to the objects a run created (C127.7);
- C103: the "create case" bulk action (C140.6);
- the core permissions: the workflow, run, secret and case scopes (C112.5, C129.4), with `secrets:read` and `workflow-runs:read` in `NOT_FOR_VIEWERS`; API keys gain an optional `case-template-ids` restriction (C129.4);
- the audit actor enum: `workflow_run` (C121.1);
- the devices of C91.2: a `trusted-at` and `trusted-by` mark (C127.9).

### C112. Workflow resources, identifiers and scopes

1. **Identifiers** (public prefixes where public, behaviour note W1): `wfl` (workflow), `wfv` (version; the OpenAPI spelling, not the Help Center's `wflv`), `wfd` (deployment), `wfr` (run), `srrun` (scheduled run), `accac` (account action, C118.7), `wsec` (workflow secret, C125). Internal row ids, served only inside a run's step list: `wfst` (step run), `wftd` (trigger delivery), `wfsc` (schedule), `wfef` (effect), `wfrf` (trigger file, C118.2). Served: `idec` (a decision record, C128.1). Each prefix joins both prefix tables with a test, as earlier phases did.
2. **Environment scope** (decision 4). A workflow belongs to one environment. Its templates, lists, report templates, secrets and providers are environment-bound, and ADR 0002 forbids sandbox providers in production, so a cross-environment version would bind production runs to sandbox objects. `POST /api/v1/workflows/:id/promote` with `meta.environment-id` copies a published version into the target environment of the same organisation **as a draft**:
   - each reference (inquiry template, report template, list, transaction type, case template, module, secret key) is re-resolved by the referenced object's name, or key where it has one; none or several candidates refuse the promote with a problem naming each reference by JSON Pointer;
   - the target draft is then published there by someone holding the rights in that environment (`environments:production` for production, C121.2);
   - the copy records `promoted-from` (the source version id) and the promote writes an audit entry in both environments;
   - **who may promote** (P13): a user only (API keys get 403), holding `workflows:write` in both environments and `environments:production` when either is production; the promote creates a draft only when the target workflow has none (409 `draft_exists`, never a replace) and is refused during a rollout; unresolved references are named only to a caller who may read the target environment.
3. **Workflow attributes:** `name` (1–80), `description` (≤ 500), `status` (`paused`, `running`, `archived`; the public open enum), `kind` (ours: `workflow` or `module`), `created-at`, `updated-at`, `archived-at`. Relationships: `latest-published-version`, `active-deployment` (both public), `draft-version` (ours).
4. **Version attributes:**
   - public: `status` (`draft`, `published`, `archived`), `trigger-type` (`trigger-event`, `trigger-api`, `trigger-schedule`, `trigger-recurring`, `trigger-direct`, `trigger-account`, `trigger-case`, `trigger-verification`; null until a trigger is set), `live`, `rollout-label` (`treatment`, `control`), `rollout-percentage` (1–100), `last-updater` (an OpenVerify id, never an email address: decision 64), `inputs[] {name, type, required}`, `outputs[] {name}`, `description` (the change note; null on drafts), `published-at`;
   - ours: `version-number`, `schema-version`, `definition` (C113), `definition-sha256`, `grant` (the derived scopes, C121.2), `egress` (the PII-egress marks, C121.4), `acknowledgements` (C116.4), `warnings`, `promoted-from`.
5. **Scopes** (ours, splitting the public `workflow.read` and `workflow.trigger`; behaviour note W34):
   - `workflows:read`: workflows, versions and deployments (definitions hold configuration only);
   - `workflow-runs:read` (decision 78; P11): runs, their steps, trigger deliveries and test runs, in `NOT_FOR_VIEWERS` (values masked fail closed without PII visibility, C120.6);
   - `workflows:write`: create, edit drafts, archive, pause and activate;
   - `workflows:publish`: publish, rollout, restore, promote;
   - `workflows:trigger`: `POST /workflows/:id/trigger`, account actions, case actions, manual runs;
   - `workflow-runs:write`: resume, rerun, cancel, and cancelling the runs of older versions;
   - `workflow-runs:redact` (P35): `DELETE /workflow-runs/:id` (admin only);
   - `secrets:read` (keys, descriptions, hosts, last four, rotation metadata) and `secrets:write` (C125.2). `secrets:read` joins `NOT_FOR_VIEWERS`.

   Built-in roles: `admin` all; `developer` all workflow and secret scopes except `workflow-runs:redact`; `reviewer` `workflows:read`, `workflows:trigger` and `workflow-runs:read` (so reviewers can run case actions under C121.3 and follow them); `viewer` every `:read` outside `NOT_FOR_VIEWERS`. Each permission gets a `PERMISSION_INFO` label and a refusal test. Phase 8 adds its permissions to the 36 that exist (28 API scopes and 8 operator permissions).
6. **Routes** (server API, `/api/v1`; zod schemas registered as plugins so they appear in `/openapi.json`):

   | Route | Scope | Notes |
   |---|---|---|
   | `GET`, `POST /workflows`; `GET`, `PATCH /workflows/:id` | read; write | `PATCH` changes name and description only |
   | `POST /workflows/:id/{pause,activate,archive}` | write | archive needs paused first, as in the public product |
   | `GET`, `PUT /workflows/:id/draft`; `POST /workflows/:id/draft/validate` | read; write | `PUT` replaces the definition (C113); validate answers the C113.6 problems without saving |
   | `POST /workflows/:id/publish` | publish | `meta.rollout-percentage` (default 100), `meta.description`, `meta.acknowledgements[]` (C116.4) |
   | `POST /workflows/:id/rollout` | publish | `meta.rollout-percentage`: 100 finishes, 0 undoes (C116.3) |
   | `POST /workflows/:id/restore` | publish | `meta.version-id`; creates a draft from that version (decision 19) |
   | `POST /workflows/:id/promote` | publish | 2 above |
   | `GET /workflow-versions?filter[workflow-id]=` (required), `GET /workflow-versions/:id`, `GET /workflow-versions/:id/diff?against=<wfv_>` | read | the public list keeps its public semantics (published versions and the draft, newest first, never archived); the diff is C116.6 |
   | `POST /workflows/:id/trigger` | trigger | public; C118.2 |
   | `POST /workflows/:id/manual-runs` | trigger | ours (H26): object ids or a CSV, one run per object (C118.5) |
   | `GET /workflow-trigger-deliveries` (`filter[workflow-id]`, `filter[status]`) | runs read | ours (H18): refused, filtered and expired deliveries with their reason codes |
   | `POST /workflows/:id/test-runs` | trigger | sandbox environments only; C120.7 |
   | `GET /workflow-runs` (`filter[workflow-id]`, `filter[status]`, `filter[workflow-version-id]`, `filter[subject-id]`, `filter[has-step-errors]`), `GET /workflow-runs/:id`, `GET /workflow-runs/:id/steps` | runs read | public list and retrieve; the public `filter[status]` filters by status (the spec's description says workflow id; we follow the parameter's name and document it) |
   | `POST /workflow-runs/:id/{resume,rerun,cancel}`; `POST /workflows/:id/cancel-runs` (`meta.before-version-id`) | runs write | C120.3–C120.5 |
   | `DELETE /workflow-runs/:id` | runs redact | C120.9 |
   | `GET /workflow-actions` | read | the action catalogue for the editor (C122.4) |
   | `GET /workflow-recipes`, `POST /workflow-recipes/:key/install` | read; write | ours (V16): the documented recipes (C140.5), installed as a disabled draft in the caller's environment; owner `api-workflows` (8B registers its case recipes in the same table) |

   Every `POST` honours C12's `Idempotency-Key`; runs also carry a unique `(workflow_id, idempotency_key)` so the guarantee outlives the 24-hour store (C118.2). Publish, rollout, restore and promote are user-only (C121.2). Each route is frozen in its implementer's route file (F15): `workflows.ts`, `workflow-versions.ts`, `workflow-runs.ts`, `workflow-test-runs.ts`, `workflow-triggers.ts`, `workflow-actions.ts`, `account-actions.ts`; each route file declares its own include map (F34).
7. **Events** (C5 names, golden payloads, webhook samples, the dashboard group "Workflows"): `workflow.published`, `workflow-version.published` (public; no webhook file in the public spec, registered here), `workflow-run.created`, `workflow-run.resumed`, `workflow-run.errored`, `workflow-run.completed` (public), and ours `workflow-run.canceled` and `workflow.paused` (C121.5). Phase 8 also registers `list.updated` with a `list` subject (C127.12) and `inquiry.reopened` (C128.6). Run events carry the run's ids, status, version, rollout label and per-step outcome codes, never step inputs or outputs (C125.5). `workflow-run.errored` carries `context.errored-step {name, key, type, action-key}` and `context.error {type, message}` (C120.2). The engine's own `workflow-run.*` events never trigger workflows (C118.8).
8. **Problems** (RFC 9457, golden fixtures): `definition_invalid` (with `errors[] {pointer, code}`), `version_locked`, `draft_missing`, `rollout_in_progress`, `promote_unresolved`, `publish_grant_missing` (the missing scopes named), `publish_acknowledgement_missing`, `trigger_input_invalid` (with `source.pointer`), `workflow_not_running`, `run_not_resumable`, `run_not_cancelable`, `test_runs_sandbox_only`, `secret_reference_invalid`, `secret_value_missing`, `draft_exists`, `publish_trigger_fanout_exceeded`, `human_review_pending` (the reopen guard on the API's decline route). The `definition_invalid` codes of the new publish rules (`pii_into_plain_sink`, `pii_literal_in_condition`, `pii_in_url`, `secret_placement_refused`, `nullable_in_boolean_position`, `on_null_missing`) and every step error code (C117.7, C122–C128) are listed with their error types in `docs/workflows/limits.md` and `errors.md`.

### C113. The definition format

1. **Shape** (ours; the public product does not publish one, behaviour note 1.5 and editor note 2.5). Strict zod objects throughout, unknown keys refused:

   ```json
   {
     "schema-version": 1,
     "trigger": { "type": "trigger-event", "config": { } },
     "inputs": [ { "name": "reference_id", "type": "string", "required": true } ],
     "variables": [ { "name": "risk", "type": "number", "default": 0 } ],
     "steps": [ { "id": "run_watchlist", "type": "action", "name": "Run watchlist report", "config": { } } ],
     "edges": [ { "id": "e1", "from": "trigger", "from-port": "next", "to": "run_watchlist" } ],
     "outputs": [ { "name": "decision", "pii": false } ],
     "layout": { "run_watchlist": { "x": 0, "y": 120 } }
   }
   ```

   Step ids match `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,39}$`, are generated by the editor, stay stable across versions (they key the diff) and read as names in paths (`steps.run_watchlist.output`). `trigger` and `output` are reserved ids.
2. **Node types** (behaviour note W13):
   - `action`: `action-key`, `action-version` (C122.1), `config` (the action's own strict schema), `continue-on-error`, `retry {intervals-seconds[] ≤ 10, each 1–3,600}` where the action allows retries (C117.6);
   - `wait`: mode `events` (`subject` as a path to an object id, `events[]` of at least one name the subject emits, an optional `filter` condition, `timeout-seconds` ≤ 2,592,000 (30 days), `on-timeout` `continue` or `error`) or mode `time` (`seconds` ≤ 2,592,000);
   - `parallel` with `branches[] {key}` (2–10) and a paired `join` node (`join-of`);
   - `conditional` with ordered `routes[] {key, label, condition, on-null}` (C114.6) and a final `else` port; a route without a condition is a catch-all; `continue-on-error` as in the public product (an expression error then takes `else`);
   - `loop`: `mode` `for-each` (`items` path), `while` (`condition`, `max-iterations` 1–100, required) or `map` (`items`, with an `array-map-output` node whose value is collected); `concurrency` 1–10 (default 1); `continue-on-error`;
   - `module`: `module-workflow-id`, `version` (`latest-published` or a `wfv_` id), `inputs` (a mapping from each module input to a path or literal);
   - `output`: one per definition, mapping each declared output to a path or literal;
   - `module-input`: the start node of a module (instead of a trigger).
3. **Edges and ports:** `next` (every node except `output`), `error` (an action, loop or module with an error edge: ours; the run follows it instead of erroring, C119.7), `route:<key>` and `else` (conditional), `branch:<key>` (parallel), `body` and `done` (loop), and the loop body's back-edge to its loop's `continue` port. An output port accepts one edge, except that `branch:*` ports belong to one parallel.
4. **Caps** (ours, editor note 2.5 and engine note 3.9): 200 nodes, 400 edges, names 1–80 characters, a node's `config` ≤ 16 KiB serialised, the whole definition ≤ 256 KiB, 32 inputs, 32 variables, 32 outputs, parallel and loop nesting depth ≤ 3.
5. **The graph check** (pure, `packages/workflow-engine/src/graph/`), run after the schema on save, validate, publish and promote:
   - exactly one `trigger` (or one `module-input` for a module) and one `output`; every edge endpoint and port exists; port rules of 3;
   - every node reachable from the start, and every node reaches `output`;
   - no cycle except a loop body's back-edge; each branch of a parallel reaches its paired `join` and nothing else leaves the branch; a loop body stays inside its loop;
   - nesting depth ≤ 3; a module contains no `module` step (no nesting, public W18);
   - module references point at published modules of the same environment; each action's `action-key` and `action-version` exist and allow the trigger type (C122.3);
   - every condition and template parses and type-checks against the variable scope at its node (C114.5, C115.3);
   - the C116.4 publish rules (on publish only).
6. **Errors:** RFC 9457 `definition_invalid` with `errors[] {pointer, code, detail}` where `pointer` is a JSON Pointer into the definition (`/steps/3/config/url`, `/edges/7`). Warnings (an unused variable, a step whose output nothing reads, an action that emits the workflow's own trigger event, C118.8) are returned beside errors and never block publish.
7. **Hash and upgrades.** `definition_sha256` is the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON (RFC 8785 key ordering) of everything except `layout`. Stored versions are never rewritten: `readDefinition(raw)` upgrades each `schema-version` to the current one through pure functions with golden fixtures, and a test loads every fixture of every schema version.
8. **Never code.** The definition is data walked by the engine. No field of it is ever passed to `eval`, `Function`, a template compiler or a module loader; Evaluate Code's source is the one place operator text is executed, inside the sandbox (C123).

### C114. Conditions: the JSONLogic dialect (ours)

1. **Format.** Conditions are stored as JSONLogic, PLAN §3's choice, labelled ours: the public product shows a picker over method chains and publishes no serialisation (behaviour note 1.3, Corrections 2). `packages/workflow-engine/src/expressions/` implements the evaluator itself; `json-logic-js` 2.0.5 (MIT) is a dev-only differential oracle for the operators whose semantics we keep (engine note 6.2).
2. **Operators** (an allowlist; anything else is a publish error):
   - logic and comparison: `and`, `or`, `!`, `!!`, `if`, `===`, `!==`, `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=` (with the three-argument between form); `==` and `!=` are accepted only as aliases of `===` and `!==` (decision 6), so no condition coerces types;
   - data: `var` (own-property paths over plain JSON values, `[n]` for array indexes), `missing`, `missing_some`;
   - arithmetic on finite numbers: `+`, `-`, `*`, `/`, `%`, `min`, `max`;
   - strings and arrays: `in`, `cat`, `substr`, `merge`, `all`, `some`, `none`, `map`, `filter`, `reduce`;
   - the public method-chain operators as named operations (behaviour note 1.5): `count`, `first`, `last`, `includes`, `where` (the public `filter('key','operator','value')`, operators `eq`, `ne`, `gt`, `gte`, `lt`, `lte`, `in`), `sort_asc`, `sort_desc`, `collect` (the public `collect_from_each`), `length`, `trim`, `split`, `replace_all`, `replace_chars`, `email_domain`, `email_username`, `convert_to` (`string`, `number`, `boolean`, `date`, `datetime`);
   - dates on ISO 8601 instants: `before`, `after`, `days_between`, `now` (the run's clock, recorded on the step so a resume reads the same value).

   No `log`, no `method`, no regular expressions, no operator that reads outside its arguments.
3. **Limits** (ours): a condition ≤ 16 KiB serialised, depth ≤ 32, ≤ 1,000 nodes, ≤ 10,000 operator evaluations per call (iteration operators count each element), arrays ≤ 1,000 elements, strings ≤ 10,000 characters. Exceeding one is the step error `expression_limit_exceeded`, never a truthy or falsy answer.
4. **Paths.** A method chain `trigger > verification_government_id > country_code` is written `{"var": "trigger.verification_government_id.country_code"}`. Roots: `trigger`, `inputs`, `vars`, `steps.<id>.output`, `loop.item` and `loop.index` inside a loop, `current_case` (an inert root in 8A that 8B fills, C140.2) and `current_run` (`id`, `created-at`, `workflow-id`, `version-id`, `environment.kind`). `current_run.environment.secrets` is never readable by a condition (C125.3).
   **The trigger view** (H27): event payloads are JSON:API documents, so `trigger` is a flattened view: the primary resource's attributes at the top (`trigger.status`, `trigger.fields.<key>`), each to-one relationship with its attributes (`trigger.template.id`, `trigger.account.reference-id`), and to-many relationships as lists of `{ id, type }`; the related resources a version's paths need are snapshotted into the trigger snapshot at run creation. A closed alias table maps public names to OpenVerify's (`verification_government_id` → the run's government-ID verification, `template` → `inquiry-template`), each with a golden vector; an unknown public name is a publish error.
5. **Typing.** At publish, every `var` is resolved against the variable scope of its node (C115.3); an unknown path or a path whose type cannot be compared with its operand is a `definition_invalid` error. Comparing a PII-marked path with a string literal or a literal array of strings is `pii_literal_in_condition` (P37: identifiers of people never go into immutable definitions; the message points to `get_list_match`); comparisons with enums, numbers, dates and booleans stay allowed. The same rule applies to literal recipients outside the C126 allowlists.
6. **Unknown is not false** (H04). The scope marks every **nullable** path: Phase 7's never-false signals (`true` or `null`), `get_list_match`'s `matched`, Run Report's `has-match`, Run Graph Query's `account-count`, every output of a step that can continue on error, and optional fields. At publish a nullable path is refused as a direct operand of `!`, `!!`, `if`, `and`, `or` and `!==` (`nullable_in_boolean_position`); `=== null` and `missing` test for it explicitly. Each conditional route whose condition reads a nullable path declares `on-null`: `error` (the step errors `condition_input_null`), `route:<key>` (take that route) or `skip-route` (evaluate the next route), with **no default** (`on_null_missing`); the editor suggests Phase 7's `ON_NULL_DEFAULTS` for signals. At run time a comparison other than `===` with `null` follows the route's `on-null`. `!==` with `null` is defined as `!(a === b)` with strict equality, and is excluded from the differential test because json-logic-js coerces. A required input that is missing never reaches a condition: the trigger refuses it (C118.2). Golden vectors cover null signals, `matched: null` and a continue-on-error report step.
   **Every condition position declares `on-null`** (V15), with no default, wherever it reads a nullable path: trigger criteria and recurring filters (`error`, `admit`, `skip`); wait filters (`error`, `keep-waiting`, `accept`); `while` conditions (`error`, `stop`); and the list predicates `where`, `filter`, `some`, `all` and `none`, which take an `on-null` argument (`error`, `include`, `exclude`), so `where` never silently drops an element whose value is unknown and `count` never reads it as "no match". Golden vectors per position.
7. **Tests:** golden vectors per operator, the limit vectors, the differential test, and property tests that evaluation never throws a host error (only the step errors of C120.2).

### C115. Templates and the typed variable model

1. **Templates are JSON values** (ours, engine note 6.4). A string leaf may contain `{{ path }}` references using the `var` path grammar of C114.4. A leaf that is exactly one reference takes the value with its JSON type; other leaves interpolate strings. The sink serialises the built value once, so a value can never break out of a JSON string. No logic, filters or helpers inside templates: conditions belong in conditional steps. No template library is used (Handlebars generates code and had critical 2026 advisories; liquidjs's limits were bypassed repeatedly in 2026).
2. **Encoding per sink** (chosen by the sink, never by the author):
   - HTTPS URL: each interpolation percent-encoded as one path segment or one query value; the scheme, host and port are literal (C124.3);
   - header values: CR, LF and NUL refused;
   - JSON bodies (HTTPS, Slack): built as values;
   - email HTML: HTML-escaped; email and SMS text: inserted as text;
   - Slack text: `&`, `<` and `>` escaped as `&amp;`, `&lt;`, `&gt;` (C126.3);
   - case comments, tags and object fields written by actions: as text, within each target's own limits.
   Caps: a template ≤ 32 KiB; a rendered value ≤ 1 MiB (or the sink's smaller cap); a missing value is the step error `template_variable_missing`, never an empty string; an unknown reference is a publish error.
3. **Variable scope per node** (engine note 6.5):
   - `trigger`: the typed trigger object (for an event trigger, the resource schema of the event's payload from `packages/contracts`; for the API trigger, the version's `inputs`);
   - `inputs` (API, manual, account and case triggers, and module inputs);
   - `steps.<id>.output`: each action's output schema from the registry, in scope only at nodes the step dominates; a parallel branch's outputs only after its join; the loop item only inside the loop body;
   - `vars.<name>`: declared variables with a small type DSL: `string`, `number`, `integer`, `boolean`, `datetime`, `id<prefix>`, `enum<…>`, `list<T>`, `object{…}`, each optionally `pii: true`.

   `z.toJSONSchema()` of the scope feeds the editor's picker (C141.4).
4. **Run variables** (public W20): Update Variable with `set`, `unset`, `push`, `remove` (the last two for lists); scoped to one run; read-only inside a module (a parent's variable passes in as an input); parallel branches that update the same variable are refused at publish (the public product leaves their order undefined; ours: refuse rather than race). Total variable state ≤ 1 MiB; reading a variable before it holds a value is the step error `variable_unset` (public 1.4).
5. **Outputs are parsed.** Each action's output is parsed with its zod schema before it is journaled or put in scope (unknown keys stripped, sizes checked), so a step cannot smuggle untyped data into later expressions. Step outputs live sealed (C117.2).
6. **PII marks propagate as taint** (P28, P20). A value is PII-tainted when it is a `pii: true` variable, a PII attribute of a contract schema, or derived from a tainted value: an Evaluate Code output when any mapped property is tainted; a `calculate` or `update_variable` result when an operand is; an HTTPS response body without a response schema that marks its members non-PII; a loop item over a tainted list; a module output fed by tainted inputs. A variable assigned a tainted value must be declared `pii: true`. Publish then:
   - marks every step whose rendered values carry taint and leave the platform (HTTPS, email, SMS, Slack) in the version's `egress` (C121.4);
   - refuses taint flowing into a **plain sink** (`pii_into_plain_sink`): tag names, labels, a case `name`, enum or status fields, run `outputs` not declared `pii: true` in the definition's `outputs`, and variables not declared `pii: true`;
   - allows PII-to-PII copies into another object (custom fields, case fields, comments, list items), which the effect row records for redaction (C120.9).

   Golden tests: Evaluate Code, a variable, Calculate, an HTTPS body and a loop item, each ending in a Slack step, must produce an egress mark; each into a tag must be refused.

### C116. Versions, publishing and rollout

1. **One draft, immutable versions** (public W23): a workflow has at most one draft, edited in place (optimistic concurrency by `lock-version`, 409 on mismatch). Publishing freezes the draft into a `published` version with its `definition_sha256` and rebuilds the workflow's trigger index (C118.1) in the same transaction, under the workflow row lock. Archiving a version (by publishing a newer one past its rollout, or archiving the workflow) never touches its runs.
2. **Runs are pinned** to the version they started on; every step reads that version's graph. Publishing changes new runs only; no in-flight run is migrated (decision 16). `POST /workflows/:id/cancel-runs` cancels the unfinished runs of versions older than a named one, with an audit entry.
3. **Staged rollout** (public W23; the assignment rule is ours). Publishing with `rollout-percentage` below 100 makes the new version `treatment` and the previous published version `control`, recorded in an active deployment (`wfd_`, `configuration.versions[] {token, percentage, label}` as public). Each trigger subject is assigned by `hash(deployment id, subject id) mod 100 < percentage`, so one object always gets one side and a retried trigger gets the same side. Editing the draft is locked during a rollout until it is finished (100) or undone (0); a run records `rollout-label`. A module cannot be published by staged rollout (public W18).
4. **Publish rules** (each a `definition_invalid` or `publish_*` problem; publish is user-only, C121.2):
   - the graph check of C113.5, the typing, null and taint rules of C114.5, C114.6 and C115.6, and `pii_in_url` (C124.3);
   - the grant (C121.2): the publisher holds every scope in it, `workflows:publish`, `pii:view` when the grant's `pii` is true, and `environments:production` in production;
   - egress (C121.4): a step that sends PII-tainted values off the platform needs the acknowledgement `egress:<step id>`;
   - irreversible steps (redaction) need `irreversible:<step id>` (decision 42);
   - adverse steps in production (C128.3): every decline and other adverse step carries a `decision-basis` and a `contest-route`, a biometric one a `biometric-basis` (C128.4), and one whose path reads an H-tier value the acknowledgement `heuristic:<step id>`;
   - approvals (C127.1): an approve reachable from `inquiry.failed` needs `override-failed:<step id>`; an approve from `inquiry.completed` without a report or list wait warns;
   - messaging: a step with `allow-unconfirmed` needs `unconfirmed:<step id>` (C126.1, C126.2);
   - secret references resolve to keys with a value in this environment, each referenced secret allows the step's host and placement (C125.3, C125.4);
   - the trigger's event is not already listened to by 50 published workflows (`publish_trigger_fanout_exceeded`, H18);
   - every action is available in this environment (a messaging action whose adapter is unbound publishes with a warning, and its runs end with `provider_not_configured`, C122.5);
   - recurring and scheduled triggers have a valid schedule (C118.4).

   Each acknowledgement is stored on the version with the publisher's id and time, shown in the console beside its step, and written to the audit entry of the publish.
5. **Restore** (decision 19; the two public statements disagree): restoring a version creates a draft from it, which is then published like any draft, so a restore never bypasses the publish rules.
6. **Diff** (`GET /workflow-versions/:id/diff?against=`): computed on the server from the two definitions keyed by step id: steps added, removed and changed (each change a list of JSON Pointers with before and after values; definitions hold configuration, never subject data), edges added and removed, trigger, input, output and variable changes, and layout-only changes listed separately.
7. **Events:** `workflow-version.published` on every publish, `workflow.published` when the workflow's live version changes (a full publish or a finished rollout), each with an audit entry.

### C117. The engine: storage and execution

1. **Model** (decision 1; engine note 2.1). A persisted state machine per run and step over Postgres; BullMQ on Valkey only wakes steps up; an append-only journal is history, never the replay source. `advance(definition, runState, completedStep) → { nextState, stepsToMakeReady }` in `packages/workflow-engine/src/interpreter/` is pure and unit-tested with golden graphs, so a future simulation can replay it, but recovery never depends on replay. No external engine is adopted: Inngest (SSPL), Restate (BSL), n8n (Sustainable Use) and Windmill (AGPL) fail rule 4; Temporal, Hatchet, Trigger.dev, Conductor and Kestra add services; DBOS (MIT) keeps run state outside Drizzle, sealing and redaction.
2. **Tables** (migration in `foundation-8a`; sealed columns row-bound, in `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` and `REWRAP_TABLES`):

   | Table | Holds |
   |---|---|
   | `workflows` | environment, name, description, kind, status, the published and draft version ids, `archived_at` |
   | `workflow_versions` | number, status, immutable `definition` jsonb, `definition_sha256`, `schema_version`, trigger type and config, `grant`, `egress`, `acknowledgements`, `published_at`, `published_by (type, id)`, `promoted_from` |
   | `workflow_deployments` | status, `versions` (token, percentage, label) |
   | `workflow_triggers` | the publish-time index `(environment_id, kind, event_name)` → workflow, version or deployment, sealed-free filter (conditions hold no subject data) |
   | `workflow_trigger_deliveries` | one row per (event, workflow) owed a run: unique `(event_id, workflow_id)`, status `pending`, `run_created`, `filtered`, `refused`, `expired`, reason code, `not_before` |
   | `workflow_runs` | version, deployment and rollout label, status, `waiting_on`, trigger kind and reference (`trigger_delivery_id` unique, `schedule_fire` unique, `idempotency_key` unique per workflow), creator `(type, id)`, root and parent run, `depth`, `trigger_depth`, step-execution count, `deadline_at`, error type and step, sealed `trigger_snapshot`, sealed `inputs`, sealed `variables`, sealed `outputs`, `redacted_at`, `values_purged_at` |
   | `workflow_run_steps` | node id plus iteration path (`loop[3]/send_email`), status (`pending`, `ready`, `running`, `waiting`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `skipped`, `canceled`, `timed_out`), `due_at`, `attempt`, `claimed_by`, `claimed_until`, `enqueued_at`, wait fields (event names, correlation key, `timeout_at`, the resolving event id), join counter, sealed `input`, sealed `output`, error type, timings; unique `(run_id, node_id, iteration_path)`; partial indexes on `ready`, `waiting` and `running`; per-table autovacuum with a low scale factor (engine note 2.4) |
   | `workflow_run_journal` | append-only (F12: a trigger refuses updates; deletion only under `openverify.environment_deletion` or `openverify.retention`, set only by the C16 pass): `run_created`, `step_ready`, `step_started`, `step_succeeded`, `step_failed`, `retry_scheduled`, `wait_started`, `wait_resolved`, `wait_timed_out`, `run_canceled`, `run_resumed`, … with attempt, duration and error type; **never a value** |
   | `workflow_step_effects` | unique `effect_key`, kind, status (`claimed`, `done`, `unknown`), sealed result (C117.5) |
   | `workflow_run_subjects` | the object ids a run was triggered for, every object an action read or wrote, and object ids in step outputs (≤ 1,000 per run; past that the run is marked `subjects_overflow`, C120.9), for redaction |
   | `workflow_run_files` (`wfrf_`) | trigger file inputs (C118.2): `encryptBytes` with the id as context through `FileStorage`, the wrapped DEK, a sealed filename, type, size; deleted with the run's values and its subjects |
   | `workflow_schedules` | C118.4 |
   | `workflow_environment_slots` | per environment: the in-flight cap and `last_served_at` (C117.8); no counter |

3. **Two transaction shapes** (engine note 2.4, measured on the pinned image: 1,577 effectful steps/s and 2,880 control-flow steps/s at 32 clients with durable commits; decision 17 keeps `synchronous_commit` on):
   - **control-flow nodes** (conditional, parallel fork and join, loop counters, Update Variable, Calculate, output) run inside the transaction that completed the previous step: nothing external happens, so they need no claim;
   - **effectful nodes** (every other action, module and wait start) get claim and complete: a sweep `SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` picks `ready` steps and a conditional `UPDATE … WHERE id = $step AND status = 'ready'` claims one with a fresh `claimed_by` token, `claimed_until = now() + lease`, `attempt + 1` (F31); completion writes only `WHERE id = $step AND claimed_by = $token AND status = 'running'` (Phase 6 C74.1), with the journal row, the step's output and the next steps in the same transaction.
4. **Leases and recovery:** the lease is the action's time limit plus a margin (an HTTPS call with a 30 s ceiling gets 45 s, a report run minutes), extended by a heartbeat every third of the lease for long actions; a retryable error releases the claim before rethrowing; two detectors (BullMQ's stalled check and a sweeper that reclaims `running` steps past `claimed_until`); a step claimed more than 10 times fails with `execution_error` (the C74.1 crash-loop cap).
5. **Effect keys and delivery** (actions note 5.2, engine note 2.2). Every side effect has the key `<wfr id>/<node id>/<iteration path>`, independent of the attempt:
   - internal effects (create, transition, tag, assign, list item, transaction, case) are **exactly once**: the effect row and the change are written in one transaction, through the target service's `…InTx` form; a created object also carries a unique `(environment_id, workflow_run_step_id)`, so a repeated attempt returns the first result; a target already in the requested state is `no_change`; a transition another actor made first is `already_decided` (as Phase 6 records `inquiry_already_decided`), never overwritten;
   - HTTPS requests are **at least once**, carrying the key as `Idempotency-Key` (C124.6);
   - messages are **at most once**: the effect is `claimed` before sending and `done` after; a crash in between leaves `unknown`, which a resume reports and does not resend (C126.1);
   - Evaluate Code is pure; its output is recorded, so a resume reads it instead of re-running it.

   The docs (`docs/workflows/delivery.md`) list each action's guarantee.
6. **Retries** (engine note 3.7): the engine owns business retries; BullMQ attempts cover infrastructure only. A failed attempt that may retry records `retry_scheduled` with a new `due_at` from the step's `retry.intervals-seconds` (or the action's default: none for internal actions, which are transactional). Retryable: network errors, timeouts, HTTP 408, 429 (honouring `Retry-After` up to the next interval) and 5xx, a provider's transient code, a serialisation or deadlock error. Never retried: other 4xx, a destination the guard refuses, validation, expression and template errors, permission errors, Evaluate Code's deterministic failures, a redacted or deleted subject. After the last attempt the step fails; the run follows its `error` edge if it has one, continues if `continue-on-error`, else ends `errored`.
7. **Limits** (decision 11; public values where public, behaviour note 1.8, else ours, engine note 3.9; each enforced with a code and published in `docs/workflows/limits.md`):

   | Limit | Value | Code |
   |---|---|---|
   | Wait (events or time) | 30 days (public) | `definition_invalid` |
   | Loop items (for-each, map) and while iterations | 100 (public); a longer list errors the step | `loop_limit_exceeded` |
   | Module nesting | none: a module has no module step (public) | `definition_invalid` |
   | Nodes / edges per version | 200 / 400 | `definition_invalid` |
   | Step executions per run (loops and retries included) | 5,000 | `step_limit_exceeded` |
   | Run duration | 90 days | `run_deadline_exceeded` (the public whole-run `run-failure`) |
   | Parallel branches / nesting | 10 / 3 | `definition_invalid` |
   | Run Workflow and Schedule Workflow depth | 5 | `run_depth_exceeded` |
   | Workflows started by one event | 50 | at publish `publish_trigger_fanout_exceeded`; at run time the 51st delivery `refused` and listed by `GET /workflow-trigger-deliveries` |
   | Subjects recorded per run | 1,000 | `subjects_overflow` on the run (C120.9) |
   | Trigger causality depth | 8 | `trigger_depth_exceeded` |
   | Trigger snapshot / step output journaled / variables | 256 KiB / 64 KiB / 1 MiB | `payload_too_large` |

8. **Fairness** (decision 10; engine note 8.1; F21). BullMQ's round-robin groups are commercial (Pro), so admission is in Postgres: a step is enqueued only while its environment has fewer than its cap of steps `running` or enqueued (default 50, sandbox and production capped separately, overridable per environment), counted through the partial index at enqueue time; the count is approximate, needs no hot row and no crash recovery (a reclaimed lease changes nothing), and the benchmark measures it; the dispatcher serves environments in rotation by `last_served_at`; two queues, `workflow-step` for interactive triggers (an inquiry or case event, a person's trigger) and `workflow-step-batch` for schedules, recurring ticks and fan-outs; per-action concurrency caps inside the slot for Evaluate Code and HTTPS (5 each, ours); run creation limited per environment by the GCRA limiter in defer mode (excess deliveries wait with `not_before`, never dropped).
9. **Jobs** carry `{ environmentId, runId, stepId, attempt }` only, with job id `<step id>-<attempt>`, removed on completion and on failure (the left-over-id trap). Timers are `due_at` rows (C119.2): a BullMQ delayed job is added only when the due time is within an hour, and a sweeper adds the rest as they enter the horizon, so a lost Valkey delays work and never loses it.
10. **Valkey check** (decision 18): the worker reads `CONFIG GET maxmemory-policy` at start and logs an error unless it is `noeviction`; it never refuses to start.
11. **Observability:** logs carry ids, node ids, action keys, outcomes, durations and error types, never variables, outputs, expression values, bodies or template output; errors reach Valkey and the journal as codes (`describeJobError`). Metrics: steps per second, queue lag (claim time minus `due_at`) per environment, in-flight against cap, timer lag, waits by age, failures by action and type, deliveries refused by reason. A non-gated benchmark script (`apps/api/scripts/workflow-benchmark.ts`) re-measures the step transactions on the pinned image, and its result is recorded in `docs/PROGRESS.md` (decision 22).

### C118. Triggers

1. **Event triggers through the outbox** (engine note 4.1, option A). Publishing rebuilds `workflow_triggers` for the workflow. `stage()` (C5) gains one indexed lookup of `(environment_id, 'trigger-event', event_name)` and inserts one `workflow_trigger_deliveries` row per match `ON CONFLICT (event_id, workflow_id) DO NOTHING`, in the event's own transaction:
   - exactly once per event and workflow by construction; a delivery exists if and only if its event committed;
   - the trigger's criteria (a C114 condition over the event payload, typically `trigger.template.id` in a list) are evaluated by the worker, never inside `stage()`, so an operator's rule can never slow or fail an inquiry transition; the worker marks the delivery `run_created` or `filtered`;
   - several workflows may listen to one event and start with no order between them (public W3); a workflow may listen to several events;
   - not retroactive: an event older than the version's activation never starts it (public);
   - simulated webhook events (`createEvent` with `simulated: true`) never trigger, and a test pins it;
   - `dispatch()` enqueues a `workflow-trigger` job per delivery after commit; a sweep re-enqueues pending deliveries, as for webhooks;
   - C16's event deletion keeps events that a pending delivery still needs, and a run keeps its own sealed trigger snapshot, so it never needs the event row later; a delivery to a paused or archived workflow is marked `refused` (`workflow_not_running`) when processed, never left pending; a pending delivery older than `event-retention-days` becomes `expired` and stops holding its event; delivery rows are deleted with the run skeleton (P27);
   - refused, filtered and expired deliveries are served by `GET /workflow-trigger-deliveries` and shown in the runs view (H18);
   - **post-commit wake-ups** (F02): `StagedEvent` gains `workflowDeliveries` and `waitCandidates`, `EventPublisherOptions` gains optional `workflowTriggers` and `workflowSteps` schedulers, and `dispatch()` enqueues them best-effort after commit (the sweeps stay the outbox); all three places that build a publisher pass them, with a test per site.
2. **API trigger** (public W4): `POST /api/v1/workflows/:id/trigger` with `{ data: { attributes: { fields: { … } } } }`; `meta.params` and top-level attributes refused; the fields are validated by the live version's `inputs` (zod built from the declared types; file values `{ filename, data }` with base64 data, as the public contract requires since 2026-10-27, decoded size ≤ 10 MiB each and the request ≤ 14 MiB (this route's own body limit; Fastify's default stays 1 MiB elsewhere), typed by magic bytes and stored in `workflow_run_files` (C117.2), in scope as file ids only, never as bytes (P41, F17); a file that reaches `create_case` or `attach_objects_to_case` goes through C135's typing, scanning and rendition path, and a file value in an HTTPS body is an egress mark); a malformed file value answers 400 with `source.pointer` (public), any other field 422 `trigger_input_invalid` with `source.pointer`; the answer is the created run (`200`, `in_progress`). A paused or archived workflow answers 409 `workflow_not_running`.
3. **Scheduled runs** (public W5): the Schedule Workflow action creates a `scheduled-run` (`srrun_`) row: target workflow, subject, base time (the step's run time or a date field of an object), offset, units (minutes, hours, days), before or after. A sweeper creates the run at its time with creator `scheduled-run`. A scheduled run is cancelled with its subject's redaction and when its target workflow is archived. Scheduled runs are the answer to waits beyond 30 days (public).
4. **Recurring trigger** (ours, decision 24; the public shape is not specified):
   - config: a five-field cron (or `every` minutes ≥ 1) in an IANA time zone (decision 9), an object type (`account`, `case`, `inquiry`, `report`, `transaction`), a filter over the object's plaintext attributes (status, template or type, tags, timestamps; the C137.2 search grammar), and a batch cap (default 100, at most 1,000 per tick);
   - each tick creates one run per matching object, oldest first, through the delivery path with GCRA admission, and stores a cursor so the next tick continues; an outage of several periods gives one catch-up tick, not a burst (the C74.3 (b) rule);
   - `workflow_schedules (cron, tz, next_fire_at, last_fired_at, cursor)`; fire times are unique `(schedule_id, fire_time)`; `cron-parser` 5.10.2 parses in strict mode with a `0` seconds field prefixed; no `H`; the console shows the next five fire times in the zone and in UTC;
   - **DST rules** (decision 8), computed with `temporal-polyfill` 1.0.5 (`ZonedDateTime` with explicit disambiguation; added to `packages/workflow-engine` in 8A Stage 1a, F18) and golden-tested in Europe/London, America/New_York, Australia/Lord_Howe, Asia/Kolkata and Pacific/Chatham whatever the library does: a fixed local time that does not exist fires at the first valid minute after the gap; a fixed local time that occurs twice fires once, at the first occurrence; intervals follow elapsed time.
5. **Manual runs** (public W7, `trigger-direct`; H26): `POST /workflows/:id/manual-runs` (ours) with `object-ids[]` (≤ 1,000) or a CSV of ids read by `readCsv` (`packages/checks/src/csv/read.ts`, added in 8A Stage 1a with Phase 7's importer options and error codes, F13), one run per object, the operator's principal, an audit entry; the public trigger route keeps its public body. Inquiry-embedded workflows wait for the Phase 9 flow builder.
6. **Verification trigger** (decision 25; one public sentence): an event trigger restricted to `verification.*` events with an optional verification-template filter.
7. **Account trigger** (public W8). An account action (`accac_`, `GET`, `POST /account-actions`, `GET`, `PATCH /account-actions/:id`, `workflows:write`) binds a running workflow whose trigger is `trigger-account` to an account type, with a label and a parameter form (the trigger's `inputs`, prefilled from `account.fields.*` paths). Prefill is rendered under the viewer's mask, as for case actions (P38): a masked field stays empty and the run reads the live value under its grant. `POST /api/v1/accounts/:id/run-action` (`data.account-action-id`, `data.parameters`) validates the parameters against the inputs, needs `workflows:trigger` and `accounts:read` plus the version's grant (C121.3), and answers `{ data: { workflow-run-id } }` as the public path does. `GET /accounts/:id/available-actions` (`accounts:read` and `workflows:trigger`) lists the account actions the caller may run, filtered by the caller's grant, so the console's actions menu works for reviewers (H14). Case actions are C140.1.
8. **Loop guards** (decision 12; engine note 4.4):
   - an event staged inside a step carries the internal `caused_by_run_id`, and an event staged by a request the internal `caused_by_principal` (C121.3); a run created from it has `trigger_depth = cause + 1`, refused past 8 with `trigger_depth_exceeded`;
   - a delivery whose workflow already appears in the causal chain of the same root run is refused with `trigger_cycle`, unless the workflow opts in with `allow-self-trigger`;
   - a per-(workflow, subject) rate guard (60 runs a minute) through the GCRA limiter in defer mode;
   - publish warns when a graph emits its own trigger event (the registry knows which events each action emits, C122.1);
   - `workflow-run.*` and `workflow*.published` events never trigger workflows.
9. **Ordering** (decision 13): no order across runs by default; actions re-read the subject's current state rather than trusting the trigger snapshot. A per-workflow option `one-run-per-subject` makes later runs for a subject wait in `in_progress` with `waiting-on: earlier-run` until the earlier one finishes.

### C119. Step semantics

1. **Action** steps call the registry entry (C122) with their rendered config; `continue-on-error` records the failure on the step and follows `next`; an `error` edge takes precedence (C119.7).
2. **Wait for time:** the step becomes `waiting` with `due_at = now() + seconds` set from the database clock; durations are minimums ("not before `due_at`, normally within seconds, at worst within the sweep interval plus a lease after an infrastructure failure").
3. **Wait for events** (engine note 3.2; F01, V01):
   - the step stores its event names, the correlation key (the subject's object id, plain: ids are not personal data), an optional filter condition over the event payload (with its `on-null`, C114.6), and `timeout_at`;
   - **the wait index:** publishing records each waited-on (environment, event name) pair of the version in `workflow_wait_index`; `stage()` inserts one `workflow_wait_candidates` row (event id, environment, event name, the subject ids, `xact = pg_current_xact_id()`) for every staged event whose (environment, name) is in the index, in the event's own transaction, with no lock and whatever is waiting;
   - **entering the wait:** a wait step enters `waiting` in the transaction that completes its predecessor, which takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hash(environment, correlation key))` **before** it writes the step row and records `waiting_snapshot = pg_current_snapshot()` and its own `pg_current_xact_id()`; it then checks the committed candidates for its key and event names that its snapshot does not see (`NOT pg_visible_in_snapshot(xact, waiting_snapshot)`, and not its own transaction's events) and resolves itself at once if one exists;
   - **the event side, after commit:** `dispatch()` enqueues a wait-match job per candidate (the 15 s `workflow-wait-match-sweep` is the outbox for unprocessed candidates); the job takes the same advisory lock, then applies one conditional update to every `waiting` step with that key and name whose snapshot does not see the candidate, recording the event id, and marks the candidate processed. An event that committed before the wait began is visible in its snapshot and never resolves it; one that committed after is seen either by the registration's check (committed before it) or by the job (which waits for the registration's lock and then sees its committed row), so exactly one side resolves it and none is lost. The lock is taken only in the engine's own transactions, never in a domain transaction, and the registration takes it before any step row, so the two cannot deadlock;
   - the worker evaluates the filter when the step resumes, and a non-matching event puts the wait back;
   - the timeout sweeper moves `waiting AND timeout_at <= now()` to `timed_out`; the event and the timeout race on the same row under Read Committed, so exactly one wins;
   - Testcontainers race tests: an event that starts before the registration and commits after its check; an event that commits between the registration's snapshot and its check; event versus timeout; two events at once; plus a golden test that an event committed before the wait never resolves it. Candidates are deleted once processed and older than the longest wait (30 days);
   - on timeout the run follows `next` (`on-timeout: continue`, the public default) or errors with `wait_timed_out` (`error`, the public "error on expiration"); the step output says which (`resolved-by: event | timeout`, the event id, and for a `case.resolved` event the resolver's type and id, `resolved-via: single | bulk` and `resolver-held-inquiries-write`, from the event's cause, C121.3), which C128.2 reads; Run Report and Run Verification waits never continue on timeout (C127.2, C127.3).
4. **Parallel** (decision 26; public W15): the fork creates one ready step per branch in its transaction; the join is a counter row decremented in each branch's completion transaction (`UPDATE … SET remaining = remaining - 1 … RETURNING`), and exactly one completion makes it ready. Join-all only: every branch must finish; a failed branch fails the parallel step unless its failing step continues on error. Branches share no order; parallel updates of one variable are refused at publish (C115.4).
5. **Conditional** (public W16): routes are evaluated in order and the first whose condition holds is taken; the `else` port is last. The step output records the route key, which the decision record reads (C128.1).
6. **Loop** (public W17): for-each over a list path, while a condition holds (with its required maximum), map collecting one `array-map-output` value per item; sequential by default, `concurrency` up to 10 runs iterations like parallel branches; each iteration's steps have their own rows (`loop[3]/…`); a list over 100 items fails the step with `loop_limit_exceeded`, never a silent truncation.
7. **Errors and edges:** a step failure follows, in order: its `error` edge (ours), its `continue-on-error`, else the run ends `errored` with the step's error type. No automatic compensation (decision 15): an author routes a failure to a review, a case or a message, and `docs/workflows/actions.md` says which actions cannot be undone.
8. **Module** (public W18): a module is a workflow of `kind: module` with a `module-input` start node and declared inputs; a module step runs the module's steps **inside the parent run** (public: one run), in their own iteration path (`module:<step id>/…`), with the module version pinned at the time the step starts (`latest-published` resolves then and is recorded) or a named `wfv_`; a module reads parent variables only as inputs; no nesting; not usable by account and case actions (public).
9. **Run Workflow** (public): starts a separate run of another workflow with mapped inputs, creator `workflow-run`, depth + 1 (≤ 5), lineage cycle refused; the step can wait for it (`wait-for-completion`, ours) or not.
10. **Output** (public): evaluates its mappings into the run's `outputs`, which the runs table and `workflow-run.completed` show (outputs are sealed and masked like step data; the event carries output names, not values).
11. **Approve and decline do not end a run** (public 1.4): the run continues to `output`.
