### C120. Runs

1. **Statuses** (decision 20): `in_progress`, `completed`, `errored` (public, as seen in the spec's examples) and ours `canceled` (spelled as the existing session status spells it). Waiting is `in_progress` with `waiting-on` (`event`, `time`, `earlier-run`, `child-run`, `admission`). Run attributes: `status`, `created-at`, `completed-at`, `meta.processing-time-seconds` (public), and ours `waiting-on`, `rollout-label`, `trigger-type`, `error`, `outputs`, `subject-ids`, `step-outcomes {succeeded, failed-continued, timed-out, unknown-effect, skipped}` (H32; the console shows "completed with N step errors"; `filter[has-step-errors]`), `test-run-id`. Relationships: `workflow`, `workflow-version`, `creator` (public union `user`, `event`, `scheduled-run`, plus ours `api-key`, `workflow-run`, `account-action`, `case-action`). An API-started run's creator is the `api-key` (ours; the public spec shows `null`, documented; H26).
2. **Errors** (decision 21; public W26, an open set in the spec): the run's `error {type, message}` and `errored-step {name, key, type, action-key}`. OpenVerify emits the six named types, `missing-input-error`, `invalid-input-error`, `configuration-error`, `resource-error`, `unknown-error`, `run-failure`, and types the field as an open string with those values documented, so adding one later is not breaking. Each internal code (C117.7, C122.5) maps to one of them; `message` is set only for configuration and input errors and never carries a value (public rule), other types carry a fixed text. The plaintext error columns hold codes only; any text a step produced (an Evaluate Code message, a provider's reason) lives sealed in the step's output (C123.6).
3. **Resume** (public W27): `POST /workflow-runs/:id/resume` on an `errored` run re-readies the failed step (attempt counters continue) and fires `workflow-run.resumed`; completed effects are not repeated (their effect rows are `done`); an effect left `unknown` blocks the resume with `run_not_resumable` and `unknown_effect` naming the step, unless `meta.skip-unknown-effects: true` acknowledges it (audited; the step's output then records `delivery: unknown` and the run continues); a run stopped by `publisher_revoked` is never resumable, only rerun (C121.5).
4. **Rerun** (public): `POST /workflow-runs/:id/rerun` creates a new run of the **latest published** version with the same trigger snapshot and inputs, creator the operator, linked by `rerun-of`.
5. **Cancel** (ours, W28): a database write first: the run becomes `canceled`, pending and waiting steps `canceled`, child runs cancelled; running steps receive BullMQ's abort signal and their completion is fenced out; queued jobs removed best-effort; effects already made stay made; `workflow-run.canceled`.
6. **Reading runs** (P11; decision 78): runs and steps are read with `workflow-runs:read` (in `NOT_FOR_VIEWERS`), definitions with `workflows:read`. Run values are masked **fail closed**: without PII visibility (`pii:view` for users, `canViewPii` for keys) every step input and output, run output, variable and trigger-snapshot value is `null`, except members that the registry's output schema (or the trigger's contract) declares non-PII: ids, enums, statuses, booleans, counts and timestamps. Untyped values (an Evaluate Code result, an HTTPS body without a schema, an undeclared variable) are always masked without PII visibility. The same rule applies to stored API logs of the run routes and to the console. Secret values never appear: inputs record the unresolved templates and outputs are scrubbed (C125.5). A test per exit (route, include, API log, console).
7. **Test runs** (decision 23; public Simulate, W33): `POST /workflows/:id/test-runs` in sandbox environments only (`test_runs_sandbox_only` elsewhere), on the draft or a published version, with a trigger object (an existing id or a synthetic payload validated by the trigger schema), optional `start-step` and `end-step`, and `overrides` of step outputs.
   - **Authority** (P36): the draft's grant is derived exactly as publish would (C121.2) and the caller must hold it; an irreversible step (redact, adverse) runs only when the request carries `meta.acknowledgements` with its key, otherwise it is simulated (`simulated: true`, no effect, no decision record: decision 77).
   - **No egress** (H15, H17, V18): every message goes to the recording provider whatever the recipient; an HTTPS step must be overridden, or it errors `test_run_egress_disabled`. A real call is made only when the request's `allow-hosts[]` names the step's host, the caller is a **user** holding `pii:view`, the request carries `egress:<step id>` for every PII-tainted step, the host is on `workflow-http-hosts` when that setting is on, and each send writes an audit entry, as for a published version (C121.4); a test.
   - **Marked:** `test-run-id` is stamped on the run, its decision records, the objects it creates and the events it causes; `overridden-steps[]` is recorded; events a test run causes trigger no workflows unless the request opts in.
   - The sandbox console capture of Evaluate Code is stored sealed on the step, shown only to the test run's creator, and purged with the run's values. The canvas simulation overlay is Phase 9.
8. **Retention** (decision 14; C16): `workflow-run-retention-days` (default 30, 1–3,650) after a run ends nulls its sealed columns (trigger snapshot, inputs, variables, outputs, step inputs and outputs, effect results) and deletes its trigger files (C118.2); `workflow-run-history-retention-days` (default 365) deletes the skeleton, with its trigger-delivery rows. Unfinished runs are never removed by retention; the 90-day run deadline bounds them.
9. **Redaction:**
   - redacting an inquiry, account, case, report or transaction finds its runs through `workflow_run_subjects` (which also records the object ids that appear in step outputs: graph-result accounts, list items, report ids): their sealed columns are nulled, trigger files deleted, waits on the subject cancelled with `subject_redacted`, scheduled runs about it cancelled, running steps fenced out; `stubEventPayloads` and `forgetStoredResponses` cover the run ids;
   - **overflow** (P33): a run that would record more than 1,000 subjects is marked `subjects_overflow`, and any redaction in its environment also nulls the values of every overflowed run (a partial index makes it a short scan); `docs/workflows/limits.md` states it;
   - **copies** (P20): a PII-to-PII copy a run made into another object (fields, case fields, comments, list items) is recorded on its effect row as `(target id, source subject ids)`; redacting a source subject redacts the comments and case fields a run wrote from it, and lists the other targets in the redaction result and audit entry;
   - `DELETE /workflow-runs/:id` (`workflow-runs:redact`, admin only: P35) nulls one run's sealed columns and deletes its files;
   - the hook is `redactRunsForSubjectsInTx` (C120.9 owner `api-engine`), called from `services/redaction.ts`, the Phase 6 report redaction and the Phase 7 transaction redaction (F35).

### C121. Authority

1. **A principal of its own** (actions note 5.1). A run acts as `{ type: 'workflow-run', id: 'wfr_…' }`: the audit actor enum gains `workflow_run`, the services' `Acting` type gains the same actor (F11), and each audit entry's metadata carries the workflow, version, node id and the publisher. Objects a run creates record it as their creator (the public list-item `creator` can be a workflow run).
2. **A frozen grant, bounded by the publisher** (P10, F06). At publish the engine derives each version's grant from the registry (C122.1) as `{ scopes, pii }`: `scopes` holds **API scopes only**, and `pii` is true when any step needs unmasked values (list probes, face lists, PII-kind graph parameters, an egress mark). Operator permissions (`pii:view`, `environments:production`) are never part of a grant; they are publish-time requirements on the publisher: publishing needs every scope in `scopes`, `workflows:publish`, `pii:view` when `pii` is true, and `environments:production` for a production environment. Publish, rollout, restore and promote are **user-only** routes (API keys get 403). The run then acts with exactly that grant, and the step executor checks every service call against it, so a derivation bug fails closed.
3. **No amplification** (P07, P08):
   - **by a principal:** a run started by a principal (the API trigger, manual runs, account and case actions) needs `workflows:trigger`, every scope in the grant (`missingPermissions(principal, scopes)`), and `canViewPii(principal)` when `pii` is true; otherwise 403 naming what is missing. Inputs are validated against the version's schema and object ids in them resolve only inside the environment;
   - **by a child run:** a child's version grant must be a subset of the parent run's effective grant (the parent version's grant, intersected with the triggering principal's permissions when a principal started the root), checked when the child is created (`latest-published` can change); otherwise the step errors `run_grant_insufficient` naming the missing scopes. Publish derives `run_workflow` and `schedule_workflow` grants as `workflows:trigger` plus the target's grant at that time and warns when the target later widens;
   - **the cause of an event** (V04, V21): `PublishEventInput` gains an internal `cause { runId, principal, testRunId, bulkId, principalHeldInquiriesWrite }`, never in a payload, filled from a request-scoped `AsyncLocalStorage` context (`apps/api/src/events/cause-context.ts`) that the auth plugin sets for requests, the step executor for runs (with the **root** run's causing principal, so an event staged by run A carries the principal that caused A, and run B started by it is bounded by it), the test-run executor, and the bulk job; `stage()` reads the context when no cause is passed, so the 17 existing call sites need no change; the publisher writes it into `events.caused_by_run_id`, `caused_by_principal`, `caused_by_test_run_id` and `caused_by_bulk_id`; a test per call path (request, run, test run, bulk, flow, system job);
   - **by an event:** an event staged by a request carries its `caused_by_principal`. When an event-triggered run, or a wait resumed by an event, reaches a `decline_inquiry`, `approve_inquiry`, `redact_object` or other adverse step (C122.1), the causing principal must hold that step's scope; otherwise the step errors `cause_lacks_scope` and the inquiry is marked for review. Events with no principal (the end user's flow, system jobs) are exempt.
4. **PII egress** (decision 41). A step whose rendered values carry PII taint (C115.6) and leave the platform (HTTPS, email, SMS, Slack) is marked in the version's `egress` with the paths and the destination host or channel; publishing it needs the publisher's `pii:view` and the acknowledgement `egress:<step id>`; the console shows the mark on the step and the version; every such send writes an audit entry with the step and destination, never the values. The default templates carry ids and console links only.
5. **When the publisher loses rights** (P09, V05, V08; decision 45): a `publisher-rights` repeatable sweep (60 s, `api-workflows`) compares each running workflow's publisher with its live version's requirements (the user's status and current permissions, through `missingPermissions`). When the publisher is deactivated or deleted, or lacks `pii:view`, `environments:production`, or a scope used by an egress, `redact_object`, `decline_inquiry` or other adverse step, the workflow is **paused** (status `paused`, an audit entry, a `workflow.paused` event, a console banner on every workflow page), and unfinished runs end their next such step with `publisher_revoked` (`configuration-error`). Such runs are **rerun-only**: resume answers `run_not_resumable` with `publisher_revoked`, and a rerun (C120.4) starts on the latest published version, once a holder of the rights has republished. Losing a scope that only internal non-adverse steps use keeps the version running on its frozen grant, flagged in the console and audited, until someone republishes. A test per case.
6. **Environment isolation:** every repository call from the engine is scoped by the run's environment; an object id from another environment resolves to "not found", never to its object (a test per action).

### C122. The action registry

1. **Registry** (`packages/workflow-engine/src/actions/registry.ts` for the contract, `apps/api/src/workflows/actions/` for the implementations), mirroring the check registry. One entry per action version:
   - `key` (snake_case; the public product documents only `run_report`, so the rest are ours, behaviour note WA26) and `version` (an integer; a change to the config or output schema is a new version, and old versions stay while any published version or unfinished run references them);
   - `kind`: `internal` (an OpenVerify object), `egress` (leaves the platform), `compute` or `control`;
   - `config` and `output` zod schemas, each output member marked PII or not (C120.6 masks by these marks); `grant` (`{ scopes, pii }`, possibly depending on its config); `emits` (the event names it can cause, for C118.8's warning); `delivery` (`exactly-once`, `at-least-once`, `at-most-once`, `pure`, `control`); `retry`; `availability` (an adapter it needs, C122.5); `triggers` and `requires`;
   - `adverse` (P42, V10; decision 80): `always`, `by-config` or `never`. `decline_inquiry` is `always`; `add_list_item` and `add_face_list_item` are adverse unless the target list's `purpose` is `allow` or `watch` (a new optional list attribute, `block` by default; a C97 amendment); `update_transaction_status` is `by-config`, adverse when the target status is in the transaction type's `adverse-statuses` (a C104 amendment, default `declined`); `update_object` archiving an account is `always`. Adverse steps get C128's record and gate;
   - `stage`: `ga`, or `beta` where the output carries H-tier results: `run_report` and `get_list_match` are `beta` (fuzzy names, faces, backgrounds and approximate IP data), and their outputs carry per-match `{ method, tier, stage, confidence, approximate }` (H10).
   - **Per-type entries** (F33): the generic actions (`tag_object`, `remove_tag`, `copy_tags`, `fetch_object`, `update_object`, `redact_object`) dispatch through a per-type table that 8A fills for inquiries, accounts, reports, transactions and verifications, and 8B extends with cases in its own files.
2. **Catalogue** (every public action marked MATCH in the behaviour note, plus ours; the owner Workflow in the last column; grants list API scopes, with `pii` marked where the step needs unmasked values):

   | Key | Public name | Service it calls | Grant | Delivery | Owner |
   |---|---|---|---|---|---|
   | `approve_inquiry`, `decline_inquiry`, `mark_inquiry_for_review` | Approve, Decline, Mark Inquiry for Review | `applyInquiryAction` under the row lock, with `meta.comment` sealed in the decision record | `inquiries:write` | exactly once | 8A |
   | `create_inquiry` | Create Inquiry | `createInquiry` (optionally linked to an account) | `inquiries:write` (+ `accounts:read` to link) | exactly once | 8A |
   | `send_inquiry_link_email`, `send_inquiry_link_sms` | Inquiry Link Email and SMS | a one-time link (never a reusable session token) placed only in the outgoing message; the output holds `{ one-time-link-id, expires-at, delivery }` (P32) | `inquiries:write`, `inquiry-sessions:write` | at most once | 8A |
   | `run_report`, `rerun_report` | Run Report; ours | Phase 6 report creation with `origin: workflow`; a manual run of existing reports chosen by a server-side selector (C127.2, C127.12) | `reports:write` (+ the link's scope, Phase 6 decision 39) | exactly once, then a wait | 8A |
   | `run_verification` | Run Verification | the verification types that need no end-user capture (listed by Stage 1a from the verification registry), then a wait | `inquiries:write` | exactly once | 8A |
   | `get_list_match`, `add_list_item`, `add_face_list_item` | Get List Match, Add Item To List, Add Item To Face List | C99.1 `matchLists`, `ListService.createItem`, the C99.7 path | `lists:read` (pii); `lists:write` (pii) | pure; exactly once | 8A |
   | `run_graph_query`, `sync_graph` | Run Graph Query, Sync Graph | `POST /graph-queries` (C102), then a wait; the account's graph-index jobs (C127.5) | `graph:write` (pii for PII-kind parameters); `graph:read` | exactly once; control | 8A |
   | `find_or_create_account` | Find or Create Account | the account service by type and identifier | `accounts:write` | exactly once | 8A |
   | `create_transaction`, `update_transaction_status` | Create Transaction, Update Transaction Status | the C105 services | `transactions:write` | exactly once | 8A |
   | `set_relation`, `unset_relation` | Set and Unset Relation | Phase 5's `addAccountRelation` for accounts, the C105 relations for transactions | the target's write scope (`accounts:write` or `transactions:write`) | exactly once | 8A |
   | `trust_device` | Trust Device | C127.9 | `devices:write` (new) | exactly once | 8A |
   | `tag_object`, `remove_tag`, `copy_tags` | Tag Object, Remove Tag, Copy Tags | the tag services of each object type | the type's write scope | exactly once | 8A (cases in 8B) |
   | `fetch_object`, `update_object` | Fetch Persona Object, Update Object | the repositories; `updateInquiry`, `updateAccount`, the C105 update | the type's read or write scope | pure; exactly once | 8A (cases in 8B) |
   | `redact_object` | Redact Object | `RedactionService` per type | the type's `:redact` scope | exactly once | 8A (cases in 8B) |
   | `evaluate_code` | Evaluate Code | C123 | none | pure | 8A |
   | `calculate`, `update_variable` | Calculate, Update Variable | the interpreter | none | control | 8A |
   | `run_workflow`, `schedule_workflow` | Run Workflow, Schedule Workflow | the engine | `workflows:trigger` plus the target's grant (C121.3) | exactly once | 8A |
   | `make_https_request` | Make HTTPS Request | C124 | none (pii when it carries PII taint) | at least once | 8A |
   | `send_email`, `send_sms`, `send_slack_message` | Send Email, Send SMS, Send Slack Message | C126 | none (pii when it carries PII taint) | at most once | 8A |
   | `create_case`, `assign_case`, `update_case_status`, `add_case_comment`, `add_case_to_queue`, `attach_objects_to_case`, `set_case_sla`, `clear_case_sla` | the case actions | C140.3 | `cases:write` (`cases:assign` to assign) | exactly once | 8B |

   Not built (decision 27): custom functions, Transliterate Fields, Send Case Message, the Zendesk, Salesforce, HubSpot, GPT and MX Bank steps (Make HTTPS Request covers them).
3. **Trigger fit:** an action declares which trigger types it serves (for example `add_face_list_item` only after an inquiry-producing step: the trigger is an inquiry event, or an earlier `create_inquiry` or `fetch_object` of an inquiry, public WA20); the graph check refuses a misplaced one.
4. **Catalogue route:** `GET /api/v1/workflow-actions` (in its own route file) returns every action version with its config schema rendered by `z.toJSONSchema()`, output schema with PII marks, grant, delivery, adverse, stage, triggers, and `available` with a reason, so the palette is built from it and never hard-codes a step (editor note 2.3).
5. **Availability:** an action whose adapter is unbound in this environment is listed as `available: false, reason: provider_not_configured`; publishing it warns, and a run that reaches it errors with `provider_not_configured` (type `configuration-error`), never a silent skip. A sandbox provider is never bound in production (ADR 0002 rule 3d).

### C123. Evaluate Code (QuickJS)

1. **Packages** (decision 29; actions note 1.2): `quickjs-emscripten-core` 0.32.0 and `@jitl/quickjs-wasmfile-release-sync` 0.32.0 (MIT, 2026-02-16; the bellard engine at its 2025-12-22 commit), pinned in the catalog. Not the umbrella package, not an `asyncify` variant (no host function is exposed, so none is needed), not the `quickjs-ng` variant (its v0.12.1 has CVE-2026-3979 and CVE-2026-37630). Emscripten's MIT/NCSA notice goes into `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`. `node:vm` (not a security mechanism), vm2 (41 critical advisories in 2026) and isolated-vm (a native addon in maintenance mode) are not used.
2. **The step** (public WA9): `config { source (≤ 64 KiB, a function body `export default function (input) { … }`), properties: { name → path or literal } (only mapped fields reach the code; ours, so PII the code does not use never enters it), return-type: boolean | number | string | object | none }`. The output is `{ value }`, checked against the return type (`type_mismatch` otherwise); it carries the taint of its mapped properties (C115.6).
3. **Where it runs** (decision 28; actions note 1.6). A `code-runner` in `packages/workflow-engine/src/code-runner/` that the worker starts as a pool of child processes:
   - **the child entry** (F19) is a dependency-free ESM file, `packages/workflow-engine/bin/code-runner-child.mjs`, so it loads the same way from source, from `dist` and in the image; the parent resolves the QuickJS packages and the `.wasm` file to their real paths (pnpm's `.pnpm` layout) and passes exactly those to `--allow-fs-read`; a startup handshake confirms the child loaded the variant (a failure is `sandbox_crashed` with a code); a workspace-layout test and an image-layout infra test;
   - `env: {}`, `execArgv: ['--permission', '--allow-fs-read=<real paths>', '--max-old-space-size=64']`;
   - **no Node IPC channel** (P16): `stdio: ['ignore', 'ignore', 'ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe']` with our own framing on fds 3 and 4: a 4-byte length prefix checked against the cap (64 KiB output plus a 1 KiB envelope) before any byte is buffered; on a violation the parent kills the child and the step is `sandbox_crashed`; frames are parsed with a depth-limited JSON parser; never `serialization: 'advanced'`;
   - **a fresh module per evaluation** (P15): the `.wasm` is compiled once per child and **instantiated per evaluation** with a fresh `WebAssembly.Memory` of fixed `maximum`, then one runtime and context, all disposed after; a child serves **one environment at a time** and is recycled when the next evaluation belongs to another environment, after 100 evaluations, and after any interrupt, out-of-memory, crash or framing error;
   - the parent kills the child with `SIGKILL` at the step's time limit plus 250 ms, whatever the guest does.

   **Placement** (P17): in the compose `app` profile the runner is its own `code-runner` service (no network, a read-only root, a non-root user, `cap_drop: [ALL]`, `no-new-privileges`, memory and PID limits, no secrets) reached over a Unix socket on a shared volume, running the same per-evaluation instantiation and child pool inside. The in-worker pool shares the worker's network namespace (Node 22's permission model has no network permission), so with `NODE_ENV=production` and `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_CODE_RUNNER=pool` the worker logs a startup warning naming the missing network isolation; `socket:<path>` is the documented production setting, with a Kubernetes sidecar example (an egress-denying `NetworkPolicy`, read-only root, non-root).
4. **What the guest gets** (actions note 1.4): one JSON string in, parsed inside the VM; one JSON string out, produced by `JSON.stringify` inside the VM and parsed on the host with its cap. No host functions, no module loader, no `fetch`, no timers, no `console` in production runs (the sandbox test run captures at most 50 lines of 1 KiB, sealed and shown only to its author, C120.7), no secrets (decision 31), never `bjson` decoding of guest bytes.
5. **Limits** (provisional, each a per-step setting that can only be lowered under an install ceiling): wall time 1,000 ms (ceiling 5,000); QuickJS memory 32 MiB (64); WASM memory maximum the QuickJS limit plus 16 MiB; QuickJS stack 512 KiB (below the build's 5 MB C stack); source 64 KiB; input 256 KiB of JSON, depth ≤ 32; output 64 KiB, depth ≤ 32; pool `max(1, CPUs − 1)` per worker; an environment budget per minute.
6. **Errors** (each a step error of type `unknown-error` except `type_mismatch` and `invalid_output`, which are `configuration-error`): `timeout`, `memory_limit`, `stack_overflow`, `invalid_output`, `type_mismatch`, `runtime_error`, `sandbox_crashed`, `code_disabled`. The guest's own message (P18) is guest-controlled text that can carry inputs: it is stored only as a sealed member of the step's output (`{ error: { code, message } }`, capped at 1 KiB, scrubbed of resolved secrets), masked fail closed (C120.6), purged and redacted with the values; the plaintext error column holds only the code; events, the journal and logs never carry it. Deterministic failures are not retried (C117.6).
7. **Availability** (decision 30): on in every environment; `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_CODE_ENABLED=false` turns it off for the install (the action is then `available: false`).
8. **Honesty and limits stated** (`docs/workflows/evaluate-code.md`): Evaluate Code is R-tier as an engine; what the code decides is the operator's logic, so the step records the source's SHA-256 and the decision record marks any decision that depended on it (C128.1). The docs say what the sandbox does not cover: a QuickJS memory-safety bug plus a V8 WebAssembly bug, Spectre-class side channels inside one process, denial of service beyond the budgets, the in-worker pool's network exposure (3), and anything the operator passes in.
9. **Tests** (test-first): an infinite loop times out; catastrophic regex backtracking and a long native call end through the interrupt or the kill, never past the limit; allocation beyond the limit gives `memory_limit`; deep recursion gives `stack_overflow` and the next evaluation is unaffected; `globalThis` has no `require`, `process`, `fetch` or timers; a frame header announcing 1 GiB gets the child killed with the worker's memory unchanged; a malformed frame is refused; a canary in the parent's environment is absent from the child's `process.env` and unreadable from the guest; a canary string written by one evaluation cannot be found by the next through a large `ArrayBuffer` scan; a guest error containing a synthetic SSN appears only in the sealed output; a recycled process starts clean.
10. **Tracking** (decision 29): a monthly check of the wrapper's releases and of NVD for `quickjs`, written into `docs/PROGRESS.md`; a CVE reachable from ordinary JavaScript that is fixed upstream before a wrapper release means building the variant from the wrapper's Makefile, in a slice of its own.

### C124. Make HTTPS Request

1. **The step** (public WA8): `config { method (GET, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE), url, headers[] {name, value} (≤ 32, 8 KiB), body (JSON, POST/PUT/PATCH only, ≤ 256 KiB, a C115 value template), response-schema (optional, a C115 type with PII marks), timeout-seconds (1–30, default 10), signing {secret: a workflow secret reference} (optional), retry }`. Output: `{ status, headers (allowlisted: content-type, location on a 3xx, retry-after, etag), body (validated against the response schema when given) }`. A body without a response schema marking its members non-PII carries PII taint (C115.6).
2. **The client** (F04, P02; actions note 2.12): one more client of `createOutboundHttp()` with its own policy object, never the webhook's or the EVM client's flags. The address guard lives in `apps/api/src/webhooks/ssrf.ts` (`classifyAddress`, `isAddressAllowed`, `WebhookNetworkPolicy`) and `webhooks/http-sender.ts` (`createVettedLookup`); `api-egress` owns both for an **additive** policy `{ allowPrivateNetworks, allowLoopback, allowCidrs, nat64Prefixes }` whose defaults keep today's behaviour, with the webhook and outbound suites green. The factory gains, opt-in per client and test-first: PUT, PATCH and DELETE; a port policy; a header policy; refusal of a non-identity `content-encoding`. **Pooling:** the HTTPS action keeps one undici `Agent` per (environment id, network-policy hash), built lazily and closed when the environment's policy settings change, so a kept-alive socket is never shared across environments or policies; the policy is enforced inside the vetted lookup, never before the request (two tests: a connection pooled under environment A is not reused by environment B for the same origin; a settings change closes the old Agent). The existing properties stay: every resolved address vetted (a mixed answer refused) and the socket bound to the vetted one; SNI and the certificate on the URL's host; `https:` only; no credentials in the URL; no redirect followed (a 3xx returns its status and `location`); metadata addresses refused before any other rule; errors carry codes, never the URL or a body.
3. **Destinations** (decisions 32–34, 36; P03, P39, P43):
   - the scheme, host and port are literal in the published definition; templates fill only percent-encoded path segments and query values, so end-user data never becomes a DNS label; a PII-tainted value in a path or query is refused at publish (`pii_in_url`): personal data goes in the JSON body or a header;
   - ports: 443 by default, plus an environment list (`workflow-http-ports`) that can never include a Fetch "bad port";
   - an optional per-environment host allowlist (`workflow-http-hosts`, off by default) for operators who want OWASP's allowlist mode;
   - private, loopback, link-local, CGNAT, documentation, reserved and multicast ranges are refused in production. The environment CIDR allowlist (`workflow-http-private-cidrs`, off by default, audit-logged, under `settings:privacy`) admits internal endpoints only inside a `private` (RFC 1918, `fc00::/7`) or `cgnat` block: an entry that overlaps unspecified, loopback, link-local, metadata, multicast, broadcast or reserved space is refused when the setting is written (422 naming the category), and matching uses the guard's canonical form (a mapped address compares as its embedded IPv4). The metadata rules gain the EKS Pod Identity Agent (`169.254.170.23`, `fd00:ec2::23`). In sandbox environments outside production installs, `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_HTTP_ALLOW_LOOPBACK=true` admits loopback for local test receivers;
   - NAT64 network-specific prefixes (`OPENVERIFY_NAT64_PREFIXES`) have their embedded IPv4 checked like `64:ff9b::/96`;
   - **one host normaliser** (`normalizeHost()`: the WHATWG URL host, lower-cased, trailing dot removed, IDNA as the URL parser gives it) is used for every allowlist, `allowed_hosts` and Slack comparison, always on the parsed URL, never on text; tests cover a suffix-extended host (`hooks.slack.com.evil.example`), a trailing dot, a percent-encoded host and an IDN;
   - a dated IANA registry fixture test pins one sample address per special-purpose row (2026-10-13), so a registry update is a reviewed change.
4. **Headers:** names must match the HTTP token syntax; refused: `host`, `content-length`, `transfer-encoding`, `connection`, `keep-alive`, `upgrade`, `expect`, `te`, `trailer`, `proxy-*`, and the names OpenVerify sets (`openverify-request-signature`, `openverify-signature`, `idempotency-key`, `openverify-workflow-run`). Values refuse CR, LF and NUL.
5. **Responses:** 2xx bodies accepted as `application/json` (parsed with a depth limit) or `text/plain` (as a string), at most 1 MiB; anything else is `response_unsupported`; a non-2xx is the output's `status` with no body unless `capture-error-body` (ours, ≤ 16 KiB) is set; response bodies are sealed and scrubbed of resolved secrets (C125.5). The response feeds conditions only through its declared schema.
6. **Signing and idempotency** (decision 35; P01): workflow requests are signed only with a workflow secret (`wsec_`, host-bound, C125.4), never with a webhook secret, and in their **own format**, so a workflow can never forge an OpenVerify webhook: header `OpenVerify-Request-Signature: t=<unix>,w1=<hex HMAC-SHA-256(secret, "workflow.<t>.<METHOD>.<target>.<raw body>")>` (target = path and query as sent), one `w1` per active secret during rotation, sent on every request including GET and DELETE over an empty body. `@openverify/node` gains `verifyWorkflowRequestSignature`; `verifyWebhookSignature` refuses any `w1` value, and a test shows a workflow-signed request never passes webhook verification even when an operator stores the same secret value in both places. Every request also carries `Idempotency-Key: <effect key>` and `OpenVerify-Workflow-Run: <wfr_ id>`. The docs tell receivers to use the workflow verifier and never webhook code, and name RFC 9421 as a possible v2.
7. **Retries and budgets** (decision 44; P31): retries only for the client's retryable errors (timeout, network, DNS), 429 and 5xx; a per-environment budget of 600 requests a minute and a per-(environment, host) concurrency of 5, both failing closed (`rate_limited`, retryable after the window).
8. **What the guard does not cover** (`docs/workflows/https-request.md`, with a "Personal data leaving OpenVerify" section on recipients, processor terms and transfers, and the fact that redaction cannot reach copies sent elsewhere): a public endpoint that trusts the operator's egress address, responses as untrusted data, secrets that leave in headers, an egress proxy (a later explicit setting; `HTTP_PROXY` stays ignored).

### C125. Workflow secrets

1. **Tables** (actions note 3.2): `workflow_secrets` (`wsec_`): organisation, `key` (snake_case, unique per organisation, immutable), `description`, `allowed_hosts` (normalised host names, C124.3), `allowed-placements` (P29: header names, default `authorization` only, and optional JSON Pointers into a request body, default none), creator, timestamps. `workflow_secret_values`: secret, environment, `value` sealed with `sealValue('workflow_secret_values.value', <row id>, …)`, `last4`, `version`, sealed `previous_value` with `previous_expires_at` (for signing secrets in rotation), `rotated_by`, `rotated_at`. Both join the rewrap lists; neither is personal data, so redaction and exports skip them.
2. **Routes and environments** (`/api/v1/workflow-secrets`; P12): `GET` list and one (metadata only, `secrets:read`, showing only the caller's environment's value metadata); `POST` (key, description, hosts, placements; `secrets:write`); `PATCH`; `PUT /:key/values/:environment-id` with `secret-value` and `POST /:key/values/:environment-id/rotate` (the previous value kept up to 24 hours for signing secrets); `DELETE /:key` (refused while a published version references it). A value route acts **only on the caller's own environment**: another environment id answers 404; setting or rotating a production value needs a **user** session in production (so `environments:production`); API keys set values only in their own environment. Changing `allowed_hosts` or `allowed-placements` needs `secrets:write` plus, when any production value exists, a production user session, and its audit entry is written in every environment that holds a value. `secret-value` joins the API-log redacted keys. Every write is audit-logged with the key name, never the value. A refusal test per case.
3. **References** (public `current_run.environment.secrets.<key>`, accepted as written and stored as `{{secrets.<key>}}`): allowed only in HTTPS header values, the HTTPS signing secret, the HTTPS JSON body and the Slack webhook URL, and further narrowed by the secret's own `allowed-placements`: any other header name or body pointer is refused at publish and again at run time (`secret_placement_refused`), and a secret may appear at most once per request. Never in a URL, a condition, an Evaluate Code property (decision 31), a message text, a tag, a comment or an object field. Publishing refuses a key without a value in the target environment. The docs state the residual risk: an allowed host whose own API stores or reflects a header.
4. **Bound to hosts:** the executor refuses to place a secret into a request whose (literal, normalised) host is not on the secret's `allowed_hosts`; the Slack URL secret is bound to `hooks.slack.com`.
5. **Keeping secrets and credentials out of everything stored:**
   - resolved last, inside the action, after every other check; the step's recorded input is the unresolved template;
   - before an output, an error message or a test-run log line is stored, every occurrence of each value resolved in that step, and of its base64, base64url and percent-encoded forms, is replaced by `[REDACTED]`; the same scrubbing applies to one-time-link tokens, which exist only in the outgoing message (P32);
   - step inputs, outputs and response bodies are sealed; run events carry no bodies; rate-limit and sink keys use secret ids, never values (the Slack limiter is keyed by `(environment id, secret id)`);
   - **the canary test** (P30): one step of each egress kind (including a failing Slack step and a sandbox Slack step, and an inquiry-link step) runs with recognisable synthetic secrets and link tokens; the test asserts they appear in no table (every text, `jsonb` and `bytea` column found through `information_schema`, sealed ones decrypted with the test KEK), no Valkey key or value (`SCAN`, plus string, hash, list and stream values), no S3 object written during the test, and no captured log line.
6. **Install credentials** (Twilio, SMTP, providers) stay environment variables, as in Phases 5 and 6; the worker now reads the messaging ones too (C126.6).

### C126. Messaging actions

Each ships as an adapter with a deterministic sandbox provider that records and never sends, refused in production; each is at most once (C117.5); recipients, budgets and content rules are enforced by the action on the **rendered** message, never only on the template.
1. **Send Email** (decisions 37, 39, 40; P24): through the existing `Mailer` and the install's `OPENVERIFY_MAIL_FROM` (no per-step SMTP server or From address); one recipient per step, no cc or bcc. The recipient is by default an **OTP-confirmed** email address of the run object, or an address or domain on the environment's `workflow-email-recipients` allowlist; an unconfirmed email field needs the step option `allow-unconfirmed` and its acknowledgement (`unconfirmed:<step id>`), and neither `send_inquiry_link_email` nor `send_inquiry_link_sms` ever takes an unconfirmed address (ours, V23: a one-time link opens the person's own flow, so it may go only to a destination that person confirmed). A literal address must be on the allowlist. Limits (GCRA, failing closed): 5 an hour and 20 a day per recipient within the environment, 1,000 an hour per environment, and 20 a day per recipient across the whole install (every environment shares the From address's reputation), keyed by an install-level HMAC of the normalised address under a subkey of the root blind-index key (label `openverify/workflow-recipient/v1`), so no address appears in Valkey (V12; a test scans the keys). Content: subject ≤ 200 characters with CR and LF removed; text ≤ 32 KiB; HTML ≤ 64 KiB from the operator's template with every interpolated value escaped; links only to `https:` hosts on `workflow-link-hosts` or the flow's own host, checked on the rendered message, so an interpolated value containing a URL or a scheme-less host with a public suffix is refused with `message_link_refused`; no attachments; no secret references.
2. **Send SMS** (decision 39; P31, P43, V23): through the existing `SmsSender`; by default only the run object's OTP-confirmed number; an unconfirmed phone field only with `allow-unconfirmed` and its acknowledgement, and never for `send_inquiry_link_sms`; a literal number only from the `workflow-sms-recipients` allowlist; destination countries limited to `workflow-sms-countries` (default: the countries the inquiry template's phone step accepts; empty, so no SMS, when no inquiry template is in scope). It consumes the C55.7 destination, prefix, country and environment rules and may use at most **half** of the environment's hourly SMS budget, so OTP sends always keep a reserved share; all fail closed. Content ≤ 480 characters; the rendered message may contain no URL except the inquiry's own flow link or a host on `workflow-link-hosts`.
3. **Send Slack Message** (decision 38): incoming webhooks only. The URL is a workflow secret bound to `hooks.slack.com` with a path matching `^/services/T[A-Z0-9]+/B[A-Z0-9]+/[A-Za-z0-9]+$`; posted through the outbound client with `unfurl_links: false` and `unfurl_media: false`; one message a second per `(environment, secret id)` with a burst of 3 and `Retry-After` honoured; `&`, `<` and `>` escaped in every interpolated value (so `<!channel>` typed by an end user pings nobody); text ≤ 3,000 characters. Slack's error codes map to step errors. The OAuth app form, GovSlack hosts and other URL shapes are later. Each operator creates their own Slack app; `docs/workflows/messaging.md` points to Slack's API terms for operators who host OpenVerify for others.
4. **Sandbox records** (F09, H16; C55.10 and C55.11 amended): workflow messages go to their **own** Valkey lists, one per environment and kind (`openverify:sandbox-messages:<environment>:<kind>`, kinds `workflow-sms`, `email`, `slack`), each body sealed, at most 500 entries, kept 24 hours; the OTP list (`otp`, 100 entries, 15 minutes) is untouched. `POST /sandbox/email-messages/search` (searched by the recipient's HMAC under the environment's subkey, as the SMS search uses the destination index) and `POST /sandbox/slack-messages/search` (by secret id) join the SMS search under `sandbox:read`, masked like it (H16); Slack records carry the secret id, never the URL. The test-run panel shows the recorded messages.
5. **Output and delivery** (H15, H22): every messaging step outputs `{ delivery: recorded | accepted | unknown, provider, sandbox, message-ref }` (never "sent" or "delivered"): `recorded` by a sandbox provider, `accepted` when the provider accepted the message, `unknown` when the outcome is not known. Retries happen only on proof of non-delivery: a 429 with `Retry-After`, a connection refused before anything was sent, or an SMTP 4xx before `DATA`; every other failure (a timeout, a 5xx after the request was sent) is `unknown`, which blocks a resume until acknowledged (C120.3). A test per adapter.
6. **Where they run** (F05; decision 76; a C63 amendment): steps execute in the worker, so the worker now binds `sms`, `slack` and the mailer for workflow messages: `PROCESS_PROVIDER_KINDS.worker` gains `sms` and `slack`; compose passes the Twilio, SMTP and `OPENVERIFY_MAIL_FROM` variables to the worker; `infra/tests/compose.test.sh` is updated with the reason (it asserted the worker had no Twilio setting since Phase 6 decision 32, which this amends for workflow messages only).
7. **PII:** the default message templates carry ids and console links, never personal data; a template that carries PII taint is an egress step (C121.4).
8. **One messenger port** (V26): every message any action sends (Send Email, Send SMS, Send Slack Message, the inquiry-link actions, case notifications) goes through `WorkflowMessenger` (`apps/api/src/workflows/actions/messaging/port.ts`, frozen in 8A Stage 1b with a stub that `api-egress` implements), which applies the recipient and link rules, budgets, sandbox lists, delivery outputs, the at-most-once effect and token scrubbing in one place; `api-actions` builds the inquiry-link actions over it after `api-egress`'s messaging checkpoint, and the canary test (C125.5) runs after both.

### C127. Object actions

1. **Inquiry decisions:** `approve_inquiry`, `decline_inquiry` and `mark_inquiry_for_review` call `applyInquiryAction` in the effect transaction with actor `workflow-run`; `meta.comment` is sealed in the decision record (C128.1; a `review` row for a mark-for-review), never in audit metadata. Outcomes (H24): a transition the state machine refuses is `invalid_transition` (absorbable by continue-on-error); an inquiry already in the requested state, by anyone, is `no_change`; an inquiry another actor decided otherwise first is `already_decided`. A decision record is written only when the run performed the transition. **Approvals** (H13): `approve_inquiry` from a run answers `review_required` on an inquiry in `needs_review` whose review cause is missing evidence (ADR 0002 §3b), an errored report, an unscreened list or an unevaluated signal, unless a human step (C128.2) is on the path actually taken; publish warns on an approve reachable from `inquiry.completed` with no report or list wait, and an approve reachable from `inquiry.failed` needs the acknowledgement `override-failed:<step id>`. Declines and other adverse steps follow C128. The run continues after a decision (public).
2. **Run Report** (Phase 6 C88; public `run_report`; H03, H09, F10): creates a report from a published report template against the run's inquiry or account, with editable report fields and `origin: workflow` (C69 amended; the report keeps Phase 6's `initial` run), then waits for `report/<type>.ready` or `.errored` on that report within `timeout-seconds` (default 3,600, ceiling 86,400; basis: Phase 6's watchlist, PEP and lookup types finish in seconds to minutes, while GDELT-budgeted media types can take hours and belong behind a case wait). A timeout is always the step error `report_timed_out` (`resource-error`); `on-timeout: continue` never applies. Output: `{ report-id, report-type, review-model, status, has-match, match-count, coverage, unavailable-fields, errors, matches[] {id, method, tier, stage, confidence, approximate}, flagged }`: `has-match` and `match-count` are `null` unless `status` is `ready`, and `has-match` is `null` for types with review model `none` (their own flag, such as `ip-address`'s `flagged`, carries the result). An `errored` report is the step error `report_errored` with the report's error codes, never `has-match: false` (ADR 0002 §3a; Phase 6 decision 47). Publish refuses reading `has-match` on a type without review and warns when it is read without `coverage.complete`. A missing template input is `missing-input-error` naming the field (public).
3. **Run Verification** (public WA13; H23): only types that need no end-user capture; it waits for the verification's terminal event within `timeout-seconds` (`verification_timed_out` on timeout) and outputs `{ verification-id, type, status, checks[] {name, status, tier, stage}, provider, sandbox }`, with no derived pass flag; a verification that ends `not_applicable` (no provider) says so. The sandbox-only `debug-forced-status` is refused outside sandbox; `complete-on-tentative` is deferred (C145).
4. **Lists** (Phase 7 C110):
   - `get_list_match` (`beta`) probes C99.1 `matchLists` with the configured kind and value and answers `{ matched: true | false | null, null-reason, matches[] {list-item-id, method, tier, stage, confidence, approximate} }` (ours: the public step answers a boolean; `null` is added because a list that could not be screened must never read as not listed): `false` only when every configured list was screened by its stated method; a list that could not be read gives `null` with its Phase 7 reason and, by default (`on-unscreened: error`), the step error `list_unavailable`; `matched` is a nullable path, so a condition must declare `on-null` (C114.6);
   - `add_list_item` creates an item with the run as creator; the list's match-key uniqueness makes a repeat `no_change`; on a block kind it is adverse (C128);
   - `add_face_list_item` adds the face of one consenting inquiry (`consent.biometric-matching`) to a face list whose basis is set (Phase 7 decision 26); refused inside a loop and with more than one inquiry per run, as bulk biometric adds are refused in C103; adverse and biometric (C128.4).
5. **Graph** (H01, H02, F27, V02, V09; decision 47 corrected):
   - `run_graph_query` creates a C102 query from a template with its fields, bound to the run's account, and in synchronous mode waits for any of `graph-query.completed`, `.errored`, `.timed-out` or `.cancelled` within its timeout. Output: `{ graph-query-id, status, account-count, node-limit-reached, skipped-hub-values, index-complete, kinds-not-indexed }`, with `account-count` `null` unless `status` is `completed`; `errored`, `timed_out` and `cancelled` are the step errors `graph_query_errored`, `graph_query_timed_out` and `graph_query_cancelled` (`resource-error`), and the step's own timeout stays `wait_timed_out`. In asynchronous mode the step's output is `{ graph-query-id, status: submitted, account-count: null }` (the public `-1` is served only on the graph-query resource, never in a step output), and publish refuses reading `account-count` from an asynchronous step unless a later wait on that query dominates the read. Publish warns when a condition reads `account-count` without `index-complete` and `node-limit-reached`;
   - `sync_graph`: Phase 7 indexes an inquiry's data through an outbox (`graph_index_jobs`, a queue and a 15 s sweep) and a resumable backfill, so Sync Graph is **not** a no-op. It is a claimed effectful step (delivery `pure`, lease = its `timeout-seconds` plus 30 s) that polls the account's `graph_index_jobs` and `graph_index_state` every 2 s within its `timeout-seconds` (default 60), and answers `{ synced: true, mode }` only when nothing is pending and every kind its mode covers is indexed; otherwise `{ synced: false, pending-jobs, kinds-not-indexed }` under `on-pending: continue`, or the step error `graph_index_pending` under `on-pending: error` (the default). A race test indexes, queries and syncs concurrently.
6. **Accounts:** `find_or_create_account` by account type and identifier kind (`reference-id` or a declared identifier field) with a concatenable value; it never merges accounts.
7. **Transactions** (H28): `create_transaction` (type, fields validated by the pinned type version), `update_transaction_status` (adverse when the target status is in the type's `adverse-statuses`), `set_relation` and `unset_relation`. Objects that a run triggered by a transaction creates (inquiries, verifications, reports, cases) are that transaction's **children** (`transaction_children`, written in the effect transaction), and transaction redaction now cascades to them through `redactTransactionChildrenInTx` (the Phase 7 deferral; C105 amended), listed in the redaction coverage test. The transaction's `reviewer` (served `null` by Phase 7 until now) is set by `update_transaction_status` to the run, and to the user on the API path through a hook in the Phase 7 transaction status service (`setTransactionReviewerInTx`, declared in 8A Stage 1b, V27); run-created and run-attached objects join its `related-objects` (at most 100; `related_objects_limit` as a step error).
8. **Tags, fetch, update, redact:** `tag_object`, `remove_tag` and `copy_tags` call each type's tag service within the 50-tag cap, and a tag name may never be built from a PII-tainted value (`pii_into_plain_sink`, C115.6); `fetch_object` reads any object type the grant allows; `update_object` takes `{ field, operator (set, unset, append, push, remove), value }` on custom fields, tags and each type's documented writable attributes only (the public operators), and records PII copies on its effect row (C120.9); `redact_object` redacts a whole object through `RedactionService` (field-level and conditional redaction deferred, decision 43), needs the `irreversible:<step id>` acknowledgement, and a run redacts at most 100 objects (decision 42).
9. **Trust Device** (decision 46; one public line; H35): sets `trusted-at`, `trusted-by` (`workflow-run` and its id) and a `trust-reason` code on a Phase 7 device (`dev_`) of the same environment; `meta.untrust: true` clears it; both audit-logged with a new `devices:write` scope. The mark is served on the device and readable by workflow conditions only: no Phase 7 signal reads it (no C90 amendment), no check reads it or is skipped because of it, and device retention clears it.
10. **Compute and control:** `calculate` (public operators `add`, `subtract`, `multiply`, `divide`, `sum`, `avg`, with `default` when the operation fails) outputs `{ value, defaulted, default-reason }` (H21), defaulted steps go into decision evidence, and publish warns when a condition reads `value` without `defaulted`; `update_variable` (C115.4); `run_workflow` and `schedule_workflow` (C118.3, C119.9, C121.3).
11. **Stopgap rules** (decision 48): Phase 6's `on-report-match`, Phase 7's `on-list-match` and `on-signal` stay, off by default, for installs without workflows; the docs name workflows as the preferred route, and the recipes of C140.5 do the same things. A workflow reacting to `.matched` sees an inquiry the rule already moved to `needs_review` and reads `no_change`, never a double transition.
12. **Re-screening** (Phase 6 C88, Phase 7 C110; H12, F10, F20, V03, V13, V20):
    - **custom lists, inside the report engine:** Phase 7 lists gain a version that every item write increments. Phase 7's list write paths (`ListService.createItem`, `ListItemWriter.insertItems` for imports and the graph add, archiving, retention, redaction, `add_list_item`) call `stageListUpdated`, which stages `list.updated` (a new event and `list` subject, ours: list id, change kind, counts, the new list version), coalesced per transaction and once per import, and writes a `list_rescreen_requests` row in the same transaction; `dispatch()` enqueues a `list-rescreen` job (the 15 s `list-rescreen-sweep` is the outbox). The job inserts a Phase 6 `list_update` run for every monitored custom-list report that selects the list. Phase 6's `report_runs` gains `trigger_list_id` and `trigger_list_version`, its check is widened so a `list_update` run names either a `trigger_sync_id` or a `(trigger_list_id, trigger_list_version)`, and a unique key `(report_id, trigger_list_id, trigger_list_version)` makes it one run per report and list version (a C74 amendment; migration in 8A Stage 1b). So monitored reports never keep reading "no match" against an old list version, with no workflow needed. Test: one run per monitored report and list version, none for an unmonitored one;
    - **`rerun_report`** (ours) inserts a manual run of existing reports chosen by a server-side selector (`{ list-id }` or `{ account-id }`), waits for each run's terminal event, and outputs `{ reports[] {report-id, status, has-match}, count, any-match, errored[] }`, where `any-match` is nullable (`null` unless every run is `ready`); more than 100 matching reports is the step error `report_selector_limit`, never a silent truncation. A rerun repeats each report's original query (Phase 6 C75.4: a report's query never changes), and the docs say so;
    - **account field changes:** the "Re-screen on account field change" recipe (C140.5) calls `run_report`, not `rerun_report`: for each monitored report of the account whose `query-sources` read a changed key, it creates a new report from the same template with the account's current fields (`origin: workflow`), so the new values are what is screened.

### C128. Automated decisions (GDPR Article 22)

Not legal advice; the operator is the controller and decides its own basis, as Phase 6 decision 34 said for screening. This section applies to every **adverse** step (C122.1; decision 80): inquiry declines, block-list and face-list adds, adverse transaction statuses and account archiving, each recorded with its subject type.
1. **The decision record** (`inquiry_decisions`, `idec_`, served: in `inquiry.decisions`, `inquiry.declined`'s `meta.decision` and `GET /decisions`; with a polymorphic subject: inquiry, list item, transaction or account): for every approve, decline and adverse step, whoever makes it, and a row of kind `review` for every mark-for-review (V17), so its comment has a home: the actor (`user`, `api-key`, `workflow-run`); for a run, the workflow, version, node id, the conditional routes taken on the executed path, and the SHA-256 of each condition and Evaluate Code source they depended on; `solely-automated` computed at run time (2); `human-step {kind, actor-id, event-id, resolved-via}` when there was one; the evidence considered as codes and ids only (check names and statuses, report and match ids, list item ids, signal names), with the tier, stage and confidence of every H-tier input (H10) and `pending-evidence[]` (reports, list screenings, graph indexing and signals still pending at the decision, H13); `decision-basis`, `contest-route` and `biometric-basis` when declared; a sealed `comment` (row AAD, masked without PII visibility; P19); `test-run-id` when made by a test run. Served as `inquiry.decisions` (an include) with `inquiries:read`, and for every subject through `GET /decisions?filter[subject-id]=` (the subject's read scope, V10). **Redaction** (P35, V25): redacting the subject (inquiry, list item, transaction or account) keeps the row's codes, ids, basis and flags (the Art. 5(2) and 22 accountability record) and nulls only its comment, through `redactDecisionCommentsInTx`, called beside `redactRunsForSubjectsInTx` for every subject type; a templated comment that carries another subject's PII is recorded as a copy on the effect row (C120.9); the coverage test has an entry per subject type. A user or API decision is recorded through `recordInquiryDecisionInTx`, called from `applyInquiryAction` (a Stage 1b hook); a simulated decision (webhook simulation, a simulated test-run step) writes no record (decision 77). `inquiry.declined` carries `meta.decision {id, solely-automated, decision-basis, contest-route}` (a C5 payload amendment with a golden sample; P40).
2. **Solely automated, decided at run time** (P04, H05, V06, V21). `solely-automated` is `false` only when, on the path actually executed, a wait was **resolved** (not timed out) by a `case.resolved` event that was resolved **singly** (`resolved-via: single`, not a bulk action) by a **user** who held `inquiries:write` when the event was staged (both recorded in the event's internal cause, C121.3, and in the wait's output), on a case that holds this inquiry (or a report of this inquiry, the shape of the review recipe), and the route taken to the decision read that case's resolution; the record then names the resolver and the wait step. A manual, CSV, API or case-action start never counts as human, nor does a resolution made by a bulk action, an API key or a run. For an API decision, `meta.solely-automated` is what the caller declares (default `null`, not declared). For a non-inquiry subject (a transaction, a list item, an account) the same rule applies with a case that holds that subject. The static path analysis only chooses the editor's wording ("a person reviews this path" or not). Golden tests: a timeout, an API-key resolution, a bulk resolution, a resolution by a user without `inquiries:write`, a CSV run, and the review recipe's single user resolution.
3. **Publish gates in production** (decision 49; V10, V24): every production `decline_inquiry` step and every other adverse step, with **no static exemption**, needs `config.decision-basis` (`contract`, `law` or `explicit-consent`) and `config.contest-route`: an `https:` URL on `workflow-link-hosts` or the flow's own host (checked at publish and shown with its host), or a contact, optionally plus a case template for review requests (C128.7); a case template alone is refused, because it does not reach the person. For a transaction or account subject, the contest route may instead come from the transaction type's or account type's own `contest-route` (a C104 and C13 amendment, both optional attributes). At run time a production adverse step that reaches a solely automated decision without them errors `decision_basis_missing`, and its subject goes to review: the inquiry is marked for review, and a transaction, list item or account gets the error recorded in its decision record with `needs-review: true`, shown in the console's review list (and, once 8B lands, a case on the subject when the step names a case template). A production adverse step whose path reads an H-tier value needs the acknowledgement `heuristic:<step id>` (H10). Sandbox environments publish with warnings instead.
4. **Biometric decisions** (P05, H07, V11): an adverse step is biometric when (i) the trigger is an inquiry, verification or list-match event and a template or type the trigger's filter admits has any biometric check or face list (a filter that does not bound the templates counts as biometric), (ii) its path reads a biometric result (selfie comparison, liveness, a face list, or an Evaluate Code or Calculate step fed by one), or (iii) it reads the inquiry status or a check status of such a template; under any other trigger (report, case, account, schedule, API), a read of an inquiry status or check status counts as biometric unless the version's paths bound the inquiry template to non-biometric ones. A biometric adverse step needs `config.biometric-basis` (`explicit-consent` or `substantial-public-interest`) in production. With `explicit-consent`, the run checks, at the decision, that the inquiry recorded both `consent.biometric` (and `consent.biometric-matching` for a face list) and a new consent key, **`consent.automated-decision`** (ours; a C42.3 amendment: an optional text key a consent step lists, whose operator text must say that automated decisions are made, recorded on the consent like the existing keys); otherwise the step errors `biometric_consent_missing` and the inquiry is marked for review. `add_face_list_item` is always biometric. The compliance doc says the Art. 22(2)(c) consent and the Art. 9(2)(a) consent must both be collected, and how.
5. **Prefer review** (decision 51): the editor's decision templates default to Mark Inquiry for Review followed by a case, with a decline as the case resolution's next step.
6. **Reopen for review** (decision 50, 79; C10 amended; P06, H06, V22): the inquiry state machine gains the edge `declined → needs_review` with its own event, `inquiry.reopened` (ours, not `inquiry.marked-for-review`, so existing review automations do not take it for a fresh review; the machine's one-event-per-target-state map gains a per-edge override, added by `contracts-8a`). `POST /inquiries/:id/reopen` takes it, only for a **user** (never an API key or a run) holding `inquiries:write`, with a required `meta.comment` (sealed in the decision record); it is a new `reopen` action of `applyInquiryAction` (a Stage 1b change), and opens **human review pending** on the decision record until a user decides. While it is open, every decline of that inquiry by a run or an API key answers `human_review_pending` (mark-for-review stays allowed; approve from a run stays allowed under C127.1), enforced by `inquiryDecisionGuardInTx` (`services/decisions/`, `api-actions`), which `applyInquiryAction` calls before every decline (a Stage 1b hook). A decline's biometric deletion (C42.2) is not undone; the docs say a reopened inquiry has no face template. A test: a workflow on `inquiry.*` cannot decline a reopened inquiry, and a user can. For other adverse kinds the remedy is the subject's own reversal by a user (a transaction status change, removing a list item, unarchiving an account), recorded in the decision record; the docs say so.
7. **Reaching the person** (P40, V07, V24): the hosted flow, when a declined inquiry's link or session is opened, shows the recorded contest route (the flow session answer gains `decision-contest-route` for declined inquiries, a C3 amendment), with a URL's host shown before the link. When the route also names a case template, an operator opens the review through `POST /inquiries/:id/review-requests` (users with `cases:write`), which opens a case on that template holding the inquiry and records the person's statement as a sealed comment; this route is frozen and built in 8B (`api-case-workflows`), and until 8B lands publish refuses a contest route that names a case template.
8. **Operator documentation** (`docs/compliance/automated-decisions.md`): the four duties (a basis per automated adverse decision, notice before collection, a contest route with a reviewer who can change the outcome, a DPIA recording where people are involved), the fields of the decision record for the DPIA, a notice paragraph per automated path, the consents for biometric decisions, and the SCHUFA reading that a score applied without review can itself be a decision.

### C129. Case resources, identifiers and scopes

1. **Identifiers** (decision 52; public where public): `case`, `ctmpl`, `cqueue`, `cscm`, `cap` (assignment policy; the public assigner id prefix), and ours `ctmplv` (template version), `catt` (attachment), `cact` (case action), `cslap` (SLA policy), `ccal` (business calendar), `cexp` (export).
2. **Case attributes** (public, behaviour note 3.1): `status`, `name` (the template key plus a per-template counter, `KYC-6`), `resolution`, `created-at`, `updated-at`, `assigned-at`, `resolved-at`, `redacted-at`, `sla-expires-at`, `creator-id`/`creator-type` (`user`, `api-key`, `workflow-run`), `assignee-id`, `assigner-id`/`assigner-type` (`case-assignment-policy`, `user`, `workflow-run`), `resolver-id`/`resolver-type` (`api-key`, `user`, `workflow-run`), `updater-id`/`updater-type`, `tags`, `fields` (keys never inflected; PII), `attachments[] {id, filename, url, byte-size, scan-status}`. Actors are OpenVerify ids (`usr_`, `key_`, `wfr_`, `cap_`), never email addresses (decision 64). Ours: `status-category` (`initial`, `intermediate`, `end`), `sla-state` (C133.4), `lock-version`, `held-object-counts`. Relationships (public): `case-template`, `case-queue` (nullable), `case-comments`, `accounts`, `inquiries`, `reports` (polymorphic `report/<type>`), `verifications` (polymorphic), `txns`; ours: `case-template-version`, `report-runs`, `graph-queries`.
3. **Other resources:** `case-template` (public attributes `status`, `name`, `resolutions`, `created-at`, `updated-at`, `archived-at`, `field-schemas`; ours: `key`, `visible-to-roles`, `published-version`, `draft-version`), `case-template-version` (C130.2), `case-queue` (public `status`, `name`, timestamps; ours: `members`, `strategy`), `case-comment` (public `message`, `created-at`, `updated-at`, `redacted-at`, `commenter-id`, `commenter-type`; ours: `mentions`), `case-assignment-policy`, `case-action`, `case-sla-policy`, `case-calendar`, `case-export`.
4. **Scopes** (ours, splitting the public `case.read`, `case.write`, `case_template.read`):
   - `cases:read`; `cases:write` (create, fields, tags, held objects, comments, attachments, status changes including resolving, single and bulk, as the public "edit" covers resolving; ours: the public dashboard has a separate bulk-resolve permission, which is folded in here because every bulk change goes through the single-case rules; self-assignment through "next case"); `cases:assign` (assign others, bulk assign); `cases:redact`; `cases:export`;
   - `case-templates:read`, `case-templates:write`, `case-templates:publish`;
   - `case-queues:read`, `case-queues:write` (queues, members, assignment policies, SLA policies, calendars).

   Built-in roles (decision 68): `admin` all; `developer` all except `cases:export`; `reviewer` `cases:read`, `cases:write`, `cases:assign`, `case-templates:read`, `case-queues:read`; `viewer` the `:read` scopes (values stay masked without `pii:view`). `cases:redact` is admin's and `developer`'s, `cases:export` admin's only, by default (decision 68; custom roles can add them); `cases:export` is not a `:read`, so viewers never get it. Every permission gets a `PERMISSION_INFO` label and a refusal test.

   **Template visibility** (P14): a template's `visible-to-roles` narrows who sees its cases. Users see a restricted template's cases only through a listed role; an API key sees them only when the key lists the template in a new `case-template-ids` restriction (set when the key is minted, never wider than the minting user's visibility). The exits that enforce it, each with a test: the case routes, `POST /cases/search`, exports, `POST /cases/next`, bulk actions, `GET /events` and `GET /events/:id` (events of templates the reader cannot see are dropped), stored API logs (entries whose resource or subject ids name such a case are dropped), workflow runs and steps whose subjects include such a case, notifications, `notify-on-create`, and the held-object modules on inquiry and account pages. Webhooks keep receiving these events (an operator-configured system exit), as the docs state.
5. **Routes** (public paths first):

   | Route | Scope | Notes |
   |---|---|---|
   | `GET /cases` (public filters: `status`, `case-template-id`, `account-id`, `reference-id`, `inquiry-id`, `report-id`; ours: `case-queue-id`, `assignee-id`, `status-category`, `sla-state`), `POST /cases`, `GET`, `PATCH`, `DELETE /cases/:id` | read; write; write; redact | `POST` body as public: `case-template-id` (required), `creator-email-address` (accepted for parity and resolved to a user id), `case-queue-id`, `fields`, `tags[]`; `meta.object-ids[]`, `meta.sla-expires-in-seconds`; `DELETE` redacts |
   | `POST /cases/:id/{set-status,assign,add-comment,add-objects,remove-objects,add-tag,remove-tag,set-tags}` | write (assign: `cases:assign`, or `cases:write` to assign oneself) | public bodies (C131–C136) |
   | `POST /cases/search` | read | public body (C137.2) |
   | `POST /cases/next` | write | ours: pull the most urgent unassigned case from the caller's queues (C132.3) |
   | `POST /cases/bulk` | write (+ assign for assignment) | ours, asynchronous (C137.4) |
   | `GET /cases/:id/comments`, `POST /case-comments/:id/redact` | read; write (own) or redact | ours (C134) |
   | `POST /cases/:id/attachments`, `GET /cases/:id/attachments/:attachment-id/content` | write; read + `pii:view` | ours (C135) |
   | `POST /cases/:id/run-action` | `workflows:trigger` + `cases:read` | ours, mirroring the public account path (C140.1) |
   | `POST /cases/:id/print` | read | ours: audits and fires `case.print-initiated` (C138.3) |
   | `GET /cases/:id/available-actions` | read + `workflows:trigger` | ours (H14): the case actions the caller may run, filtered by the caller's grant (C140.1) |
   | `GET /cases/:id/attachments/:attachment-id/original` | redact + `pii:view` | ours (P23): the sealed original, audited |
   | `POST /inquiries/:id/review-requests` | write | ours (P40; C128.7): opens a review case on the contest route's template |
   | `GET /case-templates/:id` (public), `GET`, `POST /case-templates`, `PATCH /case-templates/:id`, draft and publish routes, `GET /case-template-versions/:id` | templates read; write; publish | C130 |
   | `GET`, `POST /case-queues`, `GET`, `PATCH /case-queues/:id`, `PUT /case-queues/:id/members` | queues read; write | C132 |
   | `GET`, `POST /case-assignment-policies`, `GET`, `PATCH /case-assignment-policies/:id`; the same for `/case-sla-policies` and `/case-calendars` (with `POST /case-calendars/:id/holidays/import`) | queues read; write | C132, C133 |
   | `GET`, `POST /case-actions`, `GET`, `PATCH /case-actions/:id` | `workflows:write` | C140.1 |
   | `POST /case-exports`, `GET /case-exports` (the caller's own), `GET /case-exports/:id`, `GET /case-exports/:id/download` | `cases:export` | C138; creator-only (P26) |

6. **Events** (C5; golden payloads, webhook samples, the dashboard groups "Cases" and "Case comments"): the eleven public webhooks `case.created`, `case.assigned`, `case.resolved`, `case.reopened`, `case.status-updated`, `case.queue-updated`, `case.updated-fields`, `case.updated-tags` (with `context.added-tags` and `context.removed-tags`), `case.sla-expired`, `case.exported`, `case.redacted`, plus `case.updated`, `case.print-initiated`, `case-comment.created` and `case-template.deleted-field` (when a published version drops a field, H29) from the public events page. Not emitted, with their reasons in C145: `case.agent-reviewed` (no review agent), `case-message.*` (no email module), `case-template.converted-field` (a field's type never changes between versions), `user-notification.*` and `user.availability-updated` (notifications are console-only and availability is deferred).
7. **Problems:** `case_transition_refused`, `case_comment_required`, `case_status_tag_required`, `case_resolution_invalid`, `case_objects_limit` (1,000), `object_type_unsupported`, `object_already_attached`, `case_attachment_limit`, `attachment_type_refused`, `attachment_infected`, `attachment_scan_pending`, `case_assignee_ineligible`, `case_version_conflict` (409 on `lock-version`), `sla_out_of_range`, `calendar_invalid` (naming the dates), `export_too_large`.

### C130. Case templates and versions

1. **Template** (decision 53): environment-scoped; `name` (1–80), `key` (`^[A-Z][A-Z0-9]{1,9}$`, unique per environment, the prefix of case names), `status` (`active`, `archived`), `visible-to-roles` (role ids; empty means every role with `cases:read`). Archiving refuses new cases and keeps existing ones.
2. **Immutable versions** (`ctmplv_`; ours, as Phase 6 report templates and Phase 7 transaction types; the public templates have none): draft, published, superseded, one published, publish under the template lock; each case pins the version it was created under. A version holds:
   - `field-schemas`: the Phase 7 field-schema union (the public templates use the same union as transaction types), validated by the `packages/core/src/transactions/` walker with its cache keyed by `(kind, version id)`, with `searchable` marks (C136.4); the case subset (F37) refuses `file` (attachments carry files) and `json` (no shared compile in Phase 8), and keeps `platform-file` with Phase 7's rule (writing one needs the file's read scope, plus `pii:view` for faces);
   - `statuses[] {key, label, category, pauses-sla, required-comment, status-tags[] {key, label}, status-tag-required}` (decision 54): exactly one `initial`, at least one `end`; the defaults `Open` (initial), `Pending` (intermediate, pauses the SLA), `Approved` and `Declined` (end), as the public defaults;
   - `resolutions[]` (public; default `Approved`, `Declined`);
   - `transitions` (ours): a map from each status key to the keys it may move to; default every status to every other; publish refuses an `end` status unreachable from `initial`;
   - `default-queue-id`, `assignment-policy-id`, `sla-policy-id`, `sla-starts-on` (`created`, `entered-queue`, `assigned`);
   - `held-object-types` (a subset of C136.1), `attachment-policy {max-files ≤ 20, types ⊆ {jpeg, png, webp, pdf}}`;
   - `notify-on-create` (user ids emailed a link when a case is created, public; users who cannot see the template are refused at publish and skipped at send, P38), `layout-modules` (the visible default modules and their required permissions, C142.2).
3. **Field changes:** additive only between versions for existing keys (a key's type never changes); a field dropped by a new version stays readable on cases that pinned an older version, and the publish fires `case-template.deleted-field`; publish refuses a change that would make an existing pinned case unreadable.
4. **Routes:** as C129.5. `GET /case-templates/:id` serves the public attributes plus ours; the public `case_template.read` maps to `case-templates:read`.

### C131. Case statuses, transitions and history

1. **Machine** (pure, `packages/core/src/cases/case-state-machine.ts`, a test per edge): `set-status` with `meta.status` (required in `meta`, public), ours `meta.resolution`, `meta.comment`, `meta.status-tag`:
   - between non-`end` statuses: allowed by the version's `transitions`, else 422 `case_transition_refused`; `case.status-updated`;
   - into an `end` status: sets `resolution` (`meta.resolution` when given, which must be in `resolutions`; otherwise the status label when it is one of them; otherwise null), `resolved-at` and the resolver; `case.status-updated` then `case.resolved`;
   - out of an `end` status: `case.reopened`; `resolution` and `resolved-at` cleared (the earlier values stay in history); the SLA follows its policy's `on-reopen` (C133.5);
   - a status with `required-comment` needs `meta.comment` (422 `case_comment_required`), which becomes a comment; a status with `status-tag-required` needs `meta.status-tag` from its list.
2. **Every transition** takes the case row `FOR UPDATE`, checks `lock-version` when sent (409 `case_version_conflict`), writes history and stages its events in the same transaction.
3. **Resolving decides nothing else.** Approving or declining a held inquiry is a separate action with the inquiry's own scope, events and decision record. A workflow can carry a resolution into a decision on `case.resolved` (C140.4) only under C121.3's causing-principal rule: the resolver must hold the decision's scope (`inquiries:write`), and only a user's resolution counts as a human step (C128.2). This keeps a decision about a person explicit (ADR 0002; Phase 7 decision 18).
4. **History** (`case_history`, append-only by trigger: updates refused; deletion only under `openverify.environment_deletion` or `openverify.retention`, F12): `(case_id, seq, at, actor_type, actor_id, kind, from, to, request_id)`, PII-free by rule: field changes record keys, comment and attachment entries their ids, status changes their keys. It is the timeline the reviewer reads; platform `events` feed webhooks; `audit_logs` record reads of sealed values and every attachment view.
5. **`case.updated`** fires for an attribute change that has no specific event (name, queue-independent metadata), never in addition to a specific one.

### C132. Queues and assignment

1. **Queues** (public `cqueue_`): `name`, `status` (`active`, `archived`; archiving is permanent and returns its unassigned cases to no queue); a case is in at most one queue, set with `case-queue-id` on create or `PATCH` (`null` removes), firing `case.queue-updated`. Ours: `members[] {user-id, max-open (1–500, default 50), paused}`; a member must hold `cases:write`; deactivating a user or removing the permission removes them from every queue and returns their open cases (C132.6).
2. **Assignment policies** (public `cap_`, behaviour note 3.4): `distribution` (`round-robin`, `fewest-open`, `case-creator`, `inquiry-creator`), `sort` (`created-at` or `sla-expires-at`), `agents` (the members of the routed queue by default, or a user list), `max-capacity` per agent, and `sources[]` (case templates or queues, in priority order). A template version or a queue names its policy; a case entering a queue (or created on a template) with a policy is assigned at once if an agent is eligible, else stays unassigned for pull. Availability filters and teams are deferred (decision 71).
3. **Strategies** (editor note 3.3):
   - **manual:** `POST /cases/:id/assign` with `meta.user-id` (ours) or `meta.user-email-address` (public, resolved within the organisation; a refusal never reveals whether an address exists elsewhere); `meta.user-id: null` unassigns; the target must be an active member of the organisation with `cases:write` and able to see the template (422 `case_assignee_ineligible`);
   - **pull:** `POST /cases/next` takes the most urgent unassigned case from the caller's queues with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, ordered by `sla_expires_at NULLS LAST, created_at, id`, on a partial index `(environment_id, queue_id, sla_expires_at, created_at, id) WHERE assignee_user_id IS NULL AND status_category <> 'end' AND redacted_at IS NULL` (an `EXPLAIN` test);
   - **round-robin:** lock the queue row, pick the first eligible member after the queue's cursor in user-id order (active, not paused, under capacity), assign, move the cursor;
   - **fewest-open:** the same lock, then the eligible member with the fewest open assigned cases, ties to the least recently assigned, then user id; approximate across queues (decision 67);
