   - **case-creator** and **inquiry-creator:** the user who created the case or its first held inquiry, when eligible; otherwise unassigned.
4. **Every assignment** writes `assignee`, `assigned-at`, the assigner (`user`, `workflow-run`, or `case-assignment-policy` with the `cap_` id), a history row with the strategy and its inputs (cursor or counts), and `case.assigned`.
5. **Bulk assign** (public) replaces existing assignees (C137.4).
6. **Re-assignment sweep** (repeatable, 60 s): cases assigned to a user who left a queue, was deactivated or lost access return to their queue's policy or to unassigned, with history and `case.assigned` (`assignee-id: null`).

### C133. SLA

1. **Two ways to set a deadline:**
   - **public:** `meta.sla-expires-in-seconds` on create and `PATCH` (1 to 7,776,000, 90 days, decision 55; `null` clears) sets a wall-clock `sla-expires-at`; the Set and Clear Case SLA actions do the same with minutes, hours or days;
   - **ours:** an SLA policy on the template version: `target` in business minutes (1–525,600), a calendar or `24x7`, `starts-on`, `pause-categories` (statuses with `pauses-sla`), `on-reopen` (`continue` by default, decision 58, or `restart`).
2. **Calendars** (`ccal_`): `time-zone` (IANA; refused if the runtime does not know it), `week` (per weekday, zero or more `[start, end)` local `HH:MM` intervals, at least one business hour a week), `holidays[] {date, label}` and optional `closures`. No holiday data ships (decision 57: `date-holidays`' data is CC BY-SA 3.0); `POST /case-calendars/:id/holidays/import` takes a `date,label` CSV through `readCsv` (C118.5). A calendar edit recomputes the running deadlines of cases on it, each with a history row (decision 59).
3. **Business time** (`apps/api/src/services/cases/sla/business-time.ts`, owned by `case-logic`, on `temporal-polyfill` 1.0.5, decision 56; kept out of `packages/core` so the polyfill never enters the flow's or the console's module graph, with a flow bundle-size assertion, F30): `addBusinessTime(start, ms, calendar)` and `businessTimeBetween(a, b, calendar)`, scanning at most 3,660 days; calendars validated with `disambiguation: 'reject'` over the next two years (a boundary in a gap or repeat is refused with the dates named), deadlines computed with `'compatible'`; stored as instants. Property tests (adding then measuring returns the input; both monotonic) and tz fixtures (America/New_York's 2026 transitions, Europe/London, Asia/Kolkata, Australia/Lord_Howe, Pacific/Chatham, a zone that changes at midnight), each asserting the runtime's offsets first so a changed tz database fails loudly.
4. **State on the case:** `sla_state` (`none`, `running`, `paused`, `met`, `expired`), `sla_epoch` (incremented on every start, pause, resume, recompute and clear), `sla_consumed_ms`, `sla_segment_started_at`, `sla_expires_at` (the public attribute; null while paused or not started), `sla_expired_at`, `sla_met_at`, the pinned policy revision.
5. **Transitions:** pause on entering a pausing status (`consumed += businessTime(segment start, now)`, `expires_at = null`); resume on leaving it; resolve: `met` before the deadline, an `expired` SLA stays `expired` and the case still resolves; reopen: per `on-reopen`; a new `sla-expires-in-seconds` or a new policy re-arms it with a new epoch.
6. **Firing** (decision 60): a repeatable `case-sla` sweep (every 30 s) takes `sla_state = 'running' AND sla_expires_at <= now()` rows `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` in batches of 500 and, per row in a savepoint, sets `expired`, writes history and stages `case.sla-expired` in the same transaction: once per epoch, however many sweepers run; never for a case in an `end` status; consumers read `sla-expires-at` from the payload, not the delivery time. Escalation (reassign, notify) is a workflow on that event.
7. **Notifications:** the assignee gets a breach notification (C134.4).

### C134. Comments, mentions and notifications

1. **Comments** (public `cscm_`): `POST /cases/:id/add-comment` with `meta.message` (1–10,000 characters); `message` sealed with row-bound AAD; commenter `(type, id)`; `case-comment.created`. `GET /cases/:id/comments` (ours) lists them newest first with cursor pages.
2. **Append-only** (decision 61): no edit and no delete route. `POST /case-comments/:id/redact` nulls the message (ids, author and times kept, as the public object does after redaction), allowed to the comment's author or a holder of `cases:redact`, audit-logged.
3. **Plain text with mentions** (decision 61): rendered as React text with `whitespace-pre-wrap`; mentions stored as `@[usr_…]` tokens, each checked against the organisation and the case's read permission, kept in `mentions[]`; OpenVerify ids (`inq_`, `acc_`, `case_`) become links through our tokenizer; bare `https://` URLs are shown as text with an "open link" control that shows the host first. No Markdown, no images, no raw HTML.
4. **Notifications** (ours, minimal set; public: assigned, SLA breached, mentioned): a `user_notifications` table (user, kind, case id, read-at; no comment text), served by `GET /dashboard/notifications` and marked read by `POST /dashboard/notifications/:id/read`; when the mail adapter is configured, an email with **a link only**, never the comment text or any case field. Kept 90 days.

### C135. Attachments, renditions and scanning

1. **Upload:**
   - **public:** `PATCH /cases/:id` with `data.attributes.attachments[] {data (base64), filename}`; an array, within one request's 10 MiB decoded total (H31), with this route's body limit raised to 14 MiB for this route only; the handler hands the attachments to `acceptPatchAttachments(tx, case, attachments)`, a hook `api-case-files` owns (F15);
   - **ours:** `POST /cases/:id/attachments` (multipart, ≤ 10 MiB, `Idempotency-Key` honoured as for buffered multipart bodies), which the console uses through a streaming route handler (C142.2).
2. **Storage** (`case_attachments`, `catt_`): `encryptBytes` with the attachment id as context through `FileStorage`, `storage_key` from `newStorageKey(environment)`, `wrapped_dek`, sniffed `mime_type`, `byte_size`, `sha256`, sealed `original_filename`, uploader `(type, id)`, `scan_status`, `scan_provider`, `scanned_at`, rendition fields, `deleted_at`. Types JPEG, PNG, WebP and PDF by the existing magic-byte detectors; 20 per case by default (decision 62); filenames from clients never reach a header, a path or a key.
3. **Renditions** (decisions 62, 63; F25, P22): a new vision endpoint `POST /v1/renditions` decodes an image (Pillow, with the service's decompression-bomb limits), applies the EXIF orientation, strips every metadata segment and re-encodes it as JPEG or PNG, and renders a PDF's pages (at most 20) to PNG through the service's guarded PDF child (Phase 5); it runs on its **own limiter lane** (one slot, four queued), so verification processing keeps its two slots. Renditions are made **asynchronously**: attachments on the `case-rendition` queue at upload, inquiry images when they are processed or on first request, with a "preparing" placeholder and retry in the console. The API stores each rendition sealed beside its source. The console and every export serve only renditions; the sealed original is kept for vision and forensic checks. The console's existing inquiry image views move to renditions through a new console route (`/files/:id/rendition` → `GET /api/v1/dashboard/files/:id/rendition`, which requires the source file route's scopes, `inquiries:read` and `pii:view`, with a test; P38), cached sealed in `file_renditions`, audited as `file.viewed`; the API's own file route is unchanged. **Lifecycle** (V19): redaction keeps `files` rows and sets their `deleted_at`, so renditions are removed by a hook keyed on that, not by a cascade: `deleteRenditionsForFilesInTx` (8B, `api-case-files`), called from `services/redaction.ts` and the file-retention steps wherever a file's `deleted_at` is set, deletes the rendition rows and their storage objects; the C16 leftover-file retry (`purgeRedactedFiles`) also retries rendition objects whose delete failed; the rendition route answers 404 for a deleted or redacted source; they join `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS`, `REWRAP_TABLES` and the redaction coverage test. Renditions of inquiry images at processing are requested through a hook, `requestRenditionsForFiles`, called from the verification processing jobs at the sites the inventory names (V27).
4. **Download:** `GET /cases/:id/attachments/:attachment-id/content` needs `cases:read` and `pii:view` and serves the **rendition** (page images for a PDF), with `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="case-attachment-<id>.<ext>"`, `nosniff`, `private, no-store`, and an audit entry per view; the sealed original is served only by `…/original` under `cases:redact` and `pii:view`, audited (P23). The console proxies both, never a storage URL (stored objects are ciphertext, so presigned URLs cannot work).
5. **Scanning** (decision 66; P23, H20): an `AttachmentScanner` port with providers `none` (default: every attachment is `not_scanned` and the console says so), `clamd` (an operator-run service over a Unix socket or private network, with our own `INSTREAM` client that maps every non-clean answer, a size-limit refusal included, to `error`, never `clean`) and `sandbox` (deterministic: the EICAR test string is `infected`, everything else `clean`; refused in production). Attachments serve `scan-status`, `scan-provider` and `scanned-at`; the console shows "sandbox scan (not a real scan)" for the sandbox provider, and the contract refuses `clean` without a provider. **Serving:** with a scanner bound, only `clean` is served; `pending` answers 409 `attachment_scan_pending`; `error` is retried by the scan job up to 3 times, then stays `error` and is never served (only redacted); `infected` is never served; with the `none` provider, `not_scanned` is served behind a warning. No ClamAV image or profile in the repo (ClamAV is GPL-2.0; the docs say how an operator runs it and what scanning cannot catch).

### C136. Held objects, fields and tags

1. **Held objects** (public `meta.object-ids`): accounts, inquiries, reports, report runs, transactions, verifications, graph queries, and documents, which map to document verifications (`ver_`) as Phase 7 maps them (H31); SARs do not exist in OpenVerify and are refused with `object_type_unsupported`. One join table per type (`case_inquiries`, `case_accounts`, `case_reports`, `case_report_runs`, `case_transactions`, `case_verifications`, `case_graph_queries`) with composite foreign keys to `(environment_id, id)`, so the database refuses a cross-environment link.
2. **Rules** (public): each object attaches to a case once (a repeat is `no_change`); at most 1,000 held objects per case (decision 65; `case_objects_limit`); `remove-objects` of an absent object is a no-op.
3. **State as attached** (public: attaching takes a snapshot; how is not documented; ours): each join row records `attached-at`, who attached it, and an `attached-state` of plaintext, PII-free attributes (status, template or type id, key timestamps; for an inquiry, the ids of the verifications finished at that moment). The case page shows "as attached" beside the live object, which is read through its own route and scope. **Holding grants nothing:** a held object the reader may not see shows only its id and type with "no access".
4. **Fields** (public `fields`): validated by the pinned version's field schemas through the Phase 7 walker, stored as one sealed JSON per case, masked member-wise without `pii:view`, `case.updated-fields` with the changed keys only. Fields marked `searchable` get an HMAC under the environment's blind-index subkey through Phase 7's `identifierKey`, with the label derived per `(case template id, field key)` so equal values in different fields or templates never collide (P25); `blind-index:rebuild` recomputes them.
5. **Tags** (public): `add-tag` and `remove-tag` with `meta.tag-name` or `meta.tag-id`; `set-tags` with arrays (and OpenVerify's existing `tag-names`); `tags[]` on create creates missing tags; at most 50 tags; `case.updated-tags` with `added-tags` and `removed-tags`.

### C137. Search, filters and bulk actions

1. **List filters** (C129.5): newest first, cursor pages (C1's `created_at DESC, id DESC`).
2. **`POST /cases/search`** (public body): `query` as an `and`/`or`/`not` tree, depth ≤ 2, ≤ 20 clauses per `and` or `or`, leaves `{attribute, operator (eq, gt, gte, lt, lte), value}`; attributes `template`, `case_template_id`, `status`, `state` (read as the status category; the public meaning is not documented), `resolution`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `resolved_at`, `sla_expires_at`, `assignee`, `case_queue`, `case_queue_id`, `resolver`, `account_id`, `inquiry_id`, `tag_id`, `fields.<searchable key>`; `sort {attribute, direction}`; `page {after, before, size 1–100, default 10}`. Compiled into parameterised SQL over plaintext columns and HMAC indexes only; **read-after-write consistent** (decision 74; the public search is eventually consistent). A query with any `fields.*` leaf needs PII visibility (`pii:view` for users, `canViewPii` for keys), else 403 naming `pii:view`, and so do bulk actions and exports by such a search (P25: an equality query is otherwise a membership oracle on masked values).
3. **Recurring-trigger filter** (C118.4) reuses this grammar.
4. **Bulk actions** (public: assign, change status, change queue, add or remove tags; asynchronous; logged per case): `POST /cases/bulk` with `action`, `case-ids` (≤ 500) or a search body, and `meta`; a job applies each change through the same service as the single routes (transitions refused per case and reported), and the answer lists per-case outcomes when done; each case gets its own history rows and events.

### C138. Export and print

1. **Export** (public: CSV of the filtered list with linked ids and tags, its own permission, `case.exported`): `POST /case-exports` with a filter or search body, `format` (`csv` or `json`) and `include-personal-data` (needs `pii:view` and a confirmation in the console); an asynchronous job of at most 10,000 cases (decision 69; `export_too_large`), its file encrypted into storage, downloadable for 24 hours and then deleted. **Only the creating principal** lists, reads and downloads an export (`GET /case-exports` lists the caller's own; others get 404), and each download re-checks `cases:export`, plus `pii:view` when personal data was included, refusing if the creator has since lost either (P26). One audit entry per export and per download, recording the filter's **shape** only (attribute names and operators, values replaced by `"[value]"` except ids, enums and dates, P19) and the counts; `case.exported` per exported case.
2. **Formats:** CSV through Phase 7's neutralising writer (`packages/checks/src/csv/`): one row per case with id, name, template, status, category, resolution, queue, assignee id, created, assigned, resolved, SLA state, `sla-expires-at`, tags and the ids of held objects; sealed fields only with personal data on; comments never in CSV. JSON: a per-case bundle (the case as the API returns it, its history, comments, attachment metadata without bytes, the held objects' ids and types), masked by the requester's permissions.
3. **Print** (Phase 6 put server `print` routes in Phase 9): a print view in the console (C142.5); `POST /cases/:id/print` writes the audit entry and fires `case.print-initiated` before the browser's print dialog opens. A server-made PDF waits for Phase 9 with the font decision (decision 72).
4. **Never included:** secrets and tokens, storage keys and wrapped data keys, face templates and embeddings, raw fingerprints, EXIF and GPS metadata (renditions only), other environments' data, IP addresses and user agents without `pii:view`. Redacted content prints and exports as "redacted", never as an empty value.

### C139. Case redaction and retention

1. **Case redaction** (public `DELETE /cases/:id`, `case.redacted`; no cascade): comment messages nulled (ids and times kept), sealed fields nulled and the case's searchable-field HMAC rows deleted, attachments' objects and renditions deleted with every stored version and their rows kept with only id, type, size and scan status (`original_filename` and `sha256` nulled, P34), tags cleared, `redacted-at` set; history kept (PII-free); held objects are not redacted with the case. Copies a workflow made from a redacted source into case fields or comments are redacted with the source (C120.9). `stubEventPayloads` and `forgetStoredResponses` cover the case and comment ids; the redaction coverage test gains the case category, and the hook `redactCasesInTx` is declared in Stage 1b (F35).
2. **A held object redacted elsewhere** stays linked and shows "redacted". Redacting an inquiry or account does not redact its cases (public: cases redact only themselves); the docs say so, and an operator who wants both runs both (or a workflow does).
3. **Retention** (ours, mirroring C16): `case-retention-days` (default none, 1–3,650) counted from `resolved_at`, enforced by redaction, never deletion. Image attachments on a case that holds an inquiry with a selfie verification follow that inquiry's biometric retention (P22): they are redacted when its biometric data is.

### C140. Case workflows (Workflow 8B)

1. **Case actions** (public W9): a `case-action` (`cact_`) binds a running workflow whose trigger is `trigger-case` to one or more case templates, with a label, a placement (`header`, ours: the only placement in Phase 8; other placements and visibility rules are deferred, C145), and a parameter form (the trigger's `inputs`, each optionally prefilled from `case.fields.*`, `inquiry.fields.*` or `account.fields.*` of the case's first held inquiry or account). Prefill is rendered under the viewer's mask: a masked field stays empty, and the run reads the live value at execution under its grant (P38). `GET /cases/:id/available-actions` (`cases:read` and `workflows:trigger`) lists the actions the caller may run, filtered by the caller's grant (H14). `POST /cases/:id/run-action` (`data.case-action-id`, `data.parameters`) validates the parameters, needs `workflows:trigger` and `cases:read` plus the version's grant (C121.3), and answers `workflow-run-id`. The run's `trigger` is the case, and `current_case` is in scope. Modules are not usable in case actions (public).
2. **Case trigger events and `current_case`:** an event trigger on any `case.*` event works as every event trigger; `trigger-case` is the one-click action of 1. 8A builds `current_case` as an inert root and the human-step rule for `case.resolved` as a data-driven entry (F33); 8B fills `current_case` with the case's flattened view (C114.4) in its own scope provider file.
3. **Case steps** (public WA14): `create_case` (template, fields, tags, queue, `object-ids` from paths, an SLA), `assign_case` (a user id or a policy), `update_case_status` (status, resolution, comment), `add_case_comment`, `add_case_to_queue`, `attach_objects_to_case`, `set_case_sla`, `clear_case_sla`, each through the C131–C136 services in the effect transaction with actor `workflow-run`; `tag_object`, `update_object` and `redact_object` gain the case type.
4. **Waiting on a case:** the generic event wait on `case.resolved` (correlated by the case id) is the public "wait for the case to resolve, then decide" shape; the wait's output records the resolver's type and id, and C128.2 decides from it at run time whether a person took part.
5. **Recipes** (disabled workflows, documented, listed and installed through `GET /workflow-recipes` and `POST /workflow-recipes/:key/install` (C112.6), into any environment, and by the dev seed; H11, H12, V06, V20): "Review on report match" (`report/<type>.matched` and `report/<type>.errored` of completion reports → mark the inquiry for review → create a case holding **the inquiry and the report** → wait for `case.resolved` → approve or decline from the resolution), with `decision-basis` and `contest-route` left unset, so the production gate refuses it until the operator fills them; "Re-screen on account field change" (`account.updated-fields` → for each monitored report whose query reads a changed key, `run_report` with the account's current fields, C127.12). Custom-list re-screening needs no recipe: the report engine does it on `list.updated` (C127.12). The 8B e2e covers both the matched and the errored path of the review recipe.
6. **Graph bulk action** (C103 amended): "create case" on a stored graph result creates one case holding the selected accounts (≤ 500), needs `cases:write` and `graph:read`, and records the query id in the case's history. Its console button in Phase 7's explorer belongs to `dashboard-cases` (the explorer's bulk-action file the inventory names, for that change only; H25).
7. **8B's entries in 8A's tables** (F33): the case type in the per-type tables of the generic actions (C122.1), the case attachable types, `current_case`'s provider and the case human-step entry live in 8B's own files (`apps/api/src/workflows/actions/cases/`), registered through the tables 8A exports, so no 8B agent edits an 8A file.

### C141. Console: workflows (Workflow 8C)

1. **Stack** (editor note 2.1, 2.2): `@xyflow/react` 12.12.0 with `@xyflow/system` 0.0.83, `zustand` 4.5.7, `classcat` 5.0.5 and the d3 3.x modules (MIT, ISC, BSD-3-Clause); `@dagrejs/dagre` 3.1.1 with `@dagrejs/graphlib` 4.0.5 for layout on demand; all through the catalog. The editor renders client-only (a client component mounted after hydration) and imports `@xyflow/react/dist/base.css` through the bundler, so nothing needs inline styles under a nonce `style-src`. The "React Flow" attribution link stays visible (decision 73). `elkjs` (EPL-2.0 or GPL-3.0) is excluded for a shipped bundle.
2. **The outline view first** (editor note 2.4): an APG tree view of the definition (the trigger at the root; branches, lanes and loop bodies as child groups; each item named by its type, name and error count) with a menu per item: add a step after, add a branch, move up, move down, connect next step to (a list of valid targets filtered by the same validation as the canvas), delete (confirmed), open settings. Every canvas operation is reachable here, which is what makes the editor meet WCAG 2.2 2.1.1, 2.5.7 and 2.5.8; selection and focus follow between the two views. It is built and tested keyboard-only before the canvas.
3. **The canvas:** custom memoised nodes with a 3:1 focus ring and 24 px handle hit areas; `isValidConnection` running the shared port rules; the library's strings through `ariaLabelConfig` into the console's i18n keys; a text summary of the canvas ("12 steps, 2 branches, 1 error") pointing to the outline; positions only in the definition's `layout` member; "tidy up" runs dagre top-to-bottom as one undoable command.
4. **Editing aids:** our own undo and redo (immutable documents, 100 entries, gestures coalesced, Ctrl or Cmd with Z and with Shift and Z, active only inside the editor region); the palette built from `GET /workflow-actions`, with unavailable actions shown "not configured"; step forms generated from each config's JSON Schema with the variable picker (C115.3); the condition builder writing the C114 dialect (ordered routes, AND groups, OR between groups, else); validation problems mapped by JSON Pointer to node badges (`aria-describedby`) and an errors panel with "go to step", announced through a polite live region; the raw definition always editable in the existing `JsonEditor` as a fallback.
5. **Versions and publishing:** the draft, published and archived versions; the server diff as a list and as canvas badges (icon and word, never colour alone); publish with rollout percentage, change note and the required acknowledgements (egress, irreversible redaction, automated declines with basis and contest route, C116.4) each shown with its reason; rollout finish and undo; restore; promote; pause, activate, archive; the version's grant and egress marks.
6. **Runs:** a runs table per workflow (status, version, rollout label, trigger, created, duration, waiting-on) with filters; a run page with the path taken drawn over a read-only canvas and as a list, each step's status as an icon and a word, attempts, error type, inputs and outputs masked without `pii:view`; resume (with the unknown-effect acknowledgement), rerun and cancel; cancel-runs of older versions; the sandbox test-run panel with trigger object, start and end steps and output overrides (C120.7).
7. **Secrets, account actions and triggers:** the secrets page (keys, descriptions, allowed hosts, last four, rotate, set per environment, never a value shown); account actions (bind, label, parameter form) and the account page's actions menu with its form; manual runs from ids or a CSV.
8. **Tests:** Playwright keyboard-only authoring through the outline (a trigger, a conditional with two routes, a wait, publish), canvas and outline in sync, undo and redo, the diff view, error badges; axe on every page; the Phase 7 injected-CSP test extended with `style-src 'self' 'nonce-…'` on the editor; masked values without `pii:view`.
9. **Also in this console** (H25; owner `dashboard-workflows`): the settings of C143.7 in the settings form; the editor's decision templates (C128.5) and the recipe list (C140.5); the trigger, inputs, variables and outputs panels, with nullable and PII marks shown on every path and the publish acknowledgements; the trigger-deliveries view (C118.1); run redaction (C120.9) behind a confirmation; the per-step Evaluate Code test in sandbox; Trust Device on the Phase 7 device view; the sandbox messages in the test-run panel (C126.4); `beta` badges and tiers on report and list-match outputs.

### C142. Console: cases and the reviewer (Workflow 8C)

1. **Lists and queues:** the case list with the C129.5 filters, saved column choice per user (local preference only), bulk actions with per-case outcomes, "next case" from my queues, notifications (assigned, SLA breached, mentioned); queue, member and assignment-policy pages; template pages with the version editor (statuses with categories, transitions, resolutions, field schemas, SLA policy, attachment policy, default modules) and publish; SLA policies and calendars (week editor, holidays, CSV import, the two-year validation result).
2. **The case page** (decision 70: a fixed default layout; the drag-and-drop layout editor, checklists and the status-switcher rule are Phase 9): a header (name, status and category as text, resolution, assignee, queue, SLA state and time remaining in words), the status control honouring transitions, required comments and status tags; held objects grouped by type with "as attached" beside the live state, each linked through its own route and scope ("no access" otherwise); the modules Phase 7 deferred (list matches, graph, signals, device, location) and the report, report run and transaction modules, each visible by its permission; the timeline (history and comments, newest first); comments with mention suggestions; attachments with scan status and download; case actions with their parameter forms; export and print.
3. **The compare panel** (editor note 7; ours, C22 of the behaviour note): the ID front zoomed to the portrait box from the vision result (or the NFC `chip-portrait`) beside `selfie-center`, the left and right poses on request; "link views" applies one zoom and pan to both; `@panzoom/panzoom` 4.6.2 with our own buttons and focus-scoped keys (`+`, `-`, `0`, arrows, `R` only while the viewer has focus; page chords Alt+Shift with a letter, switchable off, listed in a dialog with `aria-keyshortcuts`); rotate in 90° steps, brightness, contrast and grayscale as viewing aids that change nothing stored; the similarity, threshold, basis and check status exactly as the selfie card shows them, never "match" when the status says otherwise, with `beta` badges for H-tier results; renditions only (C135.3), streamed through the console proxy.
4. **Decisions stay explicit:** approve, decline, mark for review and reopen are buttons with a confirmation that call the inquiry's own routes and scopes; no decision has a keyboard shortcut. The inquiry page gains its decision records (C128.1) and the reopen action for users with `inquiries:write`.
5. **Print view:** `@media print` with `@page`; navigation, toasts and controls hidden; closed `<details>` expanded; images kept with their captions; a footer with the environment, case id, the printing operator's id, the time and "contains personal data" when it does; the Print button calls `POST /cases/:id/print` before `window.print()`.
6. **Tests:** Playwright for the reviewer flow (pull a case, compare, comment with a mention, run a case action from the case page through its parameter form, resolve, decide the held inquiry), keyboard-only use of the compare panel, the print view's audit call, bulk actions, the template editor; axe on every page; the injected-CSP test on the case page; WCAG 2.2 AA criteria of editor note 7.7 (1.4.1, 1.4.10, 2.1.4, 2.4.7, 2.4.11, 2.5.7, 2.5.8, 4.1.3) each with a test or a recorded manual check.
7. **Also in this console** (H25, F32): case redaction, comment redaction, and field, tag, queue, SLA and held-object edits (owner `dashboard-reviewer` on the case page, `dashboard-cases` in bulk); the exports list (`GET /case-exports`, `dashboard-cases`); scan-status labels including "sandbox scan (not a real scan)"; attachment upload through a streaming route handler `app/cases/[id]/attachments/route.ts`, excluded from `proxy.ts`'s matcher and checking the session itself, so a 10 MiB file never passes the 1 MB server-action limit or the proxy's buffer (server actions stay at 1 MB; a Playwright test uploads a 10 MiB file); the case modules reuse Phase 7's list-match, graph, signal, device and location components with their null, `beta` and attribution rules.

### C143. Platform amendments

1. **Repeatable jobs** (C54.1 registry): `workflow-step-sweep` (15 s: ready steps not enqueued, expired leases, due timers, timed-out waits), `workflow-trigger-sweep`, `workflow-wait-match-sweep` and `workflow-schedule-sweep` (15 s), `workflow-scheduled-runs` (15 s), `case-sla` (30 s), `case-reassign` (60 s), `case-export-sweep` (60 s: expired files deleted); the retention steps of C120.8, C134.4, C138.1 and C139.3 inside the C16 pass.
2. **Queues:** `workflow-step`, `workflow-step-batch`, `workflow-trigger`, `workflow-wait-match`, `case-bulk`, `case-export`, `case-attachment-scan`, `case-rendition`. Each removes its jobs on completion and failure.
3. **Rate-limit policies** (C12 with Phase 7's per-policy windows):

   | Policy | Limit |
   |---|---|
   | `workflow-triggers` | 600 a minute per environment, 60 a minute per key |
   | `workflow-runs` | run creation per environment, defer mode (C117.8) |
   | `workflow-subject` | 60 runs a minute per workflow and subject, defer mode (C118.8) |
   | `workflow-http` | 600 a minute per environment, fail closed (C124.7) |
   | `workflow-email` | 1,000 an hour per environment; 5 an hour and 20 a day per recipient; fail closed |
   | `workflow-slack` | 1 a second per (environment, Slack secret id), burst 3 |
   | `workflow-email-install` | 20 a day per recipient across the install, keyed by the install-level recipient HMAC (C126.1), fail closed |
   | `workflow-code` | 600 evaluations a minute per environment |
   | `case-search` | 120 a minute per key |
   | `case-create` | 600 cases an hour per environment for `POST /cases`, `create_case` and the graph bulk action (H30; provisional: well above a review team's pace, low enough to stop a loop flooding queues); 429 for API callers, defer mode for steps |
   | `case-exports` | 10 an hour per environment |
   | `case-attachments` | 60 a minute per principal |

   **Fail modes** (P31): the egress and compute budgets (`workflow-http`, `workflow-email`, `workflow-slack`, `workflow-code`) fail closed; the defer-mode limiters (`workflow-runs`, `workflow-subject`, `case-create` for steps) defer, never fail open, when Valkey is down (C117.8); the route policies (`workflow-triggers`, `case-search`, `case-exports`, `case-attachments`, `case-create` for API callers) follow C12, documented. A test per policy shows it applied and its fail mode.
4. **Events and filters** (F14, F20, P38). Registered in each Stage 1a with golden payloads, webhook samples and dashboard groups ("Workflows", "Workflow runs", "Cases", "Case comments", "Lists"); `list.updated` and the `list` subject are registered by 8A (C127.12). Strict webhook filters (C70.4's switch) extend to the new subjects: a `workflow-run` resolves through its workflow id (a new filter key `workflow-ids`); a `case` through its template (`case-template-ids`) and its held inquiries' templates, matching an inquiry-template filter only when **every** held inquiry's template is in the filter; a `case-comment` through its case; a workflow or version through its workflow id; a `list` event through its list (`list-ids`). The contracts add the filter keys to the webhook contract; each Stage 1b extends `WebhookFilters` (`tables/webhooks.ts`), `EventSubject` (which gains `inquiryTemplateIds[]`) and the `subjectFilter` SQL of `listWebhooksForEvent` (`repositories/webhooks.ts`), with tests; the resolvers live in owned files (`workflows/triggers/filter-subjects.ts`, `services/cases/filter-subjects.ts`) called from `events/subject.ts`; `dashboard-workflows` adds the form fields.
5. **Masking, redaction, rewrap and rebuild lists** (each Stage 1b declares them, with a test per exit):
   - every new column and its form (sealed with a row AAD, an envelope object, a 64-hex HMAC, or plaintext by a stated decision), in a table in each Stage 1b's TDD file, checked by the coverage tests;
   - API logs: `PII_WORKFLOW_RUN_`, `PII_CASE_`, `PII_CASE_COMMENT_` and `PII_CASE_ATTACHMENT_ATTRIBUTES` from the resources and request bodies (trigger `fields`, test-run overrides, case `fields`, `meta.message`, attachments' `data` and `filename`, `meta.user-email-address`, `creator-email-address`), joining `MASKED_ATTRIBUTES` and the API-log masked set; `secret-value` joins the redacted keys;
   - redaction subjects: `REDACTION_SUBJECT_ID_PREFIXES` gains `wfr`, `case`, `cscm`, `catt`, `idec`; every new redaction calls `stubEventPayloads` and `forgetStoredResponses`;
   - rewrap: every new sealed column and DEK joins `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS`, `SEALED` and `REWRAP_TABLES` with a `kek:rewrap` test;
   - rebuild: the searchable case-field HMACs join `blind-index:rebuild`;
   - **more API-log members** (P21): `data.parameters` of both `run-action` routes, `object-ids` and CSV bodies of manual runs, test-run `overrides` and synthetic trigger payloads, `meta.comment` of decisions and reopens, review-request statements, export and search `query` leaves, holiday CSV bodies, and the trigger route's file values; step `input`, `output`, run `outputs`, `variables` and search `query` are masked **whole** for readers without PII visibility; multipart `filename` is masked on every route; problem bodies (`trigger_input_invalid`, `definition_invalid`) never echo a received value; one API-log masking test per new route;
   - **audit metadata** (P19): no new audit call writes free text or a filter value into `audit_logs.metadata`; each new call site's metadata validates against a strict zod schema with no free-text string members, checked by a coverage test.
6. **Includes** (F34): each route file declares its own include map, typed by its resource's includes (the pattern of `INQUIRY_INCLUDES` in `routes/server/inquiries.ts`): `workflow`, `workflow-version` (runs); `case-template`, `case-queue`, `case-comments` and the held-object relationships (cases); `decisions` (inquiries, wired to an `api-actions` builder).
7. **Settings** (C16 route, audit-logged):
   - under `settings:write`: `workflow-run-retention-days`, `workflow-run-history-retention-days`, `workflow-in-flight-cap`, `workflow-http-ports`, `workflow-http-hosts`, `workflow-email-recipients`, `workflow-sms-recipients`, `workflow-sms-countries`, `workflow-link-hosts`, `case-retention-days`;
   - under `settings:privacy` (Phase 7's permission): `workflow-http-private-cidrs`.
8. **Configuration** (per-process validation, `.env.example`, compose): `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_CODE_ENABLED`, `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_CODE_RUNNER` (`pool` or `socket:<path>`; `pool` in production logs the C123.3 warning), `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_HTTP_ALLOW_LOOPBACK`, `OPENVERIFY_NAT64_PREFIXES`, `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_IN_FLIGHT_CAP`, `OPENVERIFY_PROVIDER_SLACK_{SANDBOX,PRODUCTION}`, `OPENVERIFY_PROVIDER_ATTACHMENT_SCANNER_{SANDBOX,PRODUCTION}`, `OPENVERIFY_CLAMD_SOCKET`. **The worker** (F05, F11; decision 76; a C63 amendment) now also reads the SMS bindings, the Twilio credentials, `OPENVERIFY_SMTP_URL` and `OPENVERIFY_MAIL_FROM` for workflow messages, and builds the services the actions call once through `createWorkflowServices(config, infra)` (`apps/api/src/workflows/services.ts`, from the same factories `container.ts` uses); compose passes those variables to the worker and `infra/tests/compose.test.sh` is changed with the reason. Variables that carry paths or credentials join the secret-scan and startup-error redaction lists.
9. **Scopes:** `devices:write` (Trust Device, C127.9) beside Phase 7's device read scope.

### C144. Records, ADR and docs

- **ADR 0007** (Workflows: authority, egress, automated decisions and sandboxing), written by `contracts-8a`. Every other record below is corrected by `integration-8a` (F24), so Stage 1a stays small.
- **PLAN:** §2 Workflows row (about 45 actions, the five HTTPS methods, the case actions, Fetch and Update Object, Find or Create Account, Calculate, Update Variable, Copy and Remove Tag, Run Verification, Schedule Workflow and the Phase 7 steps); §2 Cases row (status categories, queues driven by assignment policies, the 1,000-object cap and the attached state); §3 `packages/workflow-engine` row (the interpreter, our JSONLogic dialect, value templates, QuickJS in a child-process pool) and the editor row (`@dagrejs/dagre`); §3 and §6 label JSONLogic as ours; ADR 0001's queue row names BullMQ 6's Postgres backend and pg-boss as the documented exit if Valkey becomes a burden (decision 2).
- **`docs/research/persona-surface.md`:** the events of the behaviour note's Corrections 1, the `wfv_` spelling, the SLA limit discrepancy, the run-list filter contradiction, the restore discrepancy.
- **`THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`:** rows for `cron-parser` 5.10.2 as a direct dependency, `quickjs-emscripten-core` and `@jitl/quickjs-wasmfile-release-sync` 0.32.0 with QuickJS's and Emscripten's (MIT or NCSA) notices, `json-logic-js` 2.0.5 (dev only), `temporal-polyfill` 1.0.5 with `temporal-spec` and `temporal-utils` (used by `packages/workflow-engine` for schedules and by the API for business time), the `@xyflow` stack, `@dagrejs/dagre` and `graphlib`, `@panzoom/panzoom`; Excluded rows for `elkjs`, `date-holidays` data (CC BY-SA 3.0, whose registry metadata says CC-BY-3.0), `ical.js`, `vm2`, Inngest, Restate, n8n and Windmill.
- **Docs:** `docs/workflows/` (index, triggers (with the line that `inquiry.escalated` and `verification.escalated` are not emitted because OpenVerify has no escalation score, H29), steps, conditions (null and taint rules), templates, actions with each action's delivery guarantee and adverse flag, limits, errors, delivery, evaluate-code, https-request and messaging (each with a "Personal data leaving OpenVerify" section: recipients, processor terms, transfers, and that redaction cannot reach copies sent elsewhere, P39), secrets, recipes); `docs/cases/` (index, templates and statuses, queues and assignment, SLA and calendars, comments, attachments and scanning, export and print, redaction); `docs/compliance/automated-decisions.md` (C128.8); `docs/checks/sandbox-providers.md` additions (Slack, the mail sink, the scanner).

### C145. Deferred

- Custom functions; importer-created runs; custom events; Transliterate Fields; the Zendesk, Salesforce, HubSpot, GPT and MX Bank steps; the Slack OAuth app and GovSlack hosts; per-step SMTP.
- Parallel joins other than join-all (`any`, `first`); migrating in-flight runs to a new version; automatic compensation.
- Run Verification's `complete-on-tentative` (its semantics are not public); the method-chain operators `page_size` and `page_number` (lists in conditions are capped at 1,000 and `first`, `last`, `where` and sorting cover the documented uses); case-action placements other than the header and their visibility rules (with the Phase 9 layout editor).
- `user-notification.*` and `user.availability-updated` events (notifications are console-only in Phase 8, and availability waits with availability-driven assignment).
- The canvas simulation overlay, inquiry-embedded workflows and flow-builder branching on workflows (Phase 9).
- The case layout editor, checklists, the status-switcher rule, preset comments, teams, availability and availability-driven assignment, case analytics (Phase 9); case review agents (an LLM; tier A at best); the email module, case messages and inbound routing; AML alert and SAR cases.
- Field-level and conditional redaction; an egress proxy setting; RFC 9421 request signatures (a possible v2).
- A server-made case PDF and its fonts (Phase 9 with the `print` routes).

### C146. Fixtures and end to end

1. **Fixtures:** synthetic only: golden definitions (valid, and one invalid per graph rule and per publish rule), golden condition and template vectors, DST and business-time vectors, synthetic report hits from the Phase 6 fixtures, synthetic list items, the EICAR string for the sandbox scanner, generated test images without real faces except the existing public-domain fixtures.
2. **Integration (Testcontainers):** wait versus timeout; wait registration versus event; concurrent join completions; claim expiry with a live worker; cancel versus completion; trigger fan-out exactly once under job retries; Valkey down at commit (work delayed, never lost); a resume that never repeats a `done` effect; the run-subject redaction; the SLA sweep once per epoch under two sweepers; round-robin and fewest-open under concurrent assignment; the pull index plan (`EXPLAIN`); the HTTPS guard against a local resolver returning private and mixed answers.
3. **Full-stack specs** (the reusable stack with shards, under `flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock`):
   - 8A: an inquiry-completed workflow that runs a sandbox watchlist report with a synthetic hit, marks the inquiry for review and, on the report's dismissal, approves it, with the decision record checked; an API-triggered workflow whose HTTPS step calls a local receiver (loopback allowed in the e2e sandbox) that verifies the signature with `@openverify/node`; an Evaluate Code step that times out and errors the run; a Slack step recorded in the sandbox sink;
   - 8B: the seeded "Review on report match" workflow end to end (case created holding the report, a reviewer resolves it through the API, the workflow declines with the basis filled, the decision record says not solely automated); a case with `sla-expires-in-seconds: 5` firing `case.sla-expired` once to a webhook; an attachment uploaded, scanned by the sandbox scanner and downloaded as a rendition without EXIF;
   - 8C: the console specs of C141.8 and C142.6.

## Workflow 8A (the engine and its actions)

**Families and checkpoints**, as in Phase 7: (E) engine (workflows, versions, runs, steps, triggers, schedules), (A) object actions and decisions, (X) egress (secrets, HTTPS, messaging, code). Stage 1a and Stage 1b commit by family, every checkpoint with every suite green and recorded in its TDD file. Ten agents.

- **Stage 1a: `contracts-8a`.** It runs alone. It first takes the inventory at the Phase 8 base into `docs/research/phase8-inventory.md` (every claim with its `path:line`), re-verifies the Starting point and every Phase 6 and Phase 7 interface 8A uses, and records drift as "Workflow 8A Stage 1a amendments". It names in the inventory: the `…InTx` forms that exist and those that are missing; each action's service and the dependencies that service takes (F11); the verification types that need no end-user capture; every Phase 7 list write path (for `list.updated`, F20); the call sites of inquiry, report and transaction redaction (F35); the exact files of the event publisher, the three places that build it, the address guard and the sandbox sink.

  It delivers, committed in this order:
  - **Common:** ids and internal prefixes (C112.1); the scopes, role lists, `NOT_FOR_VIEWERS` and `PERMISSION_INFO` labels (C112.5, C143.9); the audit actor `workflow_run` and the `Acting` actor type; problem codes with golden fixtures (C112.8); every dependency 8A needs through the catalog (`bullmq` 6.3.14, `cron-parser` 5.10.2, `temporal-polyfill` 1.0.5, `quickjs-emscripten-core` 0.32.0, `@jitl/quickjs-wasmfile-release-sync` 0.32.0, `json-logic-js` 2.0.5 as a dev dependency) with their `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` rows; `readCsv` in `packages/checks/src/csv/read.ts` with Phase 7's options and codes (F13); the `packages/workflow-engine` package (config, exports, the export lines and stubs of `definition/`, `graph/`, `interpreter/`, `expressions/`, `templates/`, `variables/`, `schedules/`, `limits/`, `actions/`, `code-runner/`); ADR 0007.
  - **(X) code:** the Evaluate Code contract (C123.2, C123.6) and the code-runner's framing protocol types. `code-sandbox` starts here (F24).
  - **(E):** the definition schema with every node type (C113.1–C113.4); the workflow, version, deployment, run, step, delivery and trigger-file contracts with PII marks; the trigger view's alias table (C114.4); the events with golden payloads and webhook samples (C112.7), `list.updated` and the `list` subject (C127.12), `inquiry.reopened`, `workflow.paused`; the route schemas of C112.6; `@openverify/node` groups `workflows`, `workflowVersions`, `workflowRuns`, `workflowTestRuns`, `accountActions` (F34).
  - **(A) registry types:** the action registry contract (C122.1) with its entry type, the `adverse` and `stage` fields, the per-type tables, and reserved keys for the 8B case actions. `engine-logic` starts here (after common, (E) and these types; F24).
  - **(A) actions:** the config and output schemas (with PII marks) of every 8A action; the decision-record contract with its polymorphic subject, `GET /decisions`, `inquiry.decisions`, the `meta.decision` member of `inquiry.declined`, and the reopen route schema (C128); the inquiry state machine's `declined → needs_review` edge with its own event `inquiry.reopened` (a per-edge event override, V22); the consent key `consent.automated-decision` (C128.4); the list `purpose` and version attributes; the device trust attributes (C127.9); `adverse-statuses` and `contest-route` on transaction types and `contest-route` on account types; `meta.solely-automated` on the approve and decline schemas; the flow session's `decision-contest-route`.

  - **In the same commit as each family's fixtures:** the include maps, the dashboard minimum to typecheck (labels, event words, `PERMISSION_INFO`), and the API fixtures. The API, dashboard and Android suites stay green.

  It owns any path its deliverables need.
- **Stage 1b: `foundation-8a`** (plumbing only). It starts after Stage 1a's common and (E) commits, beside Stage 2. Its migration is one file, extended per checkpoint before the Workflow's first push, and it measures the PGlite migration time before and after (Risks). **First**, before any checkpoint (F08): it measures the unit-api, node and integration CI jobs at the Phase 8 base and splits them (a vitest `--shard` matrix; `@openverify/workflow-engine` and the code-runner tests in their own job), so no job is within 25% of its timeout. ## Workflow 8B (cases)

**Families and checkpoints**, as in 8A: (C) cases, templates, held objects, comments and search; (Q) queues, assignment and SLA; (F) files, renditions and exports. Nine agents.

- **Stage 1a: `contracts-8b`.** It runs alone. It first re-verifies 8A as built (the action registry and its per-type tables, the event wait and its output, the trigger kinds, the decision record's human-step entry table, `current_case`), the Phase 7 field-schema walker and CSV writer, 8A's `readCsv`, and `identifierKey`, and records drift. Then it commits by family:
  - **Common:** ids (C129.1); scopes, role lists and labels (C129.4); the API key `case-template-ids` restriction; problems (C129.7); `temporal-polyfill` 1.0.5 added to `apps/api/package.json` through the catalog for business time (V14); the dashboard typecheck minimum.
  - **(C):** the case, template, version, comment and case-action contracts with PII marks; the route schemas of C129.5 for its family; the events of C129.6 with golden payloads and samples; the case action keys' config and output schemas (C140.3); `@openverify/node` groups `cases`, `caseTemplates`, `caseComments`, `caseActions` (F34).
  - **(Q):** the queue, member, assignment-policy, SLA-policy and calendar contracts; the `sla-state` attribute; the groups `caseQueues`, `casePolicies`, `caseCalendars`.
  - **(F):** the attachment, export and rendition contracts; the vision `POST /v1/renditions` request and response schemas on both sides, and the `createRendition` signature and stub in `apps/api/src/vision/index.ts` (F36); the group `caseExports`.

  It owns any path its deliverables need.
- **Stage 1b: `foundation-8b`** (plumbing only), beside Stage 2, after Stage 1a's common and (C) commits:
  - **(C):** the tables `case_templates`, `case_template_versions`, `case_name_counters`, `cases`, the held-object join tables, `case_comments`, `case_history` (append-only trigger with its escapes, F12), `case_actions`, `user_notifications`, the searchable-field HMAC table; repository stubs; the masking, rewrap, redaction and rebuild entries (C143.5); the routes of (C) frozen at 501 in their implementers' files (`cases.ts`, `case-templates.ts`, `case-comments.ts`, `case-actions.ts`, `case-print.ts`, F15); the case subject resolver stub in `services/cases/filter-subjects.ts` called from `events/subject.ts`, and the case filter keys in `WebhookFilters` and `subjectFilter` (F14); the redaction hook `redactCasesInTx` (stub, `api-cases`) called from `services/redaction.ts` (F35); the hooks `assignOnEntry(tx, case)` (stub, `api-queues-sla`) and `acceptPatchAttachments(tx, case, attachments)` (stub, `api-case-files`) that `api-cases` calls (F15); the case action keys registered through 8A's tables with stub implementations that answer `not_implemented`; the API key `case_template_ids` column with the minting check `assertKeyTemplateRestrictionInTx` (stub, `api-cases`) called from `services/api-keys.ts`, and the visibility filter `filterCaseVisibility` (stub in `services/cases/visibility.ts`, `api-cases`) called from the events routes, the API-log service, the run and step routes and the notification route (V08); the review-request route frozen in `routes/server/inquiry-review-requests.ts` (`api-case-workflows`, V07).
  - **(Q):** `case_queues`, `case_queue_members`, `case_assignment_policies`, `case_sla_policies`, `case_calendars` and the SLA columns on `cases`; the routes frozen (`case-queues.ts`, `case-assignment.ts` for `assign` and `next`, `case-assignment-policies.ts`, `case-sla-policies.ts`, `case-calendars.ts`); the `case-sla` and `case-reassign` registrations with stubs.
  - **(F):** `case_attachments`, `file_renditions` (with its foreign key on `files(id)` and the storage-delete hook called from `services/redaction.ts`, P22), `case_exports`; the `attachmentScanner` provider binding (reserved in 8A) with the `none`, `clamd` and `sandbox` provider stubs; the queues `case-bulk`, `case-export`, `case-attachment-scan`, `case-rendition`; the routes frozen (`case-attachments.ts`, `case-exports.ts`, `case-print.ts`, `routes/dashboard/file-renditions.ts`); the hooks `deleteRenditionsForFilesInTx` (called from `services/redaction.ts` and the file-retention steps, and in the C16 leftover-file retry) and `requestRenditionsForFiles` (called from the verification processing jobs at the sites the inventory names), both stubs for `api-case-files` (V19, V27); the retention-step stubs for case retention, notifications and export files, and the `case-export-sweep` registration (V27).
  - **After the last checkpoint:** the OpenAPI snapshot.
- **Stage 2** (parallel, beside Stage 1b, after Stage 1a):
  - **`case-logic`:** the case state machine with the transition map rules (C131.1), the assignment selection functions (round-robin cursor, fewest-open with its ties, eligibility; C132.3) and the search parser and validator (C137.2) producing a typed predicate tree, in `packages/core/src/cases/`; business time with its property and tz tests (C133.3) and the SLA state transitions (C133.5) as pure functions in `apps/api/src/services/cases/sla/business-time.ts` and `sla-state.ts` (F30). It owns those paths.
  - **`vision-renditions`:** `POST /v1/renditions` in the vision service (decode with the existing guards, apply orientation, strip every metadata segment, re-encode; PDF pages through the guarded PDF child; refuse what it cannot decode), its own limiter lane (F25), its tests (a JPEG with GPS EXIF comes back without it; orientation applied; a PNG text chunk stripped; a decompression bomb refused; a PDF gives page images; verification processing keeps its slots while renditions queue), and the API's `createRendition` client. It owns `services/vision/src/openverify_vision/renditions.py`, its tests, three edits in `app.py` (the router line, the limiter-lane line, and the rendition path's entry in `SINGLE_UPLOAD_PATHS` and `route_limits`, V16), and `createRendition` in `apps/api/src/vision/`.
- **Stage 3** (each after its family's checkpoint and its named Stage 2 dependency, F07):
  - **`api-cases`** (C; after `case-logic`): cases (create with names from the counter, read, update, fields, tags, held objects with their attached state, transitions and history), templates and versions with publish, comments with mentions and their redaction, notifications, `GET /cases` filters and `POST /cases/search` (compiling `case-logic`'s tree into parameterised SQL, with the PII rule for `fields.*`), bulk actions, template visibility on every exit of C129.4, case redaction (`redactCasesInTx`) and retention, the case subject resolvers. It owns `apps/api/src/services/cases/` except `cases/{queues,assignment,sla,attachments,exports,actions}/`, `routes/server/{cases,case-templates,case-comments}.ts`, `routes/dashboard/notifications.ts`, `services/cases/visibility.ts`, and `packages/db/src/repositories/{cases,case-templates,case-comments,case-history,case-objects,case-field-index,notifications}.ts`.
  - **`api-queues-sla`** (Q; after `case-logic`): queues and members, assignment policies, every strategy with the queue lock, `assignOnEntry`, `assign` and `POST /cases/next` with its index and `EXPLAIN` test, the reassign sweep, SLA policies and calendars (holiday import through `readCsv`), the SLA columns' transitions on every status change through a hook that `api-cases` calls (declared in Stage 1b), the `case-sla` sweep, calendar recompute. It owns `apps/api/src/services/cases/{queues,assignment,sla}/` except `case-logic`'s two files, `routes/server/{case-queues,case-assignment,case-assignment-policies,case-sla-policies,case-calendars}.ts`, `jobs/repeatable/cases.ts` and `packages/db/src/repositories/{case-queues,case-policies,case-sla}.ts`.
  - **`api-case-files`** (F; after `vision-renditions`): attachments (both upload routes and `acceptPatchAttachments`, typing, caps, storage, download of renditions and originals with audit), the scanner adapters and the scan job with its serving rules, renditions for attachments and for inquiry files (`file_renditions`, their lifecycle, the dashboard route), exports (CSV and JSON, creator-only access, the job, the 24-hour files and their sweep, `case.exported`), the print route. It owns `apps/api/src/services/cases/{attachments,exports}/`, `apps/api/src/providers/attachment-scanner/`, `routes/server/{case-attachments,case-exports,case-print}.ts`, `routes/dashboard/file-renditions.ts`, `jobs/cases/` and `packages/db/src/repositories/{case-attachments,file-renditions,case-exports}.ts`.
  - **`api-case-workflows`** (F22): first, after (C) and 8A as built: case actions, `GET /cases/:id/available-actions` and `POST /cases/:id/run-action`, the case trigger, `current_case`'s provider, the case human-step entry, the case type in the per-type tables, the review-request route (`POST /inquiries/:id/review-requests`) with its case creation and the lifting of 8A's publish refusal for case-template contest routes, the recipes with a case (C140.5) registered in 8A's recipe table, and the graph "create case" bulk action; then, after `api-cases` and `api-queues-sla` are done, the eight case action implementations replacing the stubs, with their exactly-once tests. It owns `apps/api/src/workflows/actions/cases/`, `apps/api/src/services/cases/actions/`, `routes/server/{case-actions,inquiry-review-requests}.ts`, the graph bulk-action file the inventory names (for that change only), and `apps/api/src/seed/case-workflows.ts`.
- **Stage 4: `integration-8b`:** the 8B full-stack specs (C146.3); `docs/cases/`; the sandbox-provider docs for the scanner; the OpenAPI snapshot and `@openverify/node` after Stage 1; CI with job times re-measured; the full validation; defects fixed test-first; the amendments of Stages 2–3; a draft of the Phase 8B entry for `docs/PROGRESS.md` in its report. It owns `e2e/`, `docs/cases/`, `docs/checks/`, `docs/api/openapi.json`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `infra/`, `turbo.json` and `docs/plans/phase-8.plan.md`.

**Shared files (Phase 8B):** the 8A list (with 8A's owned files now shared too), plus `packages/core/src/cases/index.ts` (after Stage 2 starts), `apps/api/src/vision/index.ts` (outside `createRendition`), `services/vision/src/openverify_vision/app.py` (outside its three edits), and the dashboard minimum files.

## Workflow 8C (console, review, fixes)

- **Stage 1** (parallel):
  - **`dashboard-workflows`** builds C141 (with C141.9): the editor (outline first, then the canvas), versions, publishing with acknowledgements, rollout, the diff, runs and the run page, test runs, trigger deliveries, secrets, account actions and the account page's actions menu, manual runs, recipes, settings, Trust Device; the inquiry page's decision records and reopen action (C142.4); the webhook form's new filter fields; the event words for every Phase 8 event, the permission areas, and the README section. It owns every shared dashboard file, `apps/dashboard/package.json`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and the lockfile (it adds `@xyflow/react`, `@dagrejs/dagre` and `@panzoom/panzoom` first, on request), `app/workflows/`, `app/secrets/`, `components/workflows/`, `lib/api/{workflows,workflow-versions,workflow-runs,workflow-secrets,account-actions,decisions}.ts`, `testing/routes/workflows.ts` and its fixtures, `e2e/tests/ui-workflows.spec.ts` and `e2e/tests/support/ui-workflows.ts`. It adds the other agents' navigation entries when asked.
  - **`dashboard-cases`** builds C142.1 and its part of C142.7: the case list, bulk actions, next case, notifications, queues, members, assignment policies, templates and their version editor, SLA policies and calendars, exports and the exports list. It owns `app/cases/` except `app/cases/[id]/`, `app/case-templates/`, `app/case-queues/`, `app/case-settings/`, `components/cases/` except `components/cases/reviewer/`, `lib/api/{cases,case-templates,case-queues,case-policies,case-calendars,case-exports,notifications}.ts`, `testing/routes/cases.ts` and its fixtures (F26), `e2e/tests/ui-cases.spec.ts` and `e2e/tests/support/ui-cases.ts`.
  - **`dashboard-reviewer`** builds C142.2–C142.6 and its part of C142.7: the case page and its modules (including Phase 7's deferred case modules), comments and mentions, attachments with the streaming upload route, case actions through available-actions, the compare panel, the print view, and the renditions in the existing inquiry image views. It owns `app/cases/[id]/`, `components/cases/reviewer/`, the image components it moves to renditions, `app/files/[id]/rendition/route.ts` (F28), `lib/api/{case-comments,case-attachments,case-actions,file-renditions}.ts`, `testing/routes/reviewer.ts` and its fixtures (F26), `e2e/tests/ui-reviewer.spec.ts` and `e2e/tests/support/ui-reviewer.ts`.
- **Stage 2: review of all of Phase 8** (read-only, parallel):
  - **privacy, security and law:** the sandbox and its escapes; the HTTPS guard, literal hosts, ports, headers, pooling and redirects; signatures; secrets, their environments, placements and scrubbing; grants, children, causing principals and PII taint; adverse decisions, the decision record and the reopen; attachments, renditions and scanning; masking at every exit (fail closed for runs), API logs, audit metadata and stored copies; template visibility; redaction of runs and cases;
  - **correctness and honesty:** the engine's races and fencing; effect keys and delivery guarantees; waits, joins, loops, modules, retries and limits; the trigger fan-out, refusals and loop guards; null and taint rules; errored or timed-out reports, unscreened lists, unindexed graphs and unknown message effects never read as values; case transitions, assignment, SLA epochs and business time;
  - **operations, licences and performance:** admission and fairness; the benchmark; CI job times; the PGlite migration time; queue churn and autovacuum; dependency licences and ages; the code-runner image; the e2e shards;
  - **console:** React; accessibility (the outline view, the canvas, the compare panel, the print view); the RSC payload; masking in the console; the injected-CSP tests; uploads.
- **Stage 3:** one verifier for every CRITICAL and HIGH finding. It records the 8C Stage 1 amendments.
- **Stage 4:** path-owned fixers:
  - **`fix-api`:** `apps/api`, `apps/flow`, `packages/`, `services/vision`, `e2e/` except the `ui-*` specs and their helpers, `docs/` except `docs/dashboard`, CI, `infra` and `.dockerignore`. It measures the e2e suite and the CI jobs again with the UI specs and adjusts the shards;
  - **`fix-console`:** `apps/dashboard`, and `e2e/tests/ui-*.spec.ts` with their helpers.

## Rules for every agent

- **TDD:** RED first, seen failing for the intended reason; the RED commit compiles and typechecks; then GREEN; then refactor on green. Evidence goes in `docs/tdd/phase8-<agent>.tdd.md`.
- **Shared files:** builders never edit the shared-file lists above. Route schemas, the OpenAPI snapshot, `@openverify/node` and the API fixtures change only through the integration agent. A missing hook or schema change is a `contractDeviation`. Dependencies land in Stage 1a (8A, 8B), or through `dashboard-workflows` (8C).
- **Shared resources:**
  - commits are path-scoped under `flock /tmp/openverify-git.lock`, after `git diff --cached --quiet`;
  - the full-stack e2e runs under `flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock`, as the mocked flow suite plus shards on the reusable stack;
  - long gates run in the foreground with a 600,000 ms timeout; only the stack start may run in the background;
  - flaky tests are made deterministic, never retried. Tests assert order only on keys the code orders by, ties included; time comes from the injected `Clock`, and tests that order rows stamp `created_at` (the Phase 5 lesson: PGlite's `now()` reads a wall clock that steps back under load).
- **No network in tests:** no Slack, SMTP, Twilio or outside HTTPS call from any test; local receivers on loopback, MockAgent and injected resolvers only. The pinned `scripts/fetch_*` downloads CI runs before tests are allowed.
- **Never code:** no operator-written text (conditions, templates, definitions) reaches `eval`, `Function`, a template compiler or a module loader; Evaluate Code runs only in the code runner.
- **Honesty (ADR 0002, 0005, 0006, 0007):**
  - an errored report, an unscreened list, an unindexed graph, a timed-out wait or a failed action is a step error or a `null` with its reason, never `false`, "no match" or "done";
  - no workflow passes a check; a decline is recorded with its basis and whether a person took part;
  - every constant is provisional, with a basis; no limit is tuned on test data.
- **PII:** no field value, variable, step input or output, comment, attachment byte, filename, secret, address or template output may appear in logs (outside `pii`), request URLs logged by the server, stored API logs read without `pii:view`, the journal, Valkey, metadata, problems, events or git. Synthetic data only.
- **Secrets:** a resolved secret appears only in the outgoing request it was resolved for; the canary test of C125.5 covers every egress action.
- **Licences and versions:** rule 4; versions at least 14 days old; no new build script without an `allowBuilds` decision; the QuickJS variant's `.wasm` loads from `node_modules`, never fetched at run time.
- **Content:** no Persona name, branding or copied text in product UI, docs or messages.

## Validation

```bash
pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && pnpm format:check
pnpm turbo run lint typecheck --force
pnpm test                                     # every package >= 80%, @openverify/workflow-engine included; the code-runner security tests run here
pnpm test:integration                         # engine races, fan-out exactly once, Valkey loss, resume without repeats, SLA epochs, assignment races, the pull index plan
python3 scripts/fetch_models.py && python3 scripts/fetch_data.py
(cd services/vision && uv run ruff check && uv run ruff format --check && uv run mypy src tools && uv run pytest)
(cd android && ./gradlew testDebugUnitTest testAndroidHostTest koverVerify assembleDebug :openverify-sdk:assemble lint)
pnpm --filter @openverify/flow e2e           # the mocked flow suite
OPENVERIFY_E2E_REUSE_STACK=1 pnpm --filter @openverify/e2e e2e:stack:up     # background allowed
for i in $(seq 1 $N); do OPENVERIFY_E2E_REUSE_STACK=1 pnpm --filter @openverify/e2e exec playwright test --shard=$i/$N; done
pnpm --filter @openverify/e2e e2e:stack:down
docker build -f infra/docker/apps.Dockerfile . && docker build services/vision && docker build -f infra/docker/code-runner.Dockerfile .
for t in infra/tests/*.test.sh; do bash "$t"; done   # includes the code-runner container's no-network, read-only and no-secret checks
pnpm --filter @openverify/api exec tsx scripts/workflow-benchmark.ts   # reported, not gated
gitleaks git --redact .
```

## Risks

| Risk | Likelihood | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| A sandbox escape in QuickJS reaches keys or tenants | Low–Med | No host functions; one fresh runtime per evaluation; a child process with an empty environment, the permission model and a heap cap; a parent kill; the separate no-network container in the app profile; the monthly CVE check (C123) |
| SSRF through the HTTPS action (rebinding, redirects, encoded hosts, NAT64, metadata, internal ports, pooled sockets) | Med | Literal, normalised hosts; resolve once and connect to the vetted address; one Agent per environment and policy; no redirects; 443 plus a list without bad ports; metadata refused first; NAT64 prefixes; the dated registry test; private CIDRs only inside private or CGNAT blocks by an audited setting (C124) |
| A workflow forges OpenVerify webhooks | Low | Workflow requests are signed only with workflow secrets, in their own format that webhook verification refuses (C124.6) |
| A workflow amplifies a weak principal into declines or redactions | Med | Frozen grants of API scopes bounded by the publisher; triggering principals and child runs must hold the grant; the causing principal of an event must hold an adverse step's scope; publisher revocation pauses egress and adverse workflows; acknowledgements for irreversible steps; the 100-object redaction cap (C121, C127.8) |
| Automated adverse decisions without a lawful basis or a way to contest | Med | The decision record for every adverse step; `solely-automated` decided at run time from what happened; production gates on every adverse step with no static exemption; biometric decisions detected from triggers, paths and statuses and tied to consent; the reopen with human review pending; the contest route delivered to the person; review-first templates; the compliance doc (C128) |
| A side effect repeats on resume or retry (a second email, a double decline) | Med | Effect keys; exactly-once internal effects in one transaction; at-most-once messages with `unknown` blocking resume; `Idempotency-Key` on HTTPS; tests that resume never repeats a `done` effect (C117.5, C120.3) |
| A missed or duplicated trigger | Med | Deliveries written in the event's transaction with a unique key; a sweep for lost enqueues; simulated events excluded; the fan-out race test (C118.1) |
| Lost or double wake-ups after a Valkey loss | Med | Postgres is the only record; timers as `due_at`; a sweep behind every timer and lease; the Valkey-down test (C117.9) |
| A workflow loop floods the system | Med | Causality depth 8; self-trigger off; a per-subject rate guard that defers; publish warnings; per-run step and duration caps (C118.8, C117.7) |
| One environment starves another | Med | Per-environment in-flight slots with rotation; separate sandbox and production caps; two queues; per-action concurrency (C117.8) |
| A condition or template is a DoS or an injection | Low | Our own interpreter with budgets; no code generation; value-level templates with per-sink encoding; publish-time typing (C114, C115) |
| Secrets leak into run data, logs or events | Med | References only in four fields; host binding; resolved last; value scrubbing in four encodings; sealed step data; the canary test (C125) |
| PII leaves through messages or HTTPS without anyone noticing | Med | Egress marks, the publisher's `pii:view`, acknowledgements and audit entries per send; ids-only default templates (C121.4) |
| Spam or toll fraud through workflow messages | Med | Recipients limited to confirmed contacts or allowlists; SMS countries allowlisted and the OTP budget shared; fail-closed limits (C126) |
| Engine tables churn and bloat | Med | Partial indexes on live states; per-table autovacuum; skeleton retention; the benchmark (C117.2, C117.11) |
| SLA deadlines wrong across DST or tz updates | Med | Instants stored; calendars validated with `reject`; property and tz fixture tests that check offsets first; recompute on calendar edits (C133) |
| Assignment races give two reviewers one case | Low | Queue-row locks; `SKIP LOCKED` pulls; `lock-version` on transitions; concurrency tests (C132) |
| A malicious attachment reaches a reviewer | Med | Magic-byte types, caps, renditions re-encoded without metadata, PDFs served only as page images rendered by the vision service's guarded PDF child (originals only to `cases:redact` holders), a scanner port, only `clean` served when a scanner is bound (C135) |
| The editor fails keyboard and screen-reader users | Med | The outline view built first and tested keyboard-only; focus rings, target sizes, live regions; axe and Playwright (C141.2, C141.8) |
| A new dependency breaks the console's future CSP | Low | Client-only rendering, bundled stylesheets, no style-injecting library; the injected-CSP tests (C141.1) |
| The PGlite migration grows past the db tests' hook budget | High | `packages/db/src/testing.ts` already migrates once per process and copies a snapshot (about 0.4 s); each Stage 1b measures the snapshot copy's p95 under full load before and after its migration, with a threshold of 5 s; past it, the fallback is one migrated instance reset per file, or the engine repository tests move to Testcontainers (a Stage 1b fix, test-first) (F29) |
| CI jobs time out (unit-api took 19 min 44 s of 20 on the last green run; node 15 min 49 s) | High | foundation-8a measures and splits the jobs before Stage 2 lands (a vitest shard matrix; the engine and code-runner tests in their own job); each integration agent re-measures (F08) |
| A Stage 3 agent starts before the Stage 2 logic it calls exists | Med | Each Stage 3 agent names its Stage 2 dependency and starts only after it; Stage 2 starts on the Stage 1a commits it needs (F07, F24) |
| Run values leak to viewers or keys without PII visibility | Med | `workflow-runs:read` outside the viewer role; fail-closed masking by registry marks; untyped values always masked; a test per exit (C120.6) |
| Phase 7 lands differently from its plan | Med | Each Stage 1a takes the inventory and records amendments before any Phase 8 code |
| Stage 1a or Stage 1b does not finish | Med | Plumbing only; checkpoints by family; Stage 2 beside Stage 1b on the Stage 1a commits it needs; each Stage 3 builder waits only for its family's checkpoint and its named Stage 2 dependency |
| The e2e suite outgrows its shards | High | The reusable stack and shard matrix; each integration agent measures and re-balances |

## Acceptance

- [x] **Resources (C112):** every prefix, scope, role list, route, event and problem exists with its fixture; every scope refusal is tested; publish, rollout, restore and promote refuse API keys; promote never replaces a draft.
- [x] **Definitions (C113–C115):** every node type validates; every graph rule and publish rule refuses its golden invalid definition with a JSON Pointer; nullable paths never reach a boolean position and every route reading one declares `on-null`; PII taint reaches every egress mark and never a plain sink; the dialect and the templates pass their vectors, limits and differential tests; nothing an operator writes is executed outside the code runner.
- [x] **Versions (C116):** one draft; immutable versions with hashes; staged rollout splits by subject and records the label; restore makes a draft; promote re-resolves references and never publishes into production by itself; the diff ignores layout.
- [x] **Engine (C117, C119):** claims are fenced, leases recover, the crash-loop cap holds; control-flow nodes run in the completing transaction; waits, joins, loops, modules, Run and Schedule Workflow behave as specified, with every race test green; limits are enforced with their codes; fairness slots and rotation work; the benchmark is recorded.
- [x] **Triggers (C118):** event triggers fire exactly once per event and workflow, never for simulated events, never retroactively; API, manual, scheduled, recurring (with DST vectors), verification and account triggers work; loop guards refuse cycles and depth.
- [x] **Runs (C120):** statuses, step outcomes, errors and the six error types; resume never repeats a `done` effect and stops at an `unknown` one; rerun and cancel; run values are masked fail closed on every exit; test runs derive their grant, simulate unacknowledged irreversible steps, record every message and make no HTTPS call unless allowed; retention and redaction (subjects, overflow and copies included) leave no value where their windows and subjects say.
- [x] **Authority (C121):** grants are derived and bounded; a trigger never amplifies; PII egress is marked, acknowledged and audited; environments are isolated in every action.
- [x] **Actions (C122–C127):** every catalogue action is implemented with its stated delivery; Run Report never reads an errored, pending or timed-out report as "no match"; Get List Match never answers `false` for an unscreened list; Sync Graph never answers `synced: true` with indexing pending; Run Graph Query never gives a count for an unfinished, truncated or unindexed query; face-list adds take one consenting inquiry; Trust Device never touches a check; transaction children cascade on redaction; the stopgap rules still work and the seeded workflows are disabled.
- [x] **Sandbox (C123):** every security test of C123.9 passes; the code-runner container has no network, a read-only root and no secrets.
- [x] **HTTPS and secrets (C124, C125):** the guard refuses every special-purpose range, metadata, bad ports, `Host` and hop-by-hop headers and redirects; sockets are never shared across environments or policies; workflow signatures verify with `verifyWorkflowRequestSignature` and never pass webhook verification; secret values are set only in their own environment, resolve only into their bound hosts and placements, and the canary (tables, Valkey, S3, logs) appears nowhere.
- [x] **Messaging (C126):** email, SMS and Slack go only to permitted recipients, with links checked on the rendered message, within fail-closed limits; outputs say `recorded`, `accepted` or `unknown`, never "sent"; retries only on proof of non-delivery; sandbox providers record in their own lists and never send, and are refused in production; the worker binds the messaging providers.
- [x] **Decisions (C128):** every approve, decline and adverse step has a record whose `solely-automated` follows what happened (golden cases: a timeout, an API-key resolution, a bulk resolution, a CSV run); every production adverse step has a basis and a contest route; biometric decisions are detected from triggers, paths and statuses and need consent or a declared basis; reopen is user-only, fires `inquiry.reopened` and blocks automated declines until a user decides; the contest route reaches the person; the review-first templates and `docs/compliance/automated-decisions.md` exist.
- [ ] **Cases (C129–C139):** templates version and pin; transitions follow the map with required comments and tags; queues and every assignment strategy work under concurrency; SLAs pause, resume, recompute and fire once per epoch, never for resolved cases; comments are append-only and redactable; attachments are typed, capped, scanned or marked not scanned, and served as renditions; held objects respect the cap and their own scopes; search is read-after-write; exports neutralise formulas and expire; redaction and retention are complete.
- [x] **Case workflows (C140):** case actions and the case trigger with forms; the case steps; the seeded workflows end to end; the graph "create case" bulk action.
- [x] **Platform (C143):** jobs, queues, policies (defer mode never fails open), events with strict filters, masking, redaction, rewrap and rebuild lists, settings and configuration are in place with a test each.
- [ ] **Console (C141, C142):** every Phase 8 feature is usable from the console without the API; the outline view makes the editor fully keyboard-operable; the compare panel and print view meet the stated WCAG 2.2 AA criteria; masked without `pii:view`; the injected-CSP tests pass.
- [x] **End to end (C146.3):** the 8A, 8B and 8C specs pass on the shards.
- [x] **Deferred (C145):** every deferral is listed in `docs/PROGRESS.md` under "Carried forward".
- [x] **Records (C144):** ADR 0007, the PLAN, surface-index and `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` corrections, and the docs are written.
- [x] **Process:** every existing suite stays green at the end of each checkpoint and stage. Every validation command passes locally and on GitHub. Each agent writes its `docs/tdd/phase8-*.tdd.md`, and `docs/PROGRESS.md` records Phase 8.

At the close (2026-11-04, after Workflow 8D): a tick means no known item stays open against the line and its tests passed in the validation of `f0a32761`, not that every sub-line was walked again by hand. Three lines stay open:

- **Cases (C129–C139):** K8-27's three named problems (`case_template_invalid`, `case_search_invalid`, `attachment_scan_failed`) are not built; a template or search refusal answers a generic problem, and a scan error a plain 409. Phase 9's Workflow 9B takes them (`docs/plans/phase-9.plan.md`, "Phase 7 and 8 outcomes", item 27).
