- **Console (C141, C142):** loop and module steps, loop back-edges and the condition builder's while-condition and list-predicate positions are authorable only through the raw JSON (8D review R005). Phase 9's 9C builds them in the shared graph editor (item 28). A full-stack Trust Device spec waits for Phase 11 (R006).
- **Process:** ticked after CI 41802158981 passed the push of this close (`3cc18901`, 14 of 14 jobs).

## Decisions for the owner (made here; confirm or change)

Each item states the default the build uses until the owner decides, so none blocks a Workflow. The owner's standing approval is to take the industry-best default and record it.

**Engine and definitions**
1. **Build or adopt.** Default: build the engine on Postgres and BullMQ; adopt no engine; DBOS is the design reference (Inngest, Restate, n8n and Windmill fail rule 4; the rest add services).
2. **Queues off Valkey.** Default: no; the database is the only record, so BullMQ 6's Postgres backend or pg-boss can replace Valkey later by configuration (ADR 0001 note).
3. **`bullmq` 6.3.11 → 6.3.14** (newest passing; brings `cron-parser` 5.10.2). Default: yes, in 8A Stage 1a, unless Phase 6 or 7 already did.
4. **Workflow scope.** Default: environment-scoped workflows with a promote action that copies a version as a draft and re-resolves references by name; the public product's organisation-level workflows would bind production runs to sandbox objects.
5. **Condition format.** Default: JSONLogic as stored (PLAN §3), interpreted by our own dialect, labelled ours; `json-logic-js` only as a dev oracle (it follows prototype paths, has a `log` operator and no limits).
6. **Loose equality.** Default: `==` and `!=` accepted only as aliases of `===` and `!==`.
7. **Templates.** Default: our value-level `{{ path }}` templates with per-sink encoding; no Handlebars or liquidjs.
8. **Schedule DST rules.** Default: a missing local time fires at the first valid minute after the gap; a repeated one fires once, at the first occurrence; intervals follow elapsed time.
9. **Schedules.** Default: five-field cron or `every` minutes, one-minute minimum, no `H`, an IANA zone.
10. **Fairness.** Default: 50 in-flight steps per environment, sandbox and production capped separately, overridable.
11. **Limits.** Default: the public ones where public (30-day waits, 100 loop items, no module nesting), ours otherwise (C117.7).
12. **Self-triggering.** Default: off; opt-in per workflow; causality depth 8 and the subject rate guard still apply.
13. **Ordering across runs.** Default: none; a per-workflow `one-run-per-subject` option.
14. **Run data retention.** Default: values 30 days after a run ends; the skeleton 365 days.
15. **Compensation.** Default: none automatic; error edges.
16. **In-flight runs on a new version.** Default: never migrated; an audited cancel-runs action.
17. **`synchronous_commit`.** Default: on; the measured durable throughput is ample.
18. **Valkey eviction policy.** Default: log an error at worker start unless `noeviction`; never refuse to start.
19. **Restore.** Default: creates a draft (the public pages disagree), so a restore passes the publish rules.
20. **Run statuses.** Default: `in_progress`, `completed`, `errored` and ours `canceled`; waiting is `in_progress` with `waiting-on`.
21. **Error types.** Default: the six public example values emitted, the field typed as an open string.
22. **Engine benchmark.** Default: re-measured in 8A by a non-gated script; the result recorded in `docs/PROGRESS.md`.
23. **Simulate.** Default: sandbox test runs with overrides through the API and a console panel in Phase 8; the canvas overlay in Phase 9.
24. **Recurring trigger** (not specified publicly). Default: cron or interval, an object type, a filter in the case-search grammar, 100 objects per tick (at most 1,000), one run per object, a cursor between ticks, one catch-up tick after an outage.
25. **Verification trigger** (one public sentence). Default: an event trigger on `verification.*` with a template filter.
26. **Parallel joins.** Default: join-all only (public); `any` and `first` later.
27. **Not built.** Default: custom functions, importer-created runs, custom events, Transliterate Fields, Send Case Message and the third-party app steps.

**Actions and egress**
28. **Where Evaluate Code runs.** Default: a child-process pool everywhere; a separate no-network `code-runner` container in the compose `app` profile.
29. **QuickJS updates.** Default: the published `@jitl/quickjs-wasmfile-release-sync@0.32.0`; a monthly CVE check; build the variant only for a reachable CVE fixed upstream before a wrapper release.
30. **Evaluate Code availability.** Default: on, with `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_CODE_ENABLED=false` to turn it off.
31. **Secrets in Evaluate Code.** Default: never.
32. **HTTPS destinations.** Default: scheme, host and port literal; only path and query templated (narrower than the public step).
33. **HTTPS host allowlist.** Default: available, off.
34. **HTTPS ports.** Default: 443 plus an environment list that can never include a Fetch bad port.
35. **Signing.** Default: every request (GET and DELETE over an empty body) with a host-bound workflow secret only, under `OpenVerify-Request-Signature` with `w1=` over method, target and body, which webhook verification refuses; never a webhook secret (P01).
36. **Private destinations.** Default: refused in production except an audited CIDR allowlist under `settings:privacy` whose entries must lie inside private or CGNAT blocks (never unspecified, loopback, link-local, metadata, multicast or reserved space); loopback only in sandbox by an install flag, for test receivers.
37. **Per-step SMTP and From.** Default: not offered.
38. **Slack.** Default: incoming webhooks only; OAuth and GovSlack later.
39. **Message recipients.** Default: the object's OTP-confirmed email or phone, or an environment allowlist; an unconfirmed field only with an acknowledged step option (never for inquiry-link email); SMS countries allowlisted (none without an inquiry template).
40. **Sandbox email.** Default: a per-environment sink readable with `sandbox:read`; real delivery only to allowlisted addresses.
41. **PII leaving the platform.** Default: only with the publisher's `pii:view`, the step marked and acknowledged, every send audited; default templates carry ids and links.
42. **Redaction steps.** Default: an irreversible acknowledgement at publish; at most 100 objects per run.
43. **Field-level redaction.** Default: deferred; whole objects only.
44. **Budgets** (provisional). Default: HTTPS 600 a minute per environment and 5 per (environment, host); email 1,000 an hour per environment, 5 an hour and 20 a day per recipient, and 20 a day per recipient across the install; SMS at most half the environment's OTP budget; Slack 1 a second per (environment, Slack secret); code `max(1, CPUs − 1)` per worker and 600 a minute per environment.
45. **A publisher who loses rights.** Default: deactivation, deletion, or the loss of a right that an egress, redaction, decline or other adverse step uses pauses the workflow until a holder of the rights republishes; the loss of a right only internal non-adverse steps use keeps it running, flagged.
46. **Trust Device.** Default: a reversible, audited `trusted-at` mark on a device, read by conditions, never by a check.
47. **Sync Graph** (corrected in review, H01). Default: it waits for the account's pending graph-index jobs and answers `synced: true` only when nothing is pending and every kind of its mode is indexed; otherwise `synced: false` with what is pending, or a step error (Phase 7 indexes through an outbox and a backfill, not synchronously).
48. **Stopgap rules.** Default: kept, off by default; a disabled seeded "Review on report match" workflow.

**Automated decisions**
49. **Declines without a person** (Art. 22). Default: in production, a basis and a contest route on each such decline; biometric declines need recorded consent or a declared basis; sandbox warns.
50. **Reopen.** Default: `declined → needs_review` for users with `inquiries:write`, with a comment and an audit entry (a C10 amendment); the biometric deletion of a decline is not undone.
51. **Editor templates.** Default: review and a case first; a decline as the case's resolution step.

**Cases**
52. **Prefixes.** Default: the public `case`, `ctmpl`, `cqueue`, `cscm` and `cap`.
53. **Template versions.** Default: immutable versions pinned per case (the public templates have none).
54. **Statuses.** Default: the public categories `initial` (exactly one), `intermediate`, `end`, with a `pauses-sla` flag per status and a transition map per version (ours).
55. **SLA cap.** Default: 90 days for `sla-expires-in-seconds` (the API value; the Help Center says about 61).
56. **SLA date library.** Default: `temporal-polyfill` 1.0.5; 1.0.6 (now past the release-age window) only after its changes and licence are re-read.
57. **Holidays.** Default: no data ships; operators enter dates or import a CSV.
58. **SLA on reopen.** Default: continue the consumed time; a policy may restart.
59. **Calendar edits.** Default: recompute running deadlines, with a history row per case.
60. **`case.sla-expired`.** Default: once per deadline, only while unresolved, re-armed by a new SLA.
61. **Comments.** Default: plain text with mentions; append-only; the author or a `cases:redact` holder can redact a comment.
62. **Attachments.** Default: JPEG, PNG, WebP and PDF by content, 10 MiB each, 20 per case; the sealed original kept, renditions served.
63. **Renditions for existing inquiry images.** Default: yes, in the console through a new route, made asynchronously on their own vision lane with a "preparing" placeholder; the API's file route unchanged.
64. **Actors.** Default: OpenVerify ids in the API, emails only in the console; `assign` accepts both.
65. **Held objects.** Default: at most 1,000 per case (public), with their attached state and a live link.
66. **Scanning.** Default: `none`; `clamd` as an operator-run service; no ClamAV image or compose profile in the repo.
67. **Fewest-open assignment.** Default: approximate across queues.
68. **Reviewer role.** Default: no `cases:export` and no `cases:redact`; `developer` has `cases:redact` but not `cases:export`; `admin` has both; custom roles can add them.
69. **Exports.** Default: 24-hour files, at most 10,000 cases.
70. **Case layout.** Default: a fixed default layout with per-module permissions; the layout editor, checklists and the switcher rule in Phase 9.
71. **Teams and availability.** Default: deferred, with case analytics (Phase 9).
72. **Server PDF and fonts.** Default: Phase 9; the browser prints in Phase 8 and no font ships.
73. **React Flow attribution.** Default: kept visible.
74. **Case search consistency.** Default: read-after-write over Postgres (the public search is eventually consistent).

**Records**
75. **Correct the records.** Default: `integration-8a` corrects the surface index's events and spellings, PLAN §2, §3 and §6 (JSONLogic labelled ours), ADR 0001's queue note and the licence rows (C144); ADR 0007 is written in 8A Stage 1a.

**Added by the first review round** (`docs/plans/phase-8-review-resolution.md`)
76. **Workflow messages and case notifications from the worker** (F05, V27). Default: the worker binds `sms`, `slack` and the mailer for workflow messages and case notification emails; compose passes their credentials to the worker (a C63 amendment; Phase 6 decision 32's split holds for everything else).
77. **Simulated decisions** (F16). Default: webhook simulation and test-run steps that only simulate write no decision record.
78. **Run reads not for viewers** (P11). Default: `workflow-runs:read` is a scope of its own in `NOT_FOR_VIEWERS`, and run values are masked fail closed.
79. **Reopen event** (P06, H06). Default: `inquiry.reopened` (ours), with human review pending until a user decides; automated declines are refused meanwhile.
80. **Adverse actions** (P42, H08). Default: declines, block-list and face-list adds, adverse transaction statuses and account archiving are recorded and gated like declines.

## Phase 6 and 7 outcomes that amend this plan (2026-11-02; Phase 7 settled after Workflow 7C)

Phases 6 and 7 closed after this plan was written and reviewed. These outcomes are binding on Phase 8; where they contradict a contract above, they win. The record of each is in `docs/plans/phase-6.plan.md` (the owner decisions after Workflows 6A, 6B and 6C and the 6C and 6D amendment sections) and `docs/plans/phase-7.plan.md` (its "Phase 6 outcomes" section, the 7A to 7C amendment sections, "K7C-2", and the owner decisions after 7A and 7B; 7C has no such section).

1. **Test databases (Risks; F29).** `packages/db/src/testing.ts` keeps the migrated PGlite template on disk, named by a hash of the migrations (`migratedTemplatePath`, `MIGRATED_TEMPLATES_KEPT` 8, `OPENVERIFY_TEST_DB_CACHE_DIR`), so the Risks row's "copies a snapshot" is that cached template. Each Stage 1b measures its migration cold and warm under the e2e lock with the load average; the cache is the Risks row's fallback, and a hook over budget is never fixed by raising a timeout. Upgrade tests seed rows on the previous schema with `createTestInquiryOnEarlierSchema` and `createTestVerificationOnEarlierSchema`, which since Workflow 7A write by SQL naming only the old columns and return only ids. A column added to any table an earlier upgrade test inserts into through drizzle breaks that test, so it gets the same treatment (7A did this for `migration-v5`, `-0017` and `-0018`). To extend a Workflow's one migration: `pnpm --filter @openverify/core build` (drizzle-kit reads core from its dist), remove the file, its snapshot and its journal entry, regenerate, and put the hand edits back.

2. **CI (Risks; F08; owner decision 10 after Workflow 6C).** The "19 min 44 s of 20" figure is out of date. At `f8c488ad` CI has 13 jobs: `lint` (30 minutes; added after Workflow 7B in `f2cf099e`, because CI run 41257053492 ran out of heap linting contracts: `pnpm turbo run lint --concurrency=1` under `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=4096`, measured peaks api 3.35 GB and contracts 2.85 GB), `node` (20; typecheck with `--concurrency=2` under the same heap, then every package's unit tests but api's and dashboard's), `unit-api` as two blob shards (30 each), `unit-api-coverage` (20), `unit-dashboard` (20), `format` (10), `integration` (30), `infra` (10), `vision` (30), `android` (45), `android-instrumented` (60), `e2e` (70) and `secrets` (10). A run bills about 100 minutes, every job rounded up, and main is pushed once per Workflow by the orchestrator. `foundation-8a` reads the latest green run's times with read-only `gh`, changes only a job within 25% of its timeout, prefers a raised timeout or a re-balanced shard to a new job, validates with `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh`, and the after times are read at the push after the Workflow; no agent pushes, dispatches or re-runs CI. A CI e2e shard matrix is added only if the measured suite no longer fits the one CI e2e job. The SDK host-test floor is 1,333 after Workflow 7C (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:358`, with `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh`); an integration agent raises it when the count rises and never lowers it. `flow-headers --static-only` joined the infra job before the Phase 6 and 7A push (owner decision 10 after Workflow 7A). 7B added no job; the `lint` job came with its close. 7C added none either: the floor is the only change it made to `ci.yml` (four lines). The push of `2f276d9b` ran as CI 41334061210: 12 of 14 jobs green, counting `unit-api`'s two shards, with `lint` passing on its first run; only `unit-api` shard 1 failed (item 34), so `unit-api-coverage` was skipped. The latest green run is CI 41387332060 on `f8c488ad`, 14 of 14 jobs in 18 minutes, and no job is within 25% of its timeout: `unit-api` 16.8 and 15.0 minutes of 30, `e2e` 14.7 of 70, `node` 13.9 of 20, `integration` 11.7 of 30, `unit-dashboard` 11.2 of 20, `vision` 9.4 of 30, `android-instrumented` 9.3 of 60, `android` 7.6 of 45, `lint` 5.6 of 30, `format` 1.7 of 10, `unit-api-coverage` 0.7 of 20, `infra` and `secrets` 0.3 of 10 each. It bills about 125 minutes with every job rounded up.

3. **Validation (amends Validation; the Phase 7 plan's Stage 4 amendment 14).** In this order, each step exiting 0: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm format:check`; `pnpm turbo run lint typecheck --force`; `python3 scripts/fetch_models.py` and `python3 scripts/fetch_data.py`; `pnpm --filter @openverify/watchlist build:variant-groups` (the apps image checks its SHA-256, and the C78.7 gate runs its shipped case only with it); `pnpm test` (the other packages as one call with `--force`, the API suite as blob shards and a merge: four while other agents run and two for an agent alone, each coverage run in its own `--coverage.reportsDirectory`, item 24); `benchmark:typecheck` and `benchmark:test`; `pnpm test:integration`; the vision suite (ruff, format and mypy; then pytest in two halves, item 3a); the Android gate; `openapi:write` with `git diff --exit-code docs/api/openapi.json`; the e2e gate (item 4); `docker build -t openverify/apps:dev -f infra/docker/apps.Dockerfile .` (the tag is what `flow-headers.test.sh`'s live half uses), `docker build services/vision` and `docker build -f infra/docker/code-runner.Dockerfile .`; every `infra/tests/*.test.sh`; the workflow benchmark (reported, not gated, under the e2e lock); `gitleaks git --redact .`. Acceptance's "passes locally and on GitHub" means on GitHub at the push after the Workflow.

   3a. **The vision suite's two halves.** pytest in one call passed the 10-minute cap at 89% in Workflow 7A. `addopts` gates every run at 80%, so the first half turns the gate off and the second appends to its data: `timeout 590 uv run pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider --cov-fail-under=0 $(ls tests/test_*.py | awk 'NR<=58')`, then `timeout 590 uv run pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider --cov-append $(ls tests/test_*.py | awk 'NR>58')`, back to back with no other pytest between them. 7A: 1,458 tests in 7 min 2 s and 1,075 in 5 min 50 s at a load of about 10.5, 97.42% together. 7B: 1,466 and 1,081 in 4 min 2 s and 4 min 55 s, 97.43% (101 test files; its one new file sorts before the split point). Workflow 7C changed nothing under `services/vision`: the split stays at 58, and the orchestrator's validation of `f606062c` ran the 2,547 tests in one call in 8 min 50 s with nothing else running.

4. **e2e and timings (Rules; amends the "background allowed" of Rules and Validation).** Workflow 7A built the reusable stack (`e2e/README.md`, the Phase 7 Stage 4 amendment 7). `e2e:stack:up` takes no `OPENVERIFY_E2E_REUSE_STACK` and runs in the **foreground**: it returns once the stack is ready, in 1 to 8 minutes, under `flock -w 120` so that the wait for the lock stays out of the 10-minute cap. The gate is the mocked flow suite; `e2e:stack:down` and the turbo build; `e2e:stack:up`; for each shard `OPENVERIFY_E2E_REUSE_STACK=1 ... playwright test --shard=i/N`; `e2e:stack:down`, each its own step under `/tmp/openverify-e2e.lock`. N is the count in `e2e/README.md`: 3 after Workflow 7A, kept so each shard stays under 8 minutes. A run without the variable takes the compose project down first, so the two modes never mix in a Workflow. The stack's API and worker load the code at their start and it serves the builds made then, so a change under `apps/` or `packages/` needs a fresh stack; all agents share one working tree, so a stack another agent restarted is built from the same files, and a stack that went away between shards is not a test failure. CI keeps `pnpm e2e`, which starts its own stack. 7A's suite: 95 tests in 9.9 minutes in three shards, plus 1.1 for the stack start. 7B's suite: 99 tests in shards of 34, 32 and 33, in 2.1, 3.5 and 4.0 minutes plus 0.9 for the stack start; N stays 3 and CI keeps its one e2e job (owner decision 18 after Workflow 7A), with no shard matrix. In a full-stack spec an address the stack confirms or mails is at `.test` (Phase 7 Stage 4 amendment 10), and `e2e/tests/support/phase7b.ts` and `waitForMail` (`exceptIds`) are the helpers for a confirmed email, a list or a CSV. Workflow 7C's `fix-api` measured the suite with the UI specs on one fresh stack: 108 tests in 54 files, in shards of 36, 39 and 33, in 2.3, 4.9 and 4.1 minutes plus about a minute for the stack start. K7C-2 added the 109th, and 109 tests took 11.5 minutes in one process in the validation of `f606062c`. N stays 3 and CI keeps its one e2e job. What a stack holds is item 35. Every timing (benchmarks, the PGlite migration time, a suite's duration) is taken under the same lock with the load average; the engine benchmark PROGRESS records is `integration-8a`'s, taken with no other agent running.

5. **Test hygiene (Rules).** `POSTGRES_TEST_COMMAND` for every Testcontainers Postgres; `createdAtClock` with `onTestFinished(wallClockStepsBack())` for order tests; `cpuMilliseconds` for CPU budgets; hang guards of 60 s (120 s for PGlite hooks), never raised; `TEST_MAX_WORKERS` in integration configs.

6. **A redacted subject (owner decision 5 after Workflow 6C; C120.9, C127, C128.6, C128.7).** A redacted open inquiry is never moved to `pending`, `completed` or `failed`, but still expires and emits `inquiry.expired`, so an event-triggered run can reach it. Every action that creates or changes something on a redacted subject (approve, decline, mark for review, `run_report`, `rerun_report`, `run_verification`, adverse steps) ends with the step error `subject_redacted` (`resource-error`) and writes no decision record; `redact_object` on a redacted subject is `no_change`; `POST /inquiries/:id/reopen` and `POST /inquiries/:id/review-requests` answer 410 for a redacted inquiry. Tests go through the expiry of a redacted inquiry. Phase 7's signals path has no code or helper to align with: it writes no row (`createSignalComputationInTx`), or marks a row `skipped` (the sweep), or returns without moving anything (`processInquirySignals`, `on-signal`). Workflow 7B added no public code or helper either: its list screening ends a redacted inquiry's `list_screenings` row `errored` with `last_error_code` `inquiry_redacted` (`jobs/lists/screen.ts`) and its graph-index job skips one with the same code, so `subject_redacted` stands. `ENDED_BY_REDACTION` leaves `completed -> needs_review` unguarded, so `on-list-match` checks `redacted_at` under the inquiry lock (Phase 7 Stage 3 api-lists amendment 8), and so does every Phase 8 action that moves an inquiry there.

7. **The `workflow_run` actor (C121.1; owner decision 12 after Workflow 6B).** Every check and type pinning actor types gains it: `audit_actor_type`, `report_actions_actor_valid` (`wfr_` ids) and `ReportRedactionActor`, so a run's report redaction or report action names the run, never `system`. Widening `auditActorSchema` and the `Acting` actor fails no typecheck at the two-way ternaries that read them, so each becomes exhaustive, test-first, in `contracts-8a`'s first call: `createAuditLogger.record` (`apps/api/src/services/audit-log.ts`: anything but `api-key` is stored as `user`), `reportRedactionActorOf` (`services/redaction.ts`) and `actionActor` (`services/reports/reports.ts`: anything but `user` becomes `api_key`, which `report_actions_actor_valid` refuses). The console's `labelOf` (`components/operators/audit-actor.tsx`) reads such an entry "Workflow run", with its id and a link to the run page, never "User". Workflow 7A added no actor check (`network_syncs.actor` is a sync's origin). Workflow 7B added two more checks, which gain it the same way: `transaction_status_history_actor_valid` (`packages/db/src/tables/transactions.ts`: `system`, `usr_`, `key_`) and `list_items_created_by_type_valid` (`LIST_ITEM_CREATOR_TYPES` `user`, `api-key`, `importer`, served as the item's creator relationship by `creatorOf` in `services/lists/resources.ts`), and three actor reads that become exhaustive with the others: `actorOfHistory` (`services/transactions/transactions.ts`: anything but `user` becomes `api_key`), the bulk add-to-list creator (`services/graph/actions.ts`: anything but `api-key` becomes `user`) and the creator cast in `services/lists/biometric/items.ts`. Owner decision 5 after Workflow 7B says the same: once the request principal's actor widens, every binary actor ternary becomes an exhaustive switch first. Workflow 7C's K7A-8 changed none of them (Phase 7 review amendment 10). It gave `auditActorSchema` the member `{ type: "system", id: null }`, so `ServedAuditActor` is that union and `AuditActor`, the request principal, is `Exclude<ServedAuditActor, { type: 'system' }>`; `workflow-run` widens both, and `actorIdSchema` (`filter[actor-id]`) gains `wfr_`. `toUserAuditLogResource` now reads `servedActorOf(entry)` (`audit-log.ts:117`, defined at `:99`), an exhaustive switch over the stored `actor_type`, so the new enum value fails typecheck there and that reader gets its case; the 500 this item used to name is gone. A stored null `actor_id` reads as the system before the switch, so a run's entry always carries its `wfr_` id. The console's `labelOf` reads `system` and `api-key` by name and everything else as "User", which still typechecks, so its `Workflow run` wording stays test-first.

8. **The custom-list re-screen (C127.12).** The widened check is `report_runs_trigger_sync_with_list_update` and the key `report_runs_report_id_trigger_sync_id_key`. A watchlist `list_update` run keeps its `trigger_sync_id` and its page-wise read (Workflow 6D, C004); only custom-list reports get runs with `trigger_list_id` and `trigger_list_version`, and the custom-list job reads its lists through its own reads, never `WatchlistReads`. **`lists.version` already exists** (Workflow 7B: bumped by every item write and served as `list-version` on list matches), so 8A adds only the list purpose. The list write paths that stage `list.updated` are the callers of its two bump helpers: `bumpList` in `packages/db/src/repositories/list-items.ts` (`insertListItems`, `archiveListItem`, `rewriteListItemKeys`, `redactListItems`, the last also from `redactExpiredListItems`) and `touchList` with one direct update in `list-biometrics.ts` (`insertBiometricListItem`, `completeListItemFace`, `completeListItemBackground`, `archiveUnavailableBiometricItem`, `redactBiometricItems`, `redactBiometricLists`), plus list archiving (item 25). The graph bulk add-to-list writes through `ListService.createItem` once per value (`services/graph/actions.ts`), not through `ListItemWriter.insertItems` as this plan says; the importer writes through `insertItems` in batches and stages once per import. `ListService.createItem` opens its own transaction and takes a `lists-write` token per item, so 8A declares its InTx form.

9. **Outbound HTTP (C53 and C124 amendments).** `apps/api/src/http/stream.ts` (C85.1, `followableRedirect`: one redirect keeping scheme, host and port, owner decision 20 after Workflow 6B) serves the watchlist and network syncs through `OutboundHttp.streamClient` and is not refactored. Workflow 7A's only changes there are `STREAM_NO_CONTENT_TYPE`, which a network client names to take an untyped answer (TorDNSEL), and its header comment. The HTTPS action follows no redirect. There is no change signal (owner decision 10 after Workflow 6B); HTTP conditional requests are Phase 11's.

10. **The watchlist benchmark's imports are frozen.** `apps/api/src/watchlists/{sync,reconcile,manifest,matcher-data}.ts`, `apps/api/src/jobs/reports/{screening,rescreen}.ts`, `apps/api/src/jobs/report-types/{watchlist,business-watchlist,job}.ts`, `WatchlistSettings` in `config.ts`, `packages/db/src/{index,testing}.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/index.ts`, `reportConfigDefaultValues` from `@openverify/core`, and the watchlist package's `keys`, `record`, `match/shortcuts` and `testing` modules keep their names and signatures; `WatchlistReads` gains no member. Since Workflow 7A the watchlist sync runs on `apps/api/src/sync/runner.ts`, which the benchmark reaches through `sync.ts`. Whoever changes one of these or the runner runs `benchmark:typecheck` and `benchmark:test`. The list matches the same grep of the benchmark scripts at `f8c488ad`: since `ce772d10` only `node:module` left them, because `benchmark-postgres.mjs` now starts through `startIntegrationPostgres` (K7B-13). The frozen files changed additively only: the optional `ReportJobDeps.matcherData` in `job.ts` (item 34) and `keyFrequencyReader`, now exported from `rescreen.ts` (D001).

11. **The C78 matcher stays frozen (owner decision 9 after Workflow 6C).** Run Report and Get List Match pass its results unchanged (`KEYS_VERSION` `names-2`, `SCORER_VERSION` `scorer-2`); no C78.4 constant, threshold, preset or cap changes; `packages/watchlist/src/testing/held-out/` is never read. The three C78.7 and re-screen budgets missed in Phase 6 are carried to Phase 11 (C271), not Phase 8 work.

12. **Migrations.** Phase 6 ended at `0022_watchlist_changed_page_index`, Workflow 7A added `0023_phase7a` and Workflow 7B `0024_phase7b`. Workflow 7C added its one migration, `0025_phase7c` (the one-live-file index of K7B-5, owner decision 9 after Workflow 7B), and K7C-2 a second one after the Workflow by the orchestrator's decision, `0026_transaction_relations_page_index`. Phase 7 ends at `0026`, so 8A's one migration is `0027`, and each Phase 8 Workflow's numbers after the last at its base. Its PGlite upgrade test is `packages/db/src/migration-<number>.test.ts`, as `migration-0025.test.ts` and `migration-0026.test.ts` are.

13. **Decision 3 and C67.** `bullmq` is still 6.3.11 at `f8c488ad` (`apps/api/package.json:61`), so `contracts-8a` bumps it to 6.3.14 (with `cron-parser` 5.10.2). The C67 `nodemailer` item is done: 10.1.6 since `a973e6ea`, past the three advisories 10.1.4 fixed, with its pin test; its MIT-0 licence row awaits the owner (Q11). pypdf stays 6.19.0 and pypdfium2 5.14.0: pypdf cannot move before 2026-11-04T16:45Z and Workflow 8A launches first, so both are in 8C's `known` list, and the orchestrator bumps pypdf in the first gap between Workflows after the date, with R011 (`jsdom`'s exact pin). pypdfium2 waits for the first release with PDFium 8125 or later to be 14 days old, and for the owner on HarfBuzz's licence.

14. **Phase 7 facts.** Rate-limit policies carry their own window: C143.3's join `RATE_LIMIT_POLICY_WINDOWS` and `RATE_LIMIT_DEFAULTS` in `packages/contracts/src/rate-limits.ts`, and each needs a label in `POLICY_LABELS` (`apps/dashboard/src/components/integrations/rate-limit-panel.tsx`), whose `Record` fails typecheck without one. `REDACTION_SUBJECT_ID_PREFIXES` is now `inq`, `ver`, `iqse`, `acc`, `self`, `rep`, `net` and `dev`, and C143.5 adds Phase 8's. The prefixes `dev`, `net` and `hnd` and the token `ovh_` are taken (the Starting point omits `hnd`). An optional member is served whenever its record carries it (the flow session's `decision-contest-route` follows this). `identifierKey` is in `packages/db/src/blind-index.ts`. `PROVIDER_KINDS` ends at `ipReputation`. From Workflow 7B: the field-schema walker is `packages/core/src/transactions/` (`fieldsValidatorOf` builds plain closures and never a `z.object` from a stored schema, because zod compiles object keys into `new Function`; `transactionTypeVersionProblems`, `jsonSchemaKeywordProblems`, `isCardLike`), and a body nested past the stack answers 400 through `stackSafeValidatorCompiler`; the CSV writer is `packages/checks/src/csv/index.ts` (`writeCsv`, `neutraliseCell`, `stripNeutraliser`), and 8A's `readCsv` keeps the importer's parser rules (`apps/api/src/services/importers/read-file.ts`: two row-error codes, every other parser error refuses the file, the cap on records) by moving that parse step into `packages/checks/src/csv/read.ts`; `graph_index_jobs` is keyed `(environment, inquiry)` with `pending`, `completed`, `skipped` (`inquiry_redacted`, `graph_indexing_off`) and `errored` (`graph_index_failed`), and `graph_index_state` holds the incomplete kinds; `on-list-match` is in `jobs/lists/screen.ts`; `report/custom-list` is the eleventh `REPORT_TYPES` entry, review model `matches`, tiers R and H, an unscreened list ending the run errored, `list-ids` `null` on the seeded template (`report_lists_required`), so C127's per-type tables gain both `ip-address` and `custom-list`. Workflow 7B's ids (`lst`, thirteen `li*`, `lmt`, `mprt`, `txn`, `txntp`, `txntpv`, `txnl`, `txnf`, `grphq`, `grqt`, `grqtv`, `file`) do not collide with Phase 8's. Two `matchLists` exist: Get List Match calls C99.1's engine in `services/lists/match.ts`, not `POST /lists/match`'s on-demand service. The API's graph bulk-action file is `apps/api/src/services/graph/actions.ts` (`GRAPH_BULK_ACTIONS` in `packages/core/src/graph-queries.ts`, `graphQueryActionAttributesSchema` in contracts, whose test refuses `create-case` until 8B). The console's bulk action is the server action `graphBulkAction` in `apps/dashboard/src/app/graph/actions.ts`, with its form `components/graph/bulk-actions.tsx` and its words in `components/graph/graph-words.ts`; the explorer's injected-CSP test is `e2e/tests/ui-graph.spec.ts:199`, with `injectNonceCsp` in `e2e/tests/support/ui-graph.ts`. From Workflow 7C: `RESOURCE_TYPES` gained `transaction-status-change`, `transaction-relation` and `account-graph-component`, and Phase 8's join after them with no collision; `@openverify/node` serves a type's versions as a top-level group (`transactionTypeVersions`, `graphQueryTemplateVersions`), as `inquiryTemplateVersions` is, which is the pattern for Phase 8's version groups; `TRANSACTION_LIMITS.fileMaxBytes` is 20,000,000 bytes, provisional, and binds nothing here, because a trigger file input and an attachment stop at 10 MiB and the case subset refuses `file`.

15. **Reports (owner decision 16 after Workflow 6B; C127.2).** Report review and dismissal need `pii:view`; no run action reviews or dismisses a report, and the review recipe's dismissal is a user's. `ip-address` is the tenth `REPORT_TYPES` entry, of review model `none`: every layer walks that list, so C127's per-type tables gain it. A pending `ip-address` report's resource reads `flagged` false (Stage 4 amendment 12), so Run Report's `flagged` follows `has-match`'s rule: `null` unless `status` is `ready`.

16. **Carried, not Phase 8 work.** Phase 6's Phase 9 items (the "Other lists" e2e check, coverage words, reports in the inquiry document, run notes, a create-template form) and Phase 11 items (the budgets, write cost, JIT, media quotas, the UN opt-in, HTTP conditional requests). From Workflow 7A: C90.9's sandbox-outcome control on Android and the Android debugger facts no Phase 7 signal reads (Phase 10); `MmdbSnapshot.verify` on a real City Lite file (Phase 11); the notice texts at ADR 0006's defaults until the owner confirms the wording; 221 B of the collector's gzip headroom left, so a later collector version trims first. From Workflow 7B (owner decisions after it): expanding a property node in the explorer (Phase 9); the face and background list thresholds, the fuzzy-name candidate cap of 2,000, import throughput at low load, per-country address-key forms and a parser-backed PDF page count (Phase 11). From Phase 7's close: 14 of its 15 acceptance lines are ticked, and the Process line stays open until CI reads the `f8c488ad` push; R005 is unchanged, for the owner to confirm (an address with no AS record reads `low` at capped confidence 0.5); R007's dense scenarios and R008 to R010 are Phase 11's; R011 goes with the pypdf gap; the options of `RelationForm` printing the raw target and a redacted transaction's alert not saying that its relations stay are Phase 9's; the C100.6 CSV writer has two copies (`components/lists/csv.ts` and `packages/checks/src/csv/`), and a rule change lands in both. Owner items with defaults taken: which transaction actions need a confirmation, the console holding a download of up to 20 MB in memory, and `TRANSACTION_LIMITS.fileMaxBytes`. The C67 date items are the orchestrator's.

17. **Devices (C127.9, C91.2).** Read routes use `inquiry-sessions:read`, and `devices:write` is new. `rekeyDevice` (`packages/db/src/repositories/devices.ts`) merges a device into its twin under a new blind-index key and deletes the first row, so the survivor keeps a trust mark held by either, test-first. Redaction deletes a device left with no link, and retention deletes by `last_seen_at`, which clears the mark as C127.9 says. A randomised web session has a device of its own holding only the class.

18. **Consent keys (C42.3, C128.4).** Since Workflow 7A a consent on the default template records three required keys (`notice.session-integrity` joined, flow version 3), and `consent.device-recognition` is optional. `consent.automated-decision` lands in `contracts-8a`'s third call with its text in the flow's locales and in the Android SDK (`ConsentTexts.resources` and `optionalCheckboxes`, the string resources, `FlowCoordinator.Settings.consentTextKeys`; en and es), because the SDK offers an optional text only with a text and a checkbox label and shows Unsupported for a required key it does not know.

19. **A rejected handoff (C127.1, C96.3).** (Default, on the owner's review list.) `approve_inquiry` from a run answers `review_required` on an inquiry with a rejected handoff (`hasRejectedHandoffVerifications`), which joins C127.1's list beside `signal_unevaluated`: the person said the phone session was not theirs, and the cause is recorded only as the `handoff.review-requested` system audit entry, so no review cause on the inquiry would stop a run. Since K7C-1 a verification serves `from-rejected-handoff` and a session `handoff-rejected-at`; neither is PII, neither reaches the flow view, and both reach the trigger view as any attribute does.

20. **Signals in a run (C114.6).** (Default, on the owner's review list.) With `expose-signals` off (it defaults on) the server API and webhooks serve `[]` and `computed-at` alone. `contracts-8a`'s inventory states which view the trigger view and `fetch_object` read and records the choice; an empty array never reads as a false signal, and a value that leaves through an egress step follows C121.4.

21. **Providers (C126.6).** `PROCESS_PROVIDER_KINDS.api` is `sms`, `database`, `aamva` and `ipReputation` and stays so; `.worker` is every kind but `sms`, so it gains `sms`, and `slack` and `attachmentScanner` join `PROVIDER_KINDS` after `ipReputation`. `apps/api/src/providers/index.test.ts` and the positional slices of the phase5b, phase6 and phase7 provider tests change with the plan.

22. **The OpenAPI snapshot (amends "After the last checkpoint: the OpenAPI snapshot").** A checkpoint that registers frozen routes regenerates `docs/api/openapi.json` in the same commit, because `apps/api/src/openapi.test.ts` compares it with the committed snapshot (Workflow 7A's (S) checkpoint, amendment 9). In each Workflow, `foundation` registers its routes only after `contracts` commits a marker for its last schemas: `Stage 1a (X) egress checkpoint (contracts-8a)` and `Stage 1a (F) checkpoint (contracts-8b)`.

23. **What a gate means (Rules).** A gate passes only when its command exits 0, read from the exit code, never from a printed summary or through a pipe. Lint, typecheck and test run with `--force`, because a lint failure hid behind turbo's cache through two of Workflow 7A's checkpoints.

24. **Gate scheduling under parallel load (owner decision 16 after Workflow 7B; Rules).** While another agent's suites run, the API unit suite runs as four blob shards plus the merge, each coverage run with its own `--coverage.reportsDirectory`; an agent running alone keeps two. The forced lint may run per package with `--filter` when the whole does not fit one call, and the forced whole-repo gate still runs once at each stage's end. If lint runs out of heap locally, it runs as CI's `lint` job does (item 2), never with a rule turned off. 7B's numbers: 5,871 API tests in 433 files, shards of 4.4 and 3.9 minutes; `test:integration` db 69 and api 216 files; `graph-engine.integration.test.ts` up to 153 s of its 180 s guard under load (owner decision 17 after Workflow 7B: its fix is a template database, never a raised guard).

25. **List archiving re-screens (C127.12; ours, decided in the 7B refresh).** (Default, on the owner's review list.) Archiving a list bumps its version and stages `list.updated` (change kind `archived`), so the monitored custom-list reports that select it get one `list_update` run, which reads `list_archived` in `unscreened` and ends errored rather than keep an old "no match". A `PATCH` that changes no matching member (name, retention) bumps nothing. A pending face or background item stages nothing; its `list.updated` comes when the embed job makes it matchable.

26. **Sync Graph and Phase 7's job states (C127.5; decided in the 7B refresh).** (Default, on the owner's review list.) `sync_graph` reads the account's inquiries' `graph_index_jobs` and `graph_index_state` and reuses `kindsNotIndexedFor` (`services/graph/queries.ts`). A job `skipped` with `graph_indexing_off` reads as its kinds not indexed, never `synced: true`; a job `errored` with `graph_index_failed` ends the step with `graph_index_failed` (`resource-error`) under either `on-pending`, because waiting cannot change it.

27. **Graph-query rate limits in a run (C102, C107.3; decided in the 7B refresh).** (Default, on the owner's review list.) `graph-queries` is 30 a minute per environment, shared with every user and key, and a synchronous run takes one of two slots (429 `graph_sync_limited`). `run_graph_query` maps either 429 to a retryable rate-limit step error that honours `Retry-After`; it never fails the run outright or reads as a result.

28. **Redaction cascades compose (C105.5, C127, C139; owner decision 1 after Workflow 7B; Phase 7 review amendment 18).** Workflow 7C's K7B-1 makes account and inquiry redaction reach their linked transactions; 8A makes a transaction's redaction reach its children. As built, `redactInquiry` and `redactAccount` (`services/redaction.ts`) call `lockInquiryTransactions` or `lockAccountTransactions` (`packages/db/src/repositories/transaction-redaction.ts`, in id order) before the inquiry's or account's row, because `add-objects` holds a transaction's row while its foreign key share-locks the object's; `redactLinkedTransactionsInTx` takes each through `redactTransactionInTx` with `includeNever: false`; and `catchUpLinkedTransactions` reaches, after the commit, a transaction linked while the redaction ran. Phase 8's cascades follow its rules. Transactions lock before objects, so a child inquiry's own transactions are locked before that inquiry's row. Each pass redacts an object once. An object already redacted is `no_change`, with no second audit entry and no event. No cascade loops back to the object that started it, tested with an inquiry linked to a transaction whose run created another inquiry. A cascade's audit entry is a system entry naming what reached it (`inquiry-id`, `account-id`; 8A's children name `transaction-id`). A failed object delete in a cascade is left to the C16 purge and fails nothing. C105.5's "a transaction's own redaction never reaches its objects" holds for `transaction_objects`: `redactTransactionChildrenInTx` reaches only `transaction_children`, the objects a run created (C127.7). A child a run also attached sits in both, so the cascade back from it stops at `no_change`. (Default, on the owner's review list.) A case redacts only itself (C139.1), and C139.2 stands for a case that merely holds a redacted object; a case a run created is its transaction's child, so it goes with that transaction, whatever reached the transaction.

29. **Triggers and Phase 7 events (C118).** Workflow 7B's events are `list-match.created`, `transaction.created`, `.status-updated`, `.updated-fields`, `.labeled`, `.redacted`, `.added-relation`, `.removed-relation`, `transaction-type.deleted-field`, `.converted-field` and `graph-query.submitted`, `.completed`, `.errored`, `.cancelled`, `.timed-out`, `.reran`; tag and object changes raise none. Trigger filters use the webhook filter subjects (`EventSubject` in `packages/db/src/repositories/webhooks.ts`): a transaction passes a filter that allows any one of its linked templates or account types, and a graph-query or transaction-type event fails any present filter. The `list` subject joins the contracts `EventSubject` union and its `SUBJECTS` map.

30. **The graph "create case" action (C140.6).** `create-case` joins `GRAPH_BULK_ACTIONS`, `graphQueryActionAttributesSchema` and the `authorize` and action switches of `services/graph/actions.ts`, at most `GRAPH_LIMITS.bulkAccounts` (500), with an `Idempotency-Key` on every non-preview call. The console calls it through `@openverify/node` (owner decision 21 after Workflow 7B): `graphBulkAction` already posts every action but `export` through `api.graphQueries.action`, and it links its own `/graph/<id>` route, because `explorer-url` is nullable since K7B-11 (decision 13). Adding `create-case` to `GRAPH_BULK_ACTIONS` fails the dashboard's typecheck at `ACTION_PERMISSIONS` and `membersOf` (`app/graph/actions.ts`), `questionOf` (`components/graph/bulk-actions.tsx`) and `GRAPH_ACTION_WORDS` (`components/graph/graph-words.ts`), so 8B's commit that adds it carries that dashboard minimum; the `abilities` prop stays as it is, so the action is not offered until 8C's button, which owns those files for that change only, with `abilities` through `components/graph/graph-explorer.tsx` and `app/graph/[id]/page.tsx`. C140.6's "the explorer's bulk-action file" is those files.

31. **Case modules read Phase 7's routes (C142.2).** The graph module reads `GET /accounts/:id/graph-component` (`graph:read`; K7B-2, `accounts.graphComponent`): the resource `account-graph-component`, whose id is the account's and whose `state` is `computed`, `not-computed` or `disabled`; a redacted account reads `not-computed`, because its row is gone (Phase 7 review amendment 11). The transaction module reads `GET /transactions/:id/status-changes` and `/labels` (`transactions:read`; K7B-4, `transactions.statusChanges`, `.labels`): a `transaction-status-change` has the row's number for an id, and `page[after]` takes that number (amendment 12); after redaction a status change keeps its row with its free text `null`, and a label is left out. List matches come from `GET /list-matches?filter[inquiry-id]=` or `filter[account-id]=`.

32. **The transaction module reads relations (K7C-2; C142.2, C127.7).** (Default, on the owner's review list.) C142.2 did not say; it does. `GET /transactions/:id/relations` (`transactions:read`, `transactions.relations`), newest first, serves `transaction-relation`: `relation-schema-key`, `target` and `created-at`, ids only, never a target's attributes. Each target links through its own route and scope ("no access" otherwise). A redacted transaction serves its relations as they were, and the module says so. `page[after]` is a position (`<created-at>~<key>~<target>`), so a removed row breaks no cursor. The table holds no creator, and neither does `transactions`, so this plan's "workflow-run creators on list items, transactions and relations" means list items only: a run's `set_relation`, `unset_relation` or `create_transaction` is recorded by the change's audit entry with the run as its actor, and no column is added. (Default, on the owner's review list.)

33. **Relation and object errors (K7B-6; C127.7).** `relation_cardinality_exceeded` (422, `/meta/relation-schema-key`) and `related_objects_limit` (422, `/meta/object-ids`) are in `TRANSACTION_PROBLEM_STATUSES`. `related_objects_limit` stays the step error C127.7 names, and `set_relation` on a second target of a cardinality-one relation ends with `relation_cardinality_exceeded` as a step error, which no retry can change. (Default, on the owner's review list.)

34. **No unit test reads a fetched data file (Phase 7's close).** CI run 41334061210 failed `custom-list.test.ts` because the job reached `loadMatcherData()`, which reads the generated `data/variant-groups.json` that a clean checkout does not have; the hidden-data sweep then passed all 15 unit suites in a tracked-files-only worktree. `ReportJobDeps.matcherData` (optional, `apps/api/src/jobs/report-types/job.ts`) and `matchLists`' `options.matcherData` (`services/lists/match.ts`) inject it, and the worker gives none, so it still loads the file. A Phase 8 test that reaches `matchLists` or a list-screening job (`get_list_match`, `run_report` on a custom list, the list re-screen) injects a synthetic matcher.

35. **What a stack holds (Phase 7 Stage 1 amendments; Rules).** A used stack holds one clean pass: `otp-ip-hour` allows 10 sends an hour per environment and address, every spec comes from `127.0.0.1`, and one full pass sent 9 in the validation of `f606062c` (8 earlier in Workflow 7C), so a repeated shard can fail with a 429 or a mail that never arrives, and a repeat runs on a fresh stack. A stack down is never queued behind another agent's stack up (`dashboard-signals` amendment 16): an agent that finishes takes down only the stack it started. A spec reads a seeded object by id, never from a list's first page, because the specs share one stack (`operators.spec.ts`, `011c53ab`). So a spec that adds an OTP send presents an address of its own (`presentAddress` in `e2e/tests/support/network.ts`, or `withForwardedFor` for a request outside a browser), because `otp-ip-hour` keys on the client address and the stack trusts its loopback proxy. `127.0.0.1`'s budget stays where it is, and the limit itself is never overridden for a test. Each integration agent re-counts the sends of a full pass from `127.0.0.1` and reports them; at 10, it moves a spec to an address of its own.

36. **The console's patterns (Phase 7 Stage 1 and review amendments; C141, C142).** A page slot is awaited as a function through `loadSection`, never rendered as JSX: a thrown 403 gives the section's forbidden panel and `null` leaves it out (`SlotSection` in `components/lifecycle/optional-section.tsx`). A route handler that takes a form post checks its origin as `app/graph/[id]/export/route.ts` does: a real `Origin` must match the host, and `Origin: null` is accepted only with `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin` or with that header absent. Graph exports and transaction files are read whole by the dashboard (amendment 29); that holds for those two only, and the upload route and the downloads Phase 8 builds stay as this plan says. (Default, on the owner's review list.) A list with its own cursor uses `Pagination`'s `param` and `label`. Version pages follow "New draft" or "Open the latest draft", and "Version history" (amendments 26 and 27). The modules reuse `DbIpAttribution`, `ListMatches`, `AccountGraphComponent`, `TransactionHistory` and `TransactionRelations`. `nonceCsp` in `e2e/tests/support/ui-graph.ts` has no `style-src`, so the editor's CSP test adds its own. `saveSettings` compares by value since Workflow 7C (`sameValue`), so C143.7's arrays are sent only when they changed. The navigation holds 21 links at `f8c488ad`, 27 after 8C's first slice.

37. **Console links (Phase 7 review amendment 15; C130.2).** `ApiDependencies.dashboardPublicUrl` and `WorkerGraphOptions.dashboardPublicUrl` are undefined in production without `OPENVERIFY_DASHBOARD_PUBLIC_URL`, and `explorer-url` is then `null`. A console link Phase 8 builds, such as `notify-on-create`'s email, reads the same value and never falls back to localhost; with no URL the message goes without the link. (Default, on the owner's review list.)

## Workflow 8A Stage 1a amendments (contracts-8a)

Recorded by `contracts-8a` on 2026-11-01 at base `0d27c8f5`. Items 1 to 12 are the re-verification, done before any Phase 8 code; the later items are the decisions of the Stage 1a commits. The inventory is `docs/research/phase8-inventory.md`, and the evidence `docs/tdd/phase8-contracts-8a.tdd.md`. Each item binds every later 8A agent.

### Re-verification of the Starting point and the Phase 6 and 7 interfaces

1. **The Starting point holds, with these corrections.** There are 47 permissions (38 API scopes and 9 operator permissions), not the 36 the Starting point and C112.5 name. `events.stage(` has 26 call sites in 18 files, not the 17 of C121.3; the context read inside `stage()` makes the count immaterial. The one direct `createEvent` is `apps/api/src/services/webhooks.ts:424`.
2. **The migrator runs every pending migration in one transaction, so `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` cannot be followed by a use of the new label in the same migration on an upgraded database** ("unsafe use of new value"), although it passes on a fresh one, where the type was created in that transaction. A CHECK that names a new label compares `<column>::text`. `foundation-8a`'s `report_origin` value hits the same rule: nothing in `0027` may use `workflow` as a `report_origin` literal. Each regeneration of `0027` keeps the enum lines first and is proven by the PGlite upgrade test `migration-0027.test.ts`, which runs from `0026`.
3. **The actor is pinned in places the plan does not name.** `reportActionResourceSchema.actor` (`packages/contracts/src/reports.ts:1273`) would throw at `GET /reports/:id/history` for a run's action, so it gains `wfr_`. The console's `Actor` in `components/reports/report-history.tsx` read anything but `key_` as "User", which no typecheck catches, and its audit log filter refused a `wfr_` id, the link every run entry carries; both changed test-first. The ignored-match `user` and the review event's `reviewer` stay `usr_` or `key_`, because no run reviews or dismisses a report (outcome 15).
4. **`Principal` does not widen.** A run has no `Principal`, and every `principal.kind` ternary reads a request. `AuditActor` and `Acting.actor` widen; `actorOf(principal)` stays two-way by type.
5. **`InTx` forms.** Of the C122.2 actions only `applyInquiryAction`, `matchLists`, `redactReportInTx`, `redactTransactionInTx` and `ListItemWriter.insertItems` take the caller's transaction; the inventory lists the rest for `foundation-8a`. Three are not extractions: `run_verification` has no service at all; `createGraphQuery` reads a `Principal` through `bindGraphQuery`, so its form takes `canViewPii`; and `redactInquiry` and `redactAccount` commit in stages with storage deletes after, so they cannot share a transaction with an effect row.
6. **`redact_object` on an inquiry or an account (C117.5, C127.8).** Default, on the owner's review list. Its effect row is written `claimed` before the service runs and `done` after; the service is idempotent on `redacted_at`, so a repeated attempt reads `no_change`. This is the one internal effect not written in one transaction with its change.
7. **`lists.version`, the Phase 7 list write paths and their callers** are as outcome 8 says; the inventory's section 6 is the table `foundation-8a` hooks from. `archiveList` and `updateList` bump nothing today.
8. **The verification types with no end-user capture** are `database`, `aamva` (form source only; a silent step answers `verification_created_by_server`), `database_business` and `business_website`. Each reads its config from the inquiry's pinned version, so `run_verification` needs that type's step in the pinned flow, and its values come from the step config.
9. **The flow's terminal screen** for C128.7 is `apps/flow/src/steps/outcome-screens.tsx`, chosen by `terminalPosition` in `apps/flow/src/flow/flow-position.ts`, which today shows the completion screen for a declined inquiry and "never reveals the decision". The contest route is the first thing the flow shows for `declined`.
10. **Signals in a run (outcome 20).** The trigger view and `fetch_object` read the server API's view, `signalViewOf`, never `CONSOLE_SIGNAL_VIEW`: a trigger snapshot comes from the event payload, which already holds that view, and a run's values can leave through an egress step, so a run sees no more than an API client does. An empty array never reads as a false signal: the trigger view flattens `signals` into one member per signal the registry names, each `null` when the array does not hold it, so every signal path is nullable and C114.6's `on-null` applies. Publish warns when a version reads a signal path in an environment with `expose-signals` off. (Default, on the owner's review list.)
11. **The nodemailer pin** is 10.1.6, past the three advisories, so no bump. `bullmq` moves to 6.3.14 with `cron-parser` 5.10.2 (outcome 13).
12. **Unchanged, as the plan says:** the stream client, the guard, the event publisher's files and its three construction sites, the report checks of C127.12, `ENDED_BY_REDACTION`, the report service's 410s, the rate-limit windows, the cached PGlite template and the CI jobs.

### Decisions of the Stage 1a commits

13. **Every actor read goes through `storedActorOf` (`apps/api/src/services/audit-log.ts`),** one exhaustive switch, so a new actor fails typecheck. The four ternaries of outcome 7 call it, and the list creators pass `acting.actor.type` straight through, typed by `ListItemCreatorType`, so an actor that is no creator type fails typecheck instead of reading as another kind. The console's link from a run's audit entry to its run page is `dashboard-workflows`', because the page does not exist yet; until then the entry shows "Workflow run" with its id.
14. **`workflow-run` joins `RESOURCE_TYPES` in Common, not (E),** because a list item's creator needs it as a resource identifier. The rest of the workflow resource types land in (E).
15. **`devices:write` goes to `developer` and `reviewer` as well as `admin`** (default, on the owner's review list). C127.9 names the scope and no holders. A developer publishes the workflow that holds a Trust Device step and must hold its grant (C121.2); trusting a device is a reversible, audited review outcome, as `accounts:write` is for reviewers. The area of each new permission: `workflows` for the seven workflow and run scopes, `secrets` for the two secret scopes, and `inquiry-sessions` for `devices:write`, beside the read scope devices use.
16. **A CHECK's run clause reads `actor_id is not null` first.** A check that compares `actor_id ~ ...` passes on a null id, because it answers null, so `workflow_run` with no id would have been accepted. The `user` and `api_key` clauses of `report_actions_actor_valid` and `transaction_status_history_actor_valid` have that gap today and keep it: no code path writes such a row, and tightening them is outside this slice (Phase 9 or a later fix).
17. **`0027_phase8a` is one migration from Common on,** holding the enum value and the three widened checks. `foundation-8a` extends the same file: `pnpm --filter @openverify/core build`, remove `0027_phase8a.sql`, `meta/0027_snapshot.json` and journal entry 27, `drizzle-kit generate --name phase8a`, add the trailing newline back to `_journal.json`, and run prettier on `meta/0027_snapshot.json`. No hand edit is needed yet. drizzle-kit writes the enum line first; keep it first.
18. **The problem error member gains an optional `code`** and takes `location: definition`, because C113.6's `errors[] {pointer, code, detail}` meets `problemValidationErrorSchema`, which is strict and requires `location`. Statuses the plan does not state, ours: `draft_missing` 409, because a publish without a draft is a state conflict and the workflow exists; `version_locked`, `draft_exists`, `rollout_in_progress`, `workflow_not_running`, `run_not_resumable`, `run_not_cancelable` and `human_review_pending` 409; `publish_grant_missing` and `test_runs_sandbox_only` 403; the others 422. C120.3's "naming the step" is `blocked-by` and `step-id` on `run_not_resumable`, because `problemSchema.reason` is typed to the flow's session reasons; `publish_grant_missing` carries `missing-scopes`, and `trigger_input_invalid` `source.pointer`. The API's error class for them is `foundation-8a`'s.
19. **`readCsv(bytes, options)` decodes too.** It holds the decoding, the `csv-parse` configuration and everything `readImportFile` decided, so `apps/api` has no `csv-parse` dependency any more and `services/importers/read-file.ts` and `encoding.ts` are thin callers, with their tests unchanged. `options`: `encoding` (UTF-8 by default), `maxRows`, `maxRecordCharacters`, `headerProblems`. `decodeCsv` and `resolveCsvEncoding` are exported beside it.
20. **The log rule covers Phase 8.** `PHASE5_FOLDERS` gains `packages/workflow-engine/{src,bin}`, `apps/api/src/workflows`, `apps/api/src/jobs/workflows`, `apps/api/src/services/{decisions,workflow-secrets}` and the workflow route files, with the regexp style rules, and `REFUSED_LOG_KEY_PATTERN` gains `variables`, `outputs`, `triggerSnapshot`, `templateOutput`, `secret`, `body` and `comment`.
21. **C113.4's 16 KiB cap on a node's `config` contradicts three larger caps:** Evaluate Code's `source` (64 KiB, C123.2), the HTTPS `body` (256 KiB, C124.1) and a message's HTML (64 KiB, C126.1). `engine-logic` measures the 16 KiB without those members, each of which has its own cap, and the whole definition's 256 KiB (C113.4) still bounds the sum.
22. **Evaluate Code's output is `{ value, source-sha256 }`,** one member beyond C123.2's `{ value }`, because C123.8 says the step records the digest and C128.1 reads it. A failed evaluation's sealed output is `{ error: { code, message }, source-sha256 }`.
23. **`evaluateCodeValueFits` is contracts-8a's,** so the runner and the action share one rule: `object` takes a JSON object or an array, as `typeof` does in the guest; `none` takes only `null`, which is what a function that returns nothing gives; a number must be finite.
24. **A step's `limits` take `time-ms` and `memory-bytes` up to the install ceilings** (5,000 ms and 64 MiB). C123.5's "can only be lowered under an install ceiling" reads as "never past the ceiling"; every other limit is fixed. `timeout` and `sandbox_crashed` follow the step's `retry`, because a wall-clock timeout can be load and a crash is infrastructure; the other six are `EVALUATE_CODE_DETERMINISTIC_ERRORS` (C117.6).
25. **The frames.** A response frame is capped at 65 KiB, so the guest's JSON travels as a JSON value, never as a string whose escapes would inflate it past C123.3's cap. A console line is a frame of its own, for the same reason. A request's cap allows every character of the source and input escaped: `6 × (64 KiB + 256 KiB) + 1 KiB`. A request carries `environment-id` for the socket form's pool; the child never reads it.
26. **`json-logic-js` ships no types,** and no `@types` package was added. `engine-logic` declares the module locally for its differential test.
27. **Existing tests changed with the plan,** none weakened: `API_SCOPES` and the three role lists in `permissions.test.ts`, `vocabulary.test.ts`, `role.json` and `role-options.test.ts`; `AUDIT_ACTOR_TYPES`; `RESOURCE_TYPES`; the permission areas; `lists-dependencies.test.ts`, because `csv-parse` moved; the audit log filter's message.

### Decisions of the (E) and (A) registry commits (second call)

28. **The definition schema lives in `packages/contracts/src/workflow-definition.ts`, and the vocabularies in `packages/core/src/workflows/`.** The definition names event names and ids, which are contracts', and contracts cannot read `packages/workflow-engine`. `packages/db` reads core alone, so every list a table pins is in core. This replaces the first call's guess of `packages/core/src/workflows/` for the schema. `engine-logic` imports the schema from `@openverify/contracts`; `definition/` holds what it owns, C113.5 to C113.8.
29. **C113.1's shape, where the plan left it open.** A node's members sit on the step, and only an action has a `config`. `trigger` is nullable, because a draft has none and a module has `module-input`. `join` and `array-map-output` are node types, each naming its partner (`join-of`, `map-of`). The `output` node's id is the literal `output`. An edge has an optional `to-port: continue` for a loop's back-edge. `allow-self-trigger` and `one-run-per-subject` sit on the trigger object, beside `type` and `config`, because C113.1's top level is closed and what they change is how a run starts.
30. **The schema holds what needs no registry.** Repeated step ids, edge ids and declared names are refused at their pointer, and the 256 KiB cap is measured in UTF-8 bytes, in `superRefine`. Everything else in C113.5 is `engine-logic`'s, and the 16 KiB `config` cap stays there (amendment 21). `isTriggerableEvent` refuses `workflow-run.*`, `workflow.published` and `workflow-version.published` in a trigger (C118.8); `workflow.paused` and `list.updated` can start a run.
31. **C118.6's "verification-template filter" is `verification-types`.** OpenVerify has no verification template. The filter lists `verification/<type>` resource types. (Default, on the owner's review list.)
32. **Attributes beyond C112.3 and C112.4.** A workflow has `paused-reason` (`operator` or `publisher_revoked`), because the banner of C121.5 has to say why. A version has `lock-version` (C116.1), `created-at` and `updated-at`. In an event a version's `definition` is `null`, because it can be 256 KiB and `workflows:read` is what reads it; so `definition` is nullable and `carriesNoValue` holds it to that.
33. **Attributes beyond C120.1.** A run has `errored-step` (C120.2), `updated-at`, `inputs`, `variables`, `trigger-snapshot` (C120.6 masks them, so they are served), `overridden-steps` (C120.7), `redacted-at` and `values-purged-at`, so a purged `null` never reads as a masked one, and the relationship `rerun-of` (C120.4). `test-run-id` is the run's own `wfr_` id, because a test run is a run and C112.1 gives it no prefix. The public `meta.processing-time-seconds` is a member of the resource, so the run has a `meta`.
34. **A run's creator.** `null` for a recurring tick, which nobody started: `trigger-type` says what did. `case-action` joins with its `cact_` id in `contracts-8b`'s common commits, so C120.1's union is one member short until then. `scheduled-run` is in `RESOURCE_TYPES` as an identifier; there is no route for it.
35. **Names the collision test moved.** A PII name is masked by name at every exit, so the same name can never be a plain attribute elsewhere. `workflow-version.inputs` and `.outputs` (public names, holding declarations) and `workflow-action.reason` (C122.5's name, a closed code on a route that masks nothing) are recorded exemptions: their routes never reach `mask.attributes()`, and a stored API log masks them, which fails closed on a list of names. Everything else was renamed: a delivery has `reason-code`; a diff has `trigger-changes`, `input-changes`, `output-changes`, `variable-changes` and `layout-changes`; a test run takes `trigger-payload`, because `payload` is an event's attribute; the available-actions form is `form`, because `parameters` is PII on the run body.
36. **`PII_WORKFLOW_RUN_ATTRIBUTES` leaves out `data`.** A trigger file value's `data` is PII and marked, but it sits inside `fields`, and `data` names every JSON:API body, so listing it would mask every stored log whole. `object-ids` and `csv` are listed, as C143.5 asks, though ids are not personal data.
37. **The events.** Each new event has its own golden fixture, which is also its webhook sample, so `samples.ts` needs no new branch; the four new subjects' rows in `RESOURCE_FIXTURES` and `STATUS_BY_EVENT` exist because those are `Record`s over `EventSubject`. `meta.context` is required on every run event and `{}` where it has nothing, as the public `context` is; it holds `errored-step` and `error` on `.errored` and `output-names` on `.completed`. Our run events carry no `included`: the relationships name the ids, and C112.7 asks for no more. `list.updated` carries a `list/<kind>` with `meta { change-kind, counts { items-changed }, list-version }`. `change-kind` is `items-added`, `items-archived`, `items-rewritten`, `items-redacted` or `archived`, one per write path of the inventory's section 6, and `archived` is outcome 25's. `redactedResourceStubSchema` takes `workflow-run`, because `stubEventPayloads` covers the run ids (C120.9). `inquiry.reopened` is registered and nothing emits it until the third call adds the edge.
38. **The webhook filter keys are in the contract, and `foundation-8a` stores them.** `webhookFiltersSchema` takes `workflow-ids` and `list-ids` (C143.4). `storedFilters` in `apps/api/src/services/webhooks.ts` keeps only the two old keys, so until `foundation-8a` extends it, `WebhookFilters` and `subjectFilter`, a filter sent with a new key is dropped. Nothing is released before then.
39. **`@openverify/node`.** The five groups the bullet names hold everything: the catalogue is `workflows.actions`, `.recipes` and `.installRecipe`; the deliveries are `workflowRuns.deliveries`; and the account routes are `accountActions.run` and `.available`. `PUT` joined `HttpMethod` and the JSON body rule, for `workflows.saveDraft`.
40. **Answers the plan leaves open.** `POST /workflows/:id/draft/validate` answers `{ meta: { valid, errors[], warnings[] } }`; its body is optional and takes any JSON as `definition`, so a bad shape comes back with its pointers instead of a 400. `POST /workflows/:id/manual-runs` answers `{ meta: { workflow-run-ids[], refused[] { object-id, row, code } } }`, and `POST /workflows/:id/cancel-runs` `{ meta: { canceled-count } }`. `DELETE /workflow-runs/:id` answers the run. An account action has `status` (`active`, `archived`), because the plan gives it no delete route. `prefill` maps an input to a path, never a value; the values are `form[].prefilled-value`, under the viewer's mask (P38). `GET /workflow-actions` and `GET /workflow-recipes` have no pagination, so `links.next` is `null`. A catalogue row's id is `<key>@<version>` and a recipe's its key, because neither has a stored id.
41. **Any JSON value is one schema, `workflowJsonSchema`,** named `JsonValue` in the OpenAPI document. `z.json()` is recursive, and with no name zod emitted `#/components/schemas/__shared#/$defs/schema0`, which is no valid reference. Every Phase 8 contract that holds JSON uses this constant, never `z.json()` again, or the `__shared` member comes back.
42. **The registry contract (C122.1) adds to the plan's fields.** `grantDependsOnConfig`, so the catalogue can say so; `adverseRule` (`list-purpose`, `transaction-adverse-status`, `account-archive`), set exactly when `adverse` is `by-config`, because the config alone cannot answer and the rule names what must be read; `biometric` (C128.4, `add_face_list_item`); `irreversible` (C116.4, `redact_object`); and `causeChecked` (C121.3). `ACTION_ADAPTERS` (`none`, `mailer`, `sms`, `slack`, `code-runner`) and `ACTION_REQUIREMENTS` (`inquiry-in-scope`, `not-in-loop`) close `availability` and `requires`.
43. **An output member's PII mark is required, and `createActionRegistry` throws without it.** Each top-level member of an action's output carries `.meta({ pii: true })` or `.meta({ pii: false })`. A member PII in one variant of a union is PII in all. This is what makes C120.6 fail closed for a new action: an action cannot be registered without saying. The contracts' convention, where an unmarked member is plain, does not apply to outputs.
44. **The tables are data, and Workflow 8B extends them by passing rows.** `createActionRegistry(entries)` and `createActionObjectTypeTable(entries)` take arrays, so 8B adds its rows in its own files (F33). The eight case keys are reserved in `RESERVED_CASE_ACTION_KEYS`. `ACTION_REPORT_TYPE_TABLE` walks `REPORT_TYPES`, so `ip-address` and `custom-list` are in it, and a new type joins by itself. In the object-type table a verification is read-only, with no tags (the inventory) and no redaction of its own, and a report's `update_object` sets tags and custom fields only.
45. **Existing tests changed with the plan,** none weakened: `vocabulary.test.ts` (the event names and groups, and `RESOURCE_TYPES`), `pii-collisions.test.ts` (the list and the two workflow exemptions, each with a test that it is still needed), the dashboard's `events.test.ts` (three groups) and the node group list in `resources.test.ts`.

### Decisions of the (A) actions and (X) egress commits (third call)

46. **The reopen edge is held apart from `INQUIRY_TRANSITIONS`.** `INQUIRY_REOPEN_TRANSITIONS` (`packages/core/src/inquiry-state-machine.ts`) holds `declined: ['needs_review']`, and a caller takes it only by naming it: `canTransitionInquiry(from, to, { reopen: true })` and `transitionInquiry(from, to, { reopen: true })`, which answers `inquiry.reopened`. In the ordinary table the edge would have opened `POST /inquiries/:id/mark-for-review`, the sandbox simulation, the review rules and the console's action list, so a new caller fails closed instead. `isTerminalInquiryStatus('declined')` stays true and `allowedInquiryTransitions('declined')` stays empty. The exact change for `foundation-8a`'s `reopen` action: `InquiryTransition` in `packages/db/src/repositories/inquiries.ts` gains `reopen?: boolean`, passed to `canTransitionInquiry`; `transitionInquiryInTx` takes the same option and passes it to `transitionInquiry`, and `applyInquiryAction` audits the name it returns. `STATUS_TIMESTAMP` has no `needs_review` entry, so a reopened inquiry keeps `decisioned-at` from the decline until a user decides; this is history, not a bug. A redacted inquiry answers 410 (outcome 6), and `ENDED_BY_REDACTION` does not cover this move, so the reopen checks `redacted_at` under the row lock.
47. **The record is the resource `decision`** (`packages/contracts/src/decisions.ts`, `idec_`, in `RESOURCE_TYPES`), which imports no resource, so inquiries, events, the types and the flow can all read it. Its `kind` is `approve`, `decline`, `review`, `reopen` or `adverse`; the plan's "kind `review`" and the reopen's comment are those rows. Members beyond C128.1: `run` (the workflow, version, node, `routes[]`, `condition-sha256[]`, `code-sha256[]`, `null` unless a run made it), `needs-review` and `error-code` (C128.3), and `human-review-pending` (C128.6). The subject is `relationships.subject.data`: an inquiry, a transaction, an account or a `list-item/<kind>`. `actor` is `user`, `api-key` or `workflow-run`, never the system, so a retention or simulation path can never write one. A `human-step` and `solely-automated: true` on one record are refused by the schema. Evidence is `{ kind, id, name, status, tier, stage, confidence }`, with a code pattern on `name` and `status`, so a person's name cannot go in; `tier` takes `SIGNAL_TIERS`, which holds every combined tier.
48. **`meta.comment` joins `meta.solely-automated`.** C128.1 gives every approve, decline and review a sealed comment and C143.5 masks `meta.comment` of decisions, so the approve, decline and mark-for-review bodies take an optional `comment` (`decisionCommentSchema`, at most 2,000 characters, a provisional cap), and approve and decline take `solely-automated`. A mark-for-review declares nothing, because it is no decision on the person. `foundation-8a` passes both to `recordInquiryDecisionInTx`; until it does, the routes parse them and store nothing, and nothing is released before then.
49. **The contest route** is `{ url?, contact?, case-template-id? }` (`contestRouteSchema`, at most 200 characters of contact, no control character), and a case template alone is refused by the schema. `ctmpl` joins the prefix tables in 8B, so the member is a local pattern until then. Publish still checks the URL's host against `workflow-link-hosts` and refuses a case template until 8B lands (C128.7). The route is the operator's text, never the subject's, so it is plain and may go in `meta.decision`.
50. **`decision-contest-route` on the flow session** is `{ url, host, contact }` (`flowDecisionContestRouteSchema`), with `host` held to `new URL(url).host`, so the flow shows the host before the link and the operator's case template never reaches the person. It is optional and nullable, as every flow member added since Phase 2 is. The new fixture `api/flow-session-declined.json` has an Android decoder: the SDK reads around the member, and it does not show it, because C128.7 names the hosted flow.
51. **Trust Device.** `POST /api/v1/devices/:id/trust` (`devices:write`) takes `meta { trust-reason }` or `meta { untrust: true }`, exactly one (`trustDeviceBodySchema`). The route path is ours, because C127.9 names none. `trusted-by` is `{ type, id }` of a user, an API key or a run. `trust-reason` is a snake_case code, never free text. The schema refuses a half mark, so an untrust clears all three.
52. **Attributes served before their columns exist.** `purpose`, the three trust members, `adverse-statuses` and both `contest-route`s are required on the resources, and each builder's record member is optional with the default: `block`, untrusted, `['declined']`, `null`. So the resources serve the side that fails closed until `foundation-8a`'s (A) columns land, and then it passes the values: `buildListResource({ purpose })`, `buildDeviceResource({ trustedAt, trustedBy, trustReason })`, `buildTransactionTypeResource({ adverseStatuses, contestRoute })`, `buildAccountTypeResource({ contestRoute })`. Until then a write of those members is parsed and dropped. `PATCH /transaction-types/:id` no longer needs a `name` (`updateTransactionType` answers the type unchanged without one), because `adverse-statuses` and `contest-route` are the type's, not a version's. `adverse-statuses` is held to the type's statuses by the service, because the statuses are a version's.
53. **Schemas and catalogue.** `packages/workflow-engine/src/actions/` holds one file per family (`inquiries`, `reports`, `lists`, `graph`, `objects`, `control`, `https`, `messages`), `common.ts`, `errors.ts`, `WORKFLOW_ACTION_CONTRACTS` (`contracts.ts`, by key, typed `Record<WorkflowActionKey, ActionContract>`) and `createWorkflowActionEntries(objectTypes)` (`catalogue.ts`). The entries go beyond the bullet's "config and output schemas", because C122.2's table is contract data that `engine-logic`, `api-workflows` and the catalogue route all read; `api-actions` pairs each entry with its executor by `key@version` and copies nothing. A config is parsed at publish against the stored step and again after rendering, so a member a template can fill is a bounded string, and a member that decides a grant or a gate is a literal: `object-type`, `decision-basis`, `contest-route`, `biometric-basis`, an adverse `status`, `timeout-seconds`, the `method`. A generic action names `object-type` literally because the grant must be known at publish while the id is usually a template (C121.2); `createWorkflowActionEntries` holds it to the table, so 8B adds `case` by passing a row.
54. **What a step says it did.** `ACTION_OUTCOMES` is `applied`, `no_change` and `already_decided`, on every output of a change, and `find_or_create_account` answers `found` or `created`. `decision-id` is `null` unless the outcome is `applied` (C127.1), and the schema refuses anything else on an inquiry, list or transaction action. `invalid_transition` is a step error, never an outcome.
55. **Get List Match.** `probe` is C99.4's union with each value a template, without the face and background kinds: C99.1's on-demand engine does not read those lists, so they stay behind a custom-list report. `on-unscreened` is `error` (the default) or `null`, and nothing reads as `false`; `null-reason` takes `LIST_UNSCREENED_REASONS`, and the schema holds it to `matched: null`. `list-ids` is optional, meaning every active list of the probe's kind, as `POST /lists/match` does.
