56. **Kinds and delivery, where the plan spoke twice.** Only `calculate` and `update_variable` are `control`: C117.3 runs those in the previous step's transaction with no claim, and "every other action" is claimed. So `sync_graph` is `internal`, delivery `pure` (C127.5, a claimed step), which wins over C122.2's table cell `control`; `run_workflow` and `schedule_workflow` are `internal`, exactly once.
57. **`run_graph_query`'s grant holds `pii: true`.** C122.2 says `pii` for a PII-kind parameter, and the kinds are the query template's, which a step's config does not hold. So the entry fails closed, and `api-workflows` may narrow it at publish by reading the template. (Default, on the owner's review list.) Its `mode` is required, `synchronous` or `asynchronous`, and `timeout-seconds` stops at 600, ours, so a step's lease stays short (C117.4).
58. **A secret's metadata.** The attribute is `environment-value`, because `value` is PII on a list item, and the collision test failed on it. `DELETE /workflow-secrets/:key` answers the secret, as other deletes do, and refuses with a new problem `secret_in_use` (409; C125.2 names the refusal and no code). Hosts are stored normalised, so the schema refuses a scheme, a port, a path, upper case and a trailing dot. A value is at most 8 KiB with no control character, because a header carries it. `secretKeysOf`, `hasSecretReference` and `normalizeSecretReference` read a reference in either form; where one may stand is a publish rule.
59. **A third sandbox search.** `POST /sandbox/workflow-sms-messages/search` joins the two C126.4 names, because the C55.11 route is OTP-shaped (`code`) and "untouched", which would leave the `workflow-sms` list with no reader. `foundation-8a` freezes it with the other two. Members are `message-subject`, `message-text` and `message-html`, each marked PII and listed in `PII_SANDBOX_MESSAGE_ATTRIBUTES`, and a record says `recorded-at`, never `sent-at`.
60. **The HTTPS step.** `retry` is not in its config: C113.2 puts it on the step and C124.1 repeats it, and one place is enough. `response-schema` is a list of `{ name, type, pii }` with `pii` required, so a member leaves taint behind only when declared non-PII. The output's `body` and `headers` are marked PII whole, statically; a declared schema's marks are the step's, read by the masking of C120.6. `url` is checked as text: a literal `https://host[:port]`, a template only in a path segment or a query value, no credentials, no fragment, no secret. `httpsLiteralHost(url)` is what publish hands to `normalizeHost()`.
61. **`WORKFLOW_ACTION_ERROR_TYPES`** (`actions/errors.ts`) maps each action step error to its C120.2 type, so the type of a code is said once. Where the plan names none, ours: `invalid_transition`, `review_required`, `human_review_pending`, `biometric_consent_missing`, `rate_limited`, `request_failed`, `response_unsupported`, each report, verification and graph failure are `resource-error`; `decision_basis_missing`, `cause_lacks_scope`, `run_grant_insufficient`, `report_selector_limit`, `destination_refused`, `secret_placement_refused` and `test_run_egress_disabled` are `configuration-error`; `message_link_refused` and `recipient_not_allowed`, which refuse a rendered value, are `invalid-input-error`.
62. **Shape choices the plan left open, ours.** `update_object` takes one `{ field, operator, value }` per step. `run_report` names `inquiry-id` or `account-id`, exactly one. `set_relation` and `unset_relation` take `object-type`, `object-id`, `relation-key` and `target-id`. `schedule_workflow`'s `base-time` is `run-time` or a path. `calculate`'s `default-reason` is a closed list. `update_variable`'s output says whether the variable holds a value, never the value. `emits` lists every report type's terminal events for `run_report`, because the template picks the type, and every `*.updated-tags` for the tag actions.
63. **What `foundation-8a` joins to its lists.** `PII_DECISION_ATTRIBUTES` (`comment`) and `PII_SANDBOX_MESSAGE_ATTRIBUTES` to `MASKED_ATTRIBUTES` and the API-log masked set, with `meta.comment` in the request bodies; `secret-value` to `API_LOG_REDACTED_KEYS`; `email-address` and `phone-number` of the two search bodies stay as the SMS search keeps them. `decisions` in `getInquiryDocument`, which ignores the include until the builder stub lands: `INCLUDE_PERMISSIONS` holds it with `[]`, because `inquiries:read` alone serves it (C128.1).
64. **Existing tests changed with the plan,** none weakened: `INQUIRY_INCLUDES` in `inquiries.test.ts`; `RESOURCE_TYPES` in `vocabulary.test.ts`; the problem table in `fixtures.test.ts`; `account-types.test.ts` and `lists.test.ts` for the new members; the node group list and the `DEVICE` in `resources.test.ts`; the dashboard's `signals-fixtures.ts` and `lifecycle-fixtures.ts`. `evaluate-code.ts` gained the one mark `createActionRegistry` asks for, and `workflowJsonSchema` in place of `z.json()` (amendment 41).

## Workflow 8A Stage 1b amendments (foundation-8a)

Recorded by `foundation-8a`. The evidence is `docs/tdd/phase8-foundation-8a.tdd.md`. Each item binds every later 8A agent. Items marked (default) are on the owner's review list.

### Checkpoint (E) engine

1. **CI (F08): no job changes.** The latest green run, CI 41387332060 on `f8c488ad`, reads `node` 13.9 of 20 minutes, `integration` 11.7 of 30 and `unit-api` 16.8 and 15.0 of 30, so no job is within 25% of its timeout and outcome 2's rule changes none. `@openverify/workflow-engine` lands in `node` and `lint`. Under the e2e lock its 1,904 tests take 17.9 s at a load of 10, and its lint 21.3 s at a peak of 1.29 GB, under the 4 GB heap. The runner is about as fast as this machine under load: CI's 10.6-minute test step runs what took 12.2 minutes here (watchlist 8.6, the other 13 packages 3.7, at loads of 11 to 41). So three times the local figure is a wide bound, about a minute of tests and 0.2 of typecheck, and turbo runs packages side by side, so `node` ends at about 14.5 to 15 minutes, at the 25% line and not past it. No job of its own, as the task's test says. The margin is thin: `integration-8a` reads the run after this Workflow's push, and if `node` passes 15 minutes the change is `timeout-minutes: 25` on that job. `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh` passes unchanged.
2. **Tables beyond C117.2's.** `scheduled_runs` (C118.3) and `account_actions` (C118.7) join it, because each has a repository owner and no other home. `workflow_runs` gains `scheduled_run_id` (unique), `rerun_of_id`, `waiting_on_run_id` (C118.9), `caused_by_principal` and `effective_grant` (C121.3), `error_code`, `has_step_errors` (the filter), `is_test_run`, `overridden_steps` and `step_outcomes`. `workflow_run_steps` gains `claims`, because C117.4's crash-loop cap of 10 would otherwise read `attempt`, which C117.6's retries can raise past it. Run creators pin `WORKFLOW_RUN_CREATOR_TYPES`, so `case-action` is a CHECK change in 8B's migration.
3. **`schedule_fire` is `<schedule id>/<fire time>/<object id>`, unique per environment.** C118.4 starts one run per object of a tick, so a key of (schedule, fire time) alone would admit one run a tick. `api-triggers` writes it.
4. **No foreign key in four places, each with a format check.** `workflows`' three version pointers would make a cycle with `workflow_versions`, and the workflow's row lock keeps them right. `workflow_runs`' `root_run_id`, `parent_run_id` and `rerun_of_id`, because a run's skeleton can outlive its parent's (C120.8). `promoted_from`, because it names another environment. `workflow_trigger_deliveries.event_id`, because C16 keeps an event for a pending delivery only, and the row stays until the skeleton goes (P27).
5. **The journal's kinds are a closed list, `WORKFLOW_JOURNAL_KINDS` in `packages/db/src/tables/workflows.ts`,** so a kind can never be a value. C117.2's list ended in an ellipsis; it gains `run_completed`, `run_errored`, `run_redacted`, `step_skipped`, `step_canceled`, `step_reclaimed` and `wait_restored`. A kind `api-engine` needs beyond these is a change to that list and to `0027`, which it names in `contractDeviations` while nothing is pushed. Its UPDATE is refused under every setting, and a DELETE passes only under `openverify.environment_deletion` or `openverify.retention`.
6. **A fifth cause column, `caused_by_principal_held_inquiries_write`.** C121.3 lists four columns, but C119.3 reads `resolver-held-inquiries-write` from the event's cause, and by then the cause lives on the row. The event CHECKs read `usr_` or `key_` for the principal and `wfr_` for the run ids. `caused_by_bulk_id` has no format, because 8B names its prefix.
7. **A trigger file has `redacted_at` and `deleted_at`, as a transaction file does.** C120.9's "trigger files deleted" reads: the wrapped key and the name go in the redaction's transaction, which leaves the object unreadable, and the C16 pass deletes the object (`listRedactedWorkflowRunFiles`, `markWorkflowRunFilesDeleted`), so a failed delete fails nothing (outcome 28). So `redactRunsForSubjectsInTx` returns nothing to delete after the commit.
8. **The cause context.** The auth plugin opens a mutable store on `onRequest` and enters it again on `preValidation`, each with a callback hook, because a body parser's stream callbacks run outside the context and an async hook's continuation would not carry it. `enterRequestEventCause` fills it in, in `principal-auth.ts` and `routes/dashboard/authenticate.ts`. `runWithEventCause` is what the step executor, the test-run executor and the bulk job call. A flow route and a system job name no principal, so their events have none.
9. **`workflowSteps` is the wait-match producer.** The plan names the option; its type is `WorkflowWaitMatchQueue`, because `dispatch()` enqueues wait candidates and never a step. `StagedEvent`'s two members are optional, so an existing literal holds.
10. **The retention job builds its own lazy producers,** as it does its delivery queue, and the worker passes none, so nothing is closed twice.
11. **The sweeps' stubs resolve instead of using `unbuiltRepeatableJob`.** The bullet says `unbuiltRepeatableJob`, which fails its job. That suits a job an operator turns on, but these six run on every worker, every 15 or 60 s, with nothing yet to find: no run, delivery or schedule exists until their owners land, so a failure would only fill the failed set. A stub that finds nothing reads no value and reports none.
12. **Rate-limit policies (C143.3).** A policy holds one limit and one window, so a two-rule row is two policies, as `flow-handoffs` and `flow-handoffs-ip` are: `workflow-triggers` and `workflow-triggers-key`; `workflow-email`, `workflow-email-recipient-hour` and `workflow-email-recipient-day`. C143.3 gives `workflow-runs` no number, so it takes 600 a minute per environment, provisional (default). `workflow-slack` is 1 a second, and the action owns its burst of 3. The `case-*` policies land with 8B.
13. **Settings (C143.7).** `workflow-http-hosts` and `workflow-sms-countries` are nullable: `null` is off and follows the template, and an empty list would mean nothing is allowed. `case-retention-days` is served now, and nothing reads it until 8B. Each list has a cap, `WORKFLOW_SETTING_LIMITS` (provisional, default). The contract holds each entry's shape, and `workflowEgressSettingsProblem` (`workflows/actions/https/settings-check.ts`, `api-egress`) judges the rest, called before anything is written: a Fetch bad port, and a CIDR outside a private or CGNAT block (C124.3).
14. **`settings.updated` holds how many recipients, never who.** `auditedChanges` writes `{ count }` for the two recipient lists (P19, rule 11).
15. **Configuration (C143.8).** `config.workflows` holds five settings, read the same way by the API and the worker. `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_HTTP_ALLOW_LOOPBACK` is a startup error in production. `OPENVERIFY_NAT64_PREFIXES` takes the RFC 6052 lengths. Compose gives the worker the SMS binding, Twilio's credentials and the SMTP settings now, and `compose.test.sh` changes with the reason; the config reads Twilio for the worker when `PROCESS_PROVIDER_KINDS.worker` gains `sms`, at checkpoint (X). The `pool` warning and the Valkey check run in `main`, through `logWorkflowCodeRunnerAtStart` and `checkValkeyAtStart`, which catches a failed check. C143.8's "secret-scan and startup-error redaction lists" change nothing here: a socket path is no secret, and `ConfigError` never echoes a value.
16. **Answers the plan leaves open.** `restore`, `promote`, `rerun`, `test-runs` and a recipe's `install` answer 201, because each creates something; `trigger`, `manual-runs` and `run-action` answer 200, as C118 and the public paths say. `GET /account-actions` needs `workflows:write`, as C118.7 writes it. `workflowActingOf` lives in `routes/server/workflows.ts`, and every engine route reads it.
17. **`WORKFLOW_RUN_PII_TABLES` stands aside from `redaction.test.ts`'s scan of a subject's copies,** because no run exists to seed before `api-engine`. `api-engine` owes that coverage in its own suite: a redaction leaves none of the five tables holding the marker.
18. **Existing tests changed with the plan,** none weakened: `testing.test.ts` (the table list), `schema-v2.test.ts` (the sealed columns), `key-rotation.test.ts` (the seeds), `subject-ids.test.ts` (`wfr`), `services/retention.test.ts` and `retention-run.test.ts` (four report members), `plugins/rate-limit.test.ts`, `contracts/rate-limits.test.ts` and `dashboard-api.test.ts` (the new rows), `contracts/redaction.test.ts` (`secret-value`), `services/redaction.test.ts` (item 17), and the `infra/tests/compose.test.sh` block on the worker's Twilio settings (decision 76).
19. **Not in checkpoint (E), and named so it is not lost.** C117.5's unique `(environment_id, workflow_run_step_id)` on every object a run creates is in neither checkpoint's list. It belongs with the (A) columns.
20. **A load-sensitive test, for `code-sandbox`'s owner.** `packages/workflow-engine/src/code-runner/pool.test.ts`, "finds no canary of an earlier evaluation", answered `timeout` at a load of 41 and passed at 22. Its scan runs under the default time limit on the wall clock. Rule 6 asks for it to be made deterministic, test-first, and the file is outside these paths.
21. **Rule 7's stack.** The rule tells each agent to run `e2e:stack:down` before its own fresh stack, so two agents can end each other's. It happened here both ways, with no test failure. A run that stops with "no reusable e2e stack is running" is what the rule already says it is.

### Checkpoint (A) actions and decisions

22. **What a run creates carries its step (C117.5, item 19).** `workflow_run_step_id` with a partial unique `(environment_id, workflow_run_step_id)` is on `accounts`, `graph_queries`, `inquiries`, `list_items`, `reports`, `scheduled_runs`, `transactions`, `verifications` and `workflow_runs` (a child run), through `workflowRunStepId()` and `workflowRunStepKey()` in `tables/columns.ts`. It has no foreign key, because an object outlives its run's skeleton (C120.8). `report_runs` has none, because `rerun_report` makes up to 100 of them in one step and the effect row is its key. A decision has none, because a gate can write a `review` row and an `adverse` row for one step. `cases` take it in 8B.
23. **A transaction's reviewer needed columns (C127.7, V27).** The plan names the hook and no column. `transactions` gains `reviewer_type` and `reviewer_id`, whole or not at all, a user, an API key or a run; the contract's `reviewer.data` widens from `null` to `transactionReviewerIdentifierSchema`, nullable.
24. **`inquiry_decisions`** is `tables/decisions.ts`. `run`, `human_step`, `evidence`, `pending_evidence` and `contest_route` are JSON in the shape `buildDecisionResource` reads, which is what holds them to codes and ids. The subject has no foreign key, because the record outlives what its subject becomes. It has `updated_at`, because a redaction nulls the comment and a user closes a review. `inquiry_decisions_one_review_pending_idx` makes one review open per subject, and `inquiry_decisions_pending_is_reopen` holds pending to a `reopen` row.
25. **`transaction_children` already takes `case`,** with its prefix written literally, so 8B needs no change to its checks. `report_runs_trigger_sync_with_list_update` keeps its name, so its Phase 6 test stands; it now reads `list_update` as naming either a sync or a whole `(list, version)`, never both and never half. `report_runs_trigger_list_fk` names a list of the run's environment. `list_rescreen_requests` is keyed `(environment, list, version)`.
26. **The decision is recorded before the move.** F16 says "called from `applyInquiryAction`"; the record has to exist before `transitionInquiryInTx` stages `inquiry.declined`, or `meta.decision` has nothing to say. So the action checks `canTransitionInquiry` first, then runs the guard, the record and the move, and a refused move records nothing. `transitionInquiryInTx` takes `{ reopen, decision }`; every other caller passes neither. `performInquiryAction` takes the decision as a fifth argument, and the route reads `meta.solely-automated` and `meta.comment` from the body.
27. **The reopen refuses at the action, not only the route.** `applyInquiryAction` with `{ reopen: true }` answers 403 for anything but a user and 410 for a redacted inquiry, so `api-actions`' route inherits both. The route and `reopenInquiry` stay at 501.
28. **`stageListUpdated(tx, deps, environmentId, updates)`,** with `updates` `{ listId, changeKind, items }[]`, returns `StagedEvent[]`. A staged event gains `listRescreens`, which `dispatch()` enqueues, and `api-actions` fills them. A repository cannot reach the hook, so a write says what it changed: `redactListItems` and `redactBiometricItems` answer `ListItemRedaction { storageKeys, changedLists, redactedItemIds }`, `redactBiometricLists` adds the last two, `archiveListItem` adds `changed`, and `redactExpiredListItems` takes `onRedacted`. `BiometricItemRedactor.redactBySource` answers the same, and `afterListItemsRedactedInTx` (`services/lists/list-writes.ts`) does what any item redaction owes: the comments of `redactedItemIds`, then `items-redacted`. Clearing `biometric-lists-basis` stages `archived` for each list, an empty one included. `blind-index:rebuild` stages once a page per list, because each item commits alone and a rebuild of a large list would otherwise write an event per item; a crash loses at most the page's event. `services/list-writes.test.ts` fails a new bump, and a new caller that stages nothing.
29. **`createOutboxOnlyPublisher(db)`** (`events/index.ts`) writes the same rows and enqueues nothing, because a process with no queues still owes the event, and the sweeps are the outbox. `blind-index:rebuild` uses it, and `runRetention`, `updateDashboardSettings` and `onSettingsChangedInTx` default to it where a caller passes no publisher; the retention job and the settings route pass their own.
30. **`list-rescreen` runs with the lists, not with the engine,** because C127.12 says a monitored report needs no workflow. `WorkerListOptions.rescreens` is optional, and `phase8RepeatableJobs(deps, workflows, listRescreens?)` answers its sweep with or without the engine. The queue is `jobs/list-rescreen-queue.ts`. Its job id is `<list id>-<version>`.
31. **What `api-actions` owns beyond its bullet's list:** `packages/db/src/repositories/{list-rescreen-requests,device-trust}.ts`; `services/decisions/{record,guard,redaction,resources,contest-route,service}.ts`; `workflows/actions/lists/list-updated.ts`; `workflows/actions/transactions/{children,reviewer}.ts`; `workflows/actions/devices/trust.ts`. Each stub touches nothing and answers empty, except the two route services, which answer 501, and the repository stubs, which reject. `WorkflowRepositoryOwner` gains `api-actions`.
32. **The `…InTx` forms.** `createInquiryInTx`, `updateInquiryInTx`, `changeInquiryTagsInTx` (`lockedInquiry` is exported); `generateOneTimeLinkInTx` (the caller makes the token); `createReportInTx`, `changeReportTagsInTx`, `changeReportMonitoringInTx`; `ListService.createItemInTx`; `createBiometricListItemInTx`; `createGraphQueryInTx`; `createAccountInTx`, `updateAccountInTx`, `changeAccountTagsInTx`, `addAccountRelationInTx`, `removeAccountRelationInTx` with `accountChangeIn`; `createTransactionInTx`, `updateTransactionInTx`, `changeTransactionTagsInTx`, `addTransactionRelationInTx`, `removeTransactionRelationInTx` with `transactionChangeIn`; `graphChangeIn`. Each takes no rate token and enqueues nothing: both stay with the public service, and an action takes its own and enqueues after the commit. `conflictOnDuplicate` stays on the public account services, so an action meets a raw unique violation, which aborts its transaction anyway. `bindGraphQuery` now takes `mayViewPii`, because a run has no `Principal`. Three actions have no extraction to make: `find_or_create_account`, whose lookup by a declared identifier is `api-actions`' on `createAccountInTx`; `run_verification`, which has no service; and `redact_object` on an inquiry or an account (amendment 6).
33. **`comment` is masked at any depth in a stored log,** beside `filename` in `MASKED_MEMBERS`, because `meta.comment` of a decision is no attribute (P21). It fails closed on a member of that name elsewhere.
34. **An adverse status is held to the statuses of the type's newest version,** on create and on `PATCH`, 422 with a pointer per status. An empty list is allowed: such a type has no adverse status. The `PATCH` audit entry names each member it was given.
35. **`GET /decisions` declares no scope,** because the one it needs is the subject's read scope (V10), so `listDecisions` must check it before anything else, and a request with no scope check there would serve every subject. The route is registered only with authentication until `api-actions` builds it, and it answers 501.
36. **PGlite after this checkpoint.** Cold first database 6,018, 6,805 and 6,083 ms at loads of 3.2 to 4.7, against 4,262 to 4,374 ms before: the nine new unique indexes and the new tables cost about 1.7 s of a cold migration. Warm first database 1,712, 1,351 and 3,277 ms at loads of 5.1 to 6.3, and a second database 832 to 2,301 ms, read beside other agents' suites. The hook budget of 120 s is untouched.
37. **Existing tests changed with the plan,** none weakened: `testing.test.ts` (the table list), `phase6-tables.test.ts` (`report_origin`), `subject-ids.test.ts` (`idec`), `list-biometrics.test.ts`, `services/lists/ports.test.ts` and `services/lists/biometric/redaction.test.ts` (the redaction answer), `redaction.test.ts` (`DECISION_PII_TABLES`, whose coverage per subject type `api-actions` owes), `jobs/records.test.ts` (the new column on two row fixtures), `settings-lists.test.ts` (the hook's answer), `item-writer.test.ts`, `importers.integration.test.ts`, `races.integration.test.ts` and `repeatable/phase7.test.ts` (the new `events` dep), and `lists.integration.test.ts`, `phase7-signal-tables.test.ts`, `schema-v2.test.ts`, `key-rotation.test.ts` and `migration-0026.test.ts`, the last because `createAccount` now names `workflow_run_step_id`. That brought a new helper, `createTestAccountOnEarlierSchema` in `packages/db/src/testing.ts`, which `createTestInquiryOnEarlierSchema` calls too: an upgrade test that needs an account on an earlier schema writes it with this, and `resolveInquiryAccount` no longer serves one.
38. **Two load-sensitive tests, for their owners (rule 6).** `packages/workflow-engine/src/code-runner/pool.test.ts`, "finds no canary of an earlier evaluation in the same child", still fails inside the forced non-API gate at a load of 3 to 22 and passes alone, so amendment 20 stands and the test-first fix contracts-8a gave stands with it (a native `indexOf` in place of the interpreted byte walk). `packages/core/src/transactions/card-guard.test.ts`, "stays linear on the longest text", measured 541.5 ms of CPU against 500 once at a load of about 25 and passed alone and in the next forced gate. CPU time still grows under contention on shared cores, so its bound needs a look by its owner. Neither file is `foundation-8a`'s.

### Checkpoint (X) egress

39. **`wsecv` is an internal prefix (C125.1).** The plan seals a value to `<row id>`, and C112.1 names no prefix for that row, so `workflowSecretValue: 'wsecv'` joins both prefix tables. It is never served. The generic "any id" pattern in `docs/api/openapi.json` gains it, as it holds `wfst` and the other internal prefixes.
40. **`workflow_secrets` is the organisation's, a value the environment's.** `tables/workflow-secrets.ts`. The composite keys `workflow_secret_values_secret_fk` and `workflow_secret_values_environment_fk` hold a value to its secret's organisation whatever a caller passes, and a value goes with its secret and with its environment. `previous_value` and `previous_expires_at` are whole or not at all, as are `rotated_by` and `rotated_at`. `created_by` and `rotated_by` are `usr_` or `key_`, as the contract says: a run never writes a secret. `allowed_placements` defaults to `authorization` and no pointer (P29), and a secret needs at least one host (C125.4).
41. **`openWorkflowSecretValue` is the one read of a value (C125.5).** Every other function of `repositories/workflow-secrets.ts` answers metadata, so a reader of that file can tell where a value comes out. `seedWorkflowSecret` and `seedWorkflowSecretValue` are in `@openverify/db/testing`.
42. **The Slack port (C126.3).** `SlackSender` takes `{ webhookUrl, text }`. `SandboxSlackSender` takes `{ text }` and a context naming the secret by id, so the URL never reaches a sandbox record, by type (C126.4). `SlackSendError` holds a code, `retryable` and `retryAfterSeconds`. The kind takes `incoming-webhook` and `sandbox`.
43. **`attachmentScanner` is reserved (C135.5).** The kind takes `clamd`, `sandbox` and `none`, and refuses a sandbox one for production like any kind. It binds nothing: `AttachmentScanner` is 8B's port, so `BoundProviders` has no member yet. Unset and `none` both read `null`, which is what decision 66's default means, and 8B decides how `none` is served. `OPENVERIFY_CLAMD_SOCKET` is required in the worker while a binding names `clamd` (`ApiConfig.clamdSocket`), and it is a plain string, because C135.5 allows a socket or a private address.
44. **The worker reads every kind (C126.6, outcome 21).** `PROCESS_PROVIDER_KINDS.worker` is `PROVIDER_KINDS`, so a worker with `sms` bound to `twilio` now stops without Twilio's credentials, where it ignored the binding before. `workerProviderBindings(config, infra)` (`worker.ts`) is what `main` calls, because a test could not otherwise see the credentials reach the binding. `.api` is unchanged.
45. **The mail sandbox is no provider kind (C126.4, decision 40).** The plan's "mail sandbox binding" is `WorkflowMailBinding` in `mail/sandbox.ts`: `{ sandbox: SandboxMailer, production: Mailer }` by environment kind, so a production environment reads the real mailer by type. The worker builds it in `createWorkflowServices`. No environment variable binds it.
46. **`WorkflowMessenger` (C126.8, V26).** `workflows/actions/messaging/port.ts` is frozen: `send(message, context)` answers `MessageOutput`, and a refusal rejects with `ActionStepError` carrying its step error code. A message arrives rendered. `context.effect` is `null` only for a message no run sends, a case notification. `createWorkflowMessenger` is the stub in `messenger.ts`, and `WorkflowServices` gains `messenger` and `mail`. It is an interface now, because an intersection made `triggers-engine.test.ts`'s cast unnecessary to the linter.
47. **The HTTPS action's options are a client of their own, not options on the ordinary one (amends the bullet).** The bullet says typed options that the current client refuses. Widening `OutboundHttpRequest.method` reached `sync/runner.ts`, which the watchlist benchmark imports, and three test fakes, and an ordinary client would then be able to name `PUT`. So `OutboundHttp.policyClient(options)` takes `OutboundHttpPolicyClientOptions` (`methods`, `portPolicy`, `headerPolicy`, `refuseContentEncoding`, all required) and answers an `OutboundHttpPolicyClient`. `createOutboundHttp` throws until `api-egress` builds it, and every existing client stays `GET` and `POST`. `api-egress` adds what it needs of the network policy to those options.
48. **The guard's new members are optional, and `DEFAULT_NETWORK_POLICY` states them (C124.2).** `allowLoopback`, `allowCidrs` and `nat64Prefixes` lift nothing until `api-egress` reads them, with a test, so a policy set early fails closed. `nat64Prefixes` is the exception to that reasoning: an unread prefix leaves an address classified as it is today, so `api-egress` adds it before anything passes one.
49. **The sandbox searches are served in every mode (default, on the owner's review list).** The OTP sink is registered only outside production mode, because it holds codes. A workflow message list holds none, and C126.4 shows it in a test run's panel, which a sandbox environment of a production install also has. So `sandboxMessageRoutes` joins `serverRoutes`, and a production environment's key answers 404 in the route, before anything is read.
50. **The path parameter is `:environmentId`.** find-my-way ends a parameter at a `-`, so `:environment-id` would be a parameter `environment` followed by the text `-id`. OpenAPI reads `{environmentId}`. The path a client calls is what C125.2 says.
51. **`message-subject`, `message-text` and `message-html` join `MASKED_MEMBERS` too (C143.5).** A sandbox search answers them outside any `attributes`, where the attribute list does not reach. `email-address` and `phone-number` were there already.
52. **A defect for the owners of `workflows/publish/facts.ts` and `workflows/runs/catalogue.ts`.** Both ask the API process whether Slack is bound, and `.api` never reads `slack` (outcome 21), so `send_slack_message` always reads unbound: the catalogue says `available: false`, and publish warns `provider_not_configured` that a run will end with an error, while the worker runs it. `boundAdapters` in `facts.ts` never adds `slack` at all. Likewise a sandbox environment on an install with no SMTP reads `mailer` as unbound, though its mail goes to the sandbox list. The exact change for `integration-8a`: give the API a fact, not a binding: `config.workflows.slackBound: Record<EnvironmentKind, boolean>` read from `OPENVERIFY_PROVIDER_SLACK_{SANDBOX,PRODUCTION}` by name alone, compose passing both to the API with `compose.test.sh`'s `api receives no Slack or scanner setting` narrowed; `ApiDependencies.workflowAdapters` built from it; `boundAdapters` and `unavailableReason` reading that; and `mailer` reading true for a sandbox environment.
53. **Existing tests changed with the plan,** none weakened: `providers/index.test.ts`, the positional slices of `phase5b-`, `phase6-` and `phase7-providers.test.ts`, `config.test.ts` (Twilio in the worker, and `leaves the kinds of the other process to it`, whose worker half became `stops the worker over a Twilio binding without its credentials`), `ids.test.ts` in both packages, `testing.test.ts` (the table list), `schema-v2.test.ts`, `key-rotation.test.ts` (the seeds), `services/redaction.test.ts` (`WORKFLOW_SECRET_TABLES`, because a secret is the operator's and no subject's), `workflows/runs/service.test.ts` (the two new `WorkflowServices` members) and `infra/tests/compose.test.sh`.
54. **PGlite after this checkpoint.** Cold first database 4,712, 4,496 and 4,799 ms at loads of 2.9 to 3.3, against 4,555, 4,513 and 4,463 ms before at 0.5 to 0.9. Warm first database 1,023 and 1,035 ms at 2.7 to 2.8, against 1,258, 1,009 and 1,059 ms at 1.2 to 1.5. A second database 752 to 859 ms. The two tables cost nothing a hook would notice. (The third warm run rebuilt the cache after the migration's hash changed, 4,743 ms, so it is left out.)

## Workflow 8A Stage 2 amendments

Recorded by `integration-8a` from the Stage 2 reports. The evidence is `docs/tdd/phase8-code-sandbox.tdd.md` and `docs/tdd/phase8-engine-logic.tdd.md`. Each item binds Workflows 8B and 8C. Items marked (default) are on the owner's review list.

### code-sandbox

1. **`--allow-fs-read` is not real paths alone (C123.3).** Node 22.22's ES module resolver reads a dependency's `package.json` through the link in the importer's `node_modules`, and `realpathSync` stats that link, each a checked read. So `fsRead` holds the entry, the three real package directories, and two link paths: `<core's node_modules>/@jitl/quickjs-ffi-types` and `<variant's node_modules>/@jitl/quickjs-ffi-types`. In a flat `node_modules` the links equal the real directory and dedupe. The workspace-layout and image-layout tests pin this.
2. **Stack limits (C123.5).** QuickJS's 512 KiB limit measures the shadow stack, so small recursive frames hit V8's native limit inside WebAssembly first, a host `RangeError` with the VM mid-call. The child answers `stack_overflow` for both and never touches that VM again.
3. **Recycling is wider than C123.3's list:** also after `stack_overflow`, and after any `sandbox_crashed`, including a request frame refused in the parent.
4. **The clock starts at the guest (C123.5).** The guest's clock and QuickJS memory limit start at the guest, and harness setup runs outside them; a real defect under load, found and fixed test-first. The parent's kill counts from the request's write, so instantiation (1 to 13 ms) comes out of the 250 ms grace. An evaluation that returns past its deadline, between two interrupt checks, reads `timeout`, never a value. Reading a failed guest's words gets 50 ms and 1 MiB more, since it can run a getter.
5. **Out of memory (C123.6).** With no room for an error object QuickJS throws `null`. A thrown `null` reads `memory_limit` when memory in use is within 64 KiB of the limit, and as the guest's own otherwise.
6. **`CodeEvaluationResult`'s failure gains optional `reason`,** a `CodeRunnerFailureReason` from `CODE_RUNNER_FAILURE_REASONS`, safe to log, because C123.3 says a failed handshake is `sandbox_crashed` "with a code" and `message` is reserved for guest text.
7. **The socket carries the child's frames plus `crashed` (amends Stage 1a amendment 25).** `codeRunnerSocketResponseSchema` adds `{ kind: 'crashed', id, reason }` for the socket only, because the frozen `error` frame refuses `sandbox_crashed`. A child that sends `crashed` is killed.
8. **(Default.) An evaluation the contract refuses** (a source or input past its cap, input past depth 32, an environment that is no id) answers `runtime_error` with `message: null` and `reason: input_refused`, without a child, because `EVALUATE_CODE_ERROR_CODES` has no input code. It is deterministic, so it is not retried; the action sizes inputs first.
9. **(Default.) An async function and top-level await are settled,** with the deadline read between batches. A promise that never settles is `invalid_output` (`runtime_error` at module level), because `export default async function` passes the config schema and would otherwise read as `{}`.
10. **(Default.) A failed evaluation's `runtime_error` message** is the guest's own, without its name. A module with no default function answers the fixed text `the module has no default function`.
11. **The child's contract numbers and real paths arrive as one JSON string in `argv[2]`,** which holds no secret, so they have one source.
12. **Timers, provisional and ours:** the socket client's watchdog is the time limit, plus the kill grace, plus 30 s (`ANSWER_MARGIN_MS`) for the server's queue; the handshake gets 5 s, a child's start 20 s (`CHILD_START_TIMEOUT_MS`). On expiry the client ends the connection, so everything in flight on it answers `socket_timeout`.
13. **A guest's output key `__proto__` is dropped at the parent,** because `z.json()`'s record skips it. No prototype is reached, but the worker sees less than the guest returned. Default kept: the frozen schema's output is what is returned.
14. **The Dockerfile's `pnpm deploy` runs online,** because `--offline` fails with `ERR_PNPM_NO_OFFLINE_META` on the release-age check. The install before it stays `--frozen-lockfile --offline` after `pnpm fetch`.
15. **`bin/` has no `exports` entry.** The parent finds the child by `new URL('../../bin/code-runner-child.mjs', import.meta.url)`, which holds from `src` and from `dist`, so a future `files` list keeps `bin/`.

### engine-logic

16. **`===` with an unknown (C114.6).** (Default.) "A comparison other than `===` with `null` follows on-null" reads: only the explicit test with a literal `null` answers a boolean. `{"===": [x, true]}` with `x` null is an unknown and follows `on-null`, because a plain `false` would read unknown as no match, which H04 forbids. `!==` stays `!(a === b)`.
17. **An explicit test needs no `on-null` (C114.6).** (Default.) A condition that only tests the path (`=== null`, `missing`, `missing_some`) has its answer in that null, so `on-null` could never fire. Everything else that reads a nullable path needs one, a guarded read included.
18. **`var` with a default is refused** (`var_default_refused`), because a default turns an unknown into a value, around every null rule.
19. **Three run-time semantics.** (Default.) A `while` at `max-iterations` fails with `loop_limit_exceeded` rather than stopping, following "never a silent truncation". A `while` with concurrency above 1 is refused (`loop_concurrency_refused`). A loop's or parallel's failure waits for what is still running before it fails outward, because C119.4 says every branch must finish.
20. **C113.3's "`next` belongs to every node except output",** read with its port list: a conditional has `route:*` and `else`, a parallel `branch:*`, a loop `body`, `done` and optionally `error`, and none of those has `next`; a join, wait, action, module, array-map-output and module-input have it.
21. **A root beyond C114.4's list.** A wait filter reads the waited event's view under `event`.
22. **A recurring filter in 8A is a C114 condition over `trigger`, with `on-null`.** C118.4 and C137.2 name the case-search grammar, which does not exist until 8B; 8B decides whether to replace it.
23. **Output shapes the plan leaves open:** a conditional is `{ route }` (`else` for else); for-each and while are `{ iterations }`; map is `{ iterations, values[] }`; a time wait has an empty output; an event wait adds `event-name` to C119.3's list.
24. **`typeToJsonSchema` and `checkValue` are plain functions,** not `z.toJSONSchema()` of a built scope and zod built from declared types, because zod compiles object keys into `new Function` and no name an operator writes may reach that (C113.8). The JSON Schema has the same shape.
25. **The DSL gains `file`** (an `id<wfrf>`, because a file value is in scope as its id) and the object member form `name?: type pii`. HTTPS `response-schema` is read as contracts-8a froze it, `[{ name, type, pii }]`.
26. **The counter of C119.4 is `joins[key].open` in the state,** which needs the run row held across each `advance`; Update Variable and the loop counters are read-modify-write anyway.
27. **The sink table is `templates/sinks.ts`,** not a registry field. If an action's config member changes, its row must follow. `update_transaction_status.status` is a literal.
28. **`MASKED_ATTRIBUTE_NAMES` in `expressions/trigger-view.ts` mirrors `MASKED_ATTRIBUTES` in `apps/api/src/auth/pii.ts` by hand,** because this package cannot read that list. A name added there must be added here. Carried: move the list into `@openverify/contracts`.
29. **A `case` object type reads as untyped PII** until 8B gives it a view, so paths under it publish and are tainted. `current_case` paths are refused (`current_case_unavailable`) until a provider is passed.
30. **Calculate with no `default`** fails with `expression_arithmetic_error` rather than answering a null value, though the output schema allows null there. `advance` counts each visit against `step-executions`, and the executor adds retries itself.
31. **Golden definitions.** `definition-every-node.json` and `definition-module.json` pass the graph check as written since Stage 4, and `withActionSchemas` is gone. `retry_read` in the first never raises `risk`, so it fails at `max-iterations` at run time, and a test pins that as the fixture stands.

## Workflow 8A Stage 3 amendments

Recorded by `integration-8a` from the Stage 3 reports. The evidence is `docs/tdd/phase8-api-{engine,workflows,triggers,actions,egress}.tdd.md`, and what Stage 4 fixed is in the next section.

### api-engine

32. **Run state (C117.1, C117.2).** There is no state column. The interpreter's joins, loops, modules, failures, pinned module versions, action-wait carries, acknowledged unknown effects and a test run's options live in one frame, sealed in `workflow_runs.variables` around the variables, and step outputs stay on step rows and are rehydrated on each advance. It is nulled with the run's values. Readers use `variablesOf`.
33. **Wait filters (amends C119.3).** A filter is evaluated at the match, under the advisory lock, and a non-matching event leaves the step `waiting`, journaled as `wait_restored`. C119.3's "evaluates when the step resumes and puts the wait back" would miss a matching event committed meanwhile.
34. **A wait enters `waiting` in the completing transaction with no claim,** which overrides C117.3's "wait start gets claim and complete". A module step also has no claim: it pins and starts inside the advance.
35. **The subjects of a wait candidate:** the event's resource id, plus every to-one relationship id in its payload.
36. **Action waits (C127.2, C119.9).** A handler may answer `waiting`. `recordWorkflowWaitIndex` is written when any wait is entered, so a draft's test run and an action's own wait are covered, though an event racing that first index row on a draft can be missed. A published version relies on `waitEventNames` including action waits, which it does.
37. **Run Workflow's child wait.** The parent waits for the child's `workflow-run.*` events, never a row the child touches, because a child's end locking its parent would invert a cancel's parent-then-child order. A lineage repeat is refused with `run_depth_exceeded`.
38. **An unfinished run reached by a redaction ends `errored` with `subject_redacted`,** because its state is in the values being nulled, rather than only its wait failing. An overflowed run of the same environment ends the same way, as does `DELETE /workflow-runs/:id` on an unfinished run. (Default.)
39. **Copies (C120.9).** The hook returns void, so other targets are listed in a system audit entry `workflow-run.redaction-copies-found`, source and target ids. Case comments and case fields arrive with 8B.
40. **Masking beyond C120.6's letter.** (Default.) A run output declared non-PII shows only when its value is a number or a boolean, because a string could be free text. Declared inputs and variables show only for boolean, number, integer, datetime, id and enum types. A trigger snapshot is member-wise only when it holds a `type` the view knows.
41. **Cancel's abort comes from the heartbeat,** not BullMQ's signal, so a running handler learns within a third of its lease; the frozen processor has no signal. Queued jobs are not removed: each finds its step no longer ready.
42. **`waiting-on: admission` and `step-outcomes.skipped` are never set:** admission defers steps, run-creation deferral is `not_before`, and no engine path skips a step. Both stay in the contract.
43. **Test runs (C120.7).** A synthetic payload is checked as a JSON object, and for `trigger-api` by the declared inputs; it is not validated against the event's resource schema. `readTriggerFields` may name file ids that have no stored row. An adverse step also needs `irreversible:<step id>` to run.
44. **`listWorkflowActions` takes no query:** it is an empty strict object.
45. **Lock order, binding on every action:** domain rows, then the run row, then the correlation lock, then step rows. A run's lock is `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, because an effect row's foreign key takes a share lock on the run and two parallel branches deadlocked on `FOR UPDATE`.
46. **No hot row.** Only the sweep writes `last_served_at`, which took effectful throughput from 29 to 50 steps/s at 32 clients (C117.8).
47. **Rule 5 was not kept for every slice of `api-engine`:** waits, redaction, retention, sweeps, authority and the run routes were not seen failing on missing code. The suites stand; the record says so.

### api-workflows

48. **`graph/index.ts` gained `export * from './analysis.js'` and `'./check.js'`,** outside its owner's paths, because engine-logic's handoff says these come from the root and nothing could be built without them. Accepted: additive, no collision.
49. **`PUT /draft` refuses what the draft check refuses** (422 `definition_invalid`). (Default.) So an unfinished graph cannot be stored, and the 8C editor keeps unfinished work client-side or uses validate. Only a missing trigger is allowed.
50. **Validate runs the check in `publish` mode,** so it shows taint and `trigger_missing`, but not the C116.4 rules, which need the transaction's facts.
51. **With no open draft, `PUT /draft` at `lock-version: 0` opens the next version;** a non-zero lock with no draft answers `version_locked`. A draft opened this way starts at lock-version 2.
52. **Publish never changes a workflow's status,** except that a publish by a holder of the grant moves `publisher_revoked` back to `running`. (Default.) `POST /activate` answers 409 while `publisher_revoked`.
53. **During a staged rollout `latest-published-version` stays on the control,** so `run_workflow`, `module` and rerun read the version that serves everyone. Both sides are `live`, and `workflow.published` fires only at the finish. (Default.)
54. **A staged rollout is refused** for a module, for a first publish with no control (`rollout_without_control`), and when the trigger differs from the control's (`rollout_trigger_changed`, ours: a union of the two sides' events would start a control run on an event its definition does not listen to). (Default.) `POST /rollout` asks the acting user for the treatment's publisher requirements too.
55. **Beyond C116.4.** (Default.) Literal ids anywhere in a definition, conditions included, must be active objects of the environment (`reference_unknown`), and a reference holding a non-id literal such as a recipe placeholder is refused the same way (C121.6).
56. **By-config adverse steps are resolved statically and fail closed.** (Default.) A templated `list-id` or a list of unknown purpose is adverse. `update_transaction_status` is adverse when its status is in the union of every active type's `adverse-statuses`, because a step names no type. A type's own contest route satisfies the gate only when every active type of that kind holds one. `update_object` is adverse on an account's `status` when the value is `archived` or a template.
57. **C128.4's fact.** (Default.) A template is biometric when a published version's flow holds a `selfie` step, or it screens every list and the environment has an active face or selfie-background list. A template with no published version is unknown, which fails closed.
58. **The grant.** `run_graph_query`'s `pii: true` is not narrowed. A child's grant joins only for a literal `workflow-id`; a templated target is `workflows:trigger` alone and is checked at run time. `child_grant_widened` is put on the version being published.
59. **Promote copies the workflow's latest published version,** and the target is the non-archived workflow of the same name and kind, or a new one; several answer `promote_unresolved` at pointer `''`. References are whole-string ids. Secret keys need no rewrite, because they are per organisation.
60. **`publisher_scope_lost` is the one exception to an immutable version:** its `warnings` column changes, set once with one audit entry, removed when the rights return. The definition never changes.
61. **`approve_inquiry`'s scope does not pause a workflow:** C121.5 lists adverse steps, and approve is `adverse: never`, though C121.3 checks its cause.
62. **`removeRetiredWorkflowWaitIndex` runs in the 60 s sweep,** for versions archived over a day ago with no `in_progress` run, or a retired version keeps writing a candidate per event.
63. **Archiving a workflow leaves its draft row,** because `workflow_versions_published_is_complete` allows no archived draft.
64. **`rescreen-on-account-field-change` falls short of C127.12 and C140.5:** it is one `run_report`, because no 8A action lists an account's monitored reports. To replace it, append to `WORKFLOW_RECIPE_ROWS`. Carried.
65. **`waitEventNames` adds what a step's own wait resolves on:** every report type's `.ready` and `.errored`, `verification.passed` and `.failed`, and the four graph-query terminal events of a synchronous query.
66. **`listWorkflowRecipes()` takes no arguments** and answers synchronously.

### api-triggers

67. **The trigger filter of outcome 29 has no source yet.** `eventTriggerConfigSchema` has no filter member, and `triggerIndexRows` writes `{ criteria, on-null, verification-types }`. The worker honours the four filter keys when they are in `workflow_triggers.filter`, read through `eventFilterSubjects` and `matchesWebhookFilters`, and nothing writes them. Exact change if wanted: an optional member on the event trigger config, copied by `triggerIndexRows`. A criteria path such as `trigger.template.id` is refused at publish on a transaction, because its view has no template.
68. **A delivery holds no version column,** so the worker chooses by time: the subject's rollout side if that version was published at or before the event, otherwise the newest version live at the event, otherwise `event_before_activation`. The event and verification criteria are read from the chosen version's definition. A version the worker falls back to carries no deployment or label.
69. **C118.8's causal chain walks back through delivery, event, parent run and scheduled run,** not `root_run_id`, so a sibling in one root is not a cycle. It stops at an event retention has deleted, and `trigger_depth` still caps a loop. A cause whose run is gone is `trigger_cause_missing`.
70. **Depth.** A depth of exactly 8 starts; past 8 refuses. A scheduled run keeps its source's `trigger_depth`, takes `depth + 1`, and has no `parent_run_id`, so a parent's cancel does not reach it.
71. **Test runs' opt-in** travels in `TriggerEventCause.testRunAllowsTriggers`, in memory only.
72. **C118.2's undefined cases.** A file over 10 MiB, and bytes that are not JPEG, PNG, WebP or PDF, answer 422 with a pointer; only a malformed value answers 400. A declared field given `null` counts as missing. A workflow with no `trigger-api` answers 409 `workflow_not_running`.
73. **On the API trigger a rollout's subject is the `Idempotency-Key`,** or a fresh id without one, so a retry without a key may take the other side.
74. **`idempotency_key` holds a SHA-256 of principal and key,** never the client's text. Manual runs key per object and `run-action` per call.
75. **Manual runs.** (Default.) The whole call needs every side of a rollout's grant. The CSV is one `object-id` column. A run the `workflow-runs` limiter holds is `refused` with `rate_limited`, because a manual run has no delivery row to wait on.
76. **Recurring ticks.** (Default.) A tick reads at most 1,000 objects past its cursor, a limiter refusal stops it and keeps the cursor, a filter that errors leaves an object out with its code in the log, and a schedule that cannot be read moves on a day and logs an error.
77. **Scheduled runs.** (Default.) A target must have `trigger-schedule`, checked at the step and again at fire. A paused target cancels with `workflow_not_running`. A run whose source run is gone cancels with `source_run_missing`, because nothing bounds its grant.
78. **Account actions.** A `prefill` key must be a declared input and its value an `account.fields.*` path, else 422. Updating a prefill on a workflow that is not running answers 409. An account too large to snapshot answers 422 with no pointer. `available-actions` on a redacted account is empty.
79. **The past-cap delivery is `refused` with `trigger_fanout_exceeded`,** and a delivery with no event row ends `expired` with `event_missing`.

### api-actions

80. **Outcome 19, widened.** `review_required` reads a rejected handoff at any status, and in `needs_review` any verification ended `needs_review`, any errored report, an errored or `list_unscreened` screening, and a `signal_unevaluated` review cause.
81. **Outcome 6.** A redacted subject ends with `subject_redacted` and writes no decision on approve, decline, mark-for-review, `run_report`, `run_verification`, the tag and update actions and the links. `rerun_report` does so only for a redacted account; a list selector has no redacted subject. `redact_object` reads `no_change`.
82. **A child verification redacts its whole inquiry,** because no verification-level redaction exists, which is wider than C127.7 reads. (Default.)
83. **`get_list_match`** uses probe source `request` and `onDemand: true`. A found match stands even if another list was unscreened.
84. **`rerun_report` waits for its reports in turn,** because the engine waits on one key. A report that finishes between the status read and the wait's snapshot is not seen, and the step ends `report_timed_out`: a false timeout, never a false result. Carried: a multi-key action wait.
85. **An explicit-consent `biometric-basis` is checked whatever the static rule read,** and a `sync_graph` `non-embedding` mode leaves out the opt-in kinds, weak and device recognition, because no kind is an embedding.
86. **A gate's review record from `markInquiryForReviewInTx` carries empty evidence,** unlike C128.1's every-row rule.
87. **Objects a test run creates hold no `test-run-id` column (amends C120.7).** They carry `workflow_run_step_id`, which names the step, and so the run, which is marked. Decision records carry `test-run-id`.
88. **`stampObjectRunStep` and `listAccountGraphIndexJobs` live in `transaction-children.ts`,** because `repositories/index.ts` is shared and a new file could not be exported.
89. **The handlers were not all run RED against a stub (rule 5).** `bbe4effd` and `fcd9649e` went in with their tests, and the flow screen as one commit. The TDD file says so.
90. **`routes/action-routes.test.ts` and `services/selfie-capture-races.integration.test.ts` changed with the plan,** the latter because its actor `key_X` is refused now that a decline writes a record.

### api-egress

91. **The messenger answers truthfully whether anything left; the engine claims and releases the effect (amends C126.8)** in `runOutside`.
92. **C124.7 is only partly met.** The per-(environment, host) concurrency of 5 is held per process in `withHostSlot`, because `KeyValueCache` has no atomic counter and `ports.ts` is shared. A fleet of N workers holds 5 per worker. It fails closed with `rate_limited`, retryable. Carried, and `docs/workflows/https-request.md` says so. Exact change: an atomic counter on the port.
93. **Defaults for the owner's review list.** A secret's `last4` reads `****` for a value under 8 characters. `DELETE /workflow-secrets/:key` needs a production user session when a production value exists. A key with no secret, or a secret with no value here, is `secret_placement_refused` at run time. A host in a message is any dotted name ending in two or more letters, because no public-suffix list ships, so `report.pdf` is refused. The "run object" of a confirmed recipient is the trigger's inquiry plus every `inq_` subject of the run. In a sandbox a listed address with no SMTP server is recorded, and a Slack secret's value is opened and checked. A 429 or 5xx with no retry left answers its status as output.
94. **Amends Stage 1b amendment 47.** `OutboundHttpPolicyClient.request` answers `OutboundHttpPolicyResponse` and gains `close()`. The options gain `networkPolicy` and `exposeHeaders`, the request `timeoutMs`, `captureErrorBodyBytes` and `signal`, and `OutboundHttpOptions.testDispatcher` is for tests only. The Slack adapter uses this client too, because the ordinary one drops a non-2xx body and `Retry-After`. A client's `headerPolicy.refused` leaves out the three names the action sets; the config schema still refuses them in a definition. A 2xx with no type passes only with an empty body.
95. **`SandboxMailContext.workflowRunId` is `string | null`, and `WorkflowMessageContext` gains optional `beforeSend`,** so a marked step's audit entry records a send and never a refusal. A sandbox record with no run is kept and no search serves it, because the contract requires a `wfr_` id. 8B notes this for case notifications.
96. **The settings check names the first category an entry overlaps,** so `0.0.0.0/0` reads `metadata` and `240.0.0.0/4` reads `broadcast`.
97. **C146.2's HTTPS guard test needs no container:** an injected resolver and a local server stand in, as rule 9 asks.

## Workflow 8A Stage 4 amendments (integration-8a)

Recorded by `integration-8a` on 2026-11-02. The evidence is `docs/tdd/phase8-integration-8a.tdd.md`.

### What Stage 4 settled

98. **Every action runs in the real process.** `HANDLERS` joins `OBJECT_ACTION_HANDLERS` and `EGRESS_ACTION_HANDLERS`, and `engine/actions.test.ts` fails a catalogue action no list holds. This closes Stage 3's "no action runs end to end".
99. **`WorkflowServices.egress`** is built once by `createWorkflowEgressConfig`, and the worker closes its code runner at shutdown, because idle children hold the event loop. `startWorkers` takes `workflows.inFlightCap`.
100. **`renderConfig` copies an Evaluate Code `source` unrendered.** Rendering read it as a template, so a source over 32 KiB, or holding `{{`, never reached the runner, and a valid `{{ path }}` in a JS string was filled in, against C113.8.
101. **The API process enqueues.** `ApiDependencies` gains `workflowSteps` and `workflowInFlightCap`, built in `container.ts`, so a run a route starts, resumes or tests no longer waits up to 15 s for the sweep. Without a producer the sweep still picks it up.
102. **`boundAdaptersOf` (`workflows/adapters.ts`) is the one reader of what a step can reach (Stage 1b amendment 52).** `config.workflows.slackBound` reads the two Slack variables by name alone, and `ApiDependencies.workflowAdapters` carries it with `codeEnabled`. Mail is bound in any sandbox environment. So the catalogue answers `code_disabled` by its name, publish no longer warns falsely, and compose passes the two names to the API, never a credential (`compose.test.sh` changed with the reason).
103. **Secrets at publish:** `facts.secrets` reads `listWorkflowSecretFacts`, and `hostOf` in `publish/rules.ts` reads `normalizeHostOf`. A test run's `allow-hosts` compares through `hostIsListed`.
104. **`send_inquiry_link_sms` passes the template's countries (C126.2),** and the canary no longer sets `workflow-sms-countries`.
105. **The stored API log masks `parameters` and `prefilled-value` at any depth (C143.5),** beside `comment`. Any other member of those names fails closed.
106. **An idempotency fingerprint drops what `redactKeys` drops (C125.5).** A stored digest of a short secret could be guessed offline. So two calls with one key that differ only in `secret-value` are one request, which replays.
107. **`WORKFLOW_ACTION_ERROR_TYPES` holds every code Stage 3 raises,** among them `object_not_found`, `action_input_invalid`, `unknown_effect`, `workflow_not_running`, the mail, Twilio and Slack codes. `slack_<code>` for Slack's own codes stays `unknown-error` by design of the table, and `docs/workflows/errors.md` says so.
108. **`country_code` joins `WORKFLOW_TRIGGER_ALIASES`,** a member alias of a government ID's `country`, so C114.4's own example publishes.
109. **`run_report`'s matches say what the job stored.** `matchFacts` reads `entity.{method,tier,confidence,approximate}`, then `components.methods`, then `match-types`, and `not_recorded` for a snapshot redaction took. It replaces the placeholder `method: 'name'`.
110. **`DECISION_EVIDENCE_KINDS` gains `step`,** so a Calculate that fell back on its default reaches a decision's evidence (C127.10).
111. **`find_or_create_account` on a declared field finds through a keyed digest.** `reference-id` is a plain column, so `<field-key>:<value>` would have left an identifier unsealed. It writes `<field-key>:<HMAC>` (`identifierKey`, kind `account-identifier`, input `<account type>|<field key>|<value>`), and the value stays sealed in `fields`. A rotated blind-index key finds nothing and creates a new account, the accepted cost of never merging. (Default, on the owner's review list.)
112. **`generateOneTimeLinkInTx` returns the row's id,** so `one-time-link-id` is `otl_<row id>`, no longer a piece of the token's hash.
113. **A child's biometric objects are deleted after the commit.** `redactChildInquiryInTx` dropped `biometric.storageKeys`, and no C16 purge reads them, so they stayed in storage after a redaction. `redactTransactionChildrenInTx` now answers `{ staged, storageKeys }`, `RedactedTransaction` gains `storageKeys`, and the service deletes them after each commit; a failed delete is logged by key and fails nothing (outcome 28).
114. **`childGrants` is exported,** and a test run's grant counts a child's.
115. **The flow shows the contest route, and `seed:dev` installs the recipes as paused drafts.** `FlowRenderer` passes `decision-contest-route` to `CompletionScreen`.
116. **Sync Graph has its race test on Postgres** (`actions/graph/sync-graph.integration.test.ts`): a step never says `synced: true` while its job is pending.
117. **A step logs how it ended (C117.11):** `workflow step finished`, with status, error code and type, and duration. Metrics remain unbuilt, because the codebase has no metrics facility. Carried.
118. **Four load-sensitive tests are deterministic (rule 6).** `pool.test.ts`'s scan uses a native `indexOf`, which read every candidate at no cost per byte. It failed under 48 busy loops and passes under the same. `card-guard.test.ts`'s budget is the same bytes in small pieces, measured beside it, because CPU time grows under contention on shared cores. `platform.integration.test.ts`'s running request holds until the other nine are answered. `currentPrompt` in `e2e/tests/support/flow.ts` waits past "Uploading photo N of 3…". The forced non-API gate passes in one call for the first time in this Workflow.
119. **The golden definitions hold as written,** and `withActionSchemas` is gone.
120. **`POST /workflows`' description says "with an empty draft",** and the OpenAPI snapshot is regenerated.

### New in Stage 4

121. **The `code-runner` service** joins the `app` profile: no network, a read-only root, uid 10001, no capability, `no-new-privileges`, `mem_limit` 640m, `pids_limit` 64, pool size 4 (`OPENVERIFY_CODE_RUNNER_POOL_SIZE`, `OPENVERIFY_CODE_RUNNER_MEMORY`), a shared volume `code-runner-socket`. In this profile `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_CODE_RUNNER` is fixed to the socket whatever `.env` says, so a copied `.env.example` never puts code in the worker's network. `code-runner.test.sh` proves the worker's own uid reaches the socket.
122. **The reference pages are generated.** `docs/workflows/{actions,errors,limits}.md` come from `workflows/reference-docs.ts` (`pnpm --filter @openverify/api workflow-docs:write`), and `reference-docs.test.ts` fails a stale file, because a list typed by hand drifts (rule 15). A change to the registry or a limit reruns it.
123. **The e2e stack** holds a throwaway certificate for `localhost` (`RECEIVER_TLS`, made with `openssl` at each stack start, gitignored), which the worker trusts through `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, because the HTTPS action reaches `https:` alone. `startWebhookReceiver({ tls })` serves it. The stack sets `OPENVERIFY_WORKFLOW_HTTP_ALLOW_LOOPBACK` and binds Slack to its sandbox.
124. **CI.** The infra job runs `infra/tests/code-runner.test.sh` whole, which builds the image and proves the container, so the job is live for this one test. The host-test floor rises from 1,333 to 1,334. No other job changed.
125. **The Validation order (amends Validation; outcome 3).** As outcome 3 lists it, with these two lines: `pnpm test` is one forced call for every package but the API, then the API as two blob shards and a merge, with `reportsDirectory` **outside** the blob directory, because `--merge-reports` refuses a directory that holds coverage directories (this corrects rule 8's recipe); and `pnpm --filter @openverify/api workflow-docs:write` joins `openapi:write`, as a test rather than a step. A lone shard exits 1 on the coverage gate, as it always did: the merge is the gate.

### Carried forward

126. **Not fixed, each named above:** `rerun_report`'s false timeout (84), the recipe's one report (64), install-wide host concurrency (92), run metrics (117), the trigger filter member (67), `MASKED_ATTRIBUTE_NAMES`' second copy (28), and a synthetic payload not validated against its event's schema (43).
127. **A gate's review record has empty evidence (86), and the `rule 5` gaps of 47 and 89** stand as records. No test was weakened.
128. **The commit trailer.** This session's system reminder asks for a `Claude-Session` line on commits, and team rule 4 says none. The first commits of Stage 4 carry none, following rule 4, and from `0376a5b5`'s successor on they carry it, following the reminder; the commits are not amended. The orchestrator settles rule 4.


## Orchestrator decisions after Workflow 8A (2026-11-03)

Binding on Workflows 8B and 8C. Each one comes from a question an 8A report left open.

1. **The commit trailer (settles amendment 128).** The trailer was retired only because it named an old session. An agent ends each commit message with the `Claude-Session` line its own session's system reminder gives, when it gives one, never another session's, and adds none if no reminder gives one. 8A's commits are not amended.
2. **Stage 3 gates in 8B.** A Stage 3 agent runs no full-stack e2e and only the whole test scripts of the packages it changed, the API as four blob shards and a merge, then `pnpm test:integration` and the mocked flow suite if it changed `apps/flow`. In 8A, parallel agents ended each other's stacks, lost at least eight gate attempts and gated a tree that was still moving. A lease would only have queued them. Foundation, contracts and integration keep their gates, and `integration-8b` runs every whole-repo gate alone on the final tree. This amends the gates of "Rules for every agent" for those agents; it moves a gate and weakens none.
3. **`pnpm test:integration` under parallel load** runs as separate calls, all of which are the gate: `pnpm --filter @openverify/db test:integration`; from `apps/api`, `--project graph`; from `apps/api`, `--project api --shard=i/N`, N at least 3. An `--project api` shard does not run the graph file, and `api-engine`'s gate lost it that way.
4. **The recurring filter stays a C114 condition (settles Stage 2 amendment 22; amends C118.4 and C137.3).** 8A built and documented it, and a second grammar would break published definitions. `case` joins through `triggerDocumentOf`. C137.3's "reuses this grammar" no longer holds.
5. **8B's joins in 8A files (amends C140.7).** The rule "no 8B agent edits an 8A file" cannot hold: 14 8A files need a join. `foundation-8b` makes each join a call to a stub in the owner's file, and the stub answers what the 8A file answers today, so behaviour moves only when the owner fills it.
6. **The four `case-*` rate-limit policies** go in `contracts-8b`'s Common, and `api-cases`' retention step is the reader of `case-retention-days`, which nothing reads today (review-8b R15).

## Workflow 8B Stage 1a amendments (contracts-8b)

Recorded by `contracts-8b` at base `367560b4`. Items 1 to 9 are the re-verification, done before any 8B code; the later items are the decisions of the Stage 1a commits. The inventory's section 13 holds the sites, and the evidence is `docs/tdd/phase8-contracts-8b.tdd.md`. Each item binds every later 8B agent. Items marked (default) are on the owner's review list.

### Re-verification of 8A as built and the Phase 7 interfaces

1. **PHASE 8A AS BUILT holds, line by line, at `367560b4`.** The registry (`createActionRegistry`, `RESERVED_CASE_ACTION_KEYS`, `ACTION_KEYS`, the output marks), the object-type table, `HANDLERS` and `engine/actions.test.ts`, `ACTION_SINK_ROWS`, `HUMAN_STEP_ENTRIES` (with `case.resolved` as `case-resolution`), `CASE_HOLDS_SUBJECT` answering false, `currentCase` on `ScopeContext` and `RunSeed`, the fail-closed stops at `triggerDocumentOf`, `listWorkflowObjectsAfter`, `objectIdFits`, `publish/rules.ts`, the local `ctmpl` pattern in `decisions.ts`, `KIND_OF_PREFIX`, `REFERENCE_MEMBERS` and `WORKFLOW_REFERENCE_KINDS` knowing no case object, and `caused_by_bulk_id` with no format.
2. **The Phase 7 interfaces hold.** `writeCsv(header, rows)` quotes every cell, writes CRLF records and throws `TypeError` on a row that does not fit its header; `neutraliseCell` is as PHASE 7B says; `readCsv(bytes, options)` takes `encoding`, `maxRows`, `maxRecordCharacters` and `headerProblems`; `identifierKey` holds `identifier-transaction-field`, with its rebuild third in `apps/api/scripts/blind-index-rebuild.ts`.
3. **`fieldsValidatorOf` caches by `content.id` alone, so C130.2's "`(kind, version id)`" needs no second key:** a `ctmplv_` id can never equal a `txntpv_` id. `transactionTypeVersionProblems` is not reusable as it stands, because it also runs the status and relation rules of a transaction type. `caseFieldSchemaProblems` (`packages/core/src/cases/`) runs its field half and adds the case subset of F37.
4. **`MASKED_ATTRIBUTE_NAMES` and `MASKED_ATTRIBUTES` already differ.** The first holds neither `PII_DECISION_ATTRIBUTES`, `PII_SANDBOX_MESSAGE_ATTRIBUTES` nor `PII_IMPORTER_ATTRIBUTES`, none of which a trigger view reads today. `foundation-8b` joins every `PII_CASE_*` list to both, as PHASE 8A AS BUILT says. Carried: amendment 28's move of the list into contracts would close this.
5. **C129.1 names no id for three rows 8B needs.** The bulk action's id is what `caused_by_bulk_id` holds, and C137.4's "the answer lists per-case outcomes when done" needs something to read. A notification (C134.4) needs an id for `POST /dashboard/notifications/:id/read`. They take `cblk` and `unot` (item 14).
6. **C129.5 lists no read of three things the console has to read:** a case's history (C131.4 calls it the timeline, and it is unbounded, so it stays off the resource, as owner decision 8 after 7B kept a transaction's), a case's held objects with their attached state (C136.3), and a bulk action's outcomes (C137.4). Items 19 and 22 add them. Template versions take the Phase 7 routes (owner decision 7 after 7B).
7. **C129.7 names `object_already_attached`, and C136.2 says a repeat is `no_change`.** C136.2 is the behaviour, so no route raises it. The code stays frozen with its golden problem, because Acceptance asks for every problem, and `docs/cases/` says it is reserved. (Default.)
8. **`create_case` has no `name` member,** although C115.6 and the 8A followups speak of `create_case.name` as a plain sink. A case's name is generated from the template key and the counter (C129.2), so there is nothing to render. The plain sinks of the case actions are their tags, statuses, resolutions and status tags (item 28).
9. **Three deletes of a `files` row are not a `deleted_at` (a trap the plan does not name).** `replaceVerificationFile`, `replaceServerWrittenFile` and the discarded selfie capture delete the row itself, so a cascade from `files(id)` would leave a rendition's storage object behind. The inventory's section 13.2 holds the sites, and `foundation-8b`'s (F) checkpoint decides the foreign key with that in view.

### Decisions of the Common commits

10. **Prefixes (C129.1).** The public `case`, `ctmpl`, `cqueue`, `cscm` and `cap`, then ours: `ctmplv`, `catt`, `cact`, `cslap`, `ccal`, `cexp`, and `cblk` (a bulk action) and `unot` (a notification), each in both tables. `contestRouteSchema` now reads `idSchema('ctmpl')`. A rendition row's id is left to (F) in call 2.
11. **Scopes (C129.4, decision 68).** `cases:read`, `cases:write`, `cases:assign`, `cases:redact`, `cases:export`, `case-templates:read`, `:write`, `:publish`, `case-queues:read` and `:write`, after `devices:write`. Holders:
    - `admin`: all of them.
    - `developer`: all but `cases:export`.
    - `reviewer`: `cases:read`, `cases:write`, `cases:assign`, `case-templates:read` and `case-queues:read`.
    - `viewer`: the three reads, through the `:read` rule.

    The dashboard area is "Cases", after "Transactions". `case-queues:write` also covers policies and calendars, as C129.4 says.
12. **A key's `case-template-ids` (C129.4, P14).** The attribute is optional and served when the record carries it, as `pii-masked` is, so a server before `foundation-8b`'s column sends nothing. It is set when a key is minted (`createApiKeyBodySchema`), never by `PATCH`, and takes at most 100 distinct `ctmpl_` ids (ours, provisional). An empty list reads no restricted template. A template with no `visible-to-roles` needs no entry. `assertKeyTemplateRestrictionInTx` holds the list to what the creator sees. (Default.) A clone copies the restriction, because it copies the scopes.
13. **Problems (C129.7).** `CASE_PROBLEM_STATUSES` freezes C129.7's sixteen codes and six of ours, each with a golden problem in `contracts/fixtures/problems/cases/`. Ours are `case_redacted` (410), `case_template_archived` (409), `case_template_not_published` (422), `case_template_version_not_draft` (409), `case_template_key_taken` (409) and `case_bulk_too_large` (422). Statuses the plan does not state, also ours:
    - 409: `object_already_attached` (amendment 7), `attachment_infected`, `attachment_scan_pending` and `case_version_conflict`;
    - 422: every other code.

    `case_version_conflict` carries `lock-version`, and `calendar_invalid` carries `dates`, which are not PII. No case problem carries a field value, a comment, a filename or an address.
14. **`temporal-polyfill` 1.0.5 reaches `apps/api` through the catalog (V14).** Its licence row names the use: business time in `services/cases/sla/`, kept out of `packages/core` (F30).
15. **`case-action` is a run's creator (C120.1, 8A amendment 34).** It joins `WORKFLOW_RUN_CREATOR_TYPES`, `workflowRunCreatorIdentifierSchema` (with its `cact_` id) and `RESOURCE_TYPES`. `0028_phase8b` starts here holding only the re-made `workflow_runs_creator_valid`. The column is text, so no enum label is added. `migration-0028.test.ts` proves the upgrade from `0027`. `foundation-8b` extends the file by 8A's amendment 17 recipe with `--name phase8b`.
16. **Rate limits (C143.3, review-8b R15).** `case-search` is 120 a minute per key, `case-create` 600 an hour per environment, `case-exports` 10 an hour per environment, and `case-attachments` 60 a minute per principal, each with its window and console label. Each route or action owner enforces its policy with a test of its fail mode.
17. **The recurring filter stays a C114 condition over `trigger` with `on-null` (orchestrator decision 4 after 8A).** C137.3's "reuses this grammar" no longer holds. The case-search grammar is `POST /cases/search`'s alone, and `case` gets its trigger view through `triggerDocumentOf`. Only the comment on `recurringTriggerConfigSchema.filter` changed. The schema did not.
18. **The log rule (C143.5).** `PHASE5_FOLDERS` gains `packages/core/src/cases/**`, `apps/api/src/services/cases/**`, `jobs/cases/**`, `providers/attachment-scanner/**`, every case route file, `inquiry-review-requests.ts`, and `routes/dashboard/{notifications,file-renditions}.ts`. `REFUSED_LOG_KEY_PATTERN` gains `statements?` and `mentions?`. `message` and `comments?` were refused already.

### Decisions of the (C) commits

19. **Routes beyond C129.5 (amendment 6), frozen by `foundation-8b`:**
    - `GET /cases/:id/history` (`cases:read`): newest first, paged by the row's number, `case-history-entry`.
    - `GET /cases/:id/objects` (`cases:read`): `case-held-object`, with `filter[object-type]`, paged by its own `<case>:<object>` id.
    - `GET /case-bulk-actions/:id`: read by its creating principal alone, as an export is (P26), under `cases:read`.
    - Template routes, following owner decision 7 after 7B: `POST /case-templates/:id/archive`, `GET` and `POST /case-template-versions`, `GET` and `PATCH /case-template-versions/:id`, and `POST /case-template-versions/:id/publish`.
    - `GET /dashboard/notifications` (`filter[unread]`) and `POST /dashboard/notifications/:id/read`, serving `user-notification`.
20. **The case resource.**
    - **Status.** `status` is a status key (`^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$`, as a field key reads). The defaults are `open`, `pending`, `approved` and `declined`, labelled `Open`, `Pending`, `Approved` and `Declined`, so a resolution of `Approved` matches C131.1's "the status label when it is one of them". (Default.) `meta.status` takes a key, never a label.
    - **Actors.** `assigner-type` gains `api-key`, because a key with `cases:assign` assigns. Each actor pair is whole or null, and its id prefix follows its type.
    - **Held objects.** A held-object relationship names its kind's newest 50 at most (`CASE_LIMITS['held-objects-served']`, after the public search's 50). `held-object-counts` gives the totals, and `GET /cases/:id/objects` every row. (Default.)
    - **Fields and families.** `fields` is a transaction's `{ type, value }` map, PII, member-wise masked. `sla-expires-at` is served now, `null` until the (Q) columns land. `sla-state` and `attachments` join in call 2.
    - **Includes.** `CASE_INCLUDES` are `case-template`, `case-comments` and the seven held kinds. `case-queue` joins with (Q).
21. **Bodies.**
    - **The public bodies.** `POST /cases` carries `meta.object-ids` and `meta.sla-expires-in-seconds` beside `data.attributes`. `PATCH` takes the queue and fields in `data.attributes`, and the SLA and `lock-version` in `meta`.
    - **What the service refuses, by name.** `sla-expires-in-seconds` is a plain integer in the schema, so the service answers the named `sla_out_of_range` rather than a validation error. `object-ids` takes any id, so the service answers `object_type_unsupported` for a kind a case cannot hold.
    - **Tags (C136.5).** `tag-id` reads as the tag's name, because OpenVerify's tags are names with no id of their own. (Default.)
    - **The review request (C128.7).** `POST /inquiries/:id/review-requests` takes `meta.statement` (1 to 10,000 characters, PII) and answers the case it opened.
22. **Search (C137.2).** The tree is written out to two levels, never recursively, so a body cannot nest past the stack. A leaf's `attribute` is one of C137.2's sixteen or `fields.<key>`, and its `value` is a scalar. Each `and` and `or` takes 1 to 20 clauses. Sort is ours: the four timestamps, `asc` or `desc`. `page` takes `after` or `before`, not both, and a size up to 100. The answer adds `links.prev`, for the public page's two directions. `query` is PII, so a stored log masks it whole.
23. **Bulk (C137.4).** The body is `{ data: { attributes: { action, case-ids | search } }, meta }`, with a `meta` of its own per action. The answer is 202 with a `case-bulk-action`: counts, and per-case outcomes `applied`, `no_change` or `refused`. A refusal has its problem code, and nothing else does.
24. **Events (C129.6).**
