The existing UI specs that wait for inquiry images (`ui-selfie`, `ui-lifecycle`, `ui-documents`, `ui-console-gaps`) now load renditions and are `fix-console`'s to confirm.
27. **The compare panel without a portrait box (C142.3, default).** The API serves no portrait box, so the panel cannot open zoomed to the portrait. It shows the NFC chip portrait when one exists; otherwise the whole ID front, saying that the box is missing and the reviewer zooms by hand. When no `selfie_id_comparison` names a document, it shows the inquiry's newest government ID front with the alt text "Government ID front, not compared" and a sentence that nothing compared it; the similarity, threshold, basis and result stay the check's own. No image is shown without `pii:view`. Serving the box in `id_portrait_detection`'s metadata is `fix-api`'s.
28. **Exporting from the case page (C142.2, default).** The case page links `/cases/exports`, shown only with `cases:export`, because `POST /case-exports` takes a filter or a search and neither can name one case (`CASE_SEARCH_ATTRIBUTES` has no case id). A `case_id` search leaf would let the page export its own case.
29. **Modules (C142.2, default).** The modules follow the pinned version's `layout-modules`, each filtered by its `required-permissions`. Without `case-templates:read` the console uses its copy of the API's `DEFAULT_LAYOUT_MODULES` (`load-case.ts` `DEFAULT_MODULES`), and the status control shows a forbidden panel. Phase 7's modules (list matches, graph, signals, device, location, transaction) read the case's first held inquiry, account or transaction the reader can read. Live states are read at most 10 per held kind; past that a row says "Open the object to see its state". A held object whose scope the reader lacks reads "No access", with no link and no API call. A report run has no live read and no page.
30. **Decisions on the case page (C142.4).** A held inquiry is approved, declined or marked for review only through `decideHeldInquiry`, a case-page server action calling the inquiry's own routes under `inquiries:write`, each behind a confirmation, refreshing both the case and the inquiry page; the inquiry page's `transitionInquiry` is not reused because it refreshes only that page. Reopen stays on the inquiry page.
31. **Page chords (C142.3, WCAG 2.1.4).** Alt+Shift+C focuses the new comment, Alt+Shift+V the compare panel and Alt+Shift+T the timeline, read by physical key code; they are on by default, listed with `aria-keyshortcuts` in the "Keyboard shortcuts" dialog and switched off there, the choice kept in `localStorage` under `openverify.case-page-shortcuts` with every access in try/catch. No chord assigns, resolves or decides. The compare panel's own keys (`+`, `-`, `0`, the arrows, `R`) act only while the viewer has focus, and each has a button.
32. **The print view (C142.5).** The rules sit in `app/cases/[id]/print.css`, imported by the page, every one inside `@media print` and scoped with `body:has([data-case-print])`, so no other page's printout changes; `globals.css` and the root layout are untouched. The footer's "contains personal data" follows the reader's `pii:view`.
33. **The upload route (C142.7).** It sends its own Idempotency-Key (`randomUUID`) on each upload, refuses a declared `Content-Length` above `UPLOAD_LIMIT_BYTES` (10 MiB plus 64 KiB, the API's body limit) before reading the body, and answers in its own sentences chosen by the API's problem code. The constants live in `lib/api/case-attachments.ts`, because route files export only handlers. `lib/api/case-comments.ts` and `case-actions.ts` are not created: the `@openverify/node` groups serve them.
34. **TDD gap recorded (rule 1).** Slice 2's client forms (status control, assignment, comment, queue, SLA, add objects, case action, upload, print) and the comment tokenizer were built inside the case page's GREEN; their interaction tests came after and passed at once, except one upload bug they found and fixed (the file is read from the input's `files`, not through `FormData` `instanceof File`). Every later slice went stub, RED, GREEN.
35. **Axe is not run (C142.6).** As item 25: no axe package, and only the dependency owner may add one. The checks done are keyboard-only Playwright, role and name queries, `aria-keyshortcuts`, polite live regions and focus-scoped keys; once the package lands, `ui-reviewer.spec.ts` asserts no violations after each page load.

## Workflow 8C review amendments

Recorded by the API fixer of the Workflow 8C review. Evidence: `docs/tdd/phase8-review-fixes-api.tdd.md` (the console fixer's is `phase8-review-fixes-console.tdd.md`). Each item binds every later phase. Items marked (default) are on the owner's review list.

### API, packages and docs

1. **fetch_object answers the flattened view (K8-17, item 107).** For an inquiry, account, report, transaction and verification, `steps.<id>.output.object` is `buildTriggerView` of the resource as an API client reads it under the run's mask (a transaction's with its `included`), as the case row already was; the output stays PII whole (C120.6). A route on `steps.<id>.output.object.status` reads the stored status. A verification's view stays untyped (`UNKNOWN_OBJECT`), so the check still refuses a literal comparison on it.
2. **A catch-all route in a decision's path (R001, C128.1).** A route with no condition adds no digest to `conditionSha256`; the routes taken are recorded as before. A decision reached through the "otherwise" route of a conditional now writes its record instead of ending `execution_error`.
3. **update_object on a case (K8-18, item 108).** `getCustomFields` reads a case's stored fields and answers each typed `{ type, value }` as its value, so `append`, `push` and `remove` start from what the case holds. A row of the object-type table that lacks `tag` or `update` fails the step `action_input_invalid` (`tagRow`, `updateRow`), never `applied` with nothing done.
4. **One masked list (K8-11; amendments 28, 126).** `PII_MASKED_ATTRIBUTES` in `@openverify/contracts` (`masked-attributes.ts`) is the API's `MASKED_ATTRIBUTES` and the engine's `MASKED_ATTRIBUTE_NAMES`. The engine had lacked `file`, `message-html`, `message-subject` and `message-text`, which a run's trigger view now taints. A name is added in one place.
5. **C124.7 per install (K8-8; amendments 92, 126).** `KeyValueCache` gains `acquireLease(key, leaseId, limit, ttlSeconds)` and `releaseLease(key, leaseId)`: a sorted set in Valkey scored by when each lease lapses, taken in one MULTI, so five requests a host and environment hold across every worker and a dead worker's place frees itself after 60 s (the timeout ceiling plus the 30 s margin). A step whose lease Valkey cannot grant, Valkey down included, fails closed with `rate_limited`, retryable. The amendment's "atomic counter" is this lease set, because a plain counter a crashed worker raised stays raised while traffic refreshes its TTL. `docs/workflows/https-request.md` no longer says five per worker.
6. **case.sla-expired at once (K8-23; items 72, 104).** `CaseStatusChange.events` carries the caller's publisher; a status change stages the event with its own events and dispatches it after the commit. A caller handing none keeps the outbox.
7. **Case field helpers (K8-31; items 85, 116).** `changedCaseFieldsInTx` and `recordCaseFieldsInTx` (`services/cases/service.ts`) are shared by `updateCase` and the case row; each caller still writes the row itself, so `PATCH` keeps one `lock-version` step for fields and queue together.
8. **set_case_sla answers a paused SLA (K8-26; items 85, 109; C140.3).** Its output's `sla-expires-at` is nullable and `sla-state` joins it; in a pausing status the step sets the SLA paused, as `PATCH` does, and answers `{ sla-expires-at: null, sla-state: paused }` instead of `action_refused`. `platform_file_not_allowed` is a step error typed `configuration-error`. `docs/workflows/errors.md` regenerated.
9. **The workflow-step sweep isolates a row (R002).** A reclaim, wait timeout or run deadline that throws is logged by phase, ids and error name and left for a later sweep; the after-commit work and `dispatchReadySteps` run in `finally`. `EngineDeps.logger` takes `warn`.
10. **Rendition attempts (R003).** `RENDITION_ATTEMPTS` is 3 (ours, as `scan-attempts`, P23): the third retryable failure, or a source already at the cap, is `failed` with `rendition_unavailable`. No back-off window is added.
11. **The tagged search leaf (R007, in part).** It compiles to `openverify_lower_tags(tags) @> array[lower(value)]`, which `cases_lower_tags_idx` serves. The sort keys keep their `coalesce` form (not changed: the cursor's keyset is built on them).
12. **A case transaction retried on 40P01 and 40001 (K8-35, item 77).** `caseChange` runs a transaction Postgres aborted as a deadlock or serialisation failure again from the start, at most three times in all (`CASE_TRANSACTION_ATTEMPTS`), and dispatches only the committed run's events; the queue-before-case and case-before-queue orders stay as they are. Proven on Postgres 17 by a forced deadlock in `queues-races.integration.test.ts`.
13. **Strict case audit metadata (K8-22, item 114, P19).** `services/cases/audit.ts` holds the twenty case, comment, template, version, case-action, bulk-action and review-request actions to strict schemas (ids, member names, field and status keys, counts, enums, the two redaction causes); `auditEntryOf` checks every case action, and `audit.test.ts` fails an audited case action with no schema, scanning the sources. `case-bulk-action.created` joined the item's list.
14. **Docs.** The two redaction header comments are corrected (K8-4); `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` §2.1.15 records the console's eighteen workflow-editor rows and drops the planned `@xyflow/react` row (R009); `openapi.test.ts` resolves every `$ref` of the snapshot (K8-15).
15. **e2e (K8-3 and the measurement).** `e2e/README.md` says a used stack holds one clean pass: the review's full pass sent 9 OTP codes from 127.0.0.1 (at `9cd59cb0`). The suite with the console's `ui-*` specs is 127 tests in 64 files, in shards of 44, 41 and 42 that took 3.9, 4.9 and 5.0 minutes plus 1.2 for the stack start at a load of about 1 to 4: three shards stand, each under 8 minutes, and CI's one e2e job and its timeouts are unchanged.

### Carried, not done in this call (each stays open)

16. **K8-1** pypdf 6.21.2 is 14 days old at 2026-11-04T16:45Z; 6.21.1 was the newest eligible and is not taken, because it misses the item's target and would be a second bump of the same files a day apart. **K8-2** has no pypdfium2 release past 5.14.0 and no owner answer on HarfBuzz. **K8-9** (no metrics facility), **K8-14** (no migration in 8C) and **K8-24** (no drain job fits as a local fix) go to Phase 9 to 11 as their items say. **K8-6, K8-7, K8-10, K8-12, K8-13, K8-19, K8-20, K8-21, K8-25, K8-27, K8-28, K8-29, K8-30, K8-32, K8-33, K8-34** and the review's **R004, R005, R006, R008** and R007's sort half are carried with the reasons in the fixer's report; measured branch coverage at the end of this call: `workflows/runs` 74.30% (159 of 214), `services/cases` 78.55% (1,355 of 1,725), `workflows/engine` 80.45%.

### Console

Console fixes: recorded by the orchestrator.

## Workflow 8D Stage 1 amendments

Recorded by the 8D Stage 2 verifier from the contract deviations of the six Stage 1 reports (console-editor's two calls, console-ops, platform-carry, api-carry-cases and api-carry-engine). Evidence: `docs/tdd/phase8-console-editor.tdd.md`, `phase8-console-ops.tdd.md`, `phase8-platform-carry.tdd.md`, `phase8-api-carry-cases.tdd.md` and `phase8-api-carry-engine.tdd.md`. Each item binds the 8D fixers and every later phase. Items marked (default) are on the owner's review list. api-carry-cases ended `partial`: K8-27 is decided (item 17 of its subsection) but not landed, and passes to the fixers.

### console-editor (both calls)

1. **The outline is a flat tree (C141.2).** The definition outline renders its visible items as one flat APG tree carrying aria-level, aria-posinset and aria-setsize, with an indent per level, never as groups nested inside their parent item: axe refuses a parent whose visible text holds its children's (label-content-name-mismatch). Item names, keys, aria-expanded and the single tab stop do not change.
2. **The outline's menu (C141.2).** Enter on an outline item opens its menu; Space only selects. A pointer reaches the same menu through "Actions for the selected item" beside the outline. No button lives inside a tree item. A disabled entry stays listed with aria-disabled and says why. Together with the menu's entries (set the trigger, add a step after, add a branch, move, connect, delete with confirmation, settings) this is the keyboard path for authoring a whole definition; the raw JSON stays as a fallback.
3. **The draft page reads the catalogue (C141.4).** The draft page calls `GET /workflow-actions` only for a principal holding `workflows:write`. Tests of the draft page serve the catalogue in their setup.
4. **A new workflow starts from `emptyDefinition()` (C113.5).** A workflow with neither a draft nor a published version opens with no trigger and the output node present, since the draft check needs an output; the 8C start from `steps: []` is replaced.
5. **Step templates (default, provisional).** A new wait-time step waits 3,600 s. A new wait-events step uses subject `trigger.id`, events `[inquiry.completed]`, timeout-seconds 86,400 and on-timeout continue. A new conditional has one catch-all route (key `route-<n>`, label "Route n") and else, both leading where the replaced edge led. A new parallel has branches `a` and `b` and the join `<id>_join`. A new action step has config `{}`. A step id derives from its action key or type; a repeated name gains " 2", " 3" and so on.
6. **Trigger defaults (default).** `setTrigger` gives each trigger type the config its schema requires: trigger-event `[inquiry.completed]`; trigger-verification `[verification.passed]`; trigger-recurring every-minutes 60, time-zone UTC, object-type inquiry; trigger-direct object-type inquiry; every other type `{}`. allow-self-trigger and one-run-per-subject survive a trigger change. An empty trigger next port is led to the output.
7. **Client port rules are an aid (C141.2, C141.3).** `canConnect` refuses an unknown source or port, the trigger or a module-input as a target, a self-edge, and a target that already leads back to the source (a loop body's back-edge excepted). It does not mirror the server's parallel-branch and loop-body region rules: the API's graph check stays the authority and reports those by pointer.
8. **Move, delete and connect (default).** Move up or down applies only to a step with one way in and one way on, past a neighbour of the same shape; anything else says why it cannot move. Deleting a step leads its incoming edges to its continuation (a step's next, a conditional's else, a loop's done, a parallel's join's next). A parallel is deleted with its join. Steps reachable only through the deleted step stay as "Not connected" and are not cascade-deleted. The output and a lone join cannot be deleted. The canvas deletes nothing (deleteKeyCode null): every delete goes through the outline's confirmation.
9. **The palette (C122.5, ADR 0002 3e).** An action whose `available` is false is listed as "Not configured: …" with its reason and can still be added; publish then warns and a run reaching it errors `provider_not_configured`. An action whose triggers exclude the definition's trigger is listed with its reason and cannot be added. Loop and module steps are not offered in Phase 8.
10. **Undo and its keys (C141.4, WCAG 2.1.4).** History is ours, 100 entries, one per gesture; selection is not history. Ctrl or Cmd+Z, Ctrl or Cmd+Shift+Z and Ctrl+Y act only inside the editor region and never in a text field, which keeps the browser's own undo there; each has a button. Rename typing, raw JSON typing and the nudges of one canvas node each coalesce into one entry until the field blurs or another gesture starts.
11. **The canvas renders only where it can measure (C141.1).** The @xyflow/react canvas renders after hydration and only where `ResizeObserver` exists; otherwise the editor says the outline lists every step.
12. **Handle names (C141.3, WCAG 2.5.8).** A canvas handle is a generic div, on which axe refuses an aria-label (aria-prohibited-attr), so each handle carries a `title` naming its port (for example "Route high of Conditional: Check") and no ARIA attribute. Handles have a 24 px hit area. Keyboard connection is the outline's "Connect next step to…".
13. **Library quirks recorded.** At @xyflow/react 12.12.0 the node description key `node.a11yDescription.keyboardDisabled` is the one shown while keyboard access is on; `CANVAS_ARIA_LABELS` puts the arrow-key wording under the key that is shown. The "React Flow" attribution stays visible (decision 73), restyled in the console's text colour on its surface for 4.5:1 contrast.
14. **The canvas summary's counts (C141.3).** "Steps" leaves out the output and joins; "branches" counts each conditional's routes plus its else and each parallel's branches; "errors" counts the last check's errors, never its warnings.
15. **Scope of `ui-workflow-editor.spec.ts` (C141.8).** Its error-badge test introduces the refused value through the raw JSON fallback, because the outline never writes an invalid value. Axe runs through `expectNoAxeViolations` on every page the spec opens.
16. **Problem and step-error words (C112.8, C113.6, C120.2).** The console words every engine step error code, every definition problem code and warning, and every workflow problem code. Because the dashboard does not depend on `@openverify/workflow-engine` in Phase 8, the words test reads the engine's codes through the generated `docs/workflows/errors.md` (kept current by `reference-docs.test.ts`) and the problem codes from `WORKFLOW_PROBLEM_CODES` in the contracts, so a new engine code fails the console's test once the docs are regenerated. Moving the definition codes into `@openverify/contracts` as a list is the later replacement.
17. **The errors panel (C113.6, C141.4).** Each problem reads as the console's words, then the API's own detail, then the code. When the definition changes after a check, the panel says its pointers may have moved and keeps the list until the next check.
18. **The variable picker's scope is computed in the browser (C115.3; default).** No route serves the scope at a node in Phase 8, so the editor computes it from what the API served: the trigger view from the contracts' `eventPayloadSchema` through `z.toJSONSchema()`, PII by each member's mark and by `PII_MASKED_ATTRIBUTES`; inputs and variables by the C115.3 DSL with its ` pii` suffix (an input not required is nullable); `current_run`; and the outputs of every step that dominates the node, by the catalogue's output-schema with its PII marks (every output of a continue-on-error step nullable; a wait for events adds resolved-by). The API's typing stays the authority (C113.5). Not mirrored: the trigger alias table; a parallel branch's outputs after its join; loop items. Serving the scope from the API is a later item.
19. **The condition builder (C114, C141.4; default).** It writes `===`, `!==`, `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=` on one path and one literal, `=== null` for "is unknown (null)", AND within a group and OR between groups; no condition is a catch-all route; routes are ordered and else stays last. A condition it did not write (list predicates, calculations, `!`) is left untouched and edited in the definition. A nullable path is offered only "is" and "is unknown", never "is not" or a bare boolean. Once a comparison reads a nullable path, the on-null choice appears with no default and the builder says the check refuses the condition (`on_null_missing`) until one is chosen: a route can error, skip the route or take another route by key; a trigger's criteria or filter can error, admit or skip; a wait's filter can error, keep waiting or accept. A PII string or untyped path is never offered for comparison with typed text (C114.5). The while-condition and list-predicate positions are not offered in Phase 8.
20. **Step forms (C141.4; default).** A config member is typed by its JSON Schema: string as text; integer and number as a number or exactly one `{{ path }}`; boolean as Not set, Yes or No; enum or const as a select; anything else (objects, arrays, unions, `$ref`) as JSON. An empty control leaves the member out. A value not of its type is said at its field and not applied. A field whose schema pattern names secrets is a secret reference, hinted `{{ secrets.<key> }}`, with no variable picker. A field's typing is one undo entry; a form edit keeps the step selected and focus in place. Wait steps use the same controls. An `evaluate_code` step links "Test this step" to the test-run panel with start-step and end-step set to it.
21. **The egress mark (C121.4, C115.6; default).** The step form shows "Egress: personal data leaves OpenVerify in this step (<paths>)", naming the `egress:<step id>` acknowledgement and `pii:view`, when the catalogue's kind is egress and the settings reference a PII path, a member below one, or an object holding one. It is an aid; publish's taint over the whole graph is the authority.
22. **Decision templates in the palette (C128.5).** console-ops' decision templates appear as the palette group "Decision templates". Adding one places its steps, without its trigger, where the step goes; paths that led to the template's output lead where the place led. A taken step id is replaced by a free one and every `steps.<id>` path in the template follows. The case template placeholder, decision basis and contest route are left for the operator.
23. **The run page's path (C141.6).** With `workflows:read` the run page also reads `GET /workflow-versions/:id` and `GET /workflow-actions`; without it, it says the path needs that permission and reads nothing more. Only the graph's structure (ids, types, names, action keys, route and branch keys and labels, edges, layout) reaches the browser, never a step's configuration, condition or mapping.
24. **How the path is drawn (C141.6; default).** A node's status is its top-level step row's, else its latest iteration's; the trigger reads Started. An edge is taken when both ends ran and its source led on (succeeded, failed, timed out or skipped). Statuses show as an icon plus a word (▶ Started, ✓ Succeeded, ✕ Failed, ⏱ Timed out, ⏸ Waiting, … Running, ○ Pending or Ready, ↷ Skipped, ⊘ Canceled, – Not reached). Taken edges are drawn heavier and labelled "taken by this run" or "not taken". "The run reached N of M steps" leaves out the output and joins. Beta badges come from the catalogue's stage; each match in a served output shows method, tier, stage and confidence; a masked output shows none.
25. **TDD gaps recorded (rule 1).** Some RED commits carried lint errors (unused bindings, a non-null assertion, restrict-template-expressions) fixed in their GREEN without changing an assertion; one GREEN (aeb482b3) failed tsc until stub commit f59323a0. Three authoring tests changed in GREEN only to await the editor's focus frame. The full-stack spec was written after the unit GREENs, and its first runs were the RED for the three axe fixes. The draft page's RSC payload test was written after its GREEN, and the run page's RSC test through the path passed in RED; both stand as guards.

### console-ops

1. **The reviewer forms test (rule 6).** `useFormAction` shows its success toast inside the action, before React ends the transition, so the toast is not the end of an action. The test waits for the next button by its idle name and asserts it is enabled. No retry, timeout change or skip; the product is unchanged.
2. **Promote (C112.2; default).** Offered with `workflows:publish` and a live version, never during a rollout, to the other environments in the session's `meta.environments`. The dialog chooses the target and says the copy is a draft, never replaces a draft and never publishes. `draft_exists` and `promote_unresolved` are shown as served.
3. **Cancel older runs (C116.2; default).** Offered with `workflow-runs:write` on every published version after version 1, because the version list never holds archived versions. The success toast counts the cancelled runs, and the words say effects already made stay made.
4. **Run filters and cursors (C141.6; default).** `run-status`, `run-version`, `run-subject` and `step-errors` live in the URL, each checked by the contract's own schema; a malformed value is left out, named in a polite status and never sent. Each list has its own cursor (`versions-after`, `runs-after`, `deliveries-after`), and each pagination keeps the other cursors and the filters. This settles 8C Stage 1 amendment 13.
5. **Grant and egress marks (C141.5).** A null grant reads "Derived at publish"; an empty egress list reads "No personal data leaves"; an egress mark reads "<step> sends personal data to <destination> (N paths)". Paths are never listed.
6. **The test-run panel (C120.7, C126.4, C141.9).** It lives at `/workflows/[id]/test-runs` under `workflows:read` and `workflows:trigger`, with the URL contract `?start-step=&end-step=&version-id=` (the draft when version-id is absent) plus `run=<wfr>` for a started run. Outside a sandbox environment it shows an informational "Sandbox only" notice and no form. Because the catalogue serves no irreversible flag, acknowledgement boxes are derived: `irreversible:<step>` for `redact_object` or any step whose adverse value is not "never", and `egress:<step>` for an egress kind. A range of one `evaluate_code` step is that step's test and shows only it.
7. **Recorded messages (C126.4; default).** The sandbox searches serve bodies unmasked, so the console posts the recipient to a server action (never in a URL), keeps only the started run's records, and returns subject and text only to a principal with `pii:view`; without it the origin shows and the body reads "Hidden without pii:view". A record's step-id is the step run's `wfst_` id, shown as served. Manual-run results are client state from a server action and never enter an RSC payload.
8. **Manual runs (C118.5).** Ids are split on lines, spaces or commas and checked by `anyIdSchema`; a malformed id is refused at `/data/attributes/object-ids/<i>` and never echoed. A CSV file is read as text. A refusal code without words reads "refused (<code>)". The section shows on the workflow page with `workflows:trigger` and a live version.
9. **Secret edit (C125.2).** The edit dialog always sends description (empty as null), allowed-hosts and allowed-placements `{headers, body-pointers}` whole, with no value control. A refusal is shown at its list by position. The words say a production value needs a production user session.
10. **C143.7 settings.** Each workflow setting shows only when the server reports it. `workflow-http-hosts` and `workflow-sms-countries` each have a box and a `-shown` marker; an unticked box means null. Ports are checked by entry pointer in the console, other entries by the contract. `saveSettings` drops `workflow-http-private-cidrs` without `settings:privacy`, whatever was posted. The read-only settings view does not yet show the workflow settings (a fixer item).
11. **Decision templates (C128.5).** Three review-first templates (`review-completed-inquiry`, `review-failed-inquiry`, which never approves per C127.1, and `review-report-match`) in the shape of the "Review on report match" recipe, each accepted by the engine's `checkDefinition` in draft and publish mode. An omitted case template is `CASE_TEMPLATE_PLACEHOLDER`, which publish refuses as `reference_unknown`; an omitted basis or contest route stays unset so the production gate refuses it. "Start from a template" creates the workflow, reads its draft's lock version and saves the template there; it never publishes.
12. **Trust Device (C127.9).** Shown on each recorded device of an inquiry's session cards, with words that it changes no check and no signal. Trust takes a snake_case reason code (refused at `/meta/trust-reason` without echo); untrust removes the mark. Neither asks for confirmation, being reversible and audited. The case page's device module shows no trust control or mark. No full-stack step covers it in Phase 8, because device recognition is off by default.
13. **Webhook filters (C143.4).** `workflow-ids`, `case-template-ids` and `list-ids` are sent only when not empty, like the older keys, and the edit form starts from the stored filters; each refusal shows at its own filter.
14. **List purpose, adverse statuses and contest routes (C122.1, C128.3).** A list shows and edits its purpose ("Block: adding an item is an adverse step…"). An account type's contest route is edited in its form and sent only beside a `contest-route-shown` marker, so an older form changes nothing. A transaction type's "Decisions" form offers one box per status of its newest version and sends `adverse-statuses` (possibly empty) and `contest-route` (null when both URL and contact are empty).
15. **Account and case action bindings (C118.7, C140.1, C141.7).** New routes `/account-actions[/id]` and `/case-actions[/id]` under `workflows:write`, linked from `/workflows`; the navigation is unchanged. Create offers running workflows and, for an account action, the account types; case templates are typed as `ctmpl_` ids. Edit sets the label, status (archive), case templates and one prefill path per input of the bound workflow's live version (`prefill.<input>`; empty ones left out), always sent whole. The API refuses a workflow whose trigger does not fit.
16. **TDD gaps and test changes (rule 1).** The four binding pages' tests were written after the pages (the actions they post were RED first). RED commit 31e98253 did not typecheck and was corrected in its GREEN, as were an exact label and the sandbox notice's role in other RED files. No existing test changed an assertion; `forms.test.tsx` changed only how it waits.

### platform-carry

1. **Axe's pure half lives in `e2e/src` (C141.8, C142.6).** `e2e/src/axe.ts` (the tag list `AXE_WCAG_TAGS` and `axeViolationLines`) and `e2e/src/axe.test.ts` (with the lockfile licence guard) sit in `e2e/src`, where the e2e package's coverage gate applies; `e2e/tests/support/ui-axe.ts` (`expectNoAxeViolations`) imports them.
2. **The axe tags (C141.8, C142.6).** Axe runs with `wcag2a`, `wcag2aa`, `wcag21a`, `wcag21aa` and `wcag22aa`. axe-core 4.14 tags no rule `wcag22a`, so that tag is not listed. A violation fails with one line per violation and target (rule id, impact, target, help). No rule is disabled and no element excluded.
3. **Licences.** @axe-core/playwright 4.14.0 and axe-core 4.14.0 are both MPL-2.0 (each by its own LICENSE) and both have a `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` §5 row, allowed as unmodified e2e test tooling. A lockfile test fails any importer other than e2e that depends on either.
4. **Where axe is asserted (R015, 8C Stage 1 amendments 25 and 35).** With no violation on `/workflows`, a workflow, its draft, a run, `/secrets`, an inquiry's decision records, an account's workflow actions, `/cases`, a template version, a queue, `/case-settings` and `/cases/exports`. The case page (`/cases/[id]`) is not asserted until the console fixer fixes, test-first, its `dlitem` violation (`case-sections.tsx` CaseDetails, `dl role=group`) and its `label-content-name-mismatch` violation (the compare viewer's Reset button), and then asserts it after load, after the compare panel is used and once resolved.
5. **K8-33's cause and fix (8B Stage 1a amendment 31).** The cause is not a slow test: the next prompt committed while `useGuidance` still returned the previous prompt's state, and under load the test advanced the fake clock before the new prompt's guidance began. `useGuidance` keeps its state with its prompt key and returns null until that prompt's own guidance starts, so a new prompt never shows the old prompt's message, manual shutter or auto-capture. The K8-33 test is unchanged.
6. **K8-34's cause and fix.** No thread spins; the CPU time is the coverage tracer on the test thread. Both dense-page tests in `services/vision/tests/test_pdf_measure.py` count the page index's checks instead of process CPU: refusal at exactly `MAX_SCENE_CHECKS + 1`, and 0 checks for the 6,600-character page. No product code in `services/vision` changed.
7. **The vision gate may run in appended parts.** Under load the vision suite may run as appended parts (only the last gating coverage), provided no file is dropped.
8. **The full-stack count.** `ui-workflows.spec.ts` gains a fourth test (decision records and workflow actions under axe), so the full-stack suite is 128 tests in 64 files before the 8D console specs; `e2e/README.md`'s count is updated by the fixers.

### api-carry-cases

1. **Load-sensitive tests (V8C-1).** `apps/api/src/jobs/inquiry-expiry.test.ts` proves the redacted-expiry sweep with the job's own batch size of 4 (a full batch plus one redacted inquiry, then two ordinary ones) and asserts the default batch is larger; its 120 s timeout is gone. In `packages/db/src/testing.test.ts` the migrated-template describe runs one cold build in `beforeAll` under the 120 s PGlite hook guard, from an unopenable stored template, ten older templates and an abandoned partial; its tests assert on what that build left. No assertion was dropped.
2. **Migration 0029_phase8d.** One migration holds the two tightened actor CHECKs (K8-14: a user or API-key actor needs actor_id not null), the five sort indexes (R007), the two rendition-pending indexes (K8-28) and the `case_notification_emails` table (K8-20). A later 8D change that needs a column regenerates 0029 rather than adding 0030.
3. **Case sorts (R007).** `created_at` and `updated_at` sort by their own column. `resolved_at` and `sla_expires_at` sort by a key per direction, `coalesce(col, 'infinity')` ascending and `coalesce(col, '-infinity')` descending, each served by an expression index, and the keyset cursor reads the same key. `services/cases/sort.ts` is shared by search and the export selection. `sort-plan.test.ts` explains the real page query for every attribute, direction, first page and cursor page with sequential scans off, and asserts the index name and no Sort node.
4. **Pulling a case (R008).** `pullNextCaseId` takes each queue's head with a LATERAL limit-1 `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` scan and merges the heads, so a pull from N queues locks up to N heads for its short transaction and a concurrent pull skips them. The EXPLAIN budget (ours) is one row read per queue.
5. **Case includes (K8-19).** `GET /cases/:id?include=` serves the queue and the held kinds through their own routes' functions. Each include needs its route's read scope beside `cases:read` and is left out (never 403) without it: case-queue needs `case-queues:read`; reports and report-runs `reports:read`; txns `transactions:read`; graph-queries `graph:read`. An object its route answers 404 or 410 for is left out. A report run is served as `reportRunResource` of its report. case-template and case-comments keep `cases:read` alone.
6. **Export size (R006; default).** No streamed storage put, since sealing a stream would be a new envelope format. `CASE_LIMITS['export-bytes']` is 64 MiB (provisional); the bundle is built case by case, each entry parsed by `caseExportBundleEntrySchema`, and past the limit the export fails with the existing `export_too_large`. No contract change.
7. **Export and file-work recovery (K8-28; default).** `claimCaseExportRow` claims a pending export, or a running one whose claim is older than `CASE_EXPORT_CLAIM_LEASE_MS` (15 minutes, provisional), and stamps `updated_at` with the job's clock. `case-export-sweep` also re-enqueues by id the exports left pending or running, the scans left pending and the renditions left pending for one period (60 s); the job id dedupes one still queued.
8. **Case emails go through an outbox (K8-20, decision 76).** The API case services have no mailer. A `case_notification_emails` row is written in the same transaction as each user notification and each notify-on-create case, and the worker's `case-notification-emails` sweep (30 s) sends them, so no email leaves inside a transaction or from a rolled-back one.
9. **Case email rules (K8-20; default).** One fixed sentence per kind (created, assigned, mentioned, sla-breached) plus the console link; without `OPENVERIFY_DASHBOARD_PUBLIC_URL`, no link and no case id. Recipients are checked at send: active, holding `cases:read`, able to see the template, case not redacted. A failed send is tried three times in all, each claim leased 5 minutes, then given up and logged by count. With no SMTP server configured the queue is emptied. `CASE_NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_KINDS` is created plus the three notification kinds. No new environment variable.
10. **Biometric case attachments (K8-21, P22).** In the C16 pass that has a publisher, an image attachment (JPEG, PNG or WebP, never a PDF) of a case holding a selfie inquiry is redacted once that inquiry is redacted or once its selfie verification is older than the environment's face-template-retention-days (the C42.6 cut). The case's lock-version moves, `case.updated` is staged, and the system audit entry is `case-attachment.redacted {case-id, inquiry-id, retention: biometric}`. There is no history row, because `CASE_HISTORY_KINDS` has no attachment-redaction kind; adding one is a fixer option (core CHECK change plus its words in one commit). `RetentionReport` gains `redactedBiometricAttachments`.
11. **A decline's face-data deletion does not reach case attachments (K8-21; default).** The C42.2 deletion on decline does not redact a case's image attachments; only the retention and inquiry-redaction paths of item 10 do.
12. **The assignment drain (K8-24; default).** `case-assignment-drain` runs every 60 s (provisional) and walks the policies that name sources, up to 100 policies and 50 waiting cases each per pass. Cases go by the matching source's position, then `sla_expires_at` nulls last for an sla-expires-at policy, then created_at and id. Each case is its own transaction: the case row `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, then `assignByPolicyInTx` with the policy named (queue row, then policy row), retried on 40P01 or 40001 three times in all. A round-robin or fewest-open policy that places nobody ends its pass.
13. **Batched case pages (K8-30).** `getWorkflowCases` (packages/db) reads a page in 16 queries whatever its size (window functions for the newest 50 per case, grouped counts), plus one attachment query; `getWorkflowCase` is the same read for one id. `GET /cases` and `POST /cases/search` build pages through `caseResources`. The per-case held-object helpers are removed. Responses are unchanged.
14. **The event trigger's subject filter (K8-10).** `eventTriggerConfigSchema` gains an optional `filter` of `webhookFiltersSchema` (trigger-verification is unchanged). Publish spreads its keys at the top of `workflow_triggers.filter`, beside criteria and on-null, where the worker already reads them. The golden is `contracts/fixtures/workflows/definition-event-filter.json`.
15. **The report wait index (request of api-carry-engine, K8-7).** `actionWaitEvents` answers `REPORT_TERMINAL_EVENTS` for `rescreen_account_reports`, as for `run_report` and `rerun_report`.
16. **Drain index (R007, R008).** The drain's waiting-case read uses the existing indexes; an EXPLAIN-tested index is added only if a template source's backlog grows large.
17. **K8-27, decided and passed to the fixers.** Following the `definition_invalid` rule, three named case problems join `CASE_PROBLEM_STATUSES`: `case_template_invalid` (422; `errors[]` keep the field-schema, transition-map, `field_type_changed` and notify-on-create codes), `case_search_invalid` (422; `errors[]` keep the `search_*` codes) and `attachment_scan_failed` (409, answered by the attachment content and original routes for scan status error). Each lands in one commit with its golden problem under `contracts/fixtures/problems/cases/` and its `CASE_PROBLEM_WORDS` entry in `apps/dashboard/src/components/cases/case-words.ts`, since that record is typed by `CaseProblemCode`; then the OpenAPI snapshot is regenerated and the Android gate run.

### api-carry-engine

1. **The create_case SLA test (V8C-2).** It reads the step's injected clock and asserts the deadline is exactly that clock plus 2 h; no wall-clock bound or margin remains.
2. **The dispatcher's rotation (R004; amends C117.8's implementation).** No per-environment ready-work row and no migration: `listEnvironmentsWithReadySteps` is a recursive skip scan of `workflow_run_steps_ready_idx` with two limit-1 probes per environment ordered by the index (`due_at <= now`; `due_at is null`), each with `enqueued_at is null`, ordered by `last_served_at asc nulls first` then environment id. An EXPLAIN ANALYZE test in the db integration project bounds the rows read.
3. **Every visited environment takes its turn (R004; amends 8A Stage 3 amendment 46).** When the sweep visits an environment, it writes `last_served_at` before admission whether or not it admits anything, so an environment at its in-flight cap, or whose ready steps all wait behind an action cap, goes to the back of the window. Only the sweep writes the slot; completions never do.
4. **Code runner backpressure (R005; amends 8A Stage 2 amendment 12; C123.5 gains a limit).** `EVALUATE_CODE_LIMITS['waiting-per-child']` is 4 (provisional). Past pool size × 4 waiting evaluations the pool answers at once with `sandbox_crashed`, reason `runner_busy` (a new `CodeRunnerFailureReason`), and nothing runs; the step's retry decides as for any `sandbox_crashed`.
5. **Per-evaluation timeouts (R005).** The client's timer (limit + 250 ms kill grace + 30 s margin) fails that evaluation alone with `socket_timeout` and keeps the connection. A late value, error or crashed frame for that id is dropped; its console lines stay capped at 50. Past 64 unanswered ids (`ABANDONED_MAX`, ours, provisional) the connection ends.
6. **Disconnects (R005).** On the server each connection's signal aborts when it closes, and the pool drops that connection's still-waiting jobs before a child takes them. An evaluation already running ends at its own deadline. No new frame; `CODE_RUNNER_PROTOCOL_VERSION` stays 1 and the child is unchanged.
7. **An action wait's snapshot is taken before the action reads (K8-6; amends 8A Stage 3 amendment 84 and C119.3 for action waits).** The engine takes the wait's snapshot as the first statement of a transaction handler's transaction, or just before an outside handler, and carries it to the wait registration (`currentWorkflowSnapshot(tx)`, optional `WorkflowWaitEntry.snapshot`). What committed before the snapshot the handler sees; what committed after it the registration's check or the wait-match job resolves. Wait steps, time waits and the one-key wait are unchanged, as are `rerun_report`'s outputs.
8. **A new action, `rescreen_account_reports` v1 (K8-7; C127.12, C140.5; ours).** It creates a new one-off report (origin workflow) for each monitored report of the account whose query reads a field that changed; the handler builds the query, because only the server knows which account field each query member reads (the report engine's query-source rule, `-` as `_`). The re-screen recipe `rescreen-on-account-field-change` calls it on `{{ trigger.id }}` and needs nothing configured, so C127.12's "the recipe calls run_report" now reads "calls rescreen_account_reports, which creates reports as run_report does". Errors: `object_not_found`, `subject_redacted`, `report_selector_limit` (more than 100 monitored reports, nothing made) and `report_input_missing` (with the field). It is not in the publish rules' `EVIDENCE_ACTIONS`.
9. **Its scope (K8-7; default).** "Monitored" means `next_run_at` set, not paused, not redacted. A report is stale when a query member's account field exists and differs. The old report and its monitoring are not changed.
10. **Its grant and output (K8-7; default).** Grant `reports:write` plus `accounts:read` with no pii, since values go from the account into the report service and never into the run; internal, exactly once, stage ga; it emits the report terminal events. Output `{ reports[] {report-id, status, has-match}, count, monitored-count, any-match, errored[] }`; `any-match` is null unless every new report is ready, and null when count is 0 (nothing re-screened is no answer, never false).
11. **Test changes for K8-7.** The registry's key counts move from 35 to 36 and 43 to 44; `recipes.test.ts` expects `needs-configuration: []` for the re-screen recipe, and its "publish refuses an unfilled draft" test uses `review-on-report-match`. No assertion was dropped. Until the publish-time wait index covers the action (now landed, api-carry-cases item 15), the action records its own wait-index row on first wait, as 8A amendment 36 allows.
12. **A test run's synthetic payload (K8-12, C119; default).** A non-API trigger's synthetic payload is checked against the definition's trigger root type (`triggerRootType`, now exported from `@openverify/workflow-engine`). It may be partial: a member left out reads as null, so a route on it follows its on-null. A member present must be one the view holds, of its type; an explicit null where the view holds none is refused. A refusal is 422 `trigger_input_invalid` at `/data/attributes/trigger-payload<member pointer>`, never echoing the value (C143.5). trigger-api keeps its check by declared inputs, and a payload of `{}` stays valid.
13. **TDD notes (rule 1).** Two R005 tests, one K8-12 list-member test and two coverage tests were written with or after their GREEN; K8-6's outside test was shown to fail with the snapshot at registration. The K8-6 race test's assertion changed between RED and GREEN, from "ready" to "resolved by that event, then succeeded", and still fails on the RED code.

## Workflow 8D review amendments

Recorded by the API fixer of the Workflow 8D review. Evidence: `docs/tdd/phase8-8d-review-fixes-api.tdd.md` (the console fixer's is `phase8-8d-review-fixes-console.tdd.md`). Each item binds every later phase. Items marked (default) are on the owner's review list. No contract, route schema or OpenAPI document changed.

### API, packages and docs

1. **A refused field schema's pointer (K8-38).** A field problem at publish names `/data/attributes/field-schemas/<i>/...`, the walker's own pointer under the attributes prefix, as the transition-map problems already did; the console places it at that field.
2. **Rendition hooks tested on the real hook (K8-29, Stage 1b item 47, plan item 112).** A replaced flow upload, `POST /transactions/:id/redact-biometrics` and a rejected handoff each discard the file's rendition pages in their transaction and delete the pages' objects after the commit, each proven on a real `ready` rendition (`apps/api/src/test-support/rendition-pages.ts`); each test fails when its hook is handed no file. No source changed.
3. **One RustFS starter (K8-37; as K7B-13 for Postgres).** Every Testcontainers RustFS starts through `startIntegrationRustfs` (`apps/api/src/test-support/integration-rustfs.ts`): the pinned `rustfs/rustfs:1.0.3`, Testcontainers' `/health/ready` wait at its own 60 s, then a signed S3 `ListBuckets` through the mapped port polled for up to 60 s; a start that throws or stays unready is followed by a fresh container, at most twice, each retry on stderr. The loop is `startWithRetries` of `@openverify/db/testing`, which gains the optional names `service` and `probeName` (Postgres's lines are unchanged). `integration-rustfs.test.ts` fails a RustFS image named in any other source file. The starter is in `apps/api` because `packages/db` has no `testcontainers` dependency of its own. No start timeout was raised and no test is retried.
4. **An attachment never served is never rendered, and its rendition ends (R008; amends K8-28's recovery).** For a scan of `infected` or `error` (both final), the rendition job records the rendition `failed` with `rendition_unavailable` under the row's lock, without reading the object or calling vision, so `listPendingRenditionSources` stops listing it and readers see `failed`, not `pending`. `case_attachments_rendition_pending_idx` and migration 0029 are unchanged. Serving is unchanged (the scan answers 409 first). `docs/cases/attachments.md` says so.
5. **Branch tests of runs and cases (K8-13).** Tests of what resume, rerun, cancel and a test run refuse, what masking shows with nothing to go on, a status tag, a queue exit, a comment's redaction, `inquiry-creator`'s refusals, the case pages' cursors and an unmeasurable SLA; no branch reached only by a race inside a held lock was forced. Merged API coverage is in the evidence's gates section.
6. **e2e measurement.** With Workflow 8D's `ui-*` specs the full-stack suite is 137 tests in 66 files, in shards of 46, 46 and 45 that took 4.2, 5.3 and 4.6 minutes plus 1.3 for the stack start at a load of about 1 to 3, sending 9 OTP codes from 127.0.0.1 (at `c2260b50`). Three shards stand, each under 8 minutes; CI's one e2e job, Playwright's 45-minute `globalTimeout` and the job's 70 minutes are unchanged. `e2e/README.md` says so.

### Console

Console fixes: recorded by the orchestrator.
